# Narsai's Homilies on Creation

**Author**: Narsaï  
**Series**: PO Tome 34  
**Columns**: pp. 526–706  

[p. 526] {#po.34.p526}

### ON THE CONSTITUTION OF CREATURES
ܥܠ ܬܘܩܢܐ ܕܒܪ̈ܝܬܐ ܐܡܪܢ ܥܕܪܝܢܝ ܒܪ̈ܚܡܝܟ ܘܠܫܘܡܠܝܐ ܡܢܥܝܢܝ ܐܡܝܢ
O Our Lord, help me by Thy mercy and lead me to the end. Amen.

### The creation of light
ܐܝܬܝܐ ܕܨܒܬ ܪܘܡܐ ܘܥܘܡܩܐ ܒܪ̈ܡܙܐ ܕܚܝܠܗ ܒܝܕ ܒܪܬ ܩܠܐ ܨܒܼܐ ܕܢܒܕܩ ܥܠ ܬܘܩܢ̈ܘ̈ܗܝ.
The Being who, with a sign of His power, adorned (the world) above and below, willed to announce His creations by means of a word.
ܒܪ̈ܫܝܬ ܬܩܢ ܫܡܝܐ ܘܐܪ̈ܥܐ ܘܡܝ̈ܐ ܘܐܐܪ ܘܟܢܫܝ̈ ܪ̈ܘܡܐ ܘܢܘܗܪ̈ܐ ܘܚܫܟܐ ܕܦܪܿܝܣ ܥܠ ܟܠ.
In the beginning He constituted the heaven and the earth, the water and the air, the assemblies from above, the light and the darkness spread over all things.
ܒܪܿܐ ܠܒܪܝܬܐ ܡ̣ܢ ܠܐ ܡܕܡ ܒܖܪܡܙܐ ܙܥܘܪ̈ܐ ܘܐܝܟ ܕܒܢܚܬܐ ܟܣܝ ܐܦܝ̈ܗ̇ ܒܝܕ ܚܫܘܟܐ.
With a small sign He created the world from nothing, and with darkness, He covered its surface, as with a garment.
ܬܗܖܪܿܐ ܪܒܐ ܣܥܼܖܿ ܒܪ̈ܘܝܐ ܒܪ̈ܫ ܬܘܩܢܘ̈ܗܝ ܕܒܢܼܐ ܒܝܬܐ ܘܐܛܡ ܟܘܘ̈ܗܝ ܘܡܠܝܗܝ ܚܫܟܐ.
At the beginning of His creations, the Creator realized a great wonder: He built a dwelling, obstructed its windows, and filled it with darkness.
ܒܝܕ ܥܠܠܬ̈ܐ ܨܒܼܐ ܕܢܒܕܩ ܥܠ ܬܘܩܢܘܗܝ ܘܠܝܘܠܦܢܐ ܕܡܠܝܠܘܬܐ ܬܗܝ ܥܒܕ̈ܘ̈ܗܝ.
By pretexts, He willed to announce His creations, and He deferred His works, to instruct the rational (beings) in them.
ܒܪ̈ܝ̣ܢ ܗܼܘܘ ܟܢܫ̈ܐ ܕܫܡܝܢ̈ܐ ܒܪ̈ܫܝܬ ܕܗܘܬ ܘܠܝܘܠܦܢܗܘܢ ܐܫܼܡܥ ܩܠܐ ܕܢܗܘܐ ܢܘܗܖܪܿܐ.
The assemblies of the celestial (beings) had been created in the beginning, and to instruct them, He made a word heard: "Let there be light"!
ܢܗܘܐ ܢܘܗܖܪ̈ܐ ܡܿܢܝܗܪ ܟܠܐ ܩܥܼܐ ܒܪܘܝܐ ܘܟ̣ܢܫ ܐܢܘܢ ܠܫܡܝܢ̈ܐ ܠܡܫܡܥ ܬܗܖܪ̈ܐ.
"Let the light illuminate all things"!, cried the Creator, and He gathered the celestial (beings) so that they might hear the wonder.
ܐܘ ܫܝܦܘܪܐ ܕܩܠ ܒܪ̈ܘܝܐ ܕܐܙܥܩ ܡܢܫܠܝ ܘܐܖ̈ܗܒ ܐܢܘܢ ܠܟܢܫܝ̈ ܪܘܡܐ ܒܩܠ ܢܥܡ̈ܬܗ.
O trumpet of the voice of the Creator that He suddenly made resound, and which terrified the assemblies from above at the sound of its intonations!
ܐܘ ܦܘܩܕܢܐ ܕܥܡ ܒܪܬ ܩܠܐ ܗܼܘܐ ܣܘܥܪ̈ܢܐ ܐܘ ܐܘܡܢܐ ܕܥܡܠܝܗ ܪܖܡܙܗ ܒܟܠ ܡܐ ܕܥܒܼܕ.
O commandment that was executed at the same time as the word (resounded)! O artisan whose sign operated all that He did!
ܢܗܘܐ ܢܘܗܖ̈ܐ ܢܚܬܐ ܦܐܝܐ ܕܡܨܒܬ ܟܠ ܘܒܗ ܢܬܟܣܘܢ ܪܘܡܐ ܘܥܘܡܩܐ ܘܟܠ ܡܐ ܕܒܗܘܢ.
Let the light be the beautiful garment that adorns all things, and let it cover the (world) above and below and all that (is) in them!
ܫܠܸܡ ܣܘܥܪܖ̈ܢܐ ܥܡ ܦܘܩܕܢܐ ܘܗܼܘܐ ܢܘܗܪ̈ܐ ܘܪܕܦܗ ܠܚܫܟܐ ܘܐܦܼܨܚ ܠܐܐܪ̈ ܕܟܡܝܪ̈ܐ ܗܘܼܬ.
The work was completed at the same time as the commandment, and the light was; it dissipated the darkness and rejoiced the atmosphere that was lugubrious.
ܩܥܘ ܣܕܪ̈ܝܗܘܢ ܕܪ̈ܘܡܐ ܒܪܝܟ ܒܪܘܝܐ ܕܒܪܐ ܘܐܨܒܬܗ ܠܢܘܗܪܐ ܫܦܝܪܐ ܘܐܦܨܚ ܟܠ.
The choirs of the assemblies from above exclaimed: "Blessed be the Creator, who has created and adorned the beautiful light, and has rejoiced all things"!
ܗܼܘ ܠܗ ܫܒܚ ܥܠ ܬܘܩܢܗ ܕܫܦܝܪ ܥܒܼܕܗ ܘܐܤܗܕ ܩܠܗ ܥܠ ܪܒܘܬܗ ܕܚܝܠ ܟܠ ܡܫܟܚ.
It is He who glorified it, as to its constitution, for He made it good, and His word attested that His majesty was all-powerful.


[p. 528] {#po.34.p528}
ܐܘ ܒܪܬ ܩܠܐ ܕܡܸܢ ܠܐ ܡܕܡ ܥܒܕܬܼܿ ܡܕܡ ܐܘ ܦܘܩܕܢܐ ܕܠܐ ܫܘܚܪܬܗ ܓܠܝܙܘܬ ܡܕܡ
O word that made something from nothing! O commandment that the lack of being did not hinder!
ܒܝܕ ܒܪܬ ܩܠܐ ܨܒ̣ܐ ܒܪ̈ܘܝܐ ܕܢܠܦ ܐܢܘܢ ܠܚܝܠܘ̈ܬܐ ܡܬܝܕܥ̈ܢܐ ܥܠ ܐܝܬܘܬܗ.
By a word the Creator willed to instruct the intelligent powers of His Essence.
ܠܐ ܪ̈ܓܝܫܝܢ ܗܘܼܘ ܕܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܡܡܬܘܡ ܩܕܡ ܒܪ̈ܝܬܗ ܘܠܐ ܝܕܥܝܢ ܗܘܼܘ ܡܢܘ ܨܒܬ ܠܩܢܘܡܝ̈ܝܗܘܢ.
(For) they had not perceived that He existed eternally, before His creatures, and they did not know who had adorned them themselves.
ܒܩܠܐ ܕܐܫܼܡܥ ܕܢܗܘܐ ܢܘܗܖ̈ܐ ܩܢܼܘ ܒܘܝܢܐ ܘܥܡ ܣܘܥܪ̈ܢܐ ܩܥܼܘ ܬܫܒܘܚܬܐ ܠܡܬܩܢ ܟܠܐ.
They understood it thanks to the word he made heard: "Let there be light!" and upon its realization, they proclaimed praise to the one who constituted all things.
ܒܗܢܐ ܦܘܪܣܐ ܐܫܼܡܥ ܩܠܐ ܠܐ ܩܢܐ ܩܠܐ ܕܢܠܦ ܐܢܘܢ ܠܪ̈ܘܚܢܝܐ ܚܝܠ ܟܣܝܘܬܗ.
That is why he who has no voice made a word heard, in order to instruct the spiritual (beings) of the power of his hidden (nature).
ܪܒܐ ܗܼܘܐ ܠܗܘܢ ܩܠܐ ܕܡܠܠ ܒܪ̈ܫܝܬ ܒܐܪ̈ܥܐ ܘܐܠܦ ܐܢܘܢ ܕܚܕ ܒܪ̈ܘܝܐ ܒܪܐ ܟܠ ܡܕܡ.
The voice that expressed itself at the beginning on earth was an important (thing) for them; and it taught them that a single Creator created all things.
ܟܕܢܘ ܩܠܝ̈ܗܘܢ ܒܚܘܒܐ ܫܠܡܐ ܘܣܓܕܘ ܩܘܕܡܘܗܝ ܘܝܒܒܘ ܟܠܗܘܢ ܩܠܐ ܕܩܘܕܫܐ ܠܡܠܟܐ ܕܪ̈ܘܡܐ.
Having united their voices in a perfect love, they prostrated themselves before him, and all their holy voices sang for the king of the world above.
ܗܘܝܗ ܕܢܘܗܖܪ̈ܐ ܐܠܦ ܐܢܘܢ ܚܝܠ ܒܪ̈ܘܝܐ ܘܟܼܕܢ ܐܢܘܢ ܒܢܝܪ̈ܐ ܢܝܚܐ ܕܩܠ ܬܘܕܝܬܗ.
The existence of light taught them the power of the Creator, and subjected them to the sweet yoke of thanksgiving.
ܫܠܸܡ ܚܕ ܝܘܡܐ ܠܠܝܐܝܡܡ ܒܫܘܠܡ ܢܘܗܪܐ ܘܫܖܪ̈ܝ ܚܫܟܐ ܕܢܕܪܘܫ ܐܘܪ̈ܚܐ ܠܝܘܡܐ ܕܬܪ̈ܝܢ.
With the completion of the light, a day was completed - a night and a day - and the darkness began to pave the way for a second day.
ܛܟܣܐ ܪܒܐ ܥܒܕ ܠܬܪܝܗܘܢ ܒܫܘܠܛܢܢܝ̈ܗܘܢ ܕܠܐ ܢܬܥܠܒܘܢ ܚܕ ܡܸܢ ܚܒܪܗ ܒܝܕ ܪ̈ܗܛܝܗܘܢ.
He established a great order between the two regarding their powers, so that they would not harm one another by their courses.

### The creation of the firmament
ܒܝܘܡܐ ܕܬܪ̈ܝܢ ܢܦܼܝܩ ܦܘܩܕܢܐ ܕܢܗܘܐ ܪ̈ܩܝܥܐ ܘܦܲܠܓ ܡܝ̈ܐ ܦܠܓܐ ܠܪ̈ܘܡܐ ܘܦܠܓܐ ܠܐܪܥܐ.
The second day, the following order took place: "Let there be a firmament!" and it divided the waters, half for the world above and half for the earth.
ܢܗܘܐ ܪ̈ܩܝܥܐ ܒܡܨܥܬ ܡܝ̈ܐ ܡܐܢܐ ܩܫܝܐ ܘܢܼܛܥܢ ܡܝ̈ܐ ܠܥܠ ܡܸܢ ܚܨܗ ܕܠܐ ܢܣܬܝܛ.
"Let the firmament become a solid element in the midst of the waters, and let it support the water above its surface so that it may not be burned!"
ܐܘ ܦܘܩܕܢܐ ܕܩܫܝ ܡܝ̈ܐ ܟܝܢܐ ܪܦܝܚܐ ܘܥܒܼܕ ܐܢܘܢ ܟܝܢܐ ܩܫܝܐ ܕܢܛܥܢ ܡܝ̈ܐ.
O command that solidified the water, a liquid element, and made of it a solid element that (could) carry the water!
ܐܘ ܡܬܩܠܐ ܕܦܠܓ ܡܝ̈ܐ ܩܒܝܬܐ ܪܒܐ ܘܟܢܫ ܐܢܘܢ ܠܬܪ̈ܝܢ ܝܡܡܝܢ̈ܐ ܠܪ̈ܘܡܐ ܘܥܘܡܩܐ.
O balance that divided the great reserve of water, and gathered it into two oceans, in the world above and below!
ܐܝܟ ܬܛܠܝܠܐ ܠܒܝܬܐ ܕܕܠܬܚܬ ܡܬܚ ܠܪܩܝܥܐ ܕܢܗܘܐ ܡܡܠܐ ܕܘܟܬ ܐܪܥܐ ܠܒܝܬܐ ܕܠܥܠ.
Like a roof over the house that is below, he extended the firmament, such that it takes the place of earth for the dwelling that is above.
ܒܕܡܘܬ ܢܚܬܐ ܚܘܪ̈ܓܘܢܐ ܙܩܪܗ ܪܡܙܐ ܘܨܪ ܒܗ ܛܒܥ̈ܐ ܫܡܫܐ ܘܣܗܪ̈ܐ ܕܠܩ̈ܐ ܕܢܘܗܪ̈ܐ.
Like a white-colored garment, a sign (of God) wove it and fashioned therein precious stones, the sun and the moon, luminous torches.


[p. 530] {#po.34.p530}

### The creation of the luminaries
ܬܪ̈ܝܢ ܢܗܝܪ̈ܐ ܕܠܩ̈ܐ ܕܢܘܗܪ̈ܐ ܬܠܐ ܒܪ̈ܩܝܥܐ ܕܒܕܠܩܝ̈ܗܘܢ ܢܚܕܘܢ ܠܐܪ̈ܥܐ ܠܩܘܝܡ ܐܢܫܐ.
He suspended in the firmament two luminaries, luminous torches, so that they might rejoice the earth with their torches, for the preservation of man.
ܡ̣ܢ ܗ̇ܘ ܢܘܗܪܐ ܩܕܡܐ ܕܗܘܐ ܥܒܼܕ ܢܗܝܪ̈ܐ ܘܡܢܗ ܦܠܓ ܠܫܡܫܐ ܘܣܗܪ̈ܐ ܕܠܩ̈ܐ ܙܥܘܪ̈ܐ.
He made the luminaries from the first light that existed, and he distributed it between the sun and the moon, small shreds.
ܡܢܪ̈ܬܐ ܕܫܦܝܐ ܩܒܼܥ ܒܐܣܦܝܪܐ ܕܫܡܫܐ ܪܒܐ ܘܬܛܪܗ ܕܢܘܗܪܐ ܣܡ ܒܐܣܦܝܪܐ ܕܣܗܪܐ ܪ̈ܓܝܐ.
To the sphere of the great sun, he fixed the limpid chandelier, and he placed the dim light at the sphere of the cool moon.
ܡܢܬܐ ܕܪ̈ܒܐ ܘܐܦ ܢܗܝܪ̈ܐ ܝܗܼܒ ܠܐܝܡܡܐ ܘܗܝ ܕܙܥܘܪ̈ܐ ܘܐܦ ܥܡܘܛܐ ܠܠܠܝܐ ܫܠܝܐ.
He gave to the day the largest and also the most luminous part, and to the silent night, the smallest and also the most obscure part.
ܬܚܼܡ ܪܗܛܐ ܕܫܡܫܐ ܘܣܗܪ̈ܐ ܫܥ̈ܐ ܬܪ̈ܥܣܪ ܕܡܐ ܕܫܠܸܡ ܚܕ ܢܬܠ ܐܬܪܐ ܕܢܪ̈ܕܐ ܚܒܪܗ.
He limited the course of the sun and the moon to twelve hours, so that, when one has finished (its course), it yields the place so that the other may advance.
ܒܬܪ̈ܬܝܢ ܦܢܝ̈ܢ ܥܒܼܕ ܡܪ̈ܕܝܬܗ ܕܫܡܫܐ ܪܒܐ ܕܠܐ ܢܬܕܘܕ ܛܟܣܐ ܕܐܘܪ̈ܚܗ ܕܣܗܪܐ ܙܥܘܪܐ.
He established the path of the great sun to the two extremities (of the world), so as not to disturb the order of the path of the small moon.
ܒܥܘܡܪܐ ܕܐܢܫܐ ܪܕܐ ܒܐܘܪ̈ܚܗ ܒܓܘ ܐܝܡܡܐ ܘܝܗܒ ܐܬܪܐ ܠܣܗܪܐ ܙܥܘܪܐ ܕܢܪ̈ܕܐ ܒܐܘܪ̈ܚܗ.
Over the habitat of men, it travels its path during the day, (then) it yields the place to the small moon so that it may travel its path.
ܒܪܗܛܐ ܐܡܝܢ ܚܙܩ ܪܕܐ ܒܬܪܬܝܢ ܦܢܝ̈ܢ ܟܕ ܟܪܟ ܠܗ ܠܚܘܕܪܐ ܕܪ̈ܘܡܐ ܠܠܝ ܐܝܡܡ.
Moving in a continuous course, it advances toward the two extremities (of the world), turning night and day around the globe above.
ܒܥܕܢ ܨܦܪܐ ܕܢܚ ܨܐܕܝܢ ܒܕܡܘܬ ܚܬܢܐ ܘܒܚܘܪ̈ܐ ܕܐܣܦܝܪܗ ܨܡܚܗ ܡܦܨܚ ܠܐܪ̈ܥܐ.
In the morning it appears before us, like a bridegroom; and by the brightness of its radiant sphere it rejoices the earth.
ܒܥܕܢ ܪ̈ܡܫܐ ܡܫܢܐ ܘܚܙܩ ܐܝܟ ܪܒ ܚܝܠܐ ܕܡܐ ܕܐܪܓܫ ܒܗ ܚܫܟܐ ܕܫܢܝ ܥܠ ܘܐܫܬܠܛ.
(But) in the evening, it withdraws and goes away, like an army commander, for, when the darkness perceives that it is withdrawing, they enter as sovereign.
ܣܗܪܐ ܙܥܘܪܐ ܒܝܕ ܫܘܚܠܦ̈ܐ ܥܒܕ ܪܗܛܗ ܘܒܚܕ ܝܪܚܐ ܕܕ̈ܓܐ ܬܪܥܣܪ ܣܠܩ ܘܢܚܬ.
The small moon carries out its course by phases, and it rises and descends twelve degrees per month.
ܠܐ ܡܥܘܟ ܠܗ ܢܘܗܪܗ ܕܣܗܪܐ ܠܫܡܫܐ ܪܒܐ ܡܐ ܕܦܓܥ ܒܗ ܒܫܘܠܛܢ ܪܘܡܐ ܒܫܒܝܠ ܕܢܚܗ.
The light of the moon does not obstruct the great sun, when it attains sovereignty over the world above, on the path of its rising.

### The two dwellings, terrestrial and celestial
ܠܚܝ̈ܐ ܕܐܢܫܐ ܠܐ ܒܥܐ ܘܐ ܒܪ̈ܘܝܐ ܒܝܬܐ ܪܒܐ ܘܣܡ ܫܬܐܣܘ̈ܗܝ ܒܡܝ̈ܐ ܕܓܝܐ ܘܫܪܪ ܐܢܝܢ.
The Creator built a great dwelling for the life of man, and placed its foundations on the fluid water, and he hardened it.
ܡܬܚ ܬܛܠܝܠܗ ܡ̣ܢ ܪ̈ܦܝܘܬܐ ܕܡܝ̈ܐ ܪ̈ܦܝܐ ܘܟܫܐ ܡܝ̈ܐ ܠܥܠ ܡ̣ܢ ܚܨܗ ܐܝܟ ܩܒܝܬܐ.
He extended its roof from the inconsistency of the fluid water, and above its ridge, he heaped up the waters as (in) a reservoir.
ܬܠܐ ܘܢܕܠܠܐ ܫܡܫܐ ܘܣܗܪ̈ܐ ܒܬܛܠܝܠ ܒܝܬܗ ܘܐܪ̈ܡܝ ܡܫܚܐ ܪܡܙܐ ܕܚܝܠܗ ܘܐܕܠܩ ܐܢܘܢ.
He suspended candelabras from the roof of his dwelling, the sun and the moon, and a sign of his power poured oil (therein) and lit them.
ܫܒܝܚ ܒܪ̈ܘܝܐ ܦܐܐ ܬܘܩܢܗ ܘܪܒ ܚܘܫܒܗ ܕܩܕܡ ܝܕܥ ܩܕܡ ܬܘܩܢܗ ܡܢ ܚܫܚ ܠܗ.
Glorious is the creator, beautiful his creation, immense his reflection, he who knew in advance, before his creation, what it would need!


[p. 532] {#po.34.p532}
ܥܘܡܪ̈ܐ ܥܦܝܦܐ ܥܒܼܕ ܠܒܖ̈ܝܬܐ ܡܬܿܩܢ ܟܠܐ ܬܪ̈ܝܢ ܡܕܝܪ̈ܝܢ ܨܒܬ ܘܒܢܼܐ ܠܬܪ̈ܝܢ ܥܠܡ̈ܐ.
He who constituted all things made for the creation a double dwelling; he adorned two habitations and built two worlds.
ܥܒܸܕ ܬܚܬܝܐ ܕܢܗܘܐ ܥܗܝܢ ܠܡܝܘܬܘܬܐ ܘܐܿܣܢ ܘܡܠܝܗܝ ܦܐܪ̈ܐ ܕܚܫܚܝܢ ܠܦܓܪ̈ܢܝܐ. ܥܒ̣ܕ ܥܠܝܐ ܥܘܡܪܐ ܦܐܝܐ ܕܡܠܐ ܛܘܒ̈ܐ ܕܢܬܒܣܡܘܢ ܒܗ ܪ̈ܘܚܢܝܐ ܪ̈ܘܚܢܐܝܬ.
He made the lower (dwelling) such that it befits the mortal (condition); he heaped up and filled it with fruits appropriate for corporeal (beings). He made the upper (dwelling) a beautiful dwelling filled with goods, so that the spiritual (beings) might enjoy them spiritually.
ܠܐܪ̈ܥܢܝܝܐ ܥܒܕ ܨܒܘ̈ܬܐ ܐܪ̈ܥܢܝܬܐ ܘܠܫܡܝܢ̈ܐ ܡܠܟ ܛܒ̈ܬܐ ܫܡܝܢܝ̈ܬܐ. ܒܬܪ̈ܝܢ ܥܘܡܪ̈ܐ ܡܬܒ̈ܩܐ ܗܘܼܐ ܗܽܘ ܝܕܥ ܟܠ ܘܥܕܠܐ ܢܗܘܘܢ ܫܡܝܐ ܘܐܪ̈ܥܐ ܒܗܘܢ ܚܳܐܪ ܗܘܼܐ. ܩܕܝܡ ܗܘܼܐ ܓܝܪ ܨܒܝܢ ܚܘܒܗ ܩܕܡ ܬܘܩܢܗ ܘܨܝܪ̈ܝܢ ܗܘܼܘ ܠܗ ܒܝܕ ܨܒܝܢܗ ܥܒ̈ܕܐ ܕܥܒܕ.
For the terrestrial (beings), he made terrestrial things; and to the celestials, he promised celestial goods. The Omniscient considered the two dwellings and he saw them before the heaven and the earth existed. For his will of love was prior to his creation and the works he realized were fashioned by him by means of his will.
ܫܟܠܠ ܘܒܢܼܐ ܫܡܝܐ ܘܐܪ̈ܥܐ ܒܝܬܐ ܪ̈ܘܝܚܐ ܘܐ̈ܣܢ ܘܡܠܝܗܝ ܥܘܬܪ̈ܐ ܪ̈ܒܐ ܠܕܥܡܿܪ ܒܗ. ܟܢܫ ܡܝ̈ܐ ܘܥܒܸܕ ܝܡܡ̈ܐ ܩܒܝܐ ܪܒܐ ܘܙܘܓ ܐܢܘܢ ܪܡܙܐ ܕܚܝܠܗ ܘܐܘܠܕܘ ܪ̈ܚܫܐ. ܐܘܠܕ ܥܘܒܐ ܕܡܝ̈ܐ ܪ̈ܦܝܐ ܓܘܫܡ̈ܐ ܥܒܝ̈ܐ ܘܡ̣ܢ ܝܩܝܪ̈ܐ ܥܒܕ ܦܪܚܬܐ ܕܩܠܝܠ ܓܦܗܿ. ܦܩܼܕ ܠܗ̇ ܠܐܪܥܐ ܘܒܛܢܬܼ ܘܝܠܕܬܼ ܓܢܣ̈ܐ ܘܛܘܗܡ̈ܐ ܒܥܝܪܐ ܕܫܝܢܐ ܘܚܝܘܬ ܫܝܐ ܘܟܠ ܓܢܣܢܝ̈ܢ.
He built and constructed the heaven and the earth (as) a vast dwelling; He piled it up and filled it, for the one who would inhabit it, with immense wealth. The creation of the animal and vegetable orders: He gathered the waters and made the seas an immense reservoir; and His powerful sign united them, and they brought forth reptiles. The bosom of the fluid water brought forth dense bodies; and from heavy (bodies), (God) made the bird with the light wing. He gave order to the earth, and it conceived and brought forth the species and the races, the domestic animals and the wild beasts, and all the species.
ܦܠܚܗ̇ ܠܐܪ̈ܥܐ ܘܐܪܡܝ ܒܓܘܗ̇ ܙܪ̈ܥܐ ܕܚܝ̈ܐ ܘܝܥܘ ܙܪ̈ܥܐ ܘܩܡܼܘ ܐܝܠܢ̈ܐ ܘܗܒܒܘ ܦܐܪ̈ܐ.
He cultivated the earth, cast into its bosom the seed of life, and the seeds sprouted, the trees sprang up, the fruits blossomed.

### The creation of man
ܫܡܠܝ ܘܓܡܪ ܚܝܠ ܒܪ̈ܘܝܐ ܟܠܡܐ ܕܗܼܘܐ ܘܗܝܕܝܢ ܠܝܪܬܐ ܘܡܪܐ ܕܟܠ ܓܒܠ. ܠܗ ܩܢܝܢܝܢ ܒܟܠ ܡܐ ܕܥܒ̣ܼܕ ܩܠܐ ܐܫܡܥ ܥܠ ܬܘܩܢܗ ܘܟܕ ܓܒܼܠ ܠܗ ܠܥܦܪܗ ܕܐܕܡ ܩܠܐ ܘܡܠܟܐ. ܒܟܠܡܐ ܕܗܼܘܐ ܕܢܗܘܐ ܡܕܡ ܐܡܼܪ ܘܗܘܼܐ ܘܒܬܘܩܝܐ ܕܨܠܡ ܐܢܫܐ ܬܘ ܠܡ ܢܥܒܕ.
The power of the Creator accomplished and completed all that exists; it was then that He fashioned the heir and the master of all goods. He made a word heard concerning his creation, in all that He did, and when He fashioned the dust of Adam, (there was) a word and a counsel. For all that exists, He spoke, so that it might become something, and it was; and for the constitution of man, (His) image, (He said): "Come, let us make..."
ܡܬܼܚ ܠܫܡܝܐ ܘܠܐ ܩܼܪܐ ܠܥܕܪܗ ܚܝܠܐ ܐܚܖ̈ܢܝܐ ܪܩ̣ܥ ܬܘܒ ܠܐܪ̈ܥܐ ܘܠܐ ܐܬܡܟ ܒܒܢܝ̈ ܡܠܟܗ. ܥܒܕ ܟܢܫܝ̈ܗܘܢ ܕܫܡܝܢ̈ܐ ܢܘܪܐ ܘܪ̈ܘܚܐ ܘܠܐ ܓܠܼܐ ܐܪ̈ܙܐ ܕܚܝܠ ܐܝܬܘܬܗ ܩܢܘܡ̈ܐ ܕܥܡܗ. ܒܬܘܩܢ ܐܕܡ ܡܠܟܐ ܚܕܬܐ ܘܐܦ ܚܘܫܒܐ ܟܕ ܡܫܡܥ ܠܗ ܠܩܠ ܚܘܫܒܘ̈ܗܝ ܠܟܢܫܝ̈ ܪ̈ܘܡܐ.
He stretched out the heaven without calling another force to his aid; He also deployed the earth, without consulting his counselors. From fire and wind He made the assemblies of the celestial (beings), and He did not reveal the mystery of the power of His Essence, the Persons who (are) with Him. In the constitution of Adam, (there was) new counsel and reflection, making the expression of His thoughts heard to the assemblies from above.


[p. 534] {#po.34.p534}
ܬܘ ܠܡ ܢܥܒܕ ܐܢܫܐ ܒܨܠܡܢ ܐܦ ܒܕܡܘܬܢ ܘܢܗܘܐ ܫܠܝܛ ܥܠ ܟܠ ܕܗܼܘܐ ܒܕܡܘܬ ܡܠܟܐ. ܡܢܘ ܗܢܐ ܕܡܫܥܠܦ ܐܢܬ ܐܘ ܒܪ̈ܘܝܐ ܡܠܟܐ ܚܕܬܐ ܘܐܦ ܚܘܫܒܐ ܒܥܦܪܗ ܕܐܕܡ. ܠܡܿܢ ܩܪܐ ܐܢܬ ܥܕܪܐ ܥܡܟ ܒܬܘܩܢ ܨܠܡܟ ܘܡܢܘ ܗܘܼܐ ܠܟ ܡܠܟ ܡܠܟܐ ܒܟܠܡܐ ܕܥܼܒܕܬ.
"Come, let us make man in our image and in our likeness, and let him rule, like a king, over all that exists!" Who (then) is he, that you should change, O Creator: new counsel and reflection regarding the dust of Adam? Whom do you call to your aid, to constitute your image? And who is the one who gave you counsel, in all that you did?

### Adam manifests the mystery of the Trinity
ܐܘ ܥܦܪ̈ܢܐ ܕܫܝܛ ܕܚܝܚܗ ܘܪܡ ܬܘܩܢܗ ܕܐܪ̈ܙܐ ܟܣܝ̈ܐ ܡܬܡܠܠܝܢ ܒܗ̇ ܒܓܒܝܠܘܬܗ. ܐܘ ܡܝܘܬܐ ܥܠܝܐ ܘܒܕܩ ܒܝܕ ܬܘܩܢܗ ܩܢܘܡ̈ܐ ܬܠܬܐ ܚܕܐ ܐܝܬܘܬܐ ܕܠܐ ܫܘܪ̈ܝܐ. ܒܝܕ ܪܘܟܒܐ ܕܨܠܡܗ ܕܐܕܡ ܒܕܩ ܐܒܐ ܕܒܫܘܝܘܬܐ ܕܒܪܐ ܘܪ̈ܘܚܐ ܗܼܘܐ ܟܠܡܕܡ. ܟܣܝܗ ܠܐܪ̈ܙܐ ܕܒܘܕܩ ܩܢܘܡ̈ܐ ܡ̣ܢ ܟܠ ܕܗܼܘܐ ܕܕܠܡܐ ܢܪ̈ܢܘܢ ܪ̈ܘܚܢܝܐ ܚܝܠܐ ܐܚܪ̈ܢܐ.
O earthly one, whose dust is contemptible (but) whose constitution is sublime: for hidden mysteries are expressed by your very formation! O mortal, who have revealed and manifested by your constitution, three Persons, one single Essence without beginning! In forming Adam, His image, the Father showed that everything existed with the agreement of the Son and the Spirit. He hid the mystery of the manifestation of the Persons from all that exists, for fear that the spiritual (beings) might think of a different Power.
ܒܐܕܡ ܨܠܡܗ ܐܝܟ ܕܒܐܪܙܐ ܓܠܹܐ ܠܢܒܝ̈ܐ ܕܒܪ̈ܐ ܘܪ̈ܘܚܐ ܫܘܝܢ ܥܡ ܐܒܐ ܒܫܡ ܐܝܬܘܬܐ.
Thanks to Adam, His image, He revealed to the prophets, as in a mystery, that the Son and the Spirit are equal with the Father in the name of the Essence.
ܒܗ̇ܝ ܕܒܨܠܡܢ ܟܝܢܐ ܕܫ̈ܘܐ ܐܟܙ ܘܐܠܦ ܘܒܝܕ ܕܡܘܬܐ ܩܢܘܡ̈ܐ ܬܠܬܐ ܓܠܼܐ ܘܒܕܩ ܠܨܠܡܐ. ܒܐܕܡ ܝܪ̈ܬܐ ܐܠܦ ܘܓܠ̣ܹܐ ܬܠܝܬܝܘܬܐ ܕܐܒܐ ܘܒܪܐ ܘܪ̈ܘܚܐ ܕܩܘܕܫܐ ܚܕܐ ܫܘܝܘܬܐ.
By this (word) "in our image", He announced and taught that (His) nature is equal, and by the "likeness", He revealed and showed us the three Persons. Through Adam, the heir, He taught and revealed the Trinity of the Father, of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit, and one single equality.
ܗܢܐ ܚܝܠܐ ܕܟܣܐ ܡܸܢ ܟܠ ܒܪܐ ܟܠܡܕܡ ܘܒܫܘܠܡܐ ܕܟܠܡܐ ܕܗܘܼܐ ܪܟܒ ܐܢܫܐ. ܚܕܐ ܒܪܬ ܩܠܐ ܐܫܡܥ ܟܣܝܐ ܥܠ ܟܠ ܕܗܘܼܐ ܘܒܬܘܩܝܐ ܕܨܠܡܗ ܕܐܕܡ ܫܚܠܦ ܩܠܐ.
This power, hidden from all, created everything, and formed man, as the conclusion of all that exists. The hidden (God) made only one word heard, for all that existed, (but) in constituting Adam, His image, He changed expression.
ܬܘ ܠܡ ܢܥܒܕ ܐܢܫܐ ܡܠܝܠܐ ܒܨܠܡܐ ܕܝܠܢ ܘܒܐܚܝܢܘܬܐ ܕܢܦܫܗ ܘܦܓܪ̈ܗ ܢܐܣܘܪ ܟܠܐ. ܢܗܘܐ ܐܚܝܢܐ ܠܫܡܝܢ̈ܐ ܒܚܝ̈ܐ ܕܢܦܫܐ ܐܦ ܩܪ̈ܝܒܐ ܠܐܪ̈ܥܢܝܐ ܒܪܘܟܒ ܦܓܪ̈ܐ. ܘܢܗܘܐ ܨܠܡܐ ܒܝܕ ܓܠܝܘܬܗ ܡܟܪܙ ܟܣܝܘܬܝ ܘܢܗܘܐ ܫܠܝܛ ܥܠ ܟܠ ܕܗܘܼܐ ܕܢܐܠܦ ܡܪܘܬܝ. ܒܗ ܓ̇ܠܐ ܐܢܐ ܥܠ ܐܝܬܘܬܐ ܕܟܝܢܝ ܟܣܝܐ ܘܒܗ ܡܠܦ ܐܢܐ ܠܪ̈ܘܚܢܝܐ ܫܘܠܛܢܝ ܕܥܠ ܟܠ. ܡܝܩܪ ܐܢܐ ܠܗܿ ܠܓܒܝܠܘܬܗ ܡ̣ܢ ܟܠ ܕܒܪܝܬ ܕܒܐܝܕܐ ܘܦܘܡܐ ܓܒܿܠ ܐܢܐ ܠܗ ܘܢ̇ܦܚ ܐܢܐ ܒܗ.
"Come, let us make the rational man in our own image, and by the affinity of his body and his soul, let us attach all things (to him)!" "That, by the life of the soul, he may be akin to the celestial (beings), and that by the structure of the body he may be close to the corporeal (beings)!" "That the image may announce, by its manifestation, my hidden (nature), and that it may rule over all that exists in order to teach my sovereignty!" "Through it, I manifest the essence of my hidden nature; through it also, I teach the spiritual (beings) (that) I have authority over all things." "I honor its very formation, among all that I have created, for with hand and mouth, I fashion him and I breathe into him."


[p. 536] {#po.34.p536}
ܒܐܝܕܐ ܟܣܝܬܐ ܡܙܝܚ ܐܢܐ ܠܗ ܠܥܦܪܗ ܫܝܛܐ ܘܡ̣ܢ ܦܘܡܝ ܚܝܐ ܢ̇ܦܚ ܐܢܐ ܒܗ ܪܘܚܐ ܕܚܝ̈ܐ. ܐܘ ܐܝܬܘܬܐ ܕܠܐ ܪܘܟܒܐ ܕܟܣܝܐ ܡܸܢ ܟܠ ܕܡܛܠ ܫܝܛܐ ܐܙܥܪ ܢܦܫܗ ܒܫܡ ܗܕܡ̈ܐ. ܫܩܼܠ ܠܗ ܡܪܝܐ ܥܦܪ̈ܐ ܫܝܛܐ ܡ̣ܢ ܐܕܡܬܐ ܘܪܟܒ ܠܐܕܡ ܨܠܡܐ ܡܠܝܠܐ ܡܸܢ ܕܚܝܚܐ.
"With a hidden hand, I move his vile dust, and with my living mouth, I breathe into it a breath of life." O Essence uncompounded and hidden from all, who, for a contemptible (being), has diminished Yourself by the name of "members"! The Lord took, from the ground, contemptible dust, and with the dust, He formed Adam, a rational image.
ܘܢܦܼܚ ܒܐܦܘ̈ܗܝ ܢܫܡܬܐ ܕܚܝ̈ܐ ܡܸܢ ܪܘܚ ܦܘܡܗ ܘܗܘܼܐ ܐܕܡ ܠܢܦܫ ܚܝܐ ܡܐܢܐ ܡܠܝܠܐ.
And from the breath of His mouth, He breathed into his nostrils a breath of life, and Adam became a living soul, an object that speaks.

### The creation of the woman
ܠܗ ܒܠܚܘܕܘܗܝ ܕܠܐ ܒܪܬ ܙܘܓܐ ܪܟܒ ܥܒܼܕܗ ܟܕ ܡܠܦ ܠܗ ܕܒܟܠ ܡܕܡ ܚܕܬ ܬܘܩܢܗ. ܘܫܡܠܝ ܘܓܡܖ ܗ ܠܡܐܢܐ ܡܠܝܠܐ ܕܨܠܡܗ ܚܝܐ ܘܠܝܬ ܒܪ ܦܚܡܐ ܕܕܡܐ ܠܐܕܡ ܕܢܬܒܝܐ ܒܗ. ܐܪ̈ܡܝ ܫܠܝܐ ܕܫܢܬܐ ܘܛܒܼܥ ܐܕܡ ܫܒܙ̈ܐ ܘܢܣܼܒ ܐܠܥܐ ܡ̣ܢ ܗܕܡܘ̈ܗܝ ܘܬܩܢ ܠܐܢܬܬܐ.
He formed him, and made him alone without a companion, teaching him (thus) that in relation to all things, his constitution is new. He accomplished and completed the speaking object of His living image, but there was no (being) comparable to Adam, who resembled him, to comfort him. (God) made a deep sleep fall and the young Adam was plunged (into it), and He took from his members a rib, and He constituted the woman.
ܠܗ ܡܠܝ ܠܗ ܗܕܡ ܦܓܪܗ ܐܝܟ ܩܕܡܝܐ ܘܡ̣ܢ ܗ̇ܘ ܕܝܠܗ ܪܟܒ ܦܓܪ̈ܐ ܕܕܡ̈ܐ ܠܕܝܠܗ. ܗܘܼܐ ܒܪ̈ܢܫܐ ܕܕܡ̈ܐ ܠܐܢܫܐ ܡ̣ܢ ܒܪ̈ܢܫܐ ܘܫܡܠܝ ܘܗܘܼܐ ܦܓܪ̈ܐ ܘܢܦܫܐ ܒܕܡܘܬ ܐܕܡ. ܕܡܸܟ ܛܒܝܥܐ ܘܠܐ ܐܬܕܲܓܫ ܗܘܼܐ ܒܚܘܠܨ ܦܓܪܗ ܘܢܕ ܡ̣ܢ ܫܢܬܗ ܘܚܼܙܐ ܐܢܫܐ ܕܕܡܿܐ ܠܦܓܪܗ. ܪܲܡܸܙ ܓܠܝܢܐ ܒܪܘܚ ܒܪ̈ܘܝܐ ܒܟܢܪ ܢܦܫܗ ܘܡܠܠ ܦܘܡܗ ܩ̈ܠܐ ܚܕܬ̈ܐ ܕܢܒܝܘܬܐ. ܗܢܐ ܙܒܢܝܐ ܓܪ̈ܡܐ ܐܝܬܝܗܿ ܡ̣ܢ ܗܕܡ̈ܝܐ ܒܫܡ ܐܢܬܬ ܓܒܪ̈ܐ ܡܩܝܡܬ ܥܘܡܪ̈ܐ. ܩܪܖܗܿ ܬܘܒ ܒܫܡܐ ܕܡܬܒܕܩ ܒܗ ܫܡ ܣܘܥܪ̈ܢܝܐ ܘܚܘܝ ܕܐܝܬܝܗ̇ ܐܡܐ ܕܚܝ̈ܐ ܝܠܕܬ ܚܝ̈ܐ. ܟܢܫ ܨܐܕܘܗܝ ܟܠ ܓܢܣܢܝ̈ܢ ܕܗܘܘ ܒܐܪܥܐ ܕܢܪܡܐ ܥܠܝܗܘܢ ܛܒܥܐ ܕܡܠܬܗ ܕܢܗܘܘܢ ܕܝܠܗ.
For (Adam), He completed the member of his body, as before, and from that which belonged to him, He formed a body such that it was similar to his own. A human being, similar to man, existed from (another) human being; and (God) completed it and it became a body and a soul, in the likeness of Adam. Plunged (in sleep), (Adam) slept, and not having been conscious of his bodily depredation, he awoke from his sleep, and saw a human being similar to his body. By the cithara of his soul, the Creator indicated a spiritual revelation, and his mouth expressed new prophetic words: "This time, she is the bone of my members; she shall be called by the name of woman of man, foundation of existence." He also called her by the name through which the name of (his) realization is manifested, and he showed that she is the mother of the living, she who begets life. The installation in Paradise: (Adam) gathered to him the species that existed on the earth, so that he might place upon them the seal of his word (and) that they might be his.
ܟܢܝ ܐܢܘܢ ܒܫܡܗܝ̈ܗܘܢ ܐܝܟ ܡܐ ܕܣܦܼܩ ܘܝܠܦ ܚܝܠܐ ܕܫܥܒܕ ܠܗ ܟܠ ܐܝܟ ܕܠܡܪ̈ܐ.
He called them by their name, as he was capable, and he learned that the Power had submitted all things to him as to a master.


[p. 538] {#po.34.p538}
ܒܐܬܪ̈ܐ ܫܝܛܐ ܬܩܢ ܐܢܘܢ ܠܥܦܪ̈ܢܝܐ ܘܟܢ ܣܡ ܐܢܘܢ ܒܐܬܪ̈ܐ ܕܥܕܢ ܕܦܐܐ ܘܙ̈ܓܝܓ.
(God) created the (beings) of dust in a vile place, then He placed them in the place of the splendid and pleasant Eden.
ܥܼܠܘ ܝܪ̈ܘܬܐ ܠܓܘ ܦܪ̈ܕܝܣܐ ܘܐܚܕܘ ܢܘܡ̈ܐ ܘܐܟܬܒ ܠܗܘܢ ܫܛܪ ܡܪܘܬܗ ܒܝܕ ܦܘܩܕܢܐ. ܝܗܼܒ ܒܐܝܕܝ̈ܗܘܢ ܫܘܠܛܢ ܦܐܪ̈ܐ ܪ̈ܓܝܓܐ ܕܥܕܢ ܘܟܼܠܐ ܐܢܘܢ ܡ̣ܢ ܐܝܠܢܐ ܕܡܘܠܕ ܡܘܬܐ.
They entered as heirs inside Paradise and took possession of it, and by a commandment (God) wrote for them the act of His supremacy. He gave power to their hands over the delicious fruits of Eden, (but) He forbade them the tree that begets death.

### The role of the tree of the knowledge of good and evil
ܩܼܪܐ ܠܡ ܡܪܝܐ ܠܐܕܡ ܨܠܡܗ ܘܣܡ ܠܗ ܬܚܘܡܐ ܕܠܐ ܢܬܩܪܖ̈ܒ ܢܣܒ ܦܐܖ̈ܐ ܩܢܐ ܝܕܥܬܐ. ܠܐ ܠܡ ܬܐܟܘܠ ܡ̣ܢ ܐܝܠܢܐ ܕܡܘܬܐ ܡܘܠܕ ܛܪ ܦܘܩܕܢܐ ܕܝ̇ܗܒܢܐ ܠܟ ܦܐܪ̈ܐ ܕܚܝ̈ܐ.
"The Lord called Adam, His image, and set a limit for him, that of not approaching to take of the fruit endowed with knowledge." "Do not eat of the tree that begets death! Keep the commandment because I give you the fruit of life!"
ܬܪܝܢ ܐܝܠܢ̈ܐ ܣܡ ܒܪ̈ܘܝܐ ܠܒܘܚܪܢ ܨܠܡܗ ܘܩܼܪ̈ܐ ܐܢܘܢ ܐܝܠܢ ܚܝ̈ܐ ܘܐܝܠܢ ܡܘܬܐ. ܬܪ̈ܝܢ ܬܘܩܢ̈ܐ ܐܝܟ ܕܒܐܪ̈ܙܐ ܣܡ ܒܐܝܠܢ̈ܐ ܥܘܡܪ̈ܐ ܕܐܪ̈ܥܐ ܘܡܠܟܘܬ ܪܘܡܐ ܒܗܘܢ ܒܕܩ ܠܢ. ܗܼܘܐ ܦܘܩܕܢܐ ܐܝܟ ܫܠܝܛܐ ܕܢܛܪ ܐܢܘܢ ܕܕܠܡܐ ܢܡܪܚ ܐܕܡ ܫܒܪ̈ܐ ܥܠ ܦܐܪ̈ܝܗܘܢ. ܓܠܼܹܐ ܘܒܕܩ ܠܗ ܐܝܟ ܕܒܐܪ̈ܙܐ ܒܝܕ ܦܘܩܕܢܐ ܕܚܝ̈ܐ ܚܕܬ̈ܐ ܝܗܒ ܐܢܐ ܠܟ ܐܢ ܠܐ ܬܚܛܐ. ܘܟܕ ܝܿܕܥ ܠܗ ܕܡܚܛܐ ܚܿܛܐ ܐܝܟ ܚܛܝܐ ܒܝܕ ܦܘܩܕܢܐ ܨܒ̣ܐ ܕܢܢܣܝܘܗܝ ܐܝܟ ܠܐ ܝܿܕܥ.
The Creator placed two trees to test His image, and He called them "tree of life" and "tree of death". He placed in the trees, as in a symbol, two constitutions: through them, He announced to us the dwelling of the earth and the kingdom from above. The commandment was like a sovereign who would protect them, so that the young Adam would not venture as far as their fruits. (God) revealed and manifested to him as in a mystery, by means of the commandment: "I will give you a new life if you do not sin." And while knowing for Him that (Adam) would indeed sin as a sinner, He wanted to test him by means of the commandment, as if He did not know (it).
ܘܟܕ ܝܕܥ ܗܘܼܐ ܕܡܝܘܬܘܬܐ ܣܡ ܒܟܝܢܗ ܒܥܠܬ ܦܐܖ̈ܐ ܨܒܹܐ ܕܢܕܚܠ ܠܕܒܬܪ̈ܟܢ. ܒܝܕ ܦܘܩܕܢܐ ܕܥܠ ܐܝܠܢܐ ܪ̈ܕܝܗܝ ܠܦܫܝܛܐ ܘܐܝܬܝ ܠܥܠܝܐ ܦܪܪܘܫܘܬܗ ܒܝܕ ܢܣܝܢܝܐ. ܗܼܘܐ ܦܘܩܕܢܐ ܒܕܡܘܬ ܟܘܪ̈ܐ ܠܒܘܚܪ̈ܢ ܐܢܫܐ ܘܪ̈ܓܬ ܦܐܖ̈ܐ ܒܕܡܘܬ ܪܬܚܐ ܠܥܠ ܡ̣ܢ ܕܗܒܐ. ܢܦ̣ܼܚ ܒܗ ܐܐܖ ܕܙ̈ܓܬ ܦܐܪ̈ܐ ܒܕܗܒܗ ܕܐܕܡ ܘܐܣܓܝ ܡܪܩܗ ܒܢܘܪܐ ܕܐܘܟܠܗ ܘܚܘܝ ܫܘܦܪܗ. ܕܗܒܐ ܛܒܐ ܒܪܝܗܝ ܗܘܼܐ ܠܐܕܡ ܡܬܩܢ ܟܠܐ ܘܟܣܝ ܫܘܦܪܗ ܒܠܐ ܝܕܥܬܐ ܐܝܟ ܬܚܦܝܬܐ. ܒܝܕ ܦܘܩܕܢܐ ܐܝܟ ܕܒܟܘܪ̈ܐ ܐܥܠ ܢܣܟܗ ܘܒܢܣܝܘܢܐ ܡܪܩ ܨܐܘܬܗ ܘܚܘܝ ܫܘܦܪܗ.
And although He knew that He had put mortality into his nature, He wanted (however), by the motive of the fruit, to frighten those who (would come) afterward. By the commandment that concerns the tree, He instructed the simple, and He brought his discernment to manifest itself by means of a test. The commandment was like a crucible, to test man, and the desire for the fruit (was) like the effervescence above the gold. (God) blew air - (this is) the desire for the fruit - upon the gold, (which is) Adam; and He purified him abundantly by the fire of his eating, and showed his beauty. He who constituted all things had created Adam, as good gold; and as (by) a veil, He hid his beauty by non-knowledge. By means of the commandment, He introduced him and melted him as in a crucible, and by the test, He purified him of his impurity and showed his beauty.


[p. 540] {#po.34.p540}

### The jealousy of Satan
ܐܪ̈ܙܢܐܝܬ ܦܩܕ ܐܢܘܢ ܠܕܒܝܬ ܐܕܡ ܕܠܐ ܢܨܘܬܘܢܝܗܝ ܟܢܫܝ̈ ܪܘܡܐ ܠܩܠ ܦܘܩܕܢܐ. ܒܝܢܝ ܟܣܝܐ ܠܦܓܪ̈ܢܝܐ ܗܘܼܐ ܗܢ ܐܪ̈ܙܐ ܘܠܐ ܫܘܬܦ ܒܗ̇ ܒܬܢܘܝ ܕܐܕܡ ܠܫܡܝܢ̈ܐ. ܠܐ ܫܡܝܥ ܗܘܼܐ ܠܗ ܠܪ̈ܫܐ ܕܐܐ ܗܢ ܦܘܩܕܢܐ ܘܠܐ ܝ̇ܕܥ ܗܘܼܐ ܕܡܛܠ ܡܢܐ ܟܠܝܢ ܡ̣ܢ ܦܐܪ̈ܐ.
He gave (this) order secretly to the Adamic hearth, so that the assemblies from above would not hear the word of the commandment. This secret was between the hidden (God) and the corporeal (beings), and He did not make the celestial (beings) participate in the contract of Adam. This commandment was not heard by the Prince of the air; and he did not know why the fruit was forbidden to them.
ܚܣܡܐ ܕܠܚܗ̇ ܠܫܦܝܘܬ ܢܦܫܗ ܘܚܫܟ ܡܢܫܠܝ ܕܚܙܝܗܝ ܠܐܕܡ ܕܥܒܝܕ ܡܪܐ ܥܠ ܟܠ ܕܗܘܼܐ. ܛܢܢܐ ܫܓܪܗ ܠܐܟܠܩܪ̈ܨܐ ܒܕܡܘܬ ܢܘܪܐ. ܕܡܛܠ ܡܢܐ ܥܒܕ ܥܦܪ̈ܢܐ ܡܠܟܐ ܒܐܪ̈ܥܐ.
Jealousy troubled the serenity of his soul, and suddenly he became dark, for he saw that Adam had been constituted master of all that exists. Envy inflamed the slanderer, like a fire: "Why has the (being) of dust been made king on earth?
ܠܐ ܣܦܩܬܼ ܠܗ ܠܒܪ ܕܚܝܚܐ ܕܩܼܪܝܗܝ ܨܠܡܗ ܐܠܐ ܕܫܥܒܕ ܪ̈ܘܡܐ ܘܥܘܡܩܐ ܬܚܝܬ ܫܘܠܛܢܗ. ܠܐ ܟܕܘ ܠܗ ܠܥܦܪܐ ܕܐܪ̈ܥܐ ܕܓܒܠܗ ܒܐܝܕܘ̈ܗܝ ܐܠܐ ܕܐܪܡܝ ܢܝܪ̈ܐ ܕܥܡܠܗ ܥܠ ܪ̈ܘܚܢܐ. ܠܝ ܪܘܚܢܐ ܩܢܐ ܫܘܠܛܢܐ ܒܬܓܡ̈ܐ ܕܐܟܘܬܝ ܐܦܠܐ ܒܡܠܬܐ ܨܒ̣ܐ ܕܢܒܕܩ ܥܠ ܬܘܩܢܢ.
It is not enough for the one who is born of dust that He has called him His image, but He has enslaved to his power the (world) from above and from below! It is not enough for the dust of the earth that He has fashioned it with His hands, but He has placed the yoke of his labor upon the spiritual (beings)! To me, (a) spiritual (being), who have authority over the legions of my peers, not even by a word did He want to announce our constitution!
ܗܢܐ ܫܝܛܐ ܥܦܪܐ ܕܐܪ̈ܥܐ ܒܪ ܕܚܝܚܐ ܒܡܠܟܐ ܪ̈ܒܐ ܘܒܚܘܫܒܐ ܨܒܬ ܓܒܠܗ. ܩܼܪܝܗܝ ܬܘܒ ܨܠܡܗ ܟܕ ܡܘܪܒ ܠܗ ܐܝܟ ܕܠܡܪܐ ܘܟܕܢ ܐܢܘܢ ܠܫܡܝܢ̈ܐ ܒܦܘܠܚܢ ܚܝܘ̈ܗܝ.
(But) this vile earthy dust, (this) son of the dust, He has adorned and fashioned, with great counsel and reflection. He also called him His image, exalting him as a lord, and He submitted the celestial (beings) to the service of his life."

### The seduction of the serpent
ܕܐܩܪ̈ܘܒ ܨܐܕܘܗܝ ܒܠܥܕ ܓܘܫܡܐ ܠܐ ܚܶܙܐ ܠܝ ܕܐܩܪܘܒ ܨܐܕܘܗܝ ܕܠܐ ܪ̈ܘܟܒܐ ܠܐ ܥܳܢܐ ܠܝ. ܠܐ ܫܠܝܛ ܗܘܼܐ ܗ̇ܘ ܚܣܡܐ ܒܫܘܪ̈ܝ ܙܒܢ̈ܐ ܢܫܚܠܦ ܝܬܗ ܘܢܛܥܐ ܠܐܢܫܐ ܒܝܕ ܕܡܘ̈ܬܐ. ܚܘܝܐ ܓܒ̣ܐ ܠܗ ܕܢܟܣܐ ܒܗ ܡܪ̈ܝܪ̈ܘܬܗ ܘܐܝܟ ܟܢܪܐ ܒܡܢܝ̈ ܒܣܪ̈ܐ ܙܡܪ ܙܐܦܢܐ. ܢܓܕܗ ܠܚܘܝܐ ܩܢܐ ܪ̈ܘܟܒܐ ܠܘܬ ܒܪܬ ܐܢܫܐ ܕܡܐ ܕܚܙܬܗ ܕܒܣܪ̈ܢܝܐ ܗܘܼ ܬܩܒܠ ܡܠܟܗ.
"Let me approach him without a body, he will not see me! Let me approach him, (being) uncompounded, he will not answer me!" At the beginning of time, this jealous one was not capable of transforming himself and leading man astray by appearances. He chose a serpent to hide his bitterness in it, and he sang, the simulator, like a cithara, on carnal strings. He drew the serpent, (a being) endowed with composition, to the woman, so that, when she had seen that it is a carnal (being), she would accept his counsel.
ܙܡܪ ܙܐܦܢܝܐ ܒܐܒܘܒ ܒܣܪ̈ܐ ܩ̈ܠܐ ܢܟܝ̈ܠܐ ܕܡܛܠ ܡܢܐ ܟ̈ܠܝܢ ܐܢܬܘܢ ܘܫܠܝܢ ܡ̣ܢ ܡܐܟܘܠܬܐ. ܕܠܡܐ ܬܚܘܡܐ ܣܡ ܒܪ̈ܘܝܐ ܥܠ ܐܝܠܢܐ ܕܠܐ ܬܬܒܣܡܘܢ ܡ̣ܢ ܦܐܪ̈ܝܗܘܢ ܐܝܟ ܚܝ̈ܘܬܐ. ܠܥܙܬܼ ܚܘܐ ܒܩܠܐ ܓܫܝܡܐ ܥܡ ܒܣܪ̈ܢܐ ܕܫܠܝܛ ܗܘܼ ܠܢ ܥܠ ܒܘܣܡܐ ܕܟܠܗܘܢ ܦܐܪ̈ܐ.
On a carnal flute, the simulator psalmodized deceitful words: "Why refuse yourselves and stop yourselves from eating?" "Would the Creator, concerning the trees, have fixed a limit, so that you might not enjoy their fruits, like the living (beings)?" With a material voice, Eve stammered to the carnal one: "We have the power to enjoy all the fruits."


[p. 542] {#po.34.p542}
ܡ̣ܢ ܚܕ ܒܠܚܘܕ ܬܚܼܡ ܘܟܼܠܢ ܕܡܘܬܐ ܡܘܠܕ ܘܕܠܐ ܢܡܘܬ ܠܢ ܝ ܡܛܠܗܕܐ ܟܠܢ ܡ̣ܢ ܦܐܪ̈ܘܗܝ. ܥܠܬܗ ܕܡܘܬܐ ܕܡܟܣܝ ܗܘܼܐ ܡ̣ܢ ܪ̈ܘܚܝܐ ܘܚܕܝ ܢܟܝܠܠܐ ܕܐܫܟܚ ܐܬܪܐ ܕܢܪܡܐ ܙܐܦܗ. ܦܠܚܗ̇ ܠܐܪ̈ܥܐ ܕܫܡܥܐ ܕܐܕܢ̈ܐ ܦܕܐܠܥܐ ܪܦܝܬܐ ܘܐܪ̈ܡܝ ܙܪܥܗ ܐܟܠܩܪ̈ܨܐ ܒܓܘ ܬܪ̈ܥܝܬܗܿ.
"He has forbidden only one and has forbidden it to us, for it begets death, and so that we might not die, for that reason He has forbidden us its fruits." The cause of death, which was hidden from the spiritual (beings), ... and the deceiver rejoiced at having found the occasion to deploy his ruse. He cultivated the receptivity of the ears of the weak woman, and the slanderer deposited his seed inside her spirit.
ܠܘ ܣܡ ܡܘܬܐ ܟܣܐ ܒܐܝܠܢܐ ܚܘܝܐ ܬܪܓܡ ܥܝܢ̈ܐ ܕܢܘܗܪܐ ܝܗܒ ܦܐܪ̈ܐ ܠܕܐܟܠ ܡܢܗ. ܚܝܠܐ ܪܒܐ ܩܢܐ ܐܝܠܢܐ ܒܝܕ ܡܐܟܘܠܬܗ ܘܡܨܐ ܡܫܚܠܦ ܘܥܒܕ ܐܢܫܐ ܐܝܟ ܐܠܗ̈ܐ.
"It is not the poison of death that is hidden in the tree", interpreted the serpent; "(but) the fruit confers eyes of light to the one who eats of it. The tree possesses a great power, by the (fact) of eating of it, and it can transform men and make them like gods."
ܢܒܥܐ ܪ̈ܒܐ ܕܐܠܗܘܬܐ ܟܣܐ ܒܐܝܠܢܐ ܘܐܢ ܫܬܝܢ ܐܢܬܘܢ ܡ̣ܢ ܚܠܝܘܬܗ ܩܢܝܢ ܐܢܬܘܢ ܕܕ̈ܓܐ. ܘܡܛܠܗܕܐ ܟܼܠܟܘܢ ܡܢܗ ܕܠܐ ܬܕܡܘܢ ܠܗ ܕܕܠܡܐ ܬܡܛܘܢ ܠܪ̈ܘܡܐ ܪ̈ܒܐ ܕܐܠܗܘܬܗ. ܐܡܚܣܡ ܚ̇ܣܡ ܕܠܐ ܬܗܘܘܢ ܠܗ ܒܢܝ̈ ܝܪܬܘܬܐ ܘܠܐ ܬܬܟܢܘܢ ܒܫܡܐ ܪ̈ܒܐ ܕܐܠܗܘܬܗ. ܩܪܘܒܘ ܨܝܕ ܦܐܪ̈ܐ ܘܐܟܘܠܘ ܡܢܗ ܘܚܙܘ ܢܘܗܪ̈ܐ ܘܗܐ ܩܢܝܢ ܐܢܬܘܢ ܚܝܠ ܚܟܡ̈ܬܐ ܐܝܟ ܐܠܗܐ.
The great source of divinity is hidden in the tree, and if you drink of its sweetness, you will acquire a (superior) rank. And (that is) why He forbade it to you, for fear that you might become like Him, that you might perhaps attain the sublime height of His divinity." He is very jealous, for fear that you might become His heirs, and that you might be called by the great name of His divinity! Approach the fruit, eat of it, and see the light; behold, like gods, you will possess the power of the sciences."

### The fall and its consequences
ܚܙܬܼ ܠܡ ܐܢܬܬܐ ܕܪܲܓܝܓ ܦܐܪܐ ܘܦܐܝܐ ܚܙܬܗ ܘܢܣܒܬܼ ܘܐܟܠܬܼ ܘܟܢ ܝܗܒܬܼ ܠܗ ܠܒܥܠܗ̇ ܘܐܟܼܠ. ܗܝ ܩܕܡܝܬ ܫܡܥܬܼ ܡܠܟܐ ܘܩܛܦܬܼ ܦܐܪܐ ܘܣܪܗܒ ܦܘܡܗ̇ ܥܠ ܡܐܟܘܠܬܐ ܕܬܗܘܐ ܐܠܗ.
"The woman saw that the fruit was desirable and beautiful to see; she took (it), ate (of it), then she gave it to her husband and he ate (of it)." It is she who first listened to the counsel and picked the fruit, and her mouth hastened to eat (it), so that she might become a god.
ܒܐܘܟܠܐ ܕܦܐܪ̈ܐ ܨܒܬܼ ܕܬܚܛܘܦ ܠܗ̇ ܫܡ ܐܠܗܐ ܘܬܣܩ ܕܪ̈ܓܐ ܠܥܠ ܡ̣ܢ ܒܥܠܗ̇ ܘܬܗܘܐ ܪ̈ܫܐ. ܒܪ̈ܓܬ ܐܘܟܠܐ ܣܒܪ̄ܘ ܡܝܘܬ̈ܐ ܕܠܥܠ ܡܛܝܢ ܘܬܚܬܝ ܐܢܘܢ ܥܒܪ ܦܘܩܕܢܐ ܠܥܘܡܩܐ ܕܫܝܘܠ. ܒܐܘܟܠܗ ܕܦܐܪ̈ܐ ܩܢܘ ܒܘܝܢܐ ܥܦܪ̈ܢܝܝܐ ܘܚܪܘ ܘܐܬܢܟܦܘ ܡ̣ܢ ܓܠܝܘܬܐ ܕܗܕܡܝ̈ܗܘܢ. ܗܘܬܼ ܡܐܟܘܠܬܐ ܐܝܟ ܡܚܙܝܬܐ ܠܙܘܥܝ̈ ܢܦܫܐ ܘܢܟܼܦ ܗܘܢܐ ܠܕ̈ܓܫܐ ܕܠܒܪ̈ ܒܢܚ̈ܬܝ ܛܪ̈ܦܐ. ܩܼܢܘ ܒܘܝܢܐ ܕܦܪܘܫܘܬܐ ܫܒܪ̈ܝ ܠܒܐ ܘܟܣܝܘ ܒܣܪ̈ܐ ܥܪ̈ܛܠܝܐ ܕܗܕܡܝ̈ܗܘܢ.
By eating the fruit, she wanted to snatch for herself the name of god, and to rise a degree above her husband, and to become the leader. By the desire for eating, the mortals hoped to arrive higher, but the transgression of the commandment made them descend to the bottom of Sheol. By eating the fruit, the (beings) of dust acquired judgment, they saw and were ashamed of the nakedness of their members. The eating was like a mirror for the faculties of the soul, and the spirit made the external senses chaste by garments of leaves. Those (who had) a child's heart acquired the intelligence of discernment, and they covered the naked flesh of their members.
ܡ̣ܢ ܐܝܠܢܝܐ ܕܐܟܠܘ ܡܢܗ ܥܒܕܘ ܬܟܣܝܬܐ ܘܕܐܫܠܚ ܐܢܘܢ ܛܪ̈ܦܐ ܕܡܢܗ ܟܣܝܘ ܐܢܘܢ.
Of the tree of which they had eaten, they made a garment, and because it had denuded them, the leaves that (came) from it covered them.

### The intervention of God
ܠܦܢܝܗ ܕܝܘܡܐ ܐܪܗܒ ܐܢܘܢ ܩܠ ܒܪ̈ܘܝܐ ܟܕ ܡܫܡܥ ܗܘܼܐ ܩܠ ܗܠܟܬ̈ܐ ܒܓܘ ܦܪ̈ܕܝܣܐ. ܐܘ ܪ̈ܘܚܢܐ ܕܐܫܡܥ ܩܠܐ ܕܠܐ ܗܕܡ̈ܐ ܐܘ ܝ̇ܕܥ ܟܠ ܕܡܫܐܠ ܠܗ ܠܨܠܡܗ ܕܐܝܟܐ ܐܢܬ
The voice of the Creator terrified them, at the decline of the day, when, in Paradise, a sound of footsteps was heard. O spiritual (being) who, (having) no members, made (this) sound heard! O omniscient one who asks His very image: "Where are you?"


[p. 544] {#po.34.p544}
ܝܕܥ ܕܐܝܟܘ ܘܡܫܐܠ ܠܗ ܟܕ ܚܿܙܐ ܠܗ ܡܫܡܥ ܩܠܐ ܐܦ ܩܪܐ ܠܗ ܕܢܦܢܝܘܗܝ ܨܐܕܘܗܝ.
He knows (well) where he is, and He asks him (it), although He sees him; He makes (His) voice heard and He calls him in order to bring him back to Him.
ܐܝܟܐ ܐܢܬ ܐܕܡ ܨܠܡܐ ܓܠܝܐ ܡܒܕܩ ܟܣܝܘܬܝ ܐܝܟܐ ܐܢܬ ܝܪ̈ܬܐ ܕܣܡܬܟ ܡܪܐ ܥܠ ܟܠ ܩܢܝܢܝ̄
"Where are you, Adam, visible image that manifests my hidden (nature)? Where are you, heir whom I have established master over all my possessions?"
ܒܝܕ ܢܥܡܬ̈ܐ ܪ̈ܥܘܡܬܐ ܒܥܝܗܝ ܠܐܒܝܕܐ ܘܥܦܝܦܐܝܬ ܒܩܠ ܚܢܓܬ̈ܐ ܐܕܡ ܐܕܡ.
With plaintive accents, He questioned the one (who was) lost, twice and with a sorrowful voice: "Adam, Adam!"
ܐܝܟܐ ܐܢܬ ܐܕܡ ܩܥܼܐ ܒܪ̈ܘܝܐ ܠܘܬ ܥܦܪ̈ܢܐ ܘܦܢܝ ܥܒܝܕܐ ܠܘܬ ܥܒܘܕܐ ܒܩܠܐ ܚܿܢܝܓܐ.
"Where are you, Adam?", cried the Creator to the (being) of dust, and the creature answered the one who (had made) him, with a moaning voice:
ܩܠ ܗܠܟܬܟ ܫܡܿܥܬ ܘܛܫܶܝܬ ܡ̣ܢ ܟܘܚܕܐ ܕܠܐ ܬܬܒܩܐ ܒܥܪ̈ܛܠܝܘܬܝ ܕܡܦܪ̈ܣܝ ܐܢܐ.
"I heard the sound of your footsteps, and out of modesty, I hid myself, for fear that you might behold my nakedness, for I am uncovered."
ܡܢܘ ܐܫܠܚ ܢܚܬܝ̈ ܫܘܒܚܟ ܡܠܟܐ ܕܐܪܥܐ ܘܡܢܘ ܥܒܸܕܟ ܥܪ̈ܛܠܝܐ ܡܸܢ ܬܟܣܝܬܐ.
"Who took away your garments of glory, O king of the earth, and who made you naked, without clothing?"
ܐܚܼܕ ܥܠܬܐ̇ ܢܟܦܐ ܕܒܗܼܬ ܒܐܠܥܐ ܕܡܢܝܗ ܟܕ ܣܿܒܪ ܗܘܼܐ ܕܠܐ ܡܬܚܝܒ ܒܓܙܪ̈ ܕܝܢܐ.
The chaste (Adam) pretended that he was ashamed of the rib (that came) from himself, thinking that he would not be condemned by a sentence.
ܐܢܬܬܐ ܕܥܼܒܕܬ ܦ ܗܼܝ ܝܗܒܬܼ ܠܝ ܦܐܪ̈ܐ ܘܐܿܟܠܬ ܘܐܠܘܠܐ ܗܝ ܠܐ ܥ̇ܒܪ ܗܿܘܝܬ ܥܠ ܦܘܩܕܢܟ.
"The woman whom you made, she is the one who gave me the fruit and I ate (of it), and, without her, I would not have transgressed your commandment."
ܛܥܢܗ ܠܐܕܡ ܒܡܠܐ ܙܒܢܝܐ ܐܝܟ ܕܠܫܒܪܐ ܘܫܐܠ ܠܐܢܬܬܐ ܕܡܢܘ ܗܢܐ ܥܘܠܐ ܕܥܒܕܬܝ.
(God) bore with Adam for some time, like a child, and he questioned the woman: "What is this iniquity that you have committed?"
ܘܐܦ ܗܝ ܣܣܓܬܗ̇ ܠܥܠܬܐ ܒܚܘܝܐ ܒܕܡܘܬ ܐܕܡ ܕܚܘܝܐ ܡܠܥܢܝ ܘܐܟܠܬ ܦܐܪ̈ܐ ܕܠܐ ܡ̣ܢ ܨܒܝܢܝ.
And she too, like Adam, blamed the serpent: "The serpent advised me, and I ate the fruit without wanting to."

### The punishment of the serpent
ܠܐ ܕܝܢ ܫܐܠܗ ܠܚܘܝܐ ܕܐܛܥܝ ܕܠܡܿܢ ܐܛܥܝ ܕܠܘ ܗܼܘ ܐܛܥܝ ܐܠܐ ܢܟܝܠܐ ܕܡܠܠ ܗܘܼܐ ܒܗ.
Now (God) did not ask the serpent who had led (them) astray, why he had done it, for it was not he who had led (them) astray but the Deceiver who spoke within him.
ܦܣܼܩ ܒܪܘܝܐ ܕܝܢܐ ܩܫܝܐ ܥܠ ܣܪܘܚܐ ܘܦܥܼܪ ܘܫܕܝܗܝ ܒܝܬ ܐܣܝܪ̈ܐ ܒܝܕ ܠܘܛ̈ܬܐ.
The Creator decreed a severe judgment against the evildoer; and with curses, he bound him and cast him into prison.
ܠܝܛܐ ܬܗܘܐ ܡܸܢ ܟܠ ܕܒܿܪܺܝܬ ܕܪ̈ܚ̇ܫ ܒܐܪ̈ܥܐ ܘܢܒܛܠ ܪܗܛܐ ܕܩܠܝܠܘܬܟ ܕܠܐ ܬܘܒ ܬܛܥܐ.
"Cursed be you, among all that I have created, that crawls upon the earth; may your swift pace cease so that you may lead astray no more!
ܥܦܪܐ ܫܝܛܐ ܡ̣ܢ ܓܘ ܐܪܥܐ ܢܗܘܐ ܐܘܟܠܟ ܘܠܐ ܬܬܒܣܡ ܡ̣ܢ ܚܠܝܘܬܐ ܕܦܐܪ̈ܐ ܕܐܪ̈ܥܐ.
Vile dust, issued from the earth, shall be your food, and you shall no longer enjoy the sweet fruits of the earth!


[p. 546] {#po.34.p546}
ܣܢܐܐ ܬܗܘܐ ܠܐܢܫܐ ܕܐܛܥܝܬ ܟܠܗܘܢ ܝܘܡܝ̈ܟ ܘܐܢܘܢ ܢܗܘܘܢ ܠܟ ܣܩܘܪ̈ܐ ܕܠܐ ܬܪ̈ܥܘܬܐ.
You shall be an enemy to the man whom you led astray, all the days that you (shall live), and they shall be for you irreconcilable adversaries.
ܢܕܘܫܘܢ ܪܫܟ ܘܢܩܦܚܘܢܟ ܟܕ ܙܟܼܝܢ ܠܟ ܘܐܢܬ ܡܐ ܕܚܛܘ ܬܡܚܐ ܐܢܘܢ ܒܝܕ ܨܸܢܥܬܵܟ.
They shall trample your head, and break you, triumphing over you; and you, by your artifices, shall strike them when they have sinned."
ܒܓܙܪ ܕܝܢܐ ܕܚܘܝܐ ܚܝܒ ܠܐܟܿܠܩܪ̈ܨܐ ܘܒܠܘܛܬ̈ܐ ܕܥܠ ܒܣܪ̈ܢܐ ܠܛ ܠܢܟܝܠܐ.
By the sentence (against) the serpent, (God) condemned the Slanderer; and by the curses against a carnal (being), he cursed the Deceiver.
ܠܐ ܨܒ̣ܐ ܕܢܓܠܐ ܥܠ ܡܠܐܟ̈ܐ ܒܫܘܪ̈ܝ ܙܒܢ̈ܐ ܕܠܐ ܬܣܓܐ ܠܗ̇ ܛܘܥܝܝ ܒܐܪ̈ܥܐ ܕܫܡ ܐܠܗ̈ܐ.
At the beginning of time, he did not wish to reveal (it) to the angels, so that the error of the "naming of gods" might not multiply on earth.
ܒܗܕܐ ܬܢܘܝ ܟܣܝ ܫܪܒܗ ܕܐܟܿܠܩܪܨܐ ܕܠܐ ܢܣܒܪ ܠܗ ܕܚܝܠ ܟܠ ܡܫܟܚ ܐܝܟ ܒܪ̈ܘܝܐ.
Under this pretext, he hid the story of the Slanderer, so that he might not believe himself capable of everything, like the Creator.
ܥܡ ܪ̈ܘܚܢܐ ܥܛܝܦܝ̈ ܢܘܗܪܐ ܒܪܐ ܗܘܼܐ ܐܦ ܗܼܘ ܘܡܕܝܪ ܗܘܼܐ ܥܡ ܩܕܝܫ̈ܐ ܒܐܦܝ̈ ܪ̈ܘܡܐ.
He too was created at the same time as the spiritual (beings) clothed in light, and he dwelt with the saints in the (world) above.
ܪ̈ܫܢܘܬܐ ܕܫܘܠܛܢ ܐܐܖܿ ܘܢܐ ܗܘܼܐ ܘܡܙܝܥ ܘܕܚܣܼܡ ܒܐܕܡ ܢܦܼܠ ܡ̣ܢ ܕܕ̈ܓܗ ܒܕܡܘܬ ܒܪ̈ܩܐ.
He possessed the command of the empire of the air and (directed) its movement, but because he had envied Adam, he fell from his rank like lightning.
ܘܠܗ ܠܐܛ ܗܘܼܐ ܒܗ̇ܝ ܕܥܠ ܟܪܣܟ ܬܗܠܟ ܒܐܪ̈ܥܐ ܕܠܐ ܬܘܒ ܣܿܠܩ ܠܐܦܝ̈ ܪ̈ܘܡܐ ܒܩܠܝܠܘܬܗ.
And (God) cursed him thus: "You shall walk upon the earth, upon your belly," so that he might no longer ascend into the (world) above by virtue of his speed.
ܠܗ ܬܘܒ ܡܕܼܟ ܘܥܙܪ ܐܘܟܠܐ ܥܦܪܐ ܫܝܛܐ ܟܕ ܡܬܓܙ̈ܓܚ ܒܐܦܝ̈ ܐܪ̈ܥܐ ܒܝܬ ܥܦܪ̈ܢܐ.
He also humiliated him and decided (that his) food (would be) the vile dust, since he was cast down to the earth among the (beings) of dust.
ܠܗܼܘ ܟܢܝ ܣܢܐܐ ܕܐܢܫܐ ܩܛܲܠ ܐܢܫܐ ܘܗܘܝܘ ܕܡܿܚܐ ܒܥܩܒܗ ܕܐܢܫܐ ܒܥܘܩܣܐ ܕܥܘܠܐ.
It is he whom he called the hater of man, the killer of men, and it is he who pricks the heel of man with the sting of iniquity.

### The punishment of Eve
ܪ̈ܕܝܗܝ ܠܢܟܝܠܐ ܒܢ̈ܓܕܹܐ ܩܫܝ̈ܐ ܠܦܘܬ ܣܟܠܘܬܗ ܘܐܪ̈ܝܡ ܫܒܛܗ ܥܠ ܒܪܬ ܐܢܫܐ ܕܫܡܥܬܼ ܡܠܟܗ.
He punished the deceiver with harsh penalties, in proportion to his fault, and he raised his staff against the woman who had listened to his counsel.
ܒܚܒ̈ܠܐ ܕܝܠܕܐ ܢܓܕܗܿ ܠܚܘܐ ܕܩܛܦܬܼ ܦܐܪ̈ܐ ܘܥܠ ܕܐܬܬܪ̈ܝܡܬ ܡܟܟ ܪ̈ܫܗ̇ ܬܚܬܝ ܓܒܪ̈ܐ.
By the pains of childbirth, he punished Eve who had plucked the fruit, and since she had exalted herself, he lowered her head beneath (that of) the man.

### The punishment of Adam
ܠܐܕܡ ܨܠܡܗ ܐܚܪܝܬ ܠܛܗ ܒܓܙܪ ܕܝܢܐ ܕܩܫܝܘܬ ܢܓܕ̈ܐ ܦܟܗ ܡܪ̈ܬܗ ܒܚܘܝܐ ܘܚܘܐ.
Lastly, he cursed Adam, his image, by a sentence, the bitterness of which was tempered by the harshness of the (other) punishments, in relation to the serpent and to Eve.
ܠܝܛܐ ܐܪ̈ܥܐ ܡܛܠܬܟ ܡܠܟܐ ܕܐܪ̈ܥܐ ܟܘܒ̈ܐ ܬܘܥܐ ܐܦ ܬܬܠ ܠܟ ܟܕ ܬܦܠܘܚ ܒܗܿ.
"Cursed be the ground because of you, king of the earth! May it produce thorns and may it give you (fruits) when you cultivate it!
ܒܕܘܥܬܐ ܕܐܦܝ̈ܟ ܬܐܟܘܠ ܠܚܡܐ ܟܕ ܡܫܬܚܩܬ ܘܢܫܘܛܘܢܟ ܐܦ ܩܢܝܢܝ̈ܟ ܕܫܼܛܬ ܦܘܩܕܢ̈ܝ.
You shall eat bread, by the sweat of your face, in affliction, and your possessions themselves shall despise you, since you have despised my commandments!


[p. 548] {#po.34.p548}
ܥܦܪ̈ܐ ܐܢܬ ܐܕܡ ܡܸܢ ܕܚܝܚܐ ܘܥܦܪ̈ܐ ܬܗܘܐ ܕܫܪܝܬ ܡܠܝ̈ ܢܫܪܐ ܡܘܬܐ ܫܘܠܛܢ ܚܝܝ̈ܟ. ܐܘ ܝ̇ܕܥ ܟܠ ܕܝܿܕܥ ܟܠ ܥܕܠܐ ܢܗܘܐ ܕܚܘܝ ܚܡܬܗ ܒܓܙܖ ܕܝܢܗ ܐܝܟ ܠܐ ܝܕܥ ܒܪܝܗܝ ܗܘܼܐ ܠܐܕܡ ܡ̣ܢ ܕܚܝܚܐ ܘܩܪܝܗܝ ܨܠܡܗ ܘܥܒܕܗ ܠܥܦܪܗ ܒܕܡܘܬ ܥܦܪܐ ܕܡܝܘܬܘܬܐ. ܠܗܕܐ ܣܡܗ ܡ̣ܢ ܫܘܪ̈ܝܐ ܒܪ̈ܘܟܒ ܦܓܪ̈ܗ. ܕܢܪ̈ܕܐ ܒܐܘܪܚܐ ܕܡܝܘܬܘܬܐ ܕܢܫܐܠ ܚܝ̈ܐ. ܠܘ ܡ̣ܢ ܚܡܬܐ ܓܙܪ̈ ܠܘܛ̈ܬܐ ܘܟܐܒ̈ܐ ܕܝܠܕܐ ܘܠܘ ܒܬܘܬܐ ܪܓܙ ܐܥܠ ܡܘܬܐ ܥܠ ܡܝܘܬ̈ܐ. ܟܝܢܝ̈ܬܐ ܕܣܡ ܒܟܝܢܐ ܕܡܝܘܬܘܬܐ ܗܦܟ ܣܡ ܐܢܝܢ ܐܝܟ ܠܘܚܡܐ ܠܥܘܕܪܢ ܐܢܫܐ. ܝܕܥ ܗܘܼܐ ܠܗ ܠܥܦܪܗ ܕܐܕܡ ܥܕܠܐ ܢܗܘܐ ܕܡܘܬܐ ܫܪܐ ܘܡܒܛܠ ܠܗ ܠܪ̈ܘܟܒ ܦܓܪܗ. ܓܠܝܐ ܗܘܼܬ ܠܗ ܕܡܚܛܐ ܚܛܐ ܒܙܓܬ ܦܐܪ̈ܐ ܘܝܿܕܥ ܗܘܼܐ ܠܗ ܠܝܨܪܐ ܒܝܫܐ ܕܦܪ̈ܘܫܘܬܗ. ܒܝܕ ܢܣܝܢܐ ܨܒܐ ܕܢܚܟܡ ܠܡܠܝܠܘܬܗ ܕܠܐ ܬܗܘܐ ܠܗܿ ܦܪܘܫܘܬܗ ܕܠܐ ܕܘܪܫܐ. ܦܘܪ̈ܣܐ ܪܟܒ ܒܝܕ ܦܘܩܕܢܐ ܕܢܐܠܦ ܐܕܡ ܕܙܕܩ ܗܘܼܐ ܠܗ ܕܢܛܪ ܛܟܣܐ ܕܦܘܩܕܢ ܡܪܗ. ܒܝܕ ܐܝܠܢܐ ܓܠܹܐ ܬܚܦܝܬܐ ܕܦܪ̈ܘܫܘܬܗ ܘܒܐܘܟܠܗ ܕܦܐܪܐ ܚܘܝ ܚܝܠܐ ܕܡܠܝܠܘܬܗ. ܠܘ ܐܝܠܢܐ ܩܢܐ ܗܘܼܐ ܚܝܠܐ ܕܦܪܘܫܘܬܐ ܘܠܘ ܫܬܝܩܐ ܥܒܕ ܠܡܠܝܠܐ ܕܢܕܥ ܗܠܝܢ. ܠܘ ܒܐܝܠܢܐ ܟܣܐ ܗܘܼܐ ܡܘܬܐ ܕܫܥܒܕ ܠܐܕܡ ܘܠܘ ܦܘܩܕܢܐ ܥܒܕ ܚܛܝ̈ܐ ܠܕܒܬܪ̈ܟܢ. ܪ̈ܘܟܒ ܦܓܪܗ ܕܐܕܡ ܥܕ ܥܶܐ ܕܡܝܘܬܐ ܗܘܼܐ ܐܦ ܚܒܪܘܬܐ ܕܚܘܐ ܕܗܘܬܼ ܡܠܝܬ ܚܫ̈ܐ ܗܼܝ. ܥܠܡܐ ܟܠܗ ܡ̣ܢ ܬܘܩܢܘ̈ܗܝ ܠܗܕܐ ܣܗܕ ܘܦܐܪ̈ܐ ܘܙܪ̈ܥܐ ܠܩܘܝܡ ܙܪ̈ܥܐ ܕܡܝܘܬܘܬܐ. ܩܪܝܗܝ ܠܐܝܠܢܐ ܩܢܐ ܝܕܥܬܐ ܠܘ ܒܟܝܢܗ ܐܠܐ ܕܢܒܩܐ ܚܟܡܬ ܐܕܡ ܒܥܠܬ ܦܐܪܗ. ܩܪܝܗܝ ܠܐܚܪ̈ܢܐ ܝܗܒ ܚܝ̈ܐ ܠܘ ܡ̣ܢ ܕܝܠܗ ܐܠܐ ܕܨܪܗ ܒܐܪ̈ܙ ܡܠܟܘܬܐ ܡܠܝܬ ܚܝ̈ܐ. ܒܚܕ ܐܣܪ ܒܗ ܓܙܪ̈ܝ ܕܝܢܐ ܡܠܝܢ ܠܘܚܡ̈ܐ ܘܠܐܚܪ̈ܝܢ ܝܗܒܗ ܪܗܒܘܢ ܚܝ̈ܐ ܠܦܫܝܛܘܬܐ.
You are dust, Adam, issued from the clay, and you shall become dust; because you have forsaken my words, death shall abolish your vital power!" O omniscient one who knows all things before they happen, who manifests his anger by his sentence, as if he did not know! He had created Adam from dust and he called him his image, and he made his dust similar to mortal dust. From the beginning, in composing his body, he established this: he shall travel the path of mortality to ask for life. It is not out of anger that he decided the curses and the pains of childbirth, and it is not out of regret that he applied death to mortals. The natural (things) that he had placed in mortal nature, he replaced as a threat, for the help of man. He knew the dust of Adam before it existed, (he knew) that death would dissolve and annihilate the structure of his body. It was clear to him that he would indeed sin by desire for the fruit, and he knew the evil inclination of his discernment. He wished to instruct his reason by means of a trial, so that his discernment might not be unexercised. He imagined the means (for it), through the commandment, so that Adam might learn that he had to observe the status of the commandment of his Lord. Thanks to the tree, he removed the veil of his discernment, and by the eating of the fruit, he manifested the power of his reason. It is not the tree that possessed the power of discernment, and it is not a mute (being) that made these things known to a (being) gifted with speech. It is not in the tree that the death which enslaved Adam was hidden, and it is not the commandment that made sinners of those who (came) afterwards. The structure of Adam's body proclaims that he was mortal, as well as (his) commerce with Eve, which was full of sufferings. The whole world, by its creations, attests to it; and the fruits and the seeds (are) for the maintenance of mortal seeds. He called the tree "gifted with knowledge," not in its nature, but because it would test the wisdom of Adam, by means of its fruit. He called the other (tree) "the one that procures life," not with what it has, but because it represented the mystery of the kingdom full of life. He bound the one to sentences full of threats, and he gave the other as a pledge of life to the simple.


[p. 550] {#po.34.p550}
ܠܐ ܣܦܩ ܗܘܼܐ ܐܕܡ ܫܒܪܐ ܠܓܡܝܪ̈ܘܬܐ ܘܠܐ ܡܫܟܚ ܗܘܼܐ ܠܡܨܬ ܫܡܥܐ ܕܡܠܟܘܬ ܪܘܡܐ. ܒܡܚܝ̈ܠܬܐ ܐܝܟ ܕܒܚܠܒܐ ܩܢܐ ܕܘܪ̈ܫܐ ܘܟܢ ܒܕܩ ܠܗ ܒܝܕ ܐܝܠܢܐ ܬܪ̈ܝܢ ܬܘܩܢ̈ܐ. ܠܥܘܡܪ̈ܐ ܕܐܪ̈ܥܐ ܡܬܠ ܒܐܝܠܢܐ ܡܘܠܕ ܡܘܬܐ ܘܒܐܝܠܢ ܚܝ̈ܐ ܨܪ ܬܘܩܢܐ ܕܡܠܟܘܬ ܪ̈ܘܡܐ. ܡܠܟ ܦܪ̈ܕܝܣܐ ܐܝܟ ܦܘܪ̈ܥܢܐ ܠܥܡܠ̈ܐ ܕܬܢܢ ܘܢܛܪ ܦܐܪ̈ܘܗܝ ܒܙܗܝܪ̈ܘܬܐ ܕܩܠ ܦܘܩܕܢܘ̈ܗܝ.
The young Adam did not have the capacity of mature age, and he could not lend an ear to the kingdom above. He acquired experience through poor things as with milk; then (God) announced to him by means of the tree the two creations. He assimilated the tree that engenders death to earthly existence, and by the tree of life, he represented the creation of the kingdom above. He promised paradise as a reward for the pains of here below, and he protected the fruits by the care of the word of his commandments.

### The four rivers of Paradise
ܫܒܝܚ ܗܘܼܐ ܢܒܥܗ ܕܡܫܩܐ ܪ̈ܕܝܗ ܠܐܪ̈ܒܥ ܦܢܝܢ ܘܐܝܟ ܣܝܠܘܢܐ ܒܝܡܐ ܥܒܪ ܟܕ ܠܐ ܚܠܛ. ܐܝܘ ܦܘܩܕܢܐ ܕܢܛܪ ܡܝ̈ܐ ܐܝܟ ܕܒܡܐܢܐ ܘܠܐ ܚܠܛ ܠܗ ܠܢܒܥܐ ܕܥܕܢ ܒܩܒܝܐ ܪ̈ܒܐ. ܐܝܘ ܫܪ̈ܝܘܬܐ ܕܡܢ̈ܐ ܪ̈ܦܝܐ ܕܦܣܩܝܢ ܡܝ̈ܐ ܘܟܕ ܚܕ ܐܢܘܢ ܗܘܘ ܢܘܟܪ̈ܝܐ ܡܝ̈ܐ ܠܡܝ̈ܐ. ܠܘ ܒܟܝܢܐ ܩܢܐ ܫܘܠܛܢܐ ܢܒܥܐ ܕܥܕܢ ܚܝܠ ܒܪ̈ܘܝܐ ܗܘܼ ܕܢܛܪ ܡܝ̈ܐ ܒܡܝ̈ܐ ܪ̈ܦܝܐ. ܗܿܘ ܕܒܪܐ ܟܠ ܡ̣ܢ ܠܐ ܡܕܡ ܒܚܝܠ ܪܒܘܬܗ ܪܡܙ ܠܪ̈ܦܝܐ ܕܠܐ ܢܬܚܠܛܘܢ ܥܡ ܚܒܪ̈ܝܗܘܢ. ܠܚܝܠܗ ܐܘܪܒ ܐܦ ܒܪ ܥܡܪܡ ܕܬܢܐ ܐܢܝܢ ܕܦܫܝ̈ܩܢ ܠܗ ܐܦ ܥܣܩܬ̈ܐ ܠܚܝܠ ܨܒܝܢܗ. ܒܗܕܐ ܬܢܘܝ ܦܠܓ ܐܢܘܢ ܠܡܝ̈ܐ ܕܥܕܢ ܕܢܗܘܘܢ ܣܗܕܝܢ ܥܠ ܪܒܘܬܗ ܕܚܝܠ ܟܠ ܡܫܟܚ. ܐܝܟ ܟܪ̈ܘܙܐ ܫܕ ܐܢܘܢ ܠܐܪ̈ܒܥܦܢܝܢ ܕܢܗܘܘܢ ܩܥܝܢ ܟܕ ܫܬܝܩܝܢ ܥܠ ܪ̈ܒܘܬܗ. ܒܗܢܕܘ ܡܟܪܙ ܦܝܫܘܢ ܪܒܐ ܚܝܠ ܒܪ̈ܘܝܐ ܘܒܒܪ̈ܘܠܐ ܘܡܪ̈ܓܢܝܬܐ ܡܥܬܪ ܠܐܪ̈ܥܐ. ܥܡ ܟܘܫܝ̈ܐ ܠܥܙ ܗܘܼܐ ܓܝܚܘܢ ܒܩܠܐ ܕܫܦܥܘ̈ܗܝ ܘܟܕ ܫܬܝܩ ܗܘܼ ܡܪܝܡ ܠܟܝܢܗ ܘܡܫܩܐ ܠܐܪ̈ܥܐ. ܕܩܠܬ ܩܥܐ ܐܝܟ ܫܝܦܘܪܐ ܠܐܬܘܪ̈ܝܐ ܕܗܐ ܒܪ̈ܘܝܐ ܒܓܘ ܫܬܝܩ̈ܐ ܫܡܥܘ ܡܠܠ̈ܐ. ܦܪܬ ܐܝܟ ܪܥܡܐ ܡܫܡܥ ܘܠܗ ܒܪ̈ܕܝܗ ܪܒܐ ܘܐܝܟ ܫܝܦܘܪܐ ܡܥܝܪ̈ ܥܠܠܬ̈ܐ ܒܩܠ ܫܩܘܬܗ. ܠܘ ܪܒܘܬܐ ܕܢܗܪ̈ܘܬܐ ܩܠܣ ܡܘܫܐ ܠܚܝܠ ܒܪ̈ܘܝܐ ܐܘܪܒ ܕܡܨܐ ܚܝܠ ܟܠܡܕܡ.
Glorious was its source, whose course waters the four extremities (of the world), and like a conduit in the sea, it passes through without mixing! O commandment that preserves the waters as in a vase, and that does not mix the source of Eden with the great reservoir! O fluidity of liquid water that divides one (other) water and although they are one, waters became foreign to (other) waters! It is not by nature that the source of Eden possesses a power, (but) it is the power of the Creator that preserves the water within another fluid water. He who from nothing created all things by the power of his majesty, signified to fluid (elements) not to mix with their companions. The son of Amram also magnified his power, he who recounted it, for even difficult things are easy to the power of his will. Under this pretext, he divided the waters of Eden, so that they might testify that his majesty is capable of everything. Like heralds, he sent them to the four extremities (of the world), so that, although mute, they might proclaim his greatness. The great Pishon announces in India the power of the Creator, and it enriches the land with beryls and pearls. The Gihon murmured to the Cushites the song of its floods, and while being silent, it rises and waters the region. The Tigris clamors like a trumpet to the Assyrians: "Behold, those gifted with speech have perceived the Creator through mute (beings)!" The Euphrates makes its voice heard like thunder, in its majestic course, and like a trumpet, it awakens the harvests at the sound of its irrigation. It is not the majesty of the rivers that Moses glorified, (but) he exalted the power of the Creator, who is capable of everything.


[p. 552] {#po.34.p552}
ܘܟܕ ܨܒܝܢܗ ܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܓܙܐ ܘܥܘܬܪܐ ܪܡܙܗ ܬܗܝ ܐܢܘܢ ܠܥܒܕ ܐܝܕܘ̈ܗܝ ܐܝܟ ܚܣܝܪ̈ܐ.
And although his will is his treasure, and his sign, a wealth, he prevented them from (pursuing) the work of his hands, as if he (were) deficient.
ܠܫܬܐ ܝܘܡܝ̈ܢ ܐܬܩܢ ܟܠܐ ܡܬܩܢ ܟܠܐ ܘܒܫܒܝܥܝܐ ܢܚ ܡ̣ܢ ܥܡܠܗ ܗܿܘ ܓܡܿܪ ܟܠ.
He who constituted all things in six days constituted everything, and the seventh, he who completes all things rested from his labor.
ܠܐ ܥܣܩܐ ܗܘܼܬ ܠܪܒܘܬ ܚܝܠܗ ܕܗܘ ܕܒܪܐ ܟܠ ܕܒܚܕ ܪܡܙܐ ܢܨܒܬ ܢܥܒܕ ܟܠ ܡܐ ܕܗܼܘܐ.
It was not difficult for the powerful majesty of him who created everything, that with a single sign, he adorned and made everything that exists.

### The creation, source of instruction for the angels
ܠܫܬܐ ܝܘܡܝ̈ܢ ܡܬܼܚ ܬܘܩܢܘ̈ܗܝ ܠܐ ܚܣܝܪ̈ܐ ܕܢܠܦ ܚܝܠܗ ܠܪ̈ܘܚܢܝܝܐ ܕܗܼܘ ܬܩܢ ܟܠ.
(He who is) without need prolonged his creations during six days, in order to teach the spiritual (beings) that his power constituted all things.
ܠܗܘܢ ܡܿܠܦ ܗܘܼܐ ܟܕ ܒ̇ܪ̈ܐ ܗܘܼܐ ܢܘܗܪ̈ܐ ܦܐܝܐ ܕܡ̣ܢ ܠܐ ܡܕܡ ܡܨܐ ܒܪ̈ܘܝܐ ܠܡܥܒܕ ܡܕܡ.
He taught them, when he created the beautiful light, that the Creator can make something from nothing.
ܘܠܗܘܢ ܡܘܕܥ ܐܣܦܝܪ ܫܡܫܐ ܘܬܘܩܢ ܣܗܪܐ ܕܐܦ ܡ̣ܢ ܡܕܡ ܡܨܐ ܕܢܫܚܠܦ ܘܢܥܒܕ ܡܕܡ.
And the sphere of the sun and the constitution of the moon let them know that even from one thing, he can transform (it) and make (of it) another thing.
ܩܥܼܘ ܣܕܪ̈ܝܗܘܢ ܟܕ ܚܿܙܝܢ ܗܘܼܘ ܬܗܪܐ ܪܒܐ ܕܥܡ ܦܘܩܕܢܐ ܗܼܘܐ ܣܘܥܪ̈ܢܐ ܘܠܐ ܐܫܬܘܚܪ.
Their choirs exclaimed upon seeing a great wonder: the realization took place at the same time as the commandment, and without delay!
ܝܠܦܘ ܗܘܼܘ ܚܝܠܐ ܕܒܪ̈ܘܝܘܬܐ ܒܝܕ ܬܘܩܢܘ̈ܗܝ ܘܗܼܘ ܒܠܚܘܕܘܗܝ ܗܝܡܢܘ ܕܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܕܠܐ ܫܘܪܝܐ.
Through his creations, they had been informed of the power of his creative (faculty), and they believed that it is he alone who is without beginning.
ܒܪܡܙܐ ܕܝܠܗ ܩܢܼܘ ܫܘܠܛܢܐ ܠܡܣܥܪ ܟܠܐ ܘܩܡܘ ܟܢܫܝ̈ܗܘܢ ܥܠ ܣܘܥܪ̈ܢܐ ܕܐܪ̈ܥܢܝܐ.
By his sign, they acquired the power to do everything, and their assemblies presided over the works of earthly (beings).
ܠܗܘܢ ܐܓܥܠ ܗܘܼܐ ܥܘܬܪܐ ܕܒܐ ܐܝܟ ܫܪ̈ܝܪ̈ܐ ܘܥܒ̣ܕ ܐܢܘܢ ܐܦܛܪ̈ܘܦܐ ܕܡܝܘܬܘܬܐ.
He had entrusted them with great wealth as (to people) who were trustworthy, and he made them chiefs of mortals.
ܩܢܘ ܫܘܠܛܢܐ ܕܪܘܚܢܝܐ ܥܠ ܟܠ ܕܗܼܘܐ ܘܚܝܠ ܐܢܘܢ ܪܡܙܐ ܕܚܝܠܗ ܠܘܬ ܬܫܒܘܚܬܐ.
They acquired spiritual dominion over all that exists, and a sign of his power (gave) them the power to glorify.

### The experience of Adam, seduced by the Evil One
ܠܐܟܲܠܩܪ̈ܨܐ ܥܒܕܗ ܪܫܐ ܕܡܙܝܥ ܠܐܐܪ ܘܐܝܟ ܪܒ ܚܝܠܐ ܐܫܠܡ ܒܐܝܕܗ ܠܟܢܘ̈ܬܗ.
the Calumniator, he made the chief who (sets) the air in motion, and as to a commander, into his hand he delivered his peers.
ܚܘܬܪ̈ܐ ܚܛܦܗ ܒܝܕ ܫܘܠܛܢܗ ܠܗ̇ܘ ܡܪܘܕܐ ܘܥܠ ܒܪ̈ܘܝܐ ܨܒܹܐ ܕܢܫܬܥܠܐ ܒܪܡܘܬ ܠܒܗ.
Because of his dominion, pride took hold of this rebel, and he wished, in the haughtiness of his heart, to rise up against the Creator.
ܘܕܠܐ ܐܫܟܚ ܕܢܒܐܫ ܒܝܫܐ ܠܚܝܠ ܐܝܬܘܬܐ ܨܒܼܐ ܕܢܨܚܐ ܨܠܡ ܒܪ̈ܘܝܐ ܚܠܦ ܒܪ̈ܘܝܐ.
And because the Evil One could not do harm to the power of the Essence, he wished to outrage the image of the Creator, instead of the Creator.
ܒܨܥܪܗ ܕܐܕܡ ܣܥܹܐ ܡܪ̈ܘܕܐ ܥܠ ܒܪ̈ܘܝܐ ܟܕ ܣܿܒܪ ܗܘܼܐ ܕܡܒܐܫ ܗܘܼܐ ܠܗ ܠܨܠܡ ܨܠܡܗ.
By dishonoring Adam, the rebel (acted) impudently toward the Creator, while he thought he was doing harm to his supreme image.
ܐܛܥܝ ܠܐܕܡ ܒܙ̈ܓܬ ܦܐܪ̈ܐ ܕܢܐܟܘܠ ܘܢܡܘܬ ܕܡܐ ܕܡܝܬ ܐܕܡ ܢܥܝܩ ܠܐܠܗܐ ܒܡܘܬܗ ܕܐܕܡ.
He seduced Adam by the desire for the fruit, so that he would eat (of it) and die, so that, when Adam was dead, he would afflict God by the death of Adam.


[p. 554] {#po.34.p554}
ܒܡܘܬܗ ܕܐܕܡ ܣܒܼܪ ܡܪ̈ܘܕܐ ܕܚܲܛܦ ܗܘܼܐ ܠܗ ܫܡܐ ܝܩܝܪ̈ܐ ܕܫܡ ܐܝܬܘܬܐ ܕܐܠܗ ܟܠܐ.
Through the death of Adam, the rebel thought to steal from Him the precious name of the name of the Essence of the God of all things.
ܒܥܐ ܡܪ̈ܘܕܐ ܕܢܣܒ ܕܪ̈ܓܐ ܒܛܘܥܝܝ ܕܐܕܡ ܘܣܚܦܗ ܪܡܙܐ ܒܕܡܘܬ ܒܪ̈ܩܐ ܡ̣ܢ ܫܘܠܛܢܗ.
The rebel sought to obtain a (higher) rank by leading Adam astray, but a sign, like a lightning bolt, dismissed him from his power.
ܢܦܼܠ ܚܣܡܐ ܡ̣ܢ ܪܒܘܬܗ ܘܫܠܚ ܫܘܒܚܗ ܘܟܕ ܣܒܿܪ ܗܘܼܐ ܕܡܬܒܝܐ ܗܘܼܐ ܣܓܝ ܠܗ ܟܐܒܗ.
The jealous one was cast down from his greatness and stripped of his glory, and although he believed that he would be consoled, his pain increased.
ܒܓܙܪ ܕܝܢܐ ܕܩܒܠ ܐܕܡ ܣܒܹܪ ܡܪܘܕܐ ܕܗܐ ܡ̣ܢ ܟܕܘ ܫ̇ܪܐ ܡܘܬܐ ܠܪܘܟܒ ܦܓܪܗ.
Following the sentence that Adam incurred, the rebel thought that death would immediately dissolve the structure of his body.
ܠܐ ܓܝܪ ܣܠܩܬܼ ܠܓܬ ܒܝܫܐ ܐܝܟ ܨܒܝܢܗ ܘܠܐ ܡܝܬ ܐܕܡ ܒܐܘܟܠܗ ܕܦܐܪ̈ܐ ܐܝܟ ܡܐ ܕܣܒܼܪ.
Now the desire of the Evil One was not realized according to his will, and Adam did not die by eating the fruit, as he (the rebel) thought.
ܡܬܼܚ ܓܝܪ ܚܝ̈ܘܗܝ ܬܫܥܡܐܐ ܘܝܬܝܪ ܒܬܪ̈ ܙܒܢܐ ܘܪܕܝ ܦܓܪܗ ܢܒܥܐ ܕܚܝ̈ܐ ܠܩܘܝܡ ܓܢܣܗ.
His life in fact was prolonged for nine hundred (years) and more, thereafter, and his body caused the source of life to flow, for the maintenance of his race.
ܪܕܝܗܝ ܠܘܚܡܐ ܒܓܙܪ̈ ܕܝܢܐ ܐܝܟ ܕܠܫܒܪ̈ܐ ܘܒܠܘܚܡܐ ܣܢܗ̇ ܠܚܛܝܬܐ ܥܠܬ ܡܘܬܗ.
The threat (which was) in the sentence chastised him, like a child, and because of the threat, he hated sin, the cause of his death.
ܠܘ ܚܕܬ̈ܬܐ ܩܢܘ ܡܝܘ̈ܬܐ ܒܓܙܪ ܕܝܢܐ ܒܗ ܒܟܝܢܐ ܣܡ ܒܪ̈ܘܝܐ ܗܠܝܢ ܕܓܕܫܼ.
These are not new things that mortals acquired through the sentence: the Creator had placed in nature itself those things which came to pass.
ܪܒܐ ܗܘܐ ܠܗ ܦܘܩܕܢ ܡܪܗ ܘܚܟܡ ܢܦܫܗ ܘܒܪ̈ܥܝܢܗ ܟܬܒ ܐܬܘܬ̈ܐ ܚܠܦ ܠܘܚܡܐ.
The commandment of his Lord was important to him, and he instructed his soul, and in his spirit he inscribed signs in place of the threat.
ܣܢܬܿ ܠܚܛܝܬܐ ܕܗܘܬ ܥܠܬܐ ܠܡܝܘܬܘܬܐ ܘܩܢܼܐ ܚܘܒܐ ܠܘܬ ܒܪ̈ܘܝܐ ܕܪ̈ܕܝܗܝ ܘܩܢܝܗ.
He hated the sin which became a cause of mortality, and he acquired love toward the Creator who chastised him and took charge of him.
ܩܕܝ̈ܡܢ ܗ݈ܘ̈ܝ ܗܠܝܢ ܕܐܕܡ ܠܘܬ ܒܪܘܝܐ ܘܠܘ ܒܬܘܬܐ ܐܥܠ ܡܘܬܐ ܕܠܐ ܒܨܒܝܢܗ.
These (feelings) of Adam toward the Creator were prior, and it is not out of regret that (God) introduced death, without having willed it.
ܠܥܘܕܪ̈ܢ ܐܕܡ ܡܠܠ ܐܢܝܢ ܐܝܟ ܚܕܬܬ̈ܐ ܕܒܢܣܝܢܐ ܢܝܐܠܦ ܐܕܡ ܨܒܝܢ ܡܪܗ.
He stated them as new things, for the help of Adam, so that Adam through the trial would learn the will of his Lord.
ܟܠܗܝܢ ܕܗܼܘ̈ܝ ܒܕܩ ܒܐܕܡ ܐܝܟ ܨܒܝܢܗ ܘܒܪܘܟܒܗ ܐܣܪ ܚܘܒܐ ܕܟܠ ܡܐ ܕܗܼܘܐ.
Everything that happened, he announced through Adam, as (being) his will, and to his formation he bound the love of all that exists.
ܚܝܒ ܐܕܡ ܥܠ ܟܠ ܕܗܼܘܐ ܠܡܦܪܥ ܫܘܒܚܐ ܠܗ̇ܘ ܒܪ̈ܘܝܐ ܕܚܘܝ ܚܘܒܗ ܠܘܬ ܬܘܩܢܘ̈ܗܝ܀
Adam must render glory for all that exists, to this Creator who showed his love toward his creations!


[p. 556] {#po.34.p556}
ܥܠ ܒܪܫܝܬ ܘܥܠ ܐܝܬܘܬܗ ܕܐܠܗܐ
ON (THE WORD) "IN THE BEGINNING" AND ON THE ESSENCE OF GOD

### The meaning of the first verse of Genesis
ܒܪ̈ܫܝܬ ܫܡܿܥܬ ܕܟܬܼܒ ܡܘܫܐ ܩܕܡ ܟܠܡܕܡ ܘܒܿܥܝܬ ܕܐܚܙܘܩ ܒܫܒܝܠ ܡܠܘ̈ܗܝ ܠܘܬ ܫܘܪ̈ܝܐ.
I noticed that Moses wrote before all things: "In the beginning," and I sought to traverse the path of his words toward the start.
ܚܡܫ ܐܬܘ̈ܢ ܚܿܙܝܬܗ ܕܪܵܫ̣ܼܡ ܒܪ̈ܫ ܣܘܙ̈ܓܕܐ ܘܡܠܟܢܝ ܪ̈ܥܝܢܝ ܠܡܬܗܓܝܘ ܒܚܕܬܘܬ ܣܦܪܗ.
I saw that he wrote five letters at the head of the verse and my spirit advised me to meditate on the novelty of his book.
ܐܝܟ ܕܠܟܐܦ̈ܐ ܦܣܼܠ ܐܬܘ̈ܬܐ ܒܦܪܙܠܐ ܕܪܘܚܐ ܘܒܢܼܐ ܥܠܝܗܝܢ ܒܝܬܐ ܪ̈ܒܐ ܕܪ̈ܘܡܐ ܘܥܘܡܩܐ.
He cut the letters like stones, with the hammer of the spirit, and he built upon them the great dwelling of above and below.
ܐܝܟ ܣܡܡܢ̈ܐ ܡܟܪܡܙܐ ܒܡܠ̈ܐ ܕܦܘܡܗ ܘܨܪ ܒܪ̈ܝܬܐ ܕܠܐ ܚܙܝ̈ܢ ܠܗ ܟܕ ܡܬܬܩ̈ܢܢ.
The Sign prepared like colors, with the words of his mouth, and he painted the creatures that he did not see when they were created.
ܒܕܡܘܬ ܨܠܡܐ ܪܟܒܗ ܠܥܠܡܐ ܕܗܼܘܐ ܒܪܫܝܬ ܘܫܩܼܠ ܣܡܗ ܩܕܡ ܚܙܝ̈ܐ ܕܢܬܒܩܘܢ ܒܗ.
In the manner of a painting, he composed the world that existed in the beginning, (then) he took it and placed it before the spectators so that they might contemplate it.
ܛܟܣܐ ܪܒܐ ܥܒܸܕ ܨܝܪܵܐ ܠܐܘܡܢܘܬܗ ܕܨܪ ܒܪ̈ܝܬܐ ܘܐܟܢ ܝܘܩܢܐ ܕܫܡ ܒܪ̈ܘܝܐ.
The painter put great order into his art, for he painted the creatures, then the image of the name of the Creator.
ܛܟܣܐ ܕܡܠܟܐ ܚܘܝ ܠܘܩܕܡ ܠܥܒܕܝ̈ ܡܠܟܐ ܘܐܟܢ ܐܪܝܡ ܘܐܠܐ ܕܬܪ̈ܥܐ ܕܒܝܬ ܡܠܟܘܬܐ.
To the servants of the king, he showed first the order (established) by the king, then he lifted the veil of the door of the royal palace.
ܐܝܟ ܫܬܐܣܬܐ ܣܡܗ̇ ܠܡܠܬܐ ܗّܝܬ ܕܒܪ̈ܫܝܬ ܘܠܥܠ ܡܢܗ̇ ܒܪܐ ܐܠܗܐ ܐܝܟ ܒܢܝܐ.
He placed as a foundation this word: "In the beginning," and above it, "God created," like a builder.
ܒܪ̈ܫܝܬ ܐܸܡܪ ܒܪܐ ܐܠܗܐ ܫܡܝܐ ܘܐܪܥܐ ܠܟܐ ܗܼܝ ܒܪ̈ܝܬܐ ܘܠܗܠ ܡܢܗ̇ ܡܪܐ ܒܪ̈ܝܬܐ.
"In the beginning," he says, "God created the heaven and the earth"; here is the creation, and beyond it, the master of the creation.
ܒܪ̈ܝܬܐ ܐܡܼܪ ܩܕܡ ܒܪ̈ܘܝܐ ܘܟܢ ܒܪ̈ܘܝܐ ܠܘ ܕܩܕܝܡܐ ܐܠܐ ܕܢܘܕܥ ܙܒܢܐ ܕܗܘܬ.
He says "creation" before "creator," and then "creator," not because it is prior, but to make known the time when it existed.
ܗܘܝܐ ܐܘܕܥ ܒܝܕ ܗܿܝ ܕܟܬܼܒ ܒܪ̈ܫܝܬ ܠܘܩܕܡ ܘܥܠ ܒܪ̈ܘܝܐ ܓܠܼܹܐ ܕܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܗܘܼܐ ܕܠܐ ܫܘܪ̈ܝܐ.
He made its existence known by what he wrote in the first place: "In the beginning," and concerning the Creator, he revealed that He was without beginning.

### The characteristics of the divine nature
ܕܠܐ ܫܘܪ̈ܝܐ ܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܐܝܬܝܐ ܕܐܝܬܝ ܒܪ̈ܝܬܐ ܘܕܠܐ ܫܘܠܡ ܡܩܘܐ ܒܛܟܣܐ ܕܐܡܝܢܘܬܗ.
Without beginning is the Being (by itself) that produced the creation, and He remains without end in His constant order.
ܕܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܘܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܡܨܐ ܪ̈ܥܝܢܐ ܕܢܐܡܪ ܒܠܚܘܕ ܘܕܐܝܟܢ ܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܐܚܝܕ ܬܪ̈ܥܐ ܩܕܡ ܥܘܩܒܗ.
The mind can only say that He is and that He is, but the door is closed before its search (to know) how He is.


[p. 558] {#po.34.p558}
ܠܝܬ ܠܟܝܢܗ ܕܬܚܙܝܘܗܝ ܥܝܢܐ ܕܥܒܕ ܐܝܕܘ̈ܗܝ ܘܠܐ ܠܥܒ̈ܕܘܗܝ ܕܢܬܒܩܘܢ ܒܗ ܠܦܘܬ ܪ̈ܓܬܗܘܢ.
It does not belong to His nature that the eye of the work of His hands should see Him, nor to His works to consider Him as they please.
ܕܠܐ ܪܘܟܒܐ ܗܼܘ ܘܕܠܐ ܣܟܐ ܗܼܘ ܘܕܠܐ ܚܙܬܐ ܗܼܘ ܘܠܐ ܡܫܬܚܠܦ ܡܸܢ ܗܝ ܕܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܠܘܬ ܕܠܩܘܒܠܐ.
He is uncompounded, infinite, invisible, and He cannot change from what He is into what is contrary.
ܪܡ ܗܼܘ ܟܝܢܗ ܡ̣ܸܢ ܫܘܚܠܦ̈ܐ ܘܡ̣ܢ ܚܙܝ̈ܐ ܘܠܝܬ ܒܥܒܝܕ̈ܐ ܕܣ̇ܦܩ ܗܘܢܗ ܕܢܐܡܪ ܕܟܡܐ ܗܼܘ.
His nature is too far above changes and observers, and among created (beings), there is none whose intellect is capable of saying how it is (exalted).
ܟܝܢܐ ܕܥܠ ܟܠ ܐܚܝܕ ܢܘܡ̈ܐ ܒܪ̈ܘܡܐ ܘܥܘܡܩܐ ܘܠܓܘ ܡܢܗ ܚܒܝܫ ܟܠܡܕܡ ܕܗܼܘܐ ܡܢܗ.
Nature that extends over all things, in the (world) above and below, and within which is enclosed all that has been (made) by Him!
ܡܢܗ ܡܠܝܐ ܒܪ̈ܝܬܐ ܕܒܪܐ ܡ̣ܢ ܠܐ ܡܕܡ ܘܗܢܘ ܬܗܪܐ ܕܟܕ ܠܝܬ ܡܕܡ ܒܪܐ ܟܠܡܕܡ.
The creation that He created from nothing is full of Him; and it is a marvel that, when nothing existed, He created all things!
ܒܪ̈ܡܙܗ ܩܐܡ ܥܠܡܐ ܪܒܐ ܥܠ ܠܐ ܡܕܡ ܘܗܐ ܬܕܡܘܪ̈ܬܐ ܕܣܝܡ ܟܠܡܕܡ ܥܠ ܠܐ ܡܕܡ.
By His sign, the vast world arose upon nothingness, and it is a prodigy that everything was placed upon nothing!
ܒܚܝܠ ܚܝܘܬܐ ܡܿܚܐ ܟܝܢ̈ܐ ܡܠܝ̈ܠܐ ܘܚܪ̈ܫܐ ܘܗܐ ܬܚܘܝܬܐ ܕܪܒܘܬ ܚܝܠܗ ܒܗܘܢ ܒܟܝܢ̈ܐ.
By the power of life, He makes the endowed-with-speech and mute natures live; and here is the proof of the greatness of His power in these very natures.
ܟܝܢ̈ܐ ܩܥܿܝܢ ܥܠ ܪܒܘܬܗ ܕܠܐ ܡܬܕܪ̈ܟܐ ܘܒܣܘܥܪ̈ܢܐ ܡܚܘܝܢ ܚܝܠܐ ܕܥܒܘܕܘܬܗ.
The natures proclaim that His greatness is incomprehensible, and by (their) acts, they manifest the power of His action.

### One must not search for God except through love
ܠܐ ܡܬܕܪ̈ܟܐ ܐܘܡܢܘܬܗ ܡܸܢ ܒܥܝ̈ܐ ܕܟܡܐ ܕܒܥܿܝܢ ܠܗܠ ܡܸܢ ܣܟܐ ܣܝܡܐ ܒܥܬܗ.
His art cannot be understood by those who search, for, search as they may, the search for Him is situated beyond (any) limit.
ܠܐ ܠܗ ܣܟܐ ܘܠܐ ܠܒܥܬܗ ܫܘܠܡ ܡܠܐ ܘܐܢܫܐ ܠܐܐ ܐܝܢܐ ܕܒ̇ܿܥܐ ܒܥܬܐ ܕܥܠܘܗܝ.
He has no limit, and in searching for Him, there is no end to the discourse, and the man who searches for what concerns Him grows weary.
ܦܘܫ ܒܥܝܐ ܠܐ ܬܬܦܫܚ ܒܒܥܬ ܟܣܝܐ ܕܥܝ ܠܟܝܢܟ ܘܗܒ ܪ̈ܒܘܬܐ ܠܡܬܩܢܢܟ.
Refrain, seeker, do not be distracted by the search for the hidden (Being); know yourself and give glory to Him who constituted you!
ܒܥܝ ܐܢ ܒܥܐ ܐܢܬ ܒܥܬܐ ܕܦܐ̈ܝܐ ܠܕܠܐ ܣܟܐ ܕܐܝܟܢ ܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܟܝܢܐ ܫܘܝܐ ܩܢܘܡ̈ܐ ܬܠܬܐ.
If you search, search as is fitting for one who is without limit, how He is one equal nature and three Persons.
ܒܥܝ ܕܐܝܟܢ ܐܝܬ ܒܟܝܢܐ ܕܠܐ ܫܘܪ̈ܝܐ ܐܒܗܘܬܐ ܘܝܠܝܕܘܬܐ ܘܢܦܘܩܘܬܐ.
Search how there is in the nature without beginning, Paternity, Filiation, and Procession.
ܐܦܠܐ ܗܕܐ ܠܘ ܒܒܥܬܐ ܬܒܼܥܐ ܕܐܝܟܢ ܐܠܐ ܒܚܘܒܐ ܐܝܟ ܡܐ ܕܦܐܐ ܠܐܝܢܐ ܕܝܿܠܦ.
Even this, it is not by research that you will search for the 'how' of it, but by love, as befits one who studies.
ܒܚܘܒܐ ܦܐܝܐ ܗܝ ܠܡܒܥܐ ܒܥܬܐ ܕܥܠ ܐܝܬܘܬܐ ܘܕܠܐ ܒܨܬܐ ܫܦܝܪ̈ ܗܙܪ̈ܓܐ ܕܥܠ ܟܣܝܘܬܐ.
It is with love that it befits one to search for what concerns the Essence, and without investigation, meditation on the hidden (nature) is good.
ܡܕܝܢ ܠܐ ܥܕܝܠ ܐܝܢܐ ܕܒܥ̇ܐ ܠܡܐܠܦ ܫܦܝܪ ܐܠܐ ܐܝܢܐ ܕܒܚܪ̈ܝܢܐ ܡܪ̈ܟܒ ܡܠ̈ܐ.
Thus, he who seeks to study well is not reprehensible, but he (is) who composes discourses for the sake of polemics.
ܠܡܐܠܦ ܥܿܠܬ ܠܒܝܬ ܢܡܘܣ̈ܐ ܕܡܠܟ ܡܠܟ̈ܐ ܘܕܐܚܙܐ ܛܟܣܐ ܕܒܝܬ ܡܠܟܘܬܐ ܕܠܐ ܚܙܝܐ ܠܝ.
I have entered into the tribunal of the King of kings to learn, and to see the ordinance of the royal palace that I had not seen.


[p. 560] {#po.34.p560}
ܠܘ ܕܐܬܒܩܐ ܒܟܣܝܐ ܥُܠܬ ܠܐܝܟܐ ܕܥُܿܠܬ ܐܠܐ ܕܐܫܡܥ ܡܡܠܠܐ ܚܕܬܐ ܥܠ ܟܣܝܘܬܐ.
It is not to consider the hidden (Being) that I entered where I entered, but to hear a new discourse on His hidden (nature).

### The Mosaic revelation on the divine nature
ܡܡܠܠܐ ܚܕܬܐ ܟܬܒ ܚܕܓܒܪ̈ܐ ܒܩܢܝܐ ܕܪ̈ܘܚܐ ܘܒܥ̇ܝܬ ܕܐܠܦ ܚܝܠ ܣܘܟܠܐ ܕܟܣܐ ܒܡܠܘ̈ܗܝ.
A man wrote a new discourse with the reed of the Spirit, and I sought to inform myself of the power of meaning that hides in his words.
ܒܪ̈ܫܝܬ ܬܙܪ̈ܓܡ ܚܕ ܟܪ̈ܘܙܐ ܒܝܬ ܐܪ̈ܥܢܝܐ ܘܨܿܒܝܬ ܕܐܕܥ ܕܡܢܐ ܗܝ ܥܠܬܐ ܕܟܪ̈ܘܙܘܬܗ.
Among earthly (beings), a herald explained: "In the beginning," and I wanted to know what was the reason for his proclamation.
ܒܪܫ ܦܬܓܡܘ̈ܗܝ ܚܿܙܝܬ ܟܕ ܪ݁ܗܶ̇ܛ ܫܡܐ ܕܒܪ̈ܫܝܬ ܘܠܗܠ ܡܢܗ ܫܡ ܒܪ̈ܘܝܐ ܕܒܼܪܵܐ ܒܪ̈ܫܝܬ. ܫܡ ܐܠܗܐ ܫܡܥܿ̇ܬ ܕܒ̣ܪܵܐ ܒܖ̈ܝܬܐ ܠܐ ܒܪܫܝܬ ܘܫܚܩܢܝ ܪ̈ܥܝܢܝ ܕܐܒܥܐ ܒܚܘܒܐ ܒܥܬܐ ܕܫܡܗ.
At the head of his words, I saw the word "in the beginning" running, and beyond it, the name of the Creator who created in the beginning. I heard the name of God who created the creation in the beginning, and my spirit urged me to search for His name with love.
ܫܡܗ ܐܣܦ ܒܝ ܢܘܪܐ ܕܫܡܗ ܕܐܕܥ ܠܫܡܗ ܘܗܐ ܡܛܝܒ ܐܢܐ ܕܐܩܪ̈ܝܘܗܝ ܒܫܡܐ ܕܦܐܐ ܠܫܡܗ. ܐܠܗ ܟܠܐ ܘܒ̇ܪ̈ܐ ܒܪܝܬܐ ܩܪ̈ܝܗܝ ܒܪ ܥܡܪܡ ܘܗܐ ܡܕܡܐ ܐܢܐ ܕܐܩܪܝܘܗܝ ܐܦ ܐܢܐ ܐܝܟ ܡܐ ܕܩܼܪ̈ܝܗܝ.
His name ignited in me the fire of His name, so that I might know His name, and here I am ready to call Him by the name that befits His name. "God of all things and Creator of the creation," the son of Amram called Him, and here I imitate him, for I too shall call Him as he called Him.
ܕܐܠܗ ܟܠܐ ܗܼܘ ܫܿܠܡ ܐܢܐ ܠܗ ܠܒܪ ܥܒܪ̈ܝܐ ܘܕܒܪ̈ܘܝܐ ܗܼܘ ܡܩ̇ܠܣ ܐܢܐ ܠܗ ܠܥܿܒܕ ܐܝܕ̈ܘܗܝ ܕܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܐܝܬܝܐ ܕܠܐ ܫܘܖ̈ܝܐ ܫܖ̈ܝܪ̈ܐ ܠܝ ܘܕܠܝܬ ܫܘܠܡ ܠܐܡܝܢܘܬܗ ܠܐ ܡܓܠ ܐܢܐ ܕܠܐ ܡܣܬܝܟܪ ܟܝܢ ܐܝܬܘܬܗ ܡܩ̇ܒܠ ܐܢܐ ܠܗ ܘܕܠܐ ܢܦܠ ܬܚܝܬ ܚܙܝ̈ܐ ܡܗܝܡܢ ܐܢܐ ܒܗ.
That He is the God of all things, I agree with this Hebrew, and that He is the Creator, I appeal to the very work of His hands! That He is the Being without beginning, I am certain, and that there is no end to His duration, I do not deny (it). That the nature of His Essence is infinite, I admit; and that it does not fall under the gaze, I believe.
ܕܠܐ ܡܫܬܚܠܦ ܡܸܢ ܡܐ ܕܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܪ̈ܥܝܢܝ ܣܲܗܕ ܘܕܠܐ ܚܐܲܫ ܒܡܚܝ̈ܠܬܐ ܠܫܢܝ ܩܥܿܐ. ܕܥܡ ܐܝܬܘܬܗ ܩܢܐ ܝܕܥܬܐ ܕܗܠܝܢ ܕܗܘܼ̈ܝ ܘܐܝܟ ܕܒܠܘܚ̈ܐ ܟܬܝܒܐ ܐܝܬ ܒܡܕܥܝ ܘܠܐ ܡܬܠܚܝܐ.
That He is immutable from what He is, my spirit bears witness (to it), and that He does not suffer from weaknesses, my tongue proclaims (it). That with His Essence He possesses the knowledge of what is, it is written in my spirit as on tablets, and it is indelible.
ܕܝܕܥ ܗܼܘܐ ܟܠ ܐܝܟܢ ܗܿܘܐ ܥܕܠܐ ܢܗܘܐ ܠܥܝܢ ܐܪ̈ܥܢܐ ܘܫܡܝ̈ܢܐ ܡܟܪܙ ܐܢܐ ܠܗܿ.
That He knows all things as they will be, before they exist, I announce before earthly and celestial (beings).

### The relations between the Trinity and the divine unity
ܕܐܝܬ ܒܟܝܢܗ ܩܢܘܡ̈ܐ ܫܘ̈ܝܐ ܕܫ̇ܘܝܢ ܥܡܗ ܫܡܝܐ ܘܐܪܥܐ ܢܩܥܘܢ ܥܡ ܦܘܡܝ ܕܗܟܢ ܐܝܬܝܗܿ.
That there be in His nature equal persons who, with Him, are equal, may heaven and earth proclaim with my mouth that it is so!
ܕܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܐܒܐ ܘܒܪܐ ܘܪ̈ܘܚܐ ܚܕܐ ܐܝܬܘܬܐ ܡܠܝ̈ܠܥܐ ܘܚܪ̈ܫܐ ܢܡܘܢ ܐܝܕܐ ܒܫܛܪ ܡܠܝ̈. ܕܐܒܐ ܐܒܐ ܗܼܘ ܘܒܪܐ ܝܠܕܐ ܗܼܘ ܘܪ̈ܘܚܐ ܐܝܬܝܐ ܗܼܘ ܒܫܒܝܠ ܬܘܕܝܬܝ ܢܪ̈ܕܘܢ ܐܢܫܐ ܘܪ̈ܘܚܢܝܐ.
That He is Father, Son, and Spirit, one single Essence, may the endowed-with-speech and mute (beings) subscribe to the act of my words! That the Father be Father, the Son begotten, and the Spirit Being by itself, may men and spiritual (beings) walk in the way of my profession of faith!


[p. 562] {#po.34.p562}
ܕܠܝܬ ܒܝܬ ܐܒܐ ܠܒܪܐ ܘܪ̈ܘܚܐ ܛܘܪ̈ܐ ܘܪܡܙܐ ܐܠܘ ܐܝܬ ܗܘܼܐ ܒܪ̈ܝܬܐ ܐܚܪ̈ܢܝܬܐ ܙܥܘܪ̈ܝܐ ܕܬܘܕܐ. ܕܠܐ ܡܣܬܕܩܝܢ ܒܝܕ ܦܘܪ̈ܫܢܐ ܚܕ ܡ̣ܢ ܚܒܪ̈ܗ ܠܝܬ ܒܥܒܝܕ̈ܐ ܕܢܐܡܪ ܕܟܡܐ ܗܝ ܗܺܝ ܫܘܝܘܬܐ.
That there be no interval between the Father and the Son and the Spirit, not (even) a sign, if the creation were other, (it would be) too weak to confess it! That they not be separated from one another by a distinction, among the creatures, there is none who (can) say how this equality is.
ܕܟܕ ܚܕ ܐܢܘܢ ܬܠܬܐ ܐܢܘܢ ܘܒܚܕ ܐܢܘܢ ܠܝܬ ܠܫܢܐ ܕܡܨܐ ܢܩܠܣ ܠܗ̇ܺܝ ܐܝܬܘܬܐ.
May they be three, being one, and may they be in the One; there is no tongue that can praise this Essence!
ܠܐ ܗܼܘܐ ܐܝܬܝ̈ܐ ܬܠܬܐ ܐܡܿܪܬ ܒܗ̇ܝ ܕܬܠܬܐ ܐܠܐ ܩܢܘܡ̈ܐ ܬܠܬܐ ܕܫ̇ܘܝܢ ܒܗ̇ ܒܐܝܬܘܬܐ. ܚܕܐ ܐܝܬܘܬܐ ܐܡܿܪ̄ܬ ܕܐܝܬܝܗ̇ ܘܬܘܒ ܐܡܿܪ ܐܢܐ ܘܒܗ̇ ܒܐܝܬܘܬܐ ܩܢܘܡ̈ܐ ܬܠܬܐ ܕܠܐ ܡܣܬܕܩܝܢ.
There are not three Beings, I said by this word "three," but three Persons who are equal in the same Essence. I said that there is a unique Essence, and I say it again; and in this very Essence, (there are) three Persons who are not separated.

### An enumeration has been fixed to indicate the Persons
ܠܒܘܕܩ ܩܢܘܡ̈ܐ ܣܝܡ ܡܢܝܢܐ ܚܕ ܒܬܪ ܚܕ ܕܢܕܥ ܟܠܢܫ ܕܫܪ̈ܝܪܝܢ ܛܟܣܐ ܕܩܢܘܡܝ̈ܗܘܢ. ܫܪܪ̈ܐ ܓܠܝܐ ܡܟܪ̈ܙ ܛܟܣܐ ܕܫܡܗܝ̈ܗܘܢ ܕܟܠ ܕܐܝܬ ܠܐܒܐ ܐܦ ܐܝܬ ܠܒܪܐ ܘܠܪܘܚ ܩܘܕܫܐ. ܚܕ ܗܼܘ ܟܝܢܐ ܘܚܕ ܫܘܠܛܢܐ ܘܚܕ ܨܒܝܢܐ ܘܠܝܬ ܦܘܪ̈ܫܢܐ ܕܪܒ ܐܘ ܕܙܥܘܪ ܒܝܬ ܚܕ ܠܐܚܪ̈ܝܢ. ܠܝܬ ܒܐܝܬܘܬܐ ܡܕܡ ܕܒܨܝܪ ܡ̣ܢ ܐܝܬܘܬܐ ܕܟܠ ܕܐܝܬܘܬܐ ܗܝ ܠܝܬ ܬܘܒ ܕܪ̈ܓܐ ܕܪܒ ܐܘ ܕܙܥܘܪ. ܠܐ ܩܕܝܡܐ ܐܒܗܘܬܐ ܕܐܒܐ ܠܝܠܕܗ ܘܠܐ ܬܘܒ ܒܪ̈ܘܬܐ ܕܒܪܐ ܒܨܝܪܐ ܡ̣ܢ ܝܠܘܕܗ. ܠܐ ܛܠܐ ܪܘܚܐ ܠܐ ܡ̣ܢ ܐܒܐ ܘܠܐ ܡ̣ܢ ܝܠܕܐ ܠܐ ܒܐܝܬܘܬܐ ܘܠܐ ܒܫܘܠܛܢܐ ܕܥܠ ܟܠ ܒܪ̈ܝܢ. ܚܕ ܐܝܬܝܗܘܢ ܟܡܐ ܐܢ ܣܝܡܝܢ ܚܕ ܒܬܪ ܚܕ ܬܠܬܐ ܐܢܘܢ ܟܡܐ ܐܢ ܚܕ ܗܼܘ ܟܝܢܐ ܕܒܗܘܢ. ܬܠܬܐ ܐܢܘܢ ܡܛܠ ܛܟܣܐ ܕܫܡܗܝ̈ܗܘܢ ܘܬܘܒ ܚܕ ܐܢܘܢ ܒܗ̇ܝ ܕܟܝܢܐ ܫܘܐ ܗܼܘ ܒܟܝܢܗ. ܠܘ ܒܦܣܩܐ ܘܒܦܘܪ̈ܫܢܐ ܦܝܪ̈ܝܫ ܚܕ ܡ̣ܢ ܚܕ ܐܠܐ ܒܫܡܐ ܘܒܡܢܝܢܐ ܕܠܐ ܒܘܠܒܠܐ. ܘܗܐ ܬܚܘܝܬܐ ܕܗܕܐ ܕܐܡܿܪܿܬ ܒܒܪ̈ܝܬܗܘܢ ܕܐܝܬ ܗܼܘ ܡܕܡ ܕܟܕ ܚܕ ܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܐܝܬ ܒܗ ܡܕܡ. ܚܘܪ ܒܐܣܦܝܪܐ ܕܫܡܫܐ ܪܒܐ ܕܡܿܢܗܪ ܥܠ ܟܠ ܕܟܕ ܚܕ ܐܝܬܝܗ̇ ܐܝܬ ܒܗ̇ ܢܘܗܪܐ ܘܚܡܝܡܘܬܐ.
one after the other, so that everyone may know that the order of their persons is true. The order of their names proclaims a manifest truth: everything that is the Father's, is also the Son's and the Holy Spirit's. One is the nature, one the power, one the will; and there is no distinction of "greater" or "smaller" between one and the other. There is not in the Essence something that is lesser than the Essence, for everything that is the Essence does not possess any higher or lower degree either. The fatherhood of the Father is not prior to the One whom He begat, nor is the sonship of the Son inferior to the One who begat Him. The Spirit is not posterior to the Father, nor to the Son, neither by Essence nor by power over all creatures. They are one, even if they are placed one after the other; they are three, even if the nature that is in them is unique. They are three, because of the order of their names; and they are equally one, because the nature is equal in itself. It is not by cutting off or by separation that one is distinguished from the other, but by name and by enumeration, without confusion. A philosophical principle, applied to the sun. And behold, in their creations, is the proof of what I have said: there are things which, while being one, possess within them (another) thing. Look at the globe of the great sun that illuminates all things: although it is unique, there is in it light and heat.


[p. 564] {#po.34.p564}
ܚܕ ܗܼܘ ܟܝܢܐ ܕܐܣܦܝܪܐ ܕܫܡܫܐ ܘܕܙܠܝܩܝ̈ܗ̇ ܘܠܐ ܩܕܝܡܐ ܠܐ ܗܝ ܠܕܝܠܗ̇ ܘܠܐ ܕܝܠܗ̇ ܠܗ̇. ܒܗ̇ ܐܣܝܢܝܢ ܢܘܗܪܐ ܘܚܘܡܐ ܘܗܝ ܒܬܪ̈ܝܗܘܢ ܘܟܕ ܚܕ ܐܢܘܢ ܬܠܬܐ ܐܢܘܢ ܒܣܘܥܪ̈ܢܝܗܘܢ. ܐܣܦܝܪܐ ܕܫܡܫܐ ܘܢܘܗܪܐ ܘܚܘܡܐ ܬܠܬܐ ܐܢܘܢ ܘܬܠܬܐ ܒܚܕ ܬܘܒ ܠܐ ܡܬܝܕܥܝܢ ܕܠܐ ܦܘܪ̈ܫܢܐ. ܠܐ ܐܬܚܙܝܬ ܡܬܘܡ ܐܣܦܝܪܐ ܕܠܐ ܙܠܝܩ̈ܐ ܘܠܐ ܙܠܝܩ̈ܐ ܕܢܚܘ ܡ̣ܢ ܥܘܒܗ̇ ܒܠܥܕ ܚܘܡܐ. ܗܺܝ ܐܝܟ ܕܐܝܬܝܗ̇ ܐܝܬܝܗ̇ ܒܛܟܣܐ ܕܟܢܝܫܘܬܗ̇ ܘܚܺܝܠ̈ܐ ܕܥܡܗ̇ ܒܐܡܝܢܘܬܐ ܕܠܐ ܡܣܬܕܩܐ. ܒܕܡܘܬ ܝܠܕܐ ܢܦܩ ܨܡܚܐ ܡ̣ܢ ܓܘ ܥܘܒܗ̇ ܘܟܕ ܛܒ ܢ̇ܦܩ ܠܘ ܒܦܣܩܐ ܢܿܦܩ ܡܢܗܿ. ܒܛܟܣܐ ܕܪܘܚܐ ܩܢܝܐ ܚܝܠܐ ܕܚܡܝܡܘܬܐ ܘܫܪܝܐ ܒܓܘܗ̇ ܘܢܦܩܐ ܡܢܗ̇ ܕܠܐ ܫܘܢܝܐ. ܠܟ ܬܫܒܘܚܬܐ ܒܪ̈ܘܡܐ ܘܥܘܡܩܐ ܐܝܬܝܐ ܓܢܝܙܐ ܕܒܪܝܬ ܥܠܡ̈ܐ ܕܠܥܠ ܘܕܠܬܚܬ ܒܪܡܙܐ ܕܚܝܠܟ. ܗܐ ܒܥܒܝ̈ܕܐ ܬܚܘܝܬ ܚܝܠܐ ܕܐܠܗܘܬܟ. ܕܚܿܙܩܝܢ ܘܫܳܪܺܝܢ ܕܠܐ ܫܘܢܝܐ ܡ̣ܢ ܕܘܟ ܠܐܚܪ̈ܝܢ. ܚܿܙܩܝܢ ܚܝܠ̈ܐ ܬܪ̈ܝܢ ܒܐܣܦܝܪܐ ܟܕ ܠܐ ܚܿܙܩܝܢ ܘܫܳܪܺܝܢ ܨܝܕ ܟܠ ܟܕ ܠܐ ܡܫ̈ܢܝܢ ܡ̣ܢ ܕܘܟܬܗܘܢ. ܥܒܰܪ ܚܝܠܝܐ ܕܣܥܘܪ̈ܘܬܗܘܢ ܥܠ ܟܠ ܘܒܟܠ ܘܠܐ ܡܬܥܘܟܐ ܩܠܝܠܘܬܗ ܒܡܬܚܐ ܕܥܠ ܟܠ.
One is the nature of the solar globe and its rays, and (the sun) is not prior to its (properties), nor its (properties) to it. Light and heat are accumulated in it, and it is in both; and although they are one, they are three by their operations. The solar globe, the light, and the heat are three (things), and the three together are also known indistinctly. The globe has never been seen without rays, nor have the rays appeared from its bosom without heat. It, as it is, exists according to the order of its association, and the powers that (are) with it (exist) by an indivisible continuity. Like a child, the ray comes out of its bosom, and although it truly comes out of it, it is not by detaching itself that it comes out. In a spiritual order, (the globe) possesses the power of heat, and it remains in it, and it comes out of it without movement. To You, praise in the (world) above and below, hidden Being, who, with a sign of Your power, created the upper and lower worlds! Behold, the power of Your divinity is manifested in the created (things), for they move and remain without movement, from one place to another. Two forces in the globe move, while not moving, and remain beside all things, while not moving from their place. The power of their action passes over all things and into all things, and its speed is not hindered by the distance that (reigns) to all things.
ܘܐܢܗܘ ܕܫܡܫܐ ܥܒܕܐ ܥܒܝܕܐ ܬܡܝܗ̇ ܬܘܩܢܗ ܡܶܢ ܠܐ ܢܬܡܗ ܒܚܝܠ ܥܒܘܕܐ ܕܠܐ ܡܣܬܝܟ.
And if the sun is an admirable created work in its constitution, who would not admire the infinite power of the Author?
ܠܐ ܬܝܟ ܣܟܗ ܒܣܟܐ ܕܡܣܬܝܟܢ̈ܐ ܘܠܐ ܡܬܕܪ̈ܟܐ ܒܥܬܐ ܕܥܠܘܗܝ ܡ̣ܢ ܒܥܝ̈ܐ.
Its limit is not limited by the limit of limited (beings), and the search concerning it is not attained by those who search.

### Another application of the principle to the human soul
ܘܐܢ ܐܝܬ ܕܒܥܿܐ ܕܢܒܥ ܡܠܐ ܕܗܦܟܬ̈ܐ ܢܒܥ ܡܕܝܢ ܘܢܚܘܐܢ ܗܠܝܢ ܕܝܠܢܼ
And if there is (someone) who seeks to produce contradictory words, let him produce (them) then and let him demonstrate to us these things that (are) ours:
ܢܚܘܐ ܕܐܝܟܢ ܫܪ̈ܝܐ ܢܦܫܐ ܒܓܘ ܗܕܡ̈ܐ ܘܟܣܝܐ ܡ̣ܢ ܟܠ ܘܓܠܐ ܚܝܠܐ ܕܣܥܘܪ̈ܘܬܗܿ.
Let him demonstrate how the soul remains inside the members, and (how) it is hidden from all, and (how) the power of its acting is manifest.
ܐܝܟܢ ܩܢܝܐ ܐܦ ܗܝ ܚܝܠ̈ܐ ܬܪ̈ܝܢ ܒܟܝܢܗ̇ ܘܐܝܬܝܗܘܢ ܒܗ̇ ܘܠܒܪ ܡܢܗ̇ ܕܠܐ ܦܘܪ̈ܫܢܐ.
How it also possesses two powers within itself and (how) they exist in it and outside of it indistinctly.


[p. 566] {#po.34.p566}
ܐܝܟܢ ܪܗܛ ܚܝܠ ܚܝܘܬܗ̇ ܒܟܠܗܘܢ ܕ̈ܥܫܐ ܘܠܐ ܡܬܥܘܟܐ ܩܠܝܠܘܬܗ̇ ܒܓܘܫܡ̈ܐ ܥܒܝ̈ܐ.
How the power of its vitality flows into all (its) senses, and (how) its speed is not hindered by dense bodies.
ܐܝܟܢ ܚܙܩܐ ܡܠܬܐ ܕܡܢܗ̇ ܠܒܪ ܡ̣ܢ ܦܓܪ̈ܐ ܘܠܐ ܡܬܪܚܩܐ ܠܐ ܡ̣ܢ ܨܐܕܝܗ̇ ܘܠܐ ܡ̣ܢ ܨܝܕ ܟܠ.
How the word, which is (issued) from it, goes out of the body, but does not move away either from it or from everything.
ܐܝܟܢ ܒܗܿ ܗܝ ܘܠܒܪ ܡܢܗ̇ ܒܥܬܐ ܟܠܗ̇ ܘܟܡܐ ܕܢܒܥܐ ܠܐ ܡܬܕܪ̈ܟܐ ܒܨܬܐ ܕܥܠܝܗܿ.
How all its searching is in it and outside of it, and (how), as much as we search, the examination of what concerns it is not attained.
ܡܢܘ ܡ̣ܢ ܡܬܘܡ ܚܙܗ̇ ܒܚܙܬܐ ܕܕ̈ܓܫܐ ܕܠܒܪ ܘܡܢܘ ܠܒܟܗ̇ ܘܐܬܒܩܝ ܒܗ̇ ܒܥܝܢܝ̈ ܒܣܪ̈ܐ.
Who has ever seen it, with the vision of external senses, and who has apprehended and considered it with carnal eyes?
ܘܐܢܗܘ ܕܐܝܬ ܒܢ ܡܕܡ ܕܥܣܩ ܡܢ ܒܘܚܪ̈ܢܢ ܠܡܿܢ ܠܐܝܢܢ ܠܡܒܥܐ ܒܥܬܐ ܕܠܐ ܡܬܒܥܝܐ.
And if there is in us something too difficult to scrutinize, why tire ourselves searching for what cannot be searched?

### Searching for God in faith and through love
ܠܐ ܡܬܒܥܝܐ ܒܥܬܐ ܟܣܝܝܬܐ ܕܥܠ ܐܝܬܘܬܐ ܕܐܦܢ ܒܥܐ ܐܝܢܐ ܕܒܥܐ ܡܛܥܐ ܛܥܐ.
The hidden search (concerning) the Essence cannot be done, for even if he searches, the one who searches goes very much astray!
ܒܗܝܡܢܘܬܐ ܒܠܚܘܕ ܢܒܥܐ ܐܝܟ ܕܢܗܝܡܢ ܟܕ ܐܦ ܗܕܐ ܠܘ ܒܒܥܬܐ ܐܠܐ ܒܚܘܒܐ.
Let us search by faith only, so that we may believe, although this too is not (obtained) by searching, but by love.
ܒܚܘܒܐ ܡܨܝܐ ܗܝ ܕܢܒܥܐ ܒܥܬܐ ܕܟܝܢܐ ܟܣܝܐ ܕܐܝܟܢ ܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܘܐܝܬ ܒܗ ܩܢܘܡ̈ܐ ܬܠܬܐ ܕܫܘܝܢ.
Through love, it is possible for us to search for the hidden Nature, how it is and (how) there are in it three Persons who (are) equal. That there is a Father—the one who searches (for it) does well—and that (the Father) has a Begotten and a Spirit who (proceeds) from Him, it is fitting that he confess it.

### The creation "ex nihilo" is a work of divine love
ܕܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܐܒܐ ܫܦܝܪ ܒܥܐ ܐܝܢܐ ܕܒܥܐ ܘܐܝܬ ܠܗ ܝܠܕܐ ܘܪ̈ܘܚܐ ܕܡܢܗ ܦܐܝܐ ܕܢܘܕܐ.
It is fitting for created (beings) to confess at every moment the power of the Creator, who, when they did not exist, constituted them from nothing.
ܦܐܐ ܠܥܒܝ̈ܕܐ ܕܢܘܕܘܢ ܟܠܫܥ ܠܚܝܠ ܥܒܘܕܐ ܕܟܕ ܠܝܬ ܐܢܘܢ ܬܩܢ ܐܢܘܢ ܡ̣ܢ ܠܐ ܡܕܡ.
Nothing existed when He constituted all things from nothing; and because He willed it in His love, everything existed from nothing.
ܠܝܬ ܗܘܐ ܡܕܡ ܟܕ ܬܩܢ ܟܠ ܡ̣ܢ ܠܐ ܡܕܡ ܘܕܨܒܐ ܒܚܘܒܗ ܗܘܐ ܟܠܡܕܡ ܡ̣ܢ ܠܐ ܡܕܡ.
He alone existed before all things and beyond all things, and when it pleased Him, nothingness became a great thing.
ܗܘ ܒܠܚܘܕܘܗܝ ܐܝܬ ܗܘܐ ܩܕܡ ܟܠ ܘܠܗܠ ܡ̣ܢ ܟܠ ܘܟܕ ܫܦܪܬ ܠܗ ܗܘܐ ܠܐ ܡܕܡ ܪܒܐ.
These things that existed, existed with His Essence; it is not in nature but by (His) love that they were drawn in Him.
ܥܡ ܐܝܬܘܬܗ ܐܝܬܝܗܝܢ ܗܘܝ ܗܠܝܢ ܕܗܘܝ ܠܘ ܒܟܝܢܐ ܐܠܐ ܒܚܘܒܐ ܪ̈ܫܝܡܢ ܗܘܝ ܒܗ.
In Him were drawn the worlds that existed afterward, and calculated how many, how, and when they would exist.
ܒܗ ܨܝܪ̈ܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܥܠܡ̈ܐ ܕܗܘܘ ܒܬܪ ܙܒܢܝܐ ܘܡܢܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܠܗ ܕܟܡܐ ܘܐܝܟܢ ܘܐܡܬܝ ܗ̇ܘܝܢ.
It is not recently that He had thought to create afterward, and it was not in time that He considered time itself and made it afterward.
ܠܘ ܚܕܬܐܝܬ ܪܢܐ ܗܘܐ ܕܢܬܩܢ ܒܬܪ ܙܒܢܝܐ ܘܠܐ ܗܘܐ ܒܙܒܢܐ ܚܪܪ ܒܗ ܒܙܒܢܝܐ ܘܐܟܢ ܥܒܕܗ. ܛܟܣܐ ܥܒܕ ܠܗ ܠܥܒܕ ܐܝܕܘ̈ܗܝ ܕܐܡܬܝ ܢܗܘܐ ܟܕ ܡ̣ܢ ܨܐܕܘܗܝ ܥܒܝܕ ܗܘܐ ܘܓܡܝܪ ܥܕܠܐ ܢܗܘܐ.
He put order into the work of His hands, at the time when it will exist, although it had been made and finished beside Him before it existed.


[p. 568] {#po.34.p568}
ܡ̣ܢ ܫܘܪ̈ܝܐ ܕܠܐ ܫܘܪ̈ܝܐ ܣܝܡܐ ܗܼܘܬ ܠܗ ܕܢܓܠܐ ܚܝܠܐ ܕܥܒܘܕܘܬܗ ܒܥܒܕ ܐܝܕܘ̈ܗܝ.
From the beginning that (is) without beginning, it had been established by Him that He would reveal the power of His acting through the work of His hands.
ܚܟܝܡܐܝܬ ܥܒܼܕ ܟܠ ܕܥܒܹܕ ܚܟܝܡ ܟܠ ܘܠܐ ܐܢܫ ܝ̇ܿܕܥ ܠܡܬܒܩܝܘ ܒܚܝܠܐ ܕܚܟܡ̈ܬܗ.
The omniscient did everything He did with wisdom, but no one knows how to consider the power of His sciences.
ܚܘܒܢܐܝܬ ܚܘܝ ܚܘܒܗ ܢܒܥܐ ܕܚܘܒܐ ܘܠܝܬ ܒܥܒܝ̈ܕܐ ܕܡܨܐ ܠܡܦܪ̈ܥ ܚܘܒܬ ܚܘܒܗ.
He manifested with love His love, (He) the source of love, and among His creatures, there is none that can pay the debt of His love.
ܐܘܡܢܐܝܬ ܬܩܢܗ ܠܥܠܡܐ ܡܠܐ ܝܕܥܬܐ ܠܝܬ ܠܫܢܐ ܕܣ̇ܿܦܩ ܢܬܢܝܗ̇ ܠܐܘܡܢܘܬܗ.
With art He constituted the world, (He who is) full of science; there is no tongue that is capable of describing His art.
ܬܗܪ̈ܐ ܪܒܐ ܡܠܐ ܫܘܪ̈ܝܐ ܕܥܒܘܕܘܬܗ ܘܙܥܘܪ ܥܠܡܐ ܕܢܚܘܪ ܫܦܝܪ̈ ܘܢܬܒܩܐ ܒܗ.
The beginning of His action is full of great wonder, but the world is too weak to see it well and consider it.
ܘܐܠܘ ܐܝܬ ܗܼܘܘ ܥܠܡ̈ܐ ܥܦܝ̈ܦܐ ܐܠܦ ܐܠܦܝ̈ܢ ܙܥܘܪ̈ܝܢ ܗܼܘܘ ܛܒ ܠܡܼܚܙܐ ܒܪ̈ܝܬܐ ܟܕ ܡܬܬܩܢܝܐ.
And if the worlds had been multiplied by millions, they would be far too weak to see the creation when it was constituted.
ܐܠܘ ܒܪ̈ܝܢ ܗܼܘܘ ܟܢܫܝ̈ ܪܘܡܐ ܩܕܡ ܬܘܩܢܗ̇ ܡܬܒܠܗܝܢ ܗܼܘܘ ܟܕ ܬܩܢܐ ܗܼܘܬ ܡ̣ܢ ܠܐ ܡܕܡ.
If the assemblies from above had been created before its constitution, they would have been stunned when it was constituted from nothing.
ܐܠܘ ܡܦܣܝܢ ܕܐܝܟܢ ܡܢܫܠܝ ܗܘܐ ܟܠܡܕܡ ܡܘܦܐ ܗܼܘܐ ܠܗ ܚܝܠܐ ܕܒܗܘܢ ܠܡܬܕܡܪܘ.
If they had known how everything existed suddenly, the power that (was) in them would have been insufficient to marvel at it:
ܟܡܐ ܟܝ ܗ̇ܘܐ ܗ̇ܘ ܦܘܩܕܢܐ ܕܦܩܼܕ ܘܗܘܘ ܘܠܐ ܐܬܥܘܟܬ ܩܠܝܠܘܬܗ ܒܡܬܚܐ ܪ̈ܒܐ.
What is truly this command that He gave, and they existed, and its speed was not hindered by a great distance?
ܐܝܟܢ ܟܝ ܩܡܘ ܫܡܝܐ ܘܐܪ̈ܥܐ ܡ̣ܢ ܠܐ ܡܕܡ ܘܐܝܟܢ ܡܫܟܚ ܗ̇ܘ ܠܐ ܡܕܡ ܠܡܛܥܢ ܡܕܡ.
How then did heaven and earth arise from nothingness, and how can this nothingness bear anything?
ܠܘ ܠܐ ܡܕܡ ܛܥܢܗ ܠܝܘܩܪ̈ܐ ܕܗܿܘ ܡܐ ܕܗܼܘܐ ܐܠܐ ܪ̈ܡܙܐ ܕܗܿܘ ܦܘܩܕܢܐ ܕܦܩܼܕ ܘܗܼܘܐ.
It is not nothingness that bore the weight of it when it existed, but the sign of that command which He gave, and it existed.
ܦܩܼܕ ܡ̣ܢ ܫܠܝܐ ܪܡܙܐ ܟܣܝܐ ܕܫܠܝܛ ܥܠ ܟܠ ܘܩܡ ܟܠܡܕܡ ܥܠ ܠܐ ܡܕܡ ܟܕ ܠܝܬ ܡܕܡ.
The hidden sign that is capable of all things commanded suddenly, and everything arose upon nothingness while nothing existed.

### The various creations
ܡܬܼܚ ܠܫܡܝܐ ܒܕܡܘܬ ܢܚܬܐ ܠܥܠ ܡ̣ܢ ܟܠ ܘܪܘܼܥ ܠܐܪ̈ܥܐ ܐܝܟ ܫܬܐܣܬܐ ܬܚܝܬ ܟܠܡܕܡ.
He stretched out the heaven, like a garment, above all things, and He established the earth as a foundation below all things.
ܥܒ̣ܕ ܪ̈ܘܚܢܐ ܩܢܝܝ̈ ܚܝܠܐ ܕܠܐ ܡܢܝܢܐ ܘܡܢܥܐ ܐܢܘܢ ܟܕ ܛܒ ܥܣܩܐ ܠܡܡܢܐ ܐܢܘܢ.
He made the spiritual (beings) endowed with power, innumerable, and He numbered them, although it was very difficult to number them.
ܪܡܙ ܘܐܬܬܩܢ ܟܢܫܐ ܕܐܐܪ ܩܠܝܠ ܓܦ̈ܐ ܘܦܼܪܚ ܡܢܫܠܝ ܘܐܼܚܕ ܢܘܡ̈ܐ ܡܐܪ̈ܒܥܦܢܝܢ.
He made a sign, and the aerial host with swift wings was constituted; and it flew off all at once and spread to the four extremities (of the world).
ܐܪ̈ܕܝ ܡܝ̈ܐ ܐܣܦ ܢܘܪ̈ܐ ܦܪܸܲܣ ܚܫܘܟܐ ܘܟܣܝܗ ܠܥܡܠܗ ܒܫܥ̈ܐ ܕܠܠܝܐ ܐܝܟ ܕܒܢܚܬܐ.
He made the waters flow, ignited the fire, stretched out the darkness, and He hid His work as if with a garment, by the hours of the night.
ܒܢܚܬܐ ܕܚܫܟܐ ܟܣܝܗ̇ ܠܐܪ̈ܥܐ ܒܪ̈ܫܝܬ ܕܗܘܬ ܐܝܟ ܨܝܪ̈ܐ ܕܨܿܐܪ̈ ܨܠܡܐ ܘܟܢ ܓܿܠܐ ܠܗ.
With the garment of darkness, He covered the earth at the beginning of its existence, like a painter who paints a picture and then unveils it.


[p. 570] {#po.34.p570}
ܒܥܕܢ ܪ̈ܡܫܐ ܨܪܶܗ ܠܥܠܡܐ ܡܪܐ ܕܥܠܡܐ. ܒܥܕܢ ܨܦܪܐ ܓܠܹܐ ܬܚܦܝܬܐ ܕܠܥܠ ܡܢܗ.
In the evening, the Master of the world painted the world; in the morning, He uncovered the veil that (was) above it.
ܒܕܡܘܬ ܨܠܡܐ ܪܟܒ ܘܥܒܼܕ ܫܡܝܐ ܘܐܪ̈ܥܐ ܘܩܼܪ̈ܐ ܡܠܝܠܐ ܕܢܐܬܘܢ ܢܚܙܘܢ ܦܐܝܘܬ ܫܘܦܪܗ.
Like a painting, He composed and fashioned the heaven and the earth, and He invited the rational (beings) to come and see the splendor of His beauty.

### The creation of light, a source of instruction for the angels
ܟܠܗ ܠܠܝܐ ܟܣܝܗ ܠܫܘܦܪܐ ܕܐܘܡܢܘܬܗ ܘܠܐ ܨܒܼܐ ܕܢܓܠܐ ܕܐܝܟܢ ܐܡܪ ܘܩܡ ܟܠܡܕܡ.
For a whole night, He hid the beauty of His masterpiece, and He did not wish to reveal how He had spoken and everything had arisen.
ܫܠܝܐ ܪ̈ܒܐ ܕܡܐ ܗܘܼܐ ܥܠ ܟܠ ܟܕ ܬܩܢ ܟܠ ܘܒܗܝܠܝܢ ܗܼܘܘ ܟܝ̈ܢܝܐ ܡܠܝܠ̈ܐ ܐܦ ܫܬܝܩ̈ܐ.
A great silence rested upon all things, when He constituted all things, and the natures endowed with reason and the mute (natures) were tranquil.
ܬܪ̈ܬܝܢ ܘܥܣܪ ܫܥ̈ܐ ܐܓܪ ܫܠܝܐ ܥܠ ܟܠ ܘܡܐ ܕܐܫܬܠܡ ܐܫܡܥ ܩܠܐ ܕܢܗܘܐ ܢܘܗܖܪܐ.
For twelve hours, He prolonged the silence over all things, and when it was finished, He made a word heard: "Let there be light!"
ܩܠܐ ܚܕܬܐ ܩܥܼܐ ܒܒܪ̈ܝܬܐ ܡܪ̈ܝܐ ܒܪ̈ܝܬܐ ܘܙܥܼܘ ܣܕܪ̈ܝܗܘܢ ܕܫܡܝܢ̈ܐ ܒܪܒܘܬ ܩܠܗ.
The master of creation cried out to creation a new word, and the legions of the heavenly (beings) were moved because of the majesty of His word.
ܐܝܟ ܫܝܦܘܪܐ ܐܙܥܩ ܩܠܗ ܒܓܘ ܥܡܪ̈ܬܐ ܘܟܢܫܘ ܘܐܬܘ ܪ̈ܘܚܢܝܐ ܕܢܨܘܬܘܢܝܗܝ܀
He made His voice resound, like a trumpet, in the world, and the spiritual (beings) gathered and came to listen to Him.
ܡܛܠܬܗܘܢ ܐܫܡܥ ܩܠܐ ܠܐ ܩܢܐ ܩܠܐ ܕܢܠܦ ܐܢܘܢ ܕܪ̈ܡܙܗ ܬܩܢ ܠܗܘܢ ܘܠܟܠܐ.
He who possesses no voice made (His) voice heard because of them, to teach them that His sign had constituted them, as well as all things.
ܒܝܕ ܒܪܬ ܩܠܐ ܚܟܡ ܐܢܘܢ ܠܩܢܝܝ̈ ܡܠܬܐ ܕܢܗܘܘܢ ܝܕܥܝܢ ܕܗܼܘ ܬܩܢ ܟܠ ܡ̣ܢ ܠܐ ܡܕܡ.
By a word He instructed them, they who are endowed with reason, so that they might know that it is He who constituted everything from nothingness.
ܩܠܐ ܕܕܡܿܐ ܠܙܘܥܐ ܕܒܗܘܢ ܐܫܡܥ ܐܢܘܢ ܘܐܥܝܪ̈ ܐܢܘܢ ܠܡܬܒܩܝܘ ܒܬܘܩܢ ܢܘܗܪܐ.
He made them hear a voice that resembled a faculty that (was) within them, and He awakened them so that they might consider the creation of light.
ܐܝܟ ܕܒܫܢܬܐ ܛܒܝܥܝܢ ܗܼܘܘ ܠܠܝܐ ܟܠܗ ܘܢܼܕܘ ܡ̣ܢ ܫܠܝܐ ܒܝܕ ܒܪܬ ܩܠܐ ܕܙܥܩܬܼ ܒܐܪ̈ܥܐ.
As if in a sleep, they had been plunged throughout a whole night, and they shuddered suddenly at the word that resounded upon the earth.
ܬܡܗܐ ܪ̈ܒܐ ܪܡܐ ܗܼܘܐ ܥܠܝܗܘܢ ܩܕܡ ܗܢ ܩܠܐ ܘܡܬܕܡܪ̈ܝܢ ܗܼܘܘ ܕܐܝܟܢ ܟܝ ܩܡܘ ܟܕ ܠܝܬ ܐܢܘܢ.܆܀
A great astonishment settled within them, before this voice, and they (wondered) with amazement how they had arisen, while they did not exist.
ܒܙܘܥ̈ܐ ܕܒܗܘܢ ܡܬܪ̈ܥܝܢ ܗܘܼܘ ܥܠܝܗܘܢ ܘܥܠ ܟܠ ܕܡܿܢ ܟܝ ܨܒܬ ܗܘܝܐ ܕܝܠܗܘܢ ܘܕܟܠܡܕܡ.
Thanks to the faculties that (were) within them, they reflected upon themselves and upon all things: who had adorned their own existence and that of all things?
ܪ̈ܒܐ ܗܼܘܐ ܠܗܘܢ ܩܠ ܒܪ̈ܘܝܐ ܕܐܙܥܩ ܒܐܪ̈ܥܐ ܘܐܠܦ ܐܢܘܢ ܣܦܪ̈ܐ ܚܕܬܐ ܕܠܐ ܝܕܥܝܢ ܗܼܘܘ.
The voice of the Creator, which He made resound upon the earth, was for them a great (thing), and He taught them a new book that they did not know.
ܐܝܟ ܕܠܫܒܪ̈ܐ ܟܬܼܒ ܒܪܬ ܩܠܐ ܚܠܦ ܐܬܘܬ̈ܐ ܘܐܗܓܝ ܐܢܘܢ ܒܟܬܝܒܬ̈ܐ ܕܗܘܝ ܢܘܗܪܐ.
He wrote a word instead of letters, as if for children, and He made them meditate on writings (about) the existence of light.
ܒܕܡܘܬ ܚܘܩܐ ܬܪܨܗ ܠܩܠܐ ܩܕܡ ܥܝܢܝ̈ܗܘܢ ܘܫܪ̈ܝܘ ܩܿܥܝܢ ܒܪ̈ܝܟ ܒܪ̈ܘܝܐ ܕܒܪ̈ܐ ܢܘܗܪ̈ܐ.
He arranged the word before their gaze, like a line, and they began to exclaim: "Blessed be the Creator who created the light!"


[p. 572] {#po.34.p572}
ܢܗܘܐ ܢܘܗܪ̈ܐ ܩܥܼܐ ܗܿܘ ܩܠܐ ܠܐ ܩܢܐ ܩܠܐ ܘܢܦܩܬܼ ܡܠܬܐ ܠܘܬ ܣܘܥܪ̈ܢܐ ܕܠܐ ܬܘܗܝܐ.
He who possesses no voice cried out this word: "Let there be light!", and the word was realized without delay.
ܕܢܼܚ ܡ̣ܢ ܫܠܝܐ ܢܘܗܪ̈ܐ ܦܐܝܐ ܡ̣ܢ ܠܐ ܡܕܡ ܘܪ̈ܕܦܗ ܠܚܫܟܐ ܐܝܟ ܓܢܒܪ̈ܐ ܠܕܫܝܛ ܡܢܗ.
From nothingness appeared suddenly the beautiful light, and it chased away the darkness, like a brave man (chasing) the one by whom he is despised.
ܥܼܪܩ ܚܫܘܟܐ ܘܛܫܼܐ ܒܢܩܥ̈ܐ ܕܣܘܦܝ̈ܗ ܕܐܪܥܐ ܘܬܗܪܘ ܥܝܪ̈ܐ ܒܪܒܘܬ ܚܝܠܐ ܕܝܗܼܒ ܒܗ ܚܝܠܐ.
The darkness fled and hid in the caverns of the extremities of the earth, and the angels admired the greatness of the power of Him who placed (this) force within it.
ܬܗܪ̈ܘ ܗܼܘܘ ܠܘ ܒܗ ܐܠܐ ܒܚܝܠܐ ܕܚܝܠ ܢܘܗܪܗ ܕܐܝܟܢ ܚܣܢܗ ܠܚܫܟܐ ܘܛܪ̈ܕܗ ܩܠܝܠܐܝܬ.
They admired, not the light, but the power that strengthened it, (seeing) how it had conquered the darkness and dispersed it rapidly.
ܩܠܝܠܐܝܬ ܥܼܪܩ ܚܫܘܟܐ ܡ̣ܢ ܥܡܪ̈ܬܐ ܘܩܠܝܠܐܝܬ ܥܠ ܢܗܝܪܐ ܘܐܡܠܟ ܒܐܪ̈ܥܐ.
Rapidly the darkness fled from the earth, and rapidly the light entered and reigned over the earth.
ܒܕܡܘܬ ܚܬܢܐ ܓܢܼܐ ܒܓܢܘܢܐ ܕܪ̈ܘܡܐ ܘܥܘܡܩܐ ܘܐܢܗܖܪ̈ ܥܠܡܐ ܒܓܘܢܐ ܦܐܝܐ ܕܢܗܝܪ̈ܘܬܗ.
Like a bridegroom, it lay down in the chamber of the (world) above and below, and it illuminated the world with its beautiful and luminous coloration.
ܟܢܫܝ̈ܢ ܟܢܫܝ̈ܢ ܩܡ̣ܘ ܪ̈ܘܚܢܐ ܠܡܚܙܐ ܫܘܦܪܗ ܘܠܐ ܣܒܥܝܢ ܗܼܘܘ ܠܡܩܠܣܘ ܠܡܢ ܕܩܢܢܗ.
Group by group, the spiritual (beings) rose to see its beauty, and they were not satiated with praising Him who constituted it.
ܗܢܝܐܐ ܗܼܘܬ ܚܙܬܐ ܕܢܘܗܪ̈ܗ ܠܪ̈ܥܝܢܝܗܘܢ ܘܛܒ ܡ̣ܢ ܢܘܗܪ̈ܗ ܗܢܝ ܗܼܘܐ ܠܗܘܢ ܪܡܙܐ ܕܥܒܕܗ
The sight of its light was sweet to their spirits, and more than its light, the sign that made it was sweet to them.
ܡܬܒܩܝܢ ܗܼܘܘ ܕܠܐ ܣܒܥܘܬܐ ܒܦܐܝܘܬ ܫܘܦܪܗ ܕܐܦ ܗܼܘ ܥܡܗܘܢ ܡܫܒܚܝ ܗܘܐ ܠܗܘܢ ܠܥܒܕ ܐܝܕܘ̈ܗܝ.
Insatiable, they gazed upon its sublime beauty, for he too, along with them, had glorified it, the work of his hands.
ܒܪܝܗܝ ܐܟ ܕܒܪܝܗܝ ܡ̣ܢ ܠܐ ܡܕܡ ܐܝܟ ܕܫܦܼܪ ܠܗ ܘܦܼܟܝܥܐ ܘܫܒܚ ܘܠܘ ܕܢܫܒܚ ܐܠܐ ܕܢܠܦ.
He created it as he created it from nothing, according to his pleasure, and he glorified it again, not for the sake of glorifying, but for the sake of teaching.
ܠܝܘܠܦܢ ܟܢܫ̈ܐ ܕܫܡܝܢ̈ܐ ܫܒܚܗ ܠܢܘܗܪܐ ܕܐܢܗܘ ܫܒܚ ܟܡܐ ܠܗܘܢ ܙܕܩ ܠܡܫܒܚܘ.
He glorified the light to teach the assemblies of the heavenly (beings) that, if he has glorified, how much (more) must they glorify!
ܫܒܚܘ ܘܬܗܪܘ ܘܩܠܣܘ ܘܐܘܪܒܘ ܠܡܢ ܕܩܢܢܗ ܘܐܘܕܝܘ ܘܣܓܕܘ ܘܗܝܡܢܘ ܘܐܫܪܘ ܠܚܝܠ ܐܝܬܘܬܐ.
They glorified, admired, praised, and magnified the one who constituted it; and they confessed, adored, believed, and attested to the power of the Essence.
ܚܝܠ ܐܝܬܘܬܐ ܝܠܦܘ ܪ̈ܘܚܢܐ ܒܝܕ ܢܗܝܪ̈ܐ ܘܫܼܠܡܘ ܟܠܗܘܢ ܕܡ̣ܢ ܚܕ ܚܝܠܐ ܗܼܘܐ ܟܠܡܕܡ.
The spiritual (beings) learned through the luminaries the power of the Essence, and all recognized that everything existed by one single power.
ܡܬܼܚ ܫܘܠܛܢܗ ܕܢܘܗܪ̈ܐ ܫܥ̈ܐ ܬܪ̈ܬܝܢ ܘܥܣܪ ܘܦ̣ܢܟܐ ܚܫܟܐ ܕܢܐܬܐ ܢܥܘܠ ܠܟܪ ܕܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܗܼܘܐ.
The supremacy of the light lasted for twelve hours, and the darkness returned to penetrate where it had been.
ܬܗܪ̈ܘ ܪ̈ܘܚܢܐ ܒܥܕܢ ܪ̈ܡܫܐ ܐܝܟ ܕܒܨܦܪܐ ܕܐܝܟܢ ܫܢܝ ܢܘܗܪ̈ܐ ܒܛܟܣܐ ܕܫܢܝ ܚܫܟܐ.
The spiritual (beings) marveled at the evening time as at the morning, (seeing) how the light disappeared in the (same) order in which the darkness had disappeared.

### The seers saw a marvelous spectacle that was admirable
ܚܙܬܐ ܕܬܗܪ̈ܐ ܚܙܘ ܚܙܝ̈ܐ ܕܬܗܝܪ̈ܐ ܗܘܬ ܟܝܢ̈ܐ ܚܪ̈ܫܐ ܕܢܛܪ̈ܝܢ ܛܟܣܐ ܐܝܟ ܡܠܠܐ.
the mute natures that observe order like the rational (natures).


[p. 574] {#po.34.p574}
