ܒܚܫܟܐ ܘܢܘܗܪ̈ܐ ܡܬܒܩܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܒܚܝܠ ܒܪ̈ܘܝܐ ܕܟܡܐ ܚܟܝܡ ܠܡܛܟܣܘ ܠܥܒܕ ܐܝܕܘ̈ܗܝ. ܛܟܣܐ ܪܒܐ ܩܢܘ ܐܦ ܗܢܘܢ ܒܝܕ ܫܬܝܩ̈ܐ ܘܟܦܘ ܨܘܪ̈ܝܗܘܢ ܠܡܛܥܢ ܢܝܪ̈ܐ ܕܫܡ ܒܪܘܝܐ.
In the darkness and the light, they considered the power of the Creator, how he knows how to order the work of his hands! They too acquired a great order by means of the silent (beings), and they bowed their necks to bear the yoke of the name of the Creator.

### Hypotheses on the creation of the firmament
ܫܠܡ ܚܕ ܝܘܡܐ ܠܠܝ ܐܝܡܡ ܒܚܫܟܐ ܘܢܘܗܪܐ ܘܐܙܥܩ ܡܫܫܠܝ ܩܠܐ ܕܕܡ̈ܐ ܠܗܘ ܩܕܡܝܐ. ܐܝܟ ܐܝܙܓܕܐ ܢܦܩ ܦܘܩܕܢܐ ܡ̣ܢ ܨܝܕ ܟܣܝܐ ܕܢܗܘܐ ܪ̈ܩܝܥܐ ܫܡܝܐ ܕܕܡ̈ܐ ܠܗܘ ܩܕܡܝܐ. ܗܘܬ ܒܥܒܕܐ ܘܗܘܐ ܪ̈ܩܝܥܐ ܡܨܥܬ ܡܝ̈ܐ ܘܩܡ ܒܡܨܥܬ ܐܝܟ ܬܛܠܝܠܐ ܠܥܠ ܡܢ ܐܪ̈ܥܐ. ܡ̣ܢ ܐܣܛܘܟܣܐ ܕܡܝ̈ܐ ܬܩܢܗ ܘܥܒܕܗ ܩܫܝܐ ܕܢܗܘܐ ܣܦܩ ܠܡܛܥܢ ܡܝ̈ܐ ܠܥܠ ܡ̣ܢ ܚܨܗ. ܘܦܫ ܥܠ ܐܪܥܐ ܡܢܬܐ ܕܫܘܝܐ ܥܡ ܗܝ ܕܠܥܠ. ܬܗܪܐ ܪܒܐ ܚܙܘ ܐܦ ܗܪ̈ܟܐ ܪ̈ܘܚܢܝܐ ܢܚܬܐ ܕܡܝ̈ܐ ܕܛܥܝܢ ܡܝ̈ܐ ܘܪܗܝܒ ܣܠܩ.
A day was completed—a night and a day—by the darkness and the light! And he suddenly made a word resound that (was) similar to the first. Like a messenger, a command sprang forth from the hidden (Being): "Let there be a firmament, a heaven that is similar to the first!" It was realized and there was a firmament in the midst of the waters, and it was established in the midst like a roof above the earth. (God) constituted it from the element "θαι," and he solidified it, so that it might be capable of supporting the water above its expanse. And he left on the earth a part that was equal to that which (was) above. The spiritual (beings) saw there also a great wonder: the garment of the waters, which supports the water and rises rapidly!
ܬܗܪܘ ܘܐܬܕܡܪܘ ܒܐܘܡܢܘܬܐ ܕܪ̈ܡܙܐ ܕܪܩܥܗ ܕܐܝܟܢ ܡܬܚܗ ܥܠ ܟܠܦܢܝ̈ܢ ܩܠܝܠܐܝܬ. ܠܬܗܪ̈ܐ ܫܘܐ ܘܚܟܝܡܐܝܬ ܬܗܝܪ̈ܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܕܐܝܟܢ ܩܫܝ ܠܡܝ̈ܐ ܪ̈ܦܝܐ ܘܛܥܢܘ ܡܝ̈ܐ. ܠܬܚܬ ܟܝ ܡܬܚܗ ܒܐܦܝ̈ ܐܪ̈ܥܐ ܘܟܢ ܐܪܝܡܗ ܡܕܝܢ ܥܡܗ ܣܠܩܘ ܡܠܐܟ̈ܐ ܘܢܘܗܪܐ ܘܐܐܪ. ܘܐܢ ܡ̣ܢ ܣܘܦ̈ܐ ܠܣܘܦ̈ܐ ܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܐܝܟ ܡܐ ܕܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܠܐ ܦܫ ܡܕܡ ܕܠܐ ܠܥܘ ܡܢܗ ܚܒܫܗ ܒܪ̈ܘܡܐ.
They admired and marveled at the skill of the sign that extended (the firmament), (seeing) how he deployed them promptly over all the extremities (of the world). It was worthy of admiration, and they admired with wisdom how he hardened the fluid water, and it carries (another) water. Is it indeed below that he deployed it, on the surface of the earth, and did he then raise it? Also, did the angels, the light, and the air ascend with it? And if it is (extended), just as it is, from one limit to the other (of the world), there remains nothing that he did not enclose within himself, in the (world) above.
ܘܐܠܐ ܐܝܟܢ ܡܬܥܠܐ ܗܘܐ ܡܐܢܐ ܩܫܝܐ ܘܥܒܪ̈ܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܒܗ ܟܝܢ̈ܐ ܥܒܝ̈ܐ ܘܠܐ ܡܣܬܕܩ. ܥܣܩܐ ܗܝ ܣܛܝ ܒܥܬܐ ܕܐܝܟܢ ܗܘܐ ܘܐܬܥܠܝ ܘܐܠܐ ܪܡܙܐ ܕܐܡܪ ܘܗܘܐ ܠܐ ܝܕܥ ܠܗ.
And if not, how would a solid object rise, and would dense natures pass through it without it splitting? It is very difficult to search for how it existed and was raised, and apart from the sign that spoke and it existed, it is not known.
ܪ̈ܥܝܢܝ ܫܚܩܢܝ ܕܐܒܥܐ ܕܐܝܟܢ ܘܠܐ ܣܦܩ ܐܕܪܟ ܘܬܘܒ ܫܚܩ ܠܝ ܕܟܒܪ ܡܨܝܐ ܕܐܕܪ̈ܟ ܡܕܡ.
My mind has tormented me so that I might search for the how, and it was not capable of understanding, and it still torments me, for it is perhaps possible that I might understand something.
ܠܦܘܬ ܚܘܫܒ̈ܐ ܪܢܝܬ ܐܝܟ ܕܡܨܐ ܥܒܝܕܐ ܠܡܪ̈ܢܐ ܕܟܒܪ ܒܐܬܪ̈ܐ ܕܠܥܠ ܡܬܚܗ ܠܗܢܐ ܡܐܢܐ. ܘܟܒܪ ܟܠܗ ܡܢܬܐ ܕܡܝ̈ܐ ܫܩܠ ܦܘܩܕܢܝܐ ܘܡ̣ܢ ܕܐܪ̈ܝܡܗ ܩܫܝ ܪܡܙܗ ܘܛܥܢ ܗܘ ܠܗ.
According to (my) conceptions, I thought, as a created (being) can think, that (God) perhaps deployed this object in the upper region, and that without a doubt, the command carried off a whole part of the waters, and after having raised it, his sign solidified (it) and it is he who supported it.


[p. 576] {#po.34.p576}
ܟܒܪ ܡܐ ܕܣܠܩ ܡܬܚܗ ܡܢܫܠܝ ܠܥܠ ܡ̣ܢ ܟܠܐ ܘܦܫ ܟܠܡܕܡ ܠܬܚܬ ܥܠ ܐܪ̈ܥܐ ܐܝܟ ܕܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܗܘܐ. ܕܠܐ ܬܘܗܝܐ ܗܘܐ ܬܘܩܢܗ ܩܠܝܠܐܝܬ ܘܠܐ ܡܨܐ ܦܘܡܐ ܕܢܐܡܪ ܕܟܡܐ ܗܝ ܩܠܝܠܘܬܗ.
Without a doubt, after it ascended, he deployed it all at once above everything, and below on the earth, everything remained as it was. Its constitution took place rapidly, without delay, and the mouth cannot say how fast it is!
ܩܠܝܠ ܗܘܐ ܛܒ ܗܘ ܦܘܩܕܢܐ ܕܡܬܚܗ ܒܪ̈ܘܡܐ ܘܐܢ ܐܝܬ ܡܕܡ ܕܩܠܝܠ ܣܓܝ ܡܬܝܢ ܡܢܗ. ܗܝ ܒܪܬ ܩܠܐ ܕܐܡܪ ܘܗܘܘ ܫܡܝ̈ܐ ܕܠܥܠ ܗܝ ܬ ܩܢܬܗ ܒܗܘ ܐܣܟܝܡܐ ܕܡ̣ܢ ܠܐ ܡܕܡ.
This command that deployed it in the (world) above was very rapid, and if there exists a thing that is rapid, it is much slower than it. This word that he spoke, and the upper heaven existed, it is that which constituted it in this manner, from nothing.
ܡ̣ܢ ܠܐ ܡܕܡ ܗܘܐ ܩܕܡܝܐ ܒܪܡܙܐ ܙܥܘܪܐ ܘܗܢ ܡ̣ܢ ܡܕܡ ܒܗܝ ܒܕܡܘܬܐ ܩܠܝܠܐܝܬ. ܛܟܣܐ ܦܐܝܐ ܥܒܕ ܠܒܪܝܬܗ ܦܐܐ ܒܐܝܬܘܬܗ ܘܚܟܝܡܐܝܬ ܒܪܐ ܡ̣ܢ ܡܕܡ ܘܡ̣ܢ ܠܐ ܡܕܡ. ܠܥܒܕ ܐܝܕܘ̈ܗܝ ܚܘܝ ܒܛܟܣܐ ܕܥܒܘܕܘܬܗ ܘܠܝܘܠܦܢܗܘܢ ܡܬܚܗ ܠܥܡܠܗ ܠܫܬܐ ܝܘܡܝ̈ܢ. ܡܨܐ ܗܘܐ ܕܢܒܪܐ ܠܟܠܡܕܡ ܕܒܪܐ ܒܚܕܐ ܫܥܐ ܐܠܐ ܥܒ̈ܕܘܗܝ ܠܐ ܝܠܦܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܚܝܠ ܪ̈ܒܘܬܗ. ܠܝܘܬܪ̈ܢ ܥܒܕܗ ܠܗܝ ܕܐܬܬܗܝ ܠܡܒܪ̈ܐ ܒܪ̈ܝܬܐ ܕܢܓܡܪ ܕܝܠܗ ܒܓܡܝܪ̈ܘܬܐ ܕܐܠܗܘܬܗ.
By a small sign, the first (heaven) existed from nothing, and this one, from something, in the same rapid manner. He who is sublime in his Essence put a beautiful arrangement into his creation, and he created with wisdom, from being and from nothing. He showed (it) to the work of his hands, by his ordered activity, and for their instruction, he prolonged his work for six days. He could have created everything he created in a single hour, but his works would not have been informed of the power of his greatness. It is for a profit that he did this, that he delayed in creating the creation, to perfect what is his by the fullness of his divinity.

### The diverse creations, source of instruction for the angels
ܒܫܘܪ̈ܝ ܝܘܡܐ ܥܒܕ ܠܫܡܝܐ ܘܠܡܠܐܟ̈ܐ ܘܠܐܪ̈ܥܐ ܘܡܝ̈ܐ ܘܠܚܫܘܟܐ ܘܢܘܪܐ ܘܐܐܪ.
At the beginning of the day, he made the heaven and the angels, the earth, the water, the darkness, the fire, and the air.
ܒܥܕܢ ܨܦܪܐ ܐܬܩܢ ܢܘܗܪܐ ܡ̣ܢ ܠܐ ܡܕܡ ܘܝܠܦܘ ܥܝܪ̈ܐ ܕܡ̣ܢ ܠܐ ܡܕܡ ܗܘܘ ܐܦ ܗܢܘܢ. ܒܝܘܡܐ ܕܬܪ̈ܝܢ ܥܒܕ ܠܪ̈ܩܝܥܐ ܡ̣ܢ ܓܘ ܡܝ̈ܐ ܘܚܟܡ ܐܢܘܢ ܕܐܦ ܡ̣ܢ ܡܕܡ ܦܫܝܩ ܠܗ ܕܢܒܪܐ. ܒܬܠܝܬܝܐ ܟܢܫ ܡܝ̈ܐ ܠܩܒܝܐ ܕܝܡܡ̈ܐ ܘܐܘܕܥ ܐܢܘܢ ܕܪܡܙܗ ܫܠܝܛ ܥܠ ܬܘܩܢܘ̈ܗܝ.
In the morning, he constituted the light from nothing, and the angels learned that they too existed from nothing. The second day, he made the firmament from the water, and he taught them that it was easy for him to create also from something. The third (day), he gathered the waters into the reservoir of the seas, and he let them know that his sign had power over his creations.
ܒܪ̈ܒܝܥܝܝܐ ܥܒܕ ܢܗܝܪ̈ܐ ܫܡܫܐ ܘܣܗܪܐ ܘܚܘܝ ܐܢܘܢ ܠܡܛܪ ܛܟܣܐ ܕܥܒܘܕܘܬܗ. ܒܚܡܝܫܝܐ ܙܩܦ ܡܝ̈ܐ ܘܐܘܠܕܘ ܪ̈ܚܫܐ ܘܐܘܕܥ ܐܢܘܢ ܕܐܦ ܫܬܝܩ̈ܐ ܡܫܡܠܝܢ ܡܠܬܗ.
The fourth (day), he made the luminaries, the sun and the moon, and he indicated to them to maintain the order of his action. The fifth (day), he united the waters, and they gave birth to reptiles, and he let them know that even the silent (beings) accomplish his word.
ܒܫܬܝܬܝܐ ܒܪܐ ܡ̣ܢ ܐܪ̈ܥܐ ܓܢܣ̈ܐ ܚܪ̈ܫܐ ܘܐܥܝܪ ܐܢܘܢ ܠܡܬܒܩܝܘ ܒܫܘܚܠܦܝ̈ܗܘܢ. ܐܝܟ ܕܒܨܒܥܐ ܡܚܘܐ ܗܘܐ ܠܗܘܢ ܚܝܠ ܐܝܬܘܬܗ ܕܚܘܪܘ ܡܠܐܟ̈ܐ ܕܚܝܠ ܟܠܡܕܡ ܡܨܐ ܐܢܐ ܚܝܠܐ.
The sixth (day), he created the mute species from the soil, and he awakened them so that they might consider their varieties. As with a finger, he indicated to them the power of his Essence: "Angels, look: I am absolutely all-powerful!"


[p. 578] {#po.34.p578}
ܐܝܟ ܕܒܩܢܝܝܐ ܟܬܒ ܗܼܘܐ ܠܗܘܢ ܣܦܪ̈ܐ ܒܗܘܢܝܐ ܘܡܗܓܐ ܗܼܘܐ ܠܗܘܢ ܒܟܬܝܒ̈ܬܐ ܕܬܘܩܢ ܟܠܐ. ܒܕܡܘܬ ܖܪ̈ܒܐ ܩܝܡ ܗܘܐ ܪ̈ܡܙܗ ܒܪܫ ܣܕܪ̈ܝܗܘܢ ܘܬܢܐ ܗܼܘܐ ܠܗܘܢ ܚܝܠ ܣܘܟܠܐ ܕܣܬܝܪ̈ܬܗ.
As with a reed pen, he wrote them a book in the spirit, and made them meditate on writings about the universal creation. His sign stood, like a master, at the head of their ranks, and he retold to them the power of the meaning of his secrets.
ܐܝܟ ܕܠܨܠܡܐ ܨܪܗ ܠܥܠܡܐ ܩܕܡ ܥܝܢܝ̈ܗܘܢ ܘܐܩܝܡ ܐܢܘܢ ܕܢܬܒܩܘܢ ܒܗ ܚܬܝܬܐܝܬ. ܐܝܟ ܦܠܛܝܢ ܕܡܠܟ ܡܠܟ̈ܐ ܒܢܗ̇ ܠܒܪ̈ܝܬܐ ܘܐܥܠ ܐܢܘܢ ܠܡܚܙܐ ܫܘܦܪ̈ܐ ܕܒܝܬ ܡܠܟܘܬܗ.
He painted the world like a painting, before their gaze, and he made them rise, so that they might consider it with care. Like the palace of the king of kings, he built the creation, and he made them enter so that they might see the beauty of his royal palace.
ܒܝܕ ܓܠܝܬ̈ܐ ܐܠܦ ܐܢܘܢ ܚܝܠ ܐܝܬܘܬܗ ܕܠܐ ܢܫܬܚܩܘܢ ܠܡܒܥܐ ܒܥܬܐ ܕܥܠ ܟܣܝܘܬܗ. ܟܣܐ ܗܼܘܐ ܡܢܗܘܢ ܘܠܐ ܝܕܥܝܢ ܗܼܘܘ ܖܪܒܘܬ ܚܝܠܗ ܘܐܘܕܥ ܐܢܘܢ ܒܝܕ ܬܘܩܢܘ̈ܗܝ ܕܚܕ ܒܬ ܪ ܚܕ.
He taught them the power of his Essence by means of visible things, for fear that they might worry about searching for what concerns his hidden (nature). He was hidden from them and they did not know the greatness of his power, but he made (it) known to them by means of his creations, one after the other.
ܐܝܟ ܗܕܝܐܐ ܖ݁ܗܼܛ ܦܘܩܕܢܗ ܩܕܡ ܟܢܫܝ̈ܗܘܢ ܘܢܓܕ ܐܢܘܢ ܠܘܬ ܝܘܠܦܥܫܐ ܕܟܣܝܘܬ ܚܘܒܗ. ܚܘܒܗ ܓܠܹܐ ܠܗܘܢ ܕܟܡܐ ܪ̈ܚܶܡ ܠܥ̇ܒܕ ܐܝܕܘ̈ܗܝ ܘܪܲܓܝܓ ܣܓܝ ܠܡܛܪ ܥܡܠܗ ܡ̣ܢ ܢܟܝܢ̈ܐ. ܚܘܒܢܐܝܬ ܒܼܪܵܐ ܟܠܡܕܡ ܕܟܣܐ ܘܓܠܐ ܘܕܠܐ ܥܡܠܐ ܓܡܼܪ ܨܒܝܢܗ ܘܚܘܝ ܚܘܒܗ.
Like a guide, his command hastened before their assemblies, and brought them to the instruction of his hidden love. He manifested his love to them, how much he cherishes the work of his hands, and (how) he desires greatly to preserve his work from harmful things! With love, he created everything that is hidden and visible, and he accomplished his will without effort, and he manifested his love.

### The different functions of the angels
ܚܝܠܗ ܐܫܪܝ ܒܚܝܠܝ̈ ܪܘܡܐ ܕܗܘܘ ܡܢܗ ܘܚܝܠ ܐܢܘܢ ܠܡܛܪ ܛܟܣܐ ܕܥܒܘܕܘܬܗ. ܐܝܟ ܫܠܝܛ̈ܐ ܐܩܝܡ ܐܢܘܢ ܥܠ ܟܠ ܕܒܼܪܐ ܕܢܗܘܘܢ ܝܕܥܝܢ ܠܡܕܒܪܘ ܫܡܝܐ ܘܐܪ̈ܥܐ.
He made his power dwell in the Powers above that existed by him, and he fortified them so that they might maintain the order of his action. He established them as chiefs over everything he created, such that they might know how to govern the heaven and the earth.
ܒܚܘܒܐ ܖܒܐ ܫ̣ܠܡܘ ܐܦ ܗܢܘܢ ܠܨܒܝܢ ܚܘܒܗ ܘܗܢܝܐܝܬ ܟܕܢܘ ܨܘܪ̈ܝܗܘܢ ܬܚܝܬ ܦܘܩܕܢܗ. ܦܩܼܕ ܐܝܟ ܡܠܟܐ ܘܛܟܣ ܕܕ̈ܓܐ ܕܫܘܠܛܢܝ̈ܗܘܢ ܘܟܠ ܚܕ ܡܢܗܘܢ ܩܡ ܒܥ̇ܒܕܐ ܕܐܬܚܘܝ ܠܗ. ܐܝܬ ܕܦܩܼܕ ܠܗ ܕܢܙܝܥ ܠܐܐܪ ܐܝܟ ܪܒܚܝܠܐ ܘܢܪܗܛ ܡܝ̈ܐ ܠܥܠ ܡܸܢ ܚܨܐ ܕܪܽܘܚܐ ܫܪ̈ܝܐ.
They too acquiesced with great love to his design of love, and with gentleness they bowed their necks under his command. He commanded, like a king, and he organized the rungs of their powers, and each one of them stood at the work that was indicated to him. There is (one) to whom he ordered to set the air in motion, like a captain, and to make the waters flow above the expanse of the fluid wind.
ܐܝܬ ܕܐܦܣ ܠܗ ܕܢܣܝܦ ܒܪ̈ܩܐ ܩܠܝܠܐܝܬ ܘܢܫܡܥ ܩܠ̈ܐ ܕܪ̈ܥܡܐ ܩܫܝ̈ܐ ܚܣܝܢܐܝܬ. ܐܝܬ ܕܐܘܚܕ ܠܗ ܐܣܤܣܦܝܪܖܪ ܫܡܫܐ ܒܐܝܕܐ ܕܙܘܥܘ̈ܗܝ ܘܐܡܝܢ ܪ݁ܗܶ̇ܛ ܠܠܝ ܐܝܡܡ ܕܠܐ ܫܘܘܚܪ̈ܐ.
There is (another) to whom he permitted to light the lightning bolts rapidly, and to make the violent rumbles of thunder heard with force. There is (another) to whom he entrusted the globe of the sun, by means of his faculties, and it runs continuously, night and day, without delay.
ܐܝܬ ܕܐܫܼܠܡ ܠܗ ܣܗܪܖ̈ܐ ܙܥܘܪܐ ܩܢܐ ܫܘܚܠܦ̈ܐ ܘܗܐ ܡܛܟܣ ܠܗ ܠܥܘܡܪܐ ܕܙܒܢܐ ܕܠܐ ܢܬܕܘܕ.
There is (another) to whom he entrusted the little moon, provided with changes, and here he is ordering the temporal dwelling so that it might not be troubled.


[p. 580] {#po.34.p580}
ܐܝܬ ܕܠܟܘܟܒ̈ܐ ܚܪ̈ܫܐ ܡܙܝܥܝܢ ܠܡܪܕܐ ܒܛܟܣܐ ܘܒܪ̈ܗܛܝܗܘܢ ܡܚܟܡܝܢ ܐܢܫ̈ܐ ܩܢܝܝ̈ ܡܠܬܐ. ܐܝܬ ܕܐܬܦܩܕܘ ܠܡܕܒܪܘ ܫܘܠܛܢ ܥܡܡ̈ܐ ܘܠܐܝܢ ܟܠܫܥ ܠܡܩܝܡܘ ܫܘܠܛܢܝ̈ܗܘܢ.
There are those who set the mute stars in motion so that they might advance in order, and by their evolutions, they instruct the men gifted with speech. There are those who have received the order to govern the empire of the peoples, and they toil ceaselessly to strengthen their powers.
ܐܝܬ ܕܡܥܝܪ̈ܝܢ ܘܝܪ̈ܒܐ ܒܐܪ̈ܥܐ ܥܠ ܚܝܒ̈ܐ ܘܐܝܬ ܕܡܫܿܠܝܢ ܩܐܪ̈ܣܐ ܩܫܝ̈ܐ ܡ̣ܢ ܙܟܝ̈ܐ. ܐܝܬ ܕܡܟ̇ܿܒܫܝܢ ܥܡܡ̈ܐ ܡܪ̈ܝܕܐ ܒܝܕ ܚܒܪ̈ܝܗܘܢ ܘܐܝܬ ܕܡܫܿܗܝܢ ܬܘܩܦܐ ܕܒܝ̈ܫܐ ܩܕܡ ܡܟܝ̈ܟܐ. ܐܝܬ ܗܼܘ ܕܖܪ̈ܕܝܢ ܒܪ̈ܥܡܐ ܘܒܪ̈ܩܐ ܥܙܝܙܐܝܬ ܘܐܝܬ ܗܼܘ ܕܡ̇ܿܚܬܝܢ ܡܛܪܐ ܘܛܠܐ ܒܣܝܡܐܝܬ.
There are those who, on earth, stir up wars against the guilty, and there are those who ward off hard battles from the innocent. There are those who subject rebellious peoples by means of their neighbors, and there are those who reduce the power of the wicked before the humble. There are those who advance with impetuosity among the thunders and the lightnings, and there are those who make the rain and the dew descend with gentleness.
ܐܝܬ ܗܼܘ ܕܡܿܟܝܢ ܒܝܕ ܫܘܚܠܦ̈ܐ ܕܩܝܛܐ ܘܣܬܘܐ ܘܐܝܬ ܗܼܘ ܕܢܛܪ̈ܝܢ ܦܓܪ̈ܢܝܐ ܡ̣ܢ ܢܟܝܢ̈ܐ. ܐܝܬ ܕܡܕܒܪ̈ܝܢ ܟܠܚܕ ܡܢܗܘܢ ܠܚܕ ܒܪܢܫܐ ܘܙܗܝܪ̈ܝܢ ܒܗ ܕܠܐ ܢܟܘܢܝܗܝ ܫܐ̈ܕܐ ܛܪ̈ܘܢܝܐ. ܠܨܒܝܢ ܟܣܝܐ ܡܫܡܫܝܢ ܟܠܗܘܢ ܒܚܦܝܛܘܬܐ ܘܐܝܟ ܨܒܝܢܗ ܖܪ̈ܕܝܢ ܒܐܘܪ̈ܚܐ ܕܣܘܥܪ̈ܢܝܗܘܢ. ܠܢܝܫܐ ܕܚܘܒܗ ܚܢܝܢ ܟܠܫܥ ܠܡܕܡܝܘ ܘܠܐ ܡܬܪ̈ܦܝܢ ܡ̣ܢ ܣܘܟܝܐ ܕܦܘܪ̈ܥܢ ܐ̱ܓܪܐ. ܐܝܟ ܐܬܠܝܛ̈ܐ ܪ̈ܡܝܢ ܒܐܓܘܢܐ ܗܐ ܡ̣ܢ ܕܗܘܘ ܘܠܙܟܘܬܐ ܕܒܫܘܠܡܐ ܡܣܟܝܢ ܥܡ ܟܠ. ܬܡܝܗ ܐܓܘܢܐ ܕܡܬܟܬܫܝܢ ܒܗ ܠܐ ܚܫܘ̈ܫܐ ܘܠܐ ܡܬܡܠܠܐ ܖܒܘܬ ܥܡܠܗ ܒܠܫܢ ܒܣܪ̈ܐ. ܟܡܐ ܟܝ ܗܿܘܐ ܚܝܠܐ ܕܒܗܘܢ ܠܡܐܚܕ ܟܠܐ ܕܗܐ ܒܨܝܖܿ ܩܠܝܠ ܐܠܦ̈ܐ ܐܫܬܐ ܘܠܐ ܡܬܪܦܝܢ. ܟܡܐ ܟܝ ܦܠܚܝܢ ܒܐܦܝ̈ ܪܘܡܐ ܠܠܝ ܐܝܡܡ ܘܠܐ ܠܐܝܘ ܘܬܚܒܘ ܡ̣ܢ ܡܪ̈ܕܝܬܐ ܕܥܡ ܢܗܝܪ̈ܐ.
There are those who harm by means of the alternations of summer and winter, and there are those who preserve the corporeal (beings) from evils. There are those who direct men, each of them (directing) one, and watch over him so that the tyrannical demons do not do him harm. They are all diligent servants of the will of the hidden (Being), and according to his will, they advance in the way of their activities. They strive ceaselessly to imitate the example of his love, and they do not slacken in the expectation of the reward in return. Like athletes, they are engaged in the combat since they existed, and they await, as do all things, the final victory. The combat in which the impassible (beings) struggle is admirable, and the importance of its toil cannot be expressed by a carnal tongue. What strength, indeed, there is in them to maintain all things, for here it is nearly six thousand (years) (that they have done it) and they do not slacken! How they work in the (world) above, night and day, and they are not tired nor weakened by (their) journey with the luminaries!
ܟܡܐ ܟܝ ܛܿܥܢ ܝܨܪ̈ܐ ܕܒܗܘܢ ܝܘܩܪܗ ܕܙܒܢܝܐ ܘܠܐ ܡܬܝܠܕܐ ܡܐܝܢܘܬܐ ܒܪ̈ܥܝܢܝܗܘܢ. ܟܡܐ ܟܝ ܪܬܚܝܢ ܒܪܚܡܬ ܚܘܒܐ ܕܨܝܕ ܒܪ̈ܘܝܐ ܘܠܐ ܩܪܘ ܘܗܦܟܘ ܡ̣ܢ ܛܘܝܒܐ ܕܐܝܟ ܨܒܝܢܗ.
How it supports the weight of time, the will that is in them, and weariness does not arise in their spirits! How they burn with an affectionate love toward the Creator, and they have not cooled nor turned away from the disposition (established) according to his will!
ܒܚܝܠ ܨܒܝܢܗ ܢܬܗܪ ܝܬܝܪ ܛܒ ܡ̣ܢ ܕܒܗܘܢ ܕܡܨܐ ܡܚܝܠ ܠܥܿܒܕ ܐܝܕܘ̈ܗܝ ܠܡܛܪ ܛܟܣܐ. ܠܚܝܠܗ ܢܩܠܣ ܚܝܠ ܚܘܡܣܢܐ ܕܒܢܝ̈ ܒܝܬܗ ܘܠܥܘܕܪ̈ܢܗ ܢܓܕܘܠ ܟܠܫܥ ܟܠܝܠ ܬܘܕܝܬܐ ܀
Let us admire the power of his will, greater than that which is in them, for he can strengthen the work of his hands so that it may maintain order! May the persevering strength of his intimates praise his power, and may it (for his aid) ceaselessly weave a crown of thanksgiving.


[p. 582] {#po.34.p582}
ܥܠ ܬܘܩܢܗ̇ ܕܒܖ̈ܝܬܐ ܘܡܘܕܥ ܐܦ ܥܠ ܩܢܘܡ̈ܐ ܕܬܠܝܬܝܘܬܐ
I ON THE CONSTITUTION OF CREATION AND ON THE PERSONS OF THE TRINITY

### The object of the Mosaic revelation
ܒܝܬ ܓܙܐ ܪܒܐ ܦܬܼܚ ܠܢ ܒܪ ܥܡܪ̈ܡ ܒܢܒܝܘܬܗ ܚܝܠܐ ܪܒܐ ܕܒܪ̈ܘܝܐ ܕܐܡܬܝ ܫܪ̈ܝ ܒܥ̇ܒܕܐ. ܟܣܝܐ ܗܼܘܬ ܗܕܐ ܡ̣ܢ ܟܠ ܕܐܝܟܢ ܫܪ̈ܝ ܒܒܪ̈ܝܬܐ ܘܒܚܝܠܐ ܕܡܶܢ ܐܬܬܩܢܘ ܫܡܝܐ ܘܐܪ̈ܥܐ ܘܟܠ ܕܒܗܘܢ. ܘܨܒܐ ܒܪܘܝܐ ܕܢܘܕܥ ܥܒ̈ܕܐ ܕܚܝܠܗ ܠܡܠܝܠ̈ܐ ܠܡܘܫܐ ܓܒܼܐ ܠܗ ܬܠܡܝܕܐ ܘܐܠܦܗ ܣܦܪܐ ܪܘܚܢܐ.
Through his prophecy, the son of Amram opened to us a great treasure: the great power of the Creator at the moment when he began (his) work. This was hidden from all: how he began (his) creations, and by the power of what (being) the heaven and the earth and all that (is) in them were constituted. But the Creator wished to make known to (beings) endowed with speech the work of his power; he chose Moses as his disciple and taught him the spiritual Book.
ܒܛܘܪܐ ܕܣܝܢܝ ܚܘܝ ܠܗ ܚܙܬܐ ܕܡܠܝܐ ܬܕܡܘܖ̈ܬܐ ܘܩܠܐ ܠܘܬܗ ܡܫܡܥ ܗܼܘܐ ܟܕ ܢ̇ܓܕ ܠܗ ܠܝܘܠܦܢܐ. ܐܢܫܐܝܬ ܨܒ̣ܐ ܕܢܚܘܐ ܢܦܫܗ ܠܡܘܫܐ ܒܪܘܝܐ ܘܥܠܗܕܐ ܡܬܪܓܪܓ ܗܼܘܐ ܡܘܫܐ ܠܡܚܙܐ ܠܟܝܢܗ. ܐܢܗܘ ܕܝܢ ܕܓܒܹܝܬܢܝ ܠܟ ܬܠܡܝܕܐ ܘܪ̈ܚܡ ܐܢܬ ܠܝ ܚܘܢܝ ܫܘܒܚܗ ܕܟܝܢܟ ܘܐܬܒܩܐ ܒܙܝܘܟ ܟܣܝܐ. ܣܓܝ ܛܥ̇ܐ ܐܢܬ ܐܘ ܡܘܫܐ ܡܪ̈ܝܐ ܠܘܬܗ ܐܿܡܪ ܗܼܘܐ ܠܐ ܚܿܙܐ ܠܝ ܡܝܘܬܐ ܕܪ̈ܡ ܗܼܘ ܟܝܢܝ ܡ̣ܢ ܚܿܙܝܐ. ܠܝܘܠܦܢܐ ܩ̈ܪܝܬܟ ܨܐܕܝ ܕܬܐܠܦ ܕܐܝܬܝ ܒܪܘܝܐ ܠܐ ܗܼܘܐ ܕܬܐܠܦ ܥܠ ܟܣܝܘܬܝ ܕܠܐ ܡܬܕܪ̈ܟܐ ܠܥܒܝܕ̈ܐ. ܡܪ̈ܝܐ ܐܝܬܝ ܘܐܠܗܐ ܘܗܢܘ ܫܡܝ ܕܐܝܬܝ ܘܐܝܬܝ ܘܒܚܝܠܐ ܕܡܢܝ ܐܿܩܝܡܬ ܫܡܝܐ ܘܐܪ̈ܥܐ ܘܟܠ ܕܒܗܘܢ. ܘܕܠܐ ܡܦܣܝܢ ܡܝܘܬ̈ܐ ܕܡܪܘܬܝ ܫܠܝܛܐ ܥܠ ܟܠ ܠܟ ܥܒܿܕ ܐܢܐ ܐܝܙܓܕܐ ܘܡܨܥܝܐ ܕܬܠܦ ܐܢܘܢ.
He showed him on Mount Sinai a spectacle full of wonder(s), and he made him hear (his) voice, drawing him to knowledge. The Creator wished to manifest himself to Moses in a human way, and that is why Moses desired to see his nature. "If you have truly chosen me as your disciple, and you love me, show me the glory of your nature, and I will contemplate your hidden splendor." "You are greatly mistaken, Moses," the Lord said to him, "a mortal will not see me, for my nature is too exalted for those who look! "I have called you to me for instruction, so that you may learn that I am the Creator; it is not so that you may be informed about my hidden (nature), for it is incomprehensible to (beings). "I am Lord and God, and here is my name: I am and I am, and by the power that (proceeds) from me, I have constituted the heaven and the earth and all that (is) in them. And because mortals do not know that my mastery has authority over every (being), I make you a messenger and a mediator, so that you may instruct them.
ܒܐܝܕܝ̈ܟ ܨܿܒܐ ܐܢܐ ܕܐܓܠܐ ܥܠ ܐܝܬܘܬܝ ܘܥܠ ܬܘܩܢܝ̈ ܒܟ ܨ̈ܒܐ ܐܢܐ ܕܐܚܟܡ ܠܟܠܗ ܟܝܢܐ ܕܡܠܝܠ̈ܐ.
Through your intermediary, I wish to reveal my Essence and my creations; through you, I wish to instruct every rational nature.
ܚܿܙܝ ܡܘܫܐ ܕܓܿܠܐ ܐܢܐ ܠܟ ܐܪ̈ܙܐ ܟܣܝ̈ܐ ܕܬܘܩܢܝ̈ ܚܘܬ ܘܐܟܪ̈ܙ ܒܝܬ ܡܝܘ̈ܬܐ ܕܥܒܘܕܐ ܐܢܐ ܕܟܠ ܕܗܼܘܐ.
Look, Moses! because I reveal to you the hidden mysteries of my creations; go down to announce to mortals that I am the author of all that exists!"


[p. 584] {#po.34.p584}
%8 ܒܐܪ̈ܒܥܝܢ ܝܘܡܬ̈ܐ ܓܡܪܗ ܪܒܐ ܠܬܠܡܝܕܐ ܚܕܬܐ ܘܣܦܼܩ ܗܘܢܗ ܕܡܝܘܬܐ ܕܢܐܚܘܕ ܣܟܐ ܕܟܠ ܕܗܼܘܐ. ܚܝܠܐ ܕܪ̈ܘܚܐ ܐܫܪܝ ܒܗ ܘܙܡܼܪ ܒܗ ܩܠ̈ܐ ܬܡܝܗ̈ܐ ܘܒܚܠܝܘܬܐ ܕܢܥܡܬܗ ܬܗܪܘ ܥܠܝ̈ܐ ܘܬܚܬܝ̈ܐ.
For forty days, the master perfected the new disciple, and the mind of a mortal was capable of embracing the limit of all that exists. He made the power of the Spirit dwell in him, and he chanted admirable songs in him, and the superior and inferior (beings) marveled at the sweetness of his modulations.
ܗܼܘܬ ܒܪܬ ܩܠܗ ܐܝܟ ܪܥܡܐ ܘܫܡܥܘܗܝ ܚܪ̈ܫܐ ܘܡܠܝܠ̈ܐ ܘܦܬܓܡܘ̈ܗܝ ܐܝܟ ܫܝܦܘܪ̈ܐ ܐܟܪ̈ܙܘ ܚܝܠܗ ܕܥܒܘܕܐ.
His word was like thunder, and the mute (beings) and those endowed with speech heard it; and his words, like trumpets, proclaimed the power of the Creator.

### The motives of creation
ܒܪ̈ܫܝܬ ܠܡ ܫܖܿܝ ܘܒܼܪܐ ܒܪܘܝܐ ܟܠܡܐ ܕܗܼܘܐ ܘܬܚܡ ܙܒܢ̈ܐ ܒܝܕܥܬܗ ܕܫܘܪ̈ܝܐ ܘܕܫܘܠܡܐ. ܘܟܕ ܒܐܝܬܘܬܗ ܠܐ ܫܪ̈ܝ ܕܟܝܢܗ ܕܠܐ ܫܘܖ̈ܝܐ ܗܼܘ ܠܒܪ̈ܝܬܐ ܒܙܒܢܗܿ ܥܒܕܗܿ ܐܝܟܢܐ ܕܗܼܘ ܝܕܥ ܗܼܘܐ. ܠܘ ܓܝܪ ܚܘܫܒܐ ܚܕܬܐ ܢܓܕܗ ܕܢܒܪ̈ܐ ܒܪ̈ܝܬܐ ܕܗܕܐ ܡܡܬܘܡ ܣܣܝܡܐ ܗܘܬ ܒܝܕܥܬܗ ܥܡ ܐܝܬܘܬܗ.
"In the beginning, the Creator began to create all that exists, and he delimited the times, thanks to his knowledge of the beginning and the end." And as he did not begin (to create) his Essence, for his nature is without beginning, he made creation in its time, just as he knew (it). It is not, in fact, a recent thought that incited him to create creatures, for this was eternally established in his knowledge, with his Essence.
ܘܠܘ ܬܘܒ ܠܢܝܚܗ ܬܩܢ ܫܡܝܐ ܘܐܪ̈ܥܐ ܘܟܠ ܕܒܗܘܢ ܐܠܐ ܒܚܘܒܐ ܘܒܪ̈ܚܡܐ ܓܠܹܐ ܨܒܝܢܗ ܠܥܒܝ̈ܕܐ.
And it is not for his own satisfaction either that he constituted the heaven and the earth and all that (is) in them, but out of love and affection, he revealed his will to the created (beings).
ܗܼܘ ܓܝܪ ܛܒܐ ܗܼܘ ܒܟܝܢܗ ܘܟܝܢܐ ܗܼܘ ܕܡܠܐ ܛܘܒ̈ܐ ܘܒܛܝܒܘܬܗ ܨܒܹܐ ܘܐܝܬܝ ܡ̣ܢ ܠܐ ܡܕܡ ܟܠܡܕܡ. ܕܗܕܐ ܦܐܝܐ ܠܥܒܘܕܐ ܕܒܥܒܕܘ̈ܗܝ ܢܟܪܙ ܚܝܠܗ ܘܒܬܘܩܢܐ ܕܒܪ̈ܝܬܗ ܥܒܝ̈ܕܐ ܢܩܢܘܢ ܝܕܥܬܐ.
For he is, himself, good by nature, and he is a nature that is filled with goods; and in his goodness, he wished to produce all things from nothing. For it is fitting that the Creator announce his power through his works, and that the (beings) he has made acquire knowledge, through the constitution of his creatures.

### Definition of the divine Essence
ܐܝܬܘܬܗ ܕܠܐ ܫܘܖ̈ܝܐ ܗܝ ܘܡܪ̈ܘܬܗ ܕܠܐ ܫܘܠܡܐ ܗܼܝ ܠܥܒܕܘ̈ܗܝ ܕܝܢ ܫܘܪ̈ܝܐ ܘܙܒܢܐ ܘܛܟܣܐ ܠܒܪ̈ܝܬܗ. 8܀ ܕܠܐ ܡܬܕܪ̈ܟܐ ܐܝܬܘܬܗ ܡܸܢ ܚܘܫܒܐ ܕܥܒܝ̈ܕܐ ܘܠܐ ܒܚܙܬܐ ܡܣܬܝܟ ܕܪܡ ܗܼܘ ܟܝܢܗ ܡ̣ܢ ܗܕܐ. ܠܐ ܡܬܡܫܚܐ ܐܝܬܘܬܗ ܕܟܡܐ ܩܕܝܡ ܠܒܪ̈ܫܝܬ ܘܠܝܬ ܙܒܢܐ ܕܠܐ ܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܗܼܘܐ ܕܩܕܡ ܟܠ ܗܼܘ ܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܗܼܘܐ. ܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܗܼܘܐ ܕܠܐ ܫܘܪ̈ܝܐ ܟܝܢܐ ܐܝܬܝܐ ܘܓܡܝܪܐ ܐܒܐ ܘܒܖ̈ܐ ܘܪܘܚ ܩܘܕܫܐ ܩܢܘܡ̈ܐ ܬܠܬܐ ܚܕ ܚܝܠܐ.
His Essence is without beginning, and his mastery is without end, while his works have a beginning, and his creatures, a time and a hierarchy. His Essence is incomprehensible to the reflection of the (beings) he has made, and he cannot be embraced by the gaze, for his nature is too exalted for that. His Essence is not measurable, so much is it prior to that (which existed) in the beginning, and there is no time when he did not exist, for it is before all things that he existed. The three Persons of the Trinity He was without beginning, a nature existing by itself and perfect, Father, Son and Holy Spirit, three Persons, one Power.


[p. 586] {#po.34.p586}
ܐܒܐ ܓܡܝܪ̈ܐ ܕܠܐ ܫܘܪ̈ܝ ܕܐܘܠܕ ܠܒܪ̈ܐ ܕܠܐ ܚܫܐ ܘܒܪ̈ܐ ܕܡܢܗ ܐܟܘܬܗ ܕܠܝܬ ܙܒܢܐ ܒܝܬ ܠܗ ܘܠܐܒܘܗܝ
The perfect Father is without beginning, he who begets the Son impassibly; and the Son who is (issued) from him is similar to him, so that there is no time between him and his Father.
ܘܪ̈ܘܚܐ ܕܡܢܗ ܕܟܝܢܐ ܩܢܘܡܐ ܐܝܬܝܐ ܘܫܪ̈ܝܪܐ ܕܗܝ ܫܘܝܘܬܐ ܕܟܝܢܗ ܣܗܕܐ ܕܐܝܬܝܐ ܗܼܘ ܥܡ ܐܒܐ.
And the Spirit who (proceeds) from (his) nature is a Person existing (by itself) and true, for it is the equality of nature that attests that with the Father, he exists (by himself).
ܝܠܕܗ ܕܐܒܐ ܠܗ ܕܡܐ ܕܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܥܡ ܐܒܘܗܝ ܡܡܬܘܡ ܘܡܛܠ ܕܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܥܡ ܐܒܘܗܝ ܠܐ ܚܒܫܝܢ ܙܒܢ̈ܐ ܠܝܠܕܗ.
The Begotten of the Father is similar to him, for he is always with his Father, and because he exists at the same time as his Father, the times do not contain his Begotten.
ܝܠܕܗ ܕܐܒܐ ܠܗ ܕܡܐ ܘܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܥܡܗ ܐܝܟ ܨܡܚܐ ܕܒܕܡܘܬ ܨܡܚܐ ܥܡ ܢܘܪܐ ܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܝܠܕܐ ܥܡ ܐܒܘܗܝ.
The Begotten of the Father is similar to him and is with him like a radiance, for the Begotten is with his Father like the radiance with the fire.
ܨܡܚܗ̇ ܕܢܘܪܐ ܥܡܗ̇ ܗܼܘ ܘܠܝܬܝܗ̇ ܢܘܪܐ ܕܠܐ ܨܡܚܐ ܕܗܝ ܥܡ ܨܡܚܗ̇ ܡܬܚܙܝܐ ܘܨܡܚܐ ܥܡܗ̇ ܡܬܒܕܩ.
The radiance of the fire exists with it, and there is no fire without radiance, for it is with its radiance that one sees it, and the radiance appears with it.
ܒܐܒܐ ܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܐܦ ܝܠܕܗ ܘܒܪܐ ܥܡܗ ܕܠܐ ܫܘܪܝ ܘܠܐ ܐܒܐ ܒܠܥܕ ܝܠܕܗ ܘܠܐ ܒܪܐ ܒܠܥܕ ܝܠܘܕܗ. ܠܘ ܐܝܟ ܨܡܚܐ ܡܬܩܝܡ ܕܠܐ ܩܢܘܡܐ ܒܪܐ ܡ̣ܢ ܐܒܐ ܐܠܐ ܩܢܘܡܐ ܗܼܘ ܫܪܝܪܐ ܘܨܠܡܐ ܕܕܡܐ ܠܝܠܘܕܗ.
In the Father is also his Begotten, and the Son is with him without beginning, and there is no Father without the one he begets, nor a son without the one who begets him. (Certainly) it is not like the radiance constituted without hypostasis that the Son was from the Father, but he is a true Person and the resembling image of the one who begets him.
ܠܐ ܐܒܐ ܒܪܐ ܡܬܟܢܐ ܘܠܐ ܒܪܐ ܐܒܐ ܡܫܬܡܗ ܕܦܘܪ̈ܫܢܐ ܕܫܡܗܝ̈ܗܘܢ ܫܪܝܪܐ ܡܟܪܙ ܕܩܢܘܡ̈ܐ.
The Father is not called Son nor does the Son name himself Father, so that the difference in their appellation proclaims the truth of the Persons.
ܪ̈ܘܚܐ ܩܢܘܡܐ ܗܼܘ ܡ̣ܢ ܐܒܐ ܕܫܘܐ ܥܡܗ ܒܟܠܡܕܡ ܐܝܟ ܕܫܘܐ ܒܪܐ ܥܡ ܐܒܐ ܫܘܐ ܪ̈ܘܚܐ ܒܐܒܐ ܘܒܪܐ.
The Spirit is a Person (issued) from the Father, who is equal to him in everything; just as the Son is equal to the Father, the Spirit is equal to the Father and to the Son.
ܠܐ ܣܪܚ ܠܗ̇ ܠܫܘܝܘܬܐ ܫܘܚܠܦܐ ܕܫܡܗܝ̈ܗܘܢ ܫܡܗ̈ܐ ܠܛܟܣܐ ܡܬܢܣܒܝܢ ܘܩܢܘܡ̈ܐ ܫܘܝܢ ܒܐܝܬܘܬܐ. ܒܢܦܫܢ ܨܪ ܠܢ ܬܚܘܝܬܐ ܩܢܘܡ̈ܐ ܬܠܬܐ ܕܐܝܬܘܬܐ ܕܒܪܐ ܘܪ̈ܘܚܐ ܡ̣ܢ ܐܒܐ ܐܝܟ ܡܠܬܐ ܘܚܝ̈ܐ ܘܢܦܫܐ.
The distinction of their names does not destroy the equality itself; the names are received in an order, and the Persons are equal in the Essence. Analogy of the powers of the soul, word and life, with the three divine Persons Through our soul, (God) figured for us as an example the three Persons of the Essence, of the Son and of the Spirit (issued) from the Father, like the word, the life and the soul.
ܥܡܗ̇ ܕܢܦܫܐ ܡܬܝܠܕܐ ܡܠܬܐ ܘܚܝܠܐ ܕܚܝܘܬܐ. ܘܥܡܗ ܕܐܒܐ ܐܝܬܝܗܘܢ ܒܪܐ ܘܪ̈ܘܚܐ ܕܠܐ ܫܘܪ̈ܝܐ.
The word and the vital power are born with the soul, and the Son and the Spirit exist with the Father, without beginning.
ܬܪ̈ܝܢ ܚܝܠ̈ܐ ܠܘܝܢ ܠܗ̇ ܠܢܦܫܐ ܡܠܬܐ ܘܚܝܘܬܐ ܥܡܗ̇ ܘܬܪ̈ܝܢ ܚܝܠܝ̈ܢ ܥܡ ܐܒܐ ܒܪܐ ܘܪ̈ܘܚܐ ܕܠܐ ܫܘܪ̈ܝܐ.
Two powers accompany the soul: the word and the life (are) with it; and two powers are with the Father: the Son and the Spirit, without beginning.


[p. 588] {#po.34.p588}
ܒܪܐ ܝܠܕܐ ܕܠܐ ܫܘܪ̈ܝܐ ܘܪ̈ܘܚܐ ܕܢܦܩ ܡ̣ܢ ܐܒܐ ܚܕ ܝܠܕܐ ܘܚܕ ܢܦܘܩܐ ܚܕܐ ܫܘܝܘܬܐ ܕܒܟܝܢܐ.
The Son begotten without beginning and the Spirit who proceeds from the Father are a single birth and a single procession, a same equality of nature.
ܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܪ̈ܘܚܐ ܡ̣ܢ ܐܒܐ ܘܠܐ ܡܬܟܢܐ ܒܫܡ ܝܠܕܐ ܕܠܘ ܢܘܟܪܝܐ ܗܼܘ ܒܟܝܢܐ ܘܠܐ ܗܼܘܐ ܒܛܟܣܐ ܕܫܡܗ̈ܐ. ܓܒܝܠܐ ܚܘܐ ܡ̣ܢ ܐܕܡ ܘܠܝܬܝܗ̇ ܒܪܬܗ ܐܘ ܚܬܗ ܘܟܕ ܡܢܗ ܗܝ ܒܟܝܢܐ ܐܢܬܬܗ ܒܠܚܘܕ ܡܬܩܪܝܐ.
The Spirit exists from the Father, but is not called by the name of Begotten, for he is not a stranger by nature and is not in the order of names. Analogy of the formation of Eve with the generation of the Son Eve was formed from Adam and she is neither his daughter nor his sister, and although she (comes) from him by (her) nature, she is only called his wife.
ܒܟܝܢܢ ܐܝܟ ܕܒܐܪ̈ܙܐ ܨܪ ܠܢ ܛܘܦܣܐ ܕܐܝܬܘܬܗ ܘܡܢ ܕܐܬܦܠܓ ܥܠ ܝܠܕܐ ܢܚܙܐ ܒܕܝܠܢ ܬܚܘܝܬܗ.
(God) has as if mysteriously depicted in our nature a symbol of his Essence, and whoever doubts the generation (of the son) will see the proof of it in ours.
ܒܗ ܒܟܝܢܢ ܚܟܡ ܠܢ ܕܢܣܬܟܠ ܥܠ ܪܒܘܬܗ ܕܐܢ ܕܝܠܢ ܠܐ ܓܠܝܐ ܠܢ ܟܡܐ ܟܣܝܐ ܒܥܬܐ ܕܝܠܗ. ܒܪ̈ܝܬܗ ܠܐ ܡܬܕܪ̈ܟܢ ܘܥܒܕܘ̈ܗܝ ܥܣܩܝܢ ܡܢܢ ܘܐܝܟܢ ܢܡܪܚ ܘܢܥܩܒ ܥܠ ܐܝܬܘܬܗ ܕܠܐ ܫܘܪܝ.
By our very nature, he taught us to know his greatness: if our (nature) were not manifest to us, as it (would be) hidden, the search for his (would be). God unveiled by his works His creations are incomprehensible and his works too difficult for us; and how would we dare to question his Essence without beginning?
ܥܒܝܕܐ ܠܐ ܡܨܐ ܕܢܥܩܒ ܥܠ ܐܝܬܘܬܗ ܕܥܒܘܕܐ ܟܡܐ ܕܒܥܐ ܕܐܝܟܢ ܫܪܝ ܕܠܐ ܫܘܪܝܐ ܡܫܟܚ ܠܗ.
The created (being) cannot question the Essence of (its) author, insofar as it seeks how began the one for whom there is no beginning.
ܘܟܡܐ ܕܒܥܐ ܚܘܫܒܐ ܕܢܥܩܒ ܥܠ ܐܝܬܘܬܗ ܒܚܕܐ ܒܠܚܘܕ ܡܫܟܚ ܠܗ ܕܠܐ ܡܘܬܝܟ ܒܟܝܢܗ.
And insofar as it seeks, (by) reflection, to question his Essence, it discovers for itself only one thing: that he is unlimited in his nature.
ܬܣܦܩ ܗܕܐ ܠܒܥܬܢ ܕܠܥܒܕܘ̈ܗܝ ܢܒܨܐ ܒܠܚܘܕ ܘܒܥܬܐ ܕܥܠ ܐܝܬܘܬܗ ܒܫܬܩܐ ܡܢܢ ܬܬܝܩܪ.
It will suffice for our inquiry that we scrutinize only his works, and that the search for his Essence be honored by us in silence.
ܢܫܡܥ ܕܝܢ ܥܠ ܬܘܩܢܘ̈ܗܝ ܘܢܫܒܚ ܠܥܒܕ ܐܝܕܘ̈ܗܝ ܕܚܝܒ ܗܼܘ ܥܒܝܕܐ ܕܢܘܕܐ ܠܒܪ̈ܘܝܐ ܥܠ ܟܠ ܕܒܪܐ.
Let us therefore listen to his creations and glorify the work of his hands, for the created (being) must give thanks to the Creator for all that he created!
ܕܫܪܝ ܒܪ̈ܫܝܬ ܒܒܪ̈ܝܬܐ ܗܕܐ ܫܡܥܢܢ ܡ̣ܢ ܡܘܫܐ ܘܕܩܕܝܡ ܗܼܘ ܠܒܪܫܝܬ ܕܐܝܟܢ ܘܟܡܐ ܠܐ ܢܒܥܐ.
That he undertook creation in the beginning, we have heard (said) by Moses, and that he is prior to that (which existed) in the beginning, how and how much, let us not search.
ܢܒܥܐ ܕܝܢ ܐܝܟ ܝܕܘ̈ܥܐ ܒܥܬܐ ܕܦܐܝܐ ܠܥܒܝ̈ܕܐ ܕܡܢܐ ܗܝ ܥܠܬܐ ܕܡܫܡܥ ܗܼܘܐ ܒܪܬ ܩܠܐ ܥܠ ܬܘܩܢܘ̈ܗܝ.
As intelligent (beings), let us therefore seek, in the manner that befits created (beings), for what reason He caused a word to be heard concerning His creations.

### The first creation
ܒܪ̈ܫܝܬ ܕܝܢ ܫܪܝ ܘܒܪܐ ܫܡܝܐ ܘܐܪ̈ܥܐ ܩܕܡܝܬ ܘܟܝܢ̈ܐ ܐܚܪ̈ܢܐ ܚܡܫܐ ܕܠܐ ܩܪܐ ܐܢܘܢ ܒܫܡܗ̈ܐ.
In the beginning, He undertook to create first the heaven and the earth, and the five other natures which He did not call by names.
ܒܗܕܐ ܕܝܢ ܡܠܬܐ ܚܒܝܫܝܢ ܒܓܘ ܬܘܩܢܐ ܕܒܪ̈ܫܝܬ ܢܘܪܐ ܘܡܝ̈ܐ ܘܚܫܘܟܐ ܘܡܠܐܟ̈ܐ ܡܠܝ̈ܠܐ ܘܚܪ̈ܫܐ.
By this word are included in the creation which (took place) in the beginning: fire, water, darkness, the angels, the (beings) endowed with speech, and the mute (beings).


[p. 590] {#po.34.p590}
ܒܗܕܐ ܡܠܬܐ ܕܒܪ̈ܫܝܬ ܚܒܫ ܬܘܩܢܐ ܕܡܠܐܟ̈ܐ ܘܗܘ ܒܪ̈ܘܝܐ ܕܟܠܡܕܡ ܒܪܐ ܐܢܘܢ ܡܢ ܠܐ ܡܕܡ. ܒܗܢܐ ܬܘܩܢܐ ܕܒܪ̈ܫܝܬ ܠܐ ܐܡܪ ܕܐܫܡܥ ܩܠܐ ܕܠܝܬ ܗܘܐ ܥܒܝܕܐ ܕܢܫܡܥ ܗܘܐ ܕܢܩܒܠ ܗܘܐ ܝܘܠܦܢܐ. ܟܕ ܕܝܢ ܫܪܝ ܒܟܝܢ̈ܐ ܕܚܕ ܒܬܪ ܚܕ ܗܘܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܒܒܪ̈ܬ ܩܠܐ ܨܒܐ ܕܢܒܕܩ ܥܠ ܬܘܩܢܗ ܠܡܠܐܟ̈ܐ.
In this word: "In the beginning...", He included the constitution of the angels, and it is He, the Creator of all things, who created them from nothing. (Moses) does not say that (God) caused (His) voice to be heard in this creation which took place in the beginning, for there was no created (being) that could listen in order to receive instruction. But when He began (to create) the substances that existed one after the other, He wished, by a word, to announce His creation to the angels.
ܥܠܬܐ ܕܒܪܬ ܩܠܐ ܗܕܐ ܗܝ ܕܠܪ̈ܘܚܢܐ ܢܠܦ ܗܘܐ ܕܗܘ ܡܪܐ ܕܟܠܗܝܢ ܡܬܩܢ ܗܘܝܘ ܕܐܩܝܡ ܟܢܫܝ̈ܗܘܢ. ܐܝܘ ܩܠܐ ܕܡܢ ܟܠ ܚܣܝܢ ܕܒܪ̈ܫܝܬ ܡܬܡܠܠ ܗܘܐ ܘܠܟܝܢ̈ܐ ܡܬܝܕܥܢ̈ܐ ܒܚܘܒܐ ܠܘܬܗ ܢܓܕ ܗܘܐ. ܡܟܣܐ ܗܘܐ ܕܝܢ ܚܫܘܟܐ ܠܪܘܡܐ ܘܥܘܡܩܐ ܫܘܝܐܝܬ ܘܐܪܥܐ ܒܡܝ̈ܐ ܛܡܝܪܐ ܗܘܬ ܥܕܠܐ ܬܩܒܠ ܬܘܩܢܐ.
The motive for (this) word is that He would teach the spiritual (beings) that the Master who constituted all things is He who caused their assemblies to arise. O word superior to all, which was uttered in the beginning! It urged the intelligent natures to love Him! Darkness then covered both the world above and below, and the earth was immersed in water, before it received (its) structure.
ܕܠܐ ܚܙܬܐ ܘܠܐ ܬܘܩܢܐ ܐܝܬܝܗ̇ ܗܘܬ ܐܪܥܐ ܒܪܫܝܬ ܘܠܐ ܡܬܩܢܬܐ ܩܪܐ ܠܗ̇ ܕܠܐ ܡܨܒܬܐ ܗܘܬ ܒܐܝܠܢ̈ܐ. ܪ̈ܘܚܐ ܠܡ ܡܬܝܒܠ ܗܘܐ ܠܥܠ ܡܢ ܡܝ̈ܐ ܕܬܗܘܡ̈ܐ ܟܕ ܩܪܐ ܪ̈ܘܚܐ ܕܐܐܪ ܠܡܫܒܐ ܕܪ̈ܘܚܐ ܩܛܝܢܐ.
In the beginning, the earth was invisible and without structure, and (Moses) calls it "unstructured" because it was not (yet) adorned with trees. "The Spirit was led above the water of the abyss," calling the breath of the subtle wind the "spirit of the air."

### The creation of light, fruit of a divine word
ܗܘܬ ܕܝܢ ܡܠܬܗ ܕܥܒܘܕܐ ܚܠܦ ܬܘܩܢܐ ܕܝܘܠܦܢܐ. ܘܐܫܡܥ ܩܠܐ ܠܡܠܐܟ̈ܐ ܕܢܗܘܐ ܢܘܗܪܐ ܡܢܗܪ ܟܠ. ܐܝܘ ܩܠܐ ܕܡܢ ܠܐ ܡܕܡ ܐܝܬܝ ܢܘܗܪܐ ܠܗܘܝܐ ܐܝܘ ܢܘܗܪܐ ܕܥܡܗ ܕܫܡܥܐ ܟܝܢܐ ܕܠܝܬܘܗܝ ܐܬܬܩܢ.
To constitute an instruction, there was then the word of the Creator, and He caused (His) voice to be heard by the angels: "Let there be light, which illuminates all things!" O word which, from nothing, brought light into existence, O light whose substance was created, which did not (yet) exist, at the same time as one heard [|
ܪܕܦܗ ܢܘܗܪܐ ܠܚܫܘܟܐ ܕܦܪܝܣ ܗܘܐ ܥܠ ܒܪ̈ܝܬܐ ܘܬܗܪܘ ܐܟܚܕ ܪ̈ܘܚܢܐ ܒܚܝܠܐ ܕܝܗܒ ܠܗ ܫܘܠܛܢܐ. ܘܥܠ ܡܠܬܐ ܕܒܪܐ ܢܘܗܪܐ ܐܫܡܥ ܬܘܒ ܩܠܐ ܐܚܪ̈ܢܐ ܕܡܫܒܚ ܠܗ ܠܬܘܩܢܗ ܕܫܦܝܪ ܡܢܗ ܐܬܬܩܢ. ܐܝܘ ܐܘܡܢܐ ܚܟܝܡܐ ܕܟܡܐ ܙܗܝܪ ܒܗ ܒܬܘܩܢܗ ܣܡ ܠܗ ܬܚܘܡܐ ܠܢܘܗܪܐ ܕܟܡܐ ܘܠܐ ܕܢܫܬܠܛ. ܦܪܫ ܒܝܬ ܢܘܗܪܐ ܠܚܫܘܟܐ ܕܠܐ ܢܣܥܐ ܚܕ ܥܠ ܚܒܪܗ ܘܣܡ ܢܡܘܣܐ ܠܪ̈ܗܛܝܗܘܢ ܒܫܥ̈ܐ ܕܠܠܝܐ ܘܕܐܝܡܡܐ.
The light dissipated the darkness that extended over the creatures, and the spiritual (beings) admired together the Power that gave it the capacity (for it). And in addition to the word (by) which He created the light, He caused yet another word to be heard, by which He glorified His creation itself for having been created beautiful by Him. O wise artisan, who takes such care of His creation: He fixed a limit to the light, so necessary is it that it be mastered! He separated the light from the darkness, for fear that one might attack the others, and He established a law for their evolutions, through the hours of the night and the day.
ܩܪܐ ܕܝܢ ܠܢܘܗܪܐ ܐܝܡܡܐ ܘܠܚܫܘܟܐ ܩܪܝܗܝ ܠܠܝܐ ܘܒܫܡܗ̈ܐ ܟܢܝ ܐܢܘܢ ܠܦܘܬ ܣܘܥܪ̈ܢܐ ܕܫܥܝ̈ܗܘܢ.
He called the light "day," and the darkness He called "night," and He named them by names, according to the realizations of their hours.


[p. 592] {#po.34.p592}
ܗܘܐ ܕܝܢ ܪܡܫܐ ܐܦ ܨܦܪܐ ܝܘܡܐ ܚܕ ܐܝܟ ܡܐ ܕܟܬܝܒ ܘܩܪܝܗܝ ܝܘܡܐ ܩܕܡܝܐ ܕܠܝܬ ܗܘܐ ܐܚܪ̈ܢܐ ܕܐܟܘܬܗ. ܣܡ ܠܗ ܕܝܢ ܐܦ ܫܘܪ̈ܝܐ ܠܠܝܐ ܠܝܘܡܐ ܩܕܡܝܐ ܕܢܛܪ ܛܟܣܐ ܕܒܪܝܬܗ ܕܚܫܘܟܐ ܗܘܐ ܩܕܡܝܬ.
"There was an evening and a morning, one day," as it is written; and (Moses) called it "first day," for there had been no other like it. He also fixed the night as the beginning of the first day, to preserve the order of His creation, for the darkness existed first.

### The creation of the firmament
ܫܠܡ ܕܝܢ ܝܘܡܐ ܩܕܡܝܐ ܠܠܝܐ ܐܝܡܡ ܚܕ ܝܘܡܐ ܘܐܙܥܩ ܬܘܒ ܩܠܐ ܐܚܪ̈ܢܐ ܕܢܗܘܐ ܪܩܝܥܐ ܡܢ ܡܝ̈ܐ. ܐܝܘ ܩܠܐ ܩܢܐ ܫܘܠܛܢܐ ܕܥܡܗ̇ ܕܡܠܬܐ ܣܘܥܪ̈ܢܐ ܐܝܘ ܚܝܠܐ ܕܡܢ ܪ̈ܦܝܬܐ ܚܝܨ ܘܥܒܕ ܩܫܝ̈ܬܐ.
The first day then ended: night and day are one single day; and He proclaimed again another word: "Let there be a firmament from the water!" O word which possesses the power to be realized at the same time as (it is) uttered! O power which, from soft (elements), contracts and hardens them!
ܡܢ ܡܝ̈ܐ ܒܪܝܗܝ ܠܪܩܝܥܐ ܕܢܛܥܢ ܡܝ̈ܐ ܠܥܠ ܡܢܗ ܘܩܪܝܗܝ ܒܫܡܐ ܕܫܡܝܐ ܗܘ ܩܕܡܝܐ ܕܒܪ̈ܫܝܬ. ܐܝܟ ܬܛܠܝܠܐ ܩܪ̈ܝܒܐ ܥܒܕ ܠܪܩܝܥܐ ܒܡܨܥܬܐ ܕܢܗܘܐ ܩܪܝܒ ܠܚܙܬܐ ܘܦܝܪܫ ܒܝܬ ܡܝ̈ܐ ܠܡܝ̈ܐ. ܐܝܘ ܡܬܩܠܐ ܕܠܐ ܪܥܠ ܕܦܠܓ ܡܝ̈ܐ ܫܘܝܐܝܬ ܦܠܓܐ ܠܐܪܥܐ ܠܚܫܚܬܗ̇ ܘܦܠܓܐ ܠܚܨܗ ܕܪ̈ܩܝܥܐ. ܐܝܘ ܐܘܡܢܐ ܡܬܩܢ ܟܠ ܕܝܕܥܬܗ ܠܐ ܡܬܕܪ̈ܟܐ ܕܥܕܠܐ ܢܫܪܐ ܒܥܒܕܐ ܝܕܥ ܗܘܐ ܡܢ ܚܫܚ ܠܗ. ܥܬܝܕ ܗܘܐ ܕܢܬܠܐ ܢܗܝܪ̈ܐ ܟܝܢܐ ܕܢܘܪ̈ܐ ܒܪ̈ܩܝܥܐ ܘܐܣܢ ܡܝ̈ܐ ܠܥܠ ܡܢܗ ܕܠܐ ܢܣܬܝܛ ܡܢ ܢܘܪܐ. ܝܕܥ ܗܘܐ ܬܘܒ ܠܐ ܒܪ̈ܘܝܐ ܥܕܠܐ ܢܒܪܐ ܟܠ ܕܗܘܐ ܕܥܘܡܪܐ ܐܚܪܢܐ ܚܫܚ ܗܘܐ ܠܡܠܝ̈ܠܐ ܒܫܘܠܡ ܙܒܢ̈ܐ.
He created the firmament from the water, such that it would support the water (which is) above it, and He called by the name of "heaven" the first (thing) that (He made) in the beginning. Like a nearby roof, He made the firmament in the midst, so that it might be near to the gaze and separate the waters from one another. O Balance that does not oscillate, which divided the waters equally, half to the earth for its use and half for the vault of the firmament! O artisan who constitutes all things, whose science is incomprehensible, for, before He undertook (His) work, He knew what would be useful to Him! He was to suspend the luminaries, substance of fire, in the firmament, and He heaped the water above it, so that it would not be consumed by the fire. The Creator also knew, before He created all that exists, that another dwelling would be necessary for the reasonable (beings) at the end of time.
ܘܥܠܗܕܐ ܒܪܝܗܝ ܠܪܩܝܥܐ ܐܝܟ ܬܛܠܝܠܐ ܒܡܨܥܬܐ ܕܒܫܘܠܡܐ ܢܗܘܐ ܠܢ ܕܘܟܬ ܐܪܥܐ ܠܢܝܚܢ. ܗܘܐ ܕܝܢ ܨܦܪܐ ܐܝܟ ܪ̈ܡܫܐ ܘܝܘܡܐ ܕܬܪ̈ܝܢ ܐܫܬܠܡ ܘܦܝܚܐ ܘܫܒܚܗ ܠܬܘܩܢܗ ܕܫܦܝܪ ܡܢܗ ܐܬܬܩܢ. ܗܘܐ ܕܝܢ ܪܡܫܐ ܕܬܠܬܐ ܕܠܐ ܡܫܓܢܝ ܡܢ ܩܕܡܝܐ ܕܚܕ ܗܘ ܚܝܠܐ ܦܩܕ ܗܘܐ ܕܢܗܘܐ ܡܕܡ ܘܗܘܐ ܠܗ.
And that is why He created the firmament, as an intermediate roof, so that at the end (of time) it might serve us as earth, for our rest. And there was a morning as (there was) an evening, and the second day ended; and He glorified again His creation for having been created beautiful by Him. Then there was the third evening, which was not different from the first, for it is one single Power that commanded that a thing be, and it existed.

### The gathering of the waters and the appearance of the earth
ܢܬܟܢܫܘܢ ܡܝ̈ܐ ܐܡܪ ܘܢܗܘܘܢ ܝܡܡ̈ܐ ܒܐܬܪ̈ܐ ܚܕ ܘܢܬܓܠܝܢ ܐܦܝ̈ܗ̇ ܕܐܪܥܐ ܕܬܗܘܐ ܚܫܚܐ ܠܥܡܘܪ̈ܝܐ. ܐܝܘ ܦܘܩܕܢܐ ܕܠܐ ܠܐܐ ܐܝܘ ܡܠܬܐ ܕܠܐ ܡܬܥܘܟܐ ܕܥܡܗ ܕܦܩܕ ܨܒܝܢܗ ܐܬܟܢܫܘ ܡܝ̈ܐ ܠܝܡܡ̈ܐ.
"Let the waters be gathered," He said, "and let there be seas in one place, and let the surface of the earth appear, so that it may be capable of being inhabited!" O command that does not toil! O word that has no obstacle, for, at the same time as His will commands it, the waters gathered into oceans!


[p. 594] {#po.34.p594}
ܢܗܪ̈ܬܼ ܐܪ̈ܥܐ ܘܐܬܦܨܚܬ ܘܢܦܼܩ ܥܠܝܗ̇ ܦܘܩܕܢܐ ܬܦܩ ܠܡ ܐܪܥܐ ܘܬܘܥܐ ܥܣܒ̈ܐ ܘܙܪ̈ܥܐ ܘܐܝܠܢ̈ܐ. ܗܼܘܐ ܦܘܩܕܢܐ ܒܥܿܒܕܐ ܘܐܨܛܒܬܬ ܐܪܥܐ ܒܦܐܪ̈ܐ ܘܗܘܘ ܙܪ̈ܥܐ ܘܐܝܠܢ̈ܐ ܘܫܒܚܗ ܛܒܐ ܠܬܘܩܢܗ.
The earth shone and resplendent, and a command was produced concerning it: "Let the earth bring forth and produce herbs, seeds, and trees!" The command was realized and the earth was adorned with fruits, and there were seeds and trees, and the good (God) glorified His creation.
ܗܼܘܐ ܕܝܢ ܪܡܫܐ ܐܦ ܨܦܪ̈ܐ ܝܘܡܐ ܫܠܡܐ ܕܬܠܬܐ ܘܢܦܼܩ ܦܘܩܕܢܐ ܐܚܪ̈ܢܐ ܘܦܩܕ ܕܢܗܘܘܢ ܢܗܝܪ̈ܐ.
And there was an evening and a morning, the third day ended; and another command was produced: He ordered that there be luminaries.

### The creation of the luminaries
ܢܗܘܘܢ ܠܡ ܕܝܢ ܢܗܝܪ̈ܐ ܒܪ̈ܩܝܥܐ ܠܥܠ ܡ̣ܢ ܐܪܥܐ ܕܙܒܢ̈ܐ ܘܝܪ̈ܚܐ ܘܫܢܝ̈ܐ ܒܝܕ ܪ̈ܗܛܝܗܘܢ ܢܬܝܕܥܘܢ. ܢܦܩܬܼ ܡܠܬܐ ܠܥ̇ܒܕܐ ܘܗܘܘ ܡܢܫܠܝ ܢܗܝܪ̈ܐ ܘܣܡ ܢܡܘܣܐ ܕܠܐ ܥܒܪ ܠܪܗܛܐ ܕܠܠܝܐ ܘܕܐܝܡܡܐ. ܐܝܘ ܐܘܡܢܐ ܡܨܒܬ ܟܠ ܕܒܟܠ ܫܠܝܛ ܨܒܝܢܗ ܕܡ̣ܢ ܡܕܡ ܘܡ̣ܢ ܠܐ ܡܕܡ ܦܫܝܩ ܠܗ ܕܢܥܒܕ ܟܠܡܕܡ.
"Let there be luminaries in the firmament, above the earth, so that the times, months, and years may be known by means of their evolutions!" The word was carried out, and there were suddenly luminaries, and He established a law that will not pass, for the course of the night and the day. O artisan who adorns all things, whose will is all-powerful, for whom it is easy to make all things, from one thing and from nothing!
ܡ̣ܢ ܢܘܗܪܐ ܒܼܪܐ ܢܗܝܪ̈ܐ ܡ̣ܢ ܗܿܘ ܢܘܗܪ̈ܐ ܩܕܡܝܐ ܟܕ ܦܠܓܗ ܠܡܢܘ̈ܬܐ ܠܣܗܖ̈ܐ ܘܟܘܟܒ̈ܐ ܘܠܫܡܫܐ. ܠܐ ܩܒܼܥ ܐܢܘܢ ܒܪܩܝܥܐ ܕܠܐ ܢܬܥܘܟܘܢ ܪ̈ܗܛܝܗܘܢ ܐܝܟ ܩܢܕܠܐ ܬܠܐ ܐܢܘܢ ܘܛܟܣ ܪܗܛܐ ܕܫܥܝ̈ܗܘܢ.
He created (in fact) the luminaries from the light, from the first light, by dividing it into portions for the moon, the stars, and the sun. He did not fix them to the firmament, for fear that their evolutions might be hindered, (but) He suspended them like lamps, and He ordered the course of their hours.
ܫܠܡ ܕܝܢ ܝܘܡܐ ܪ̈ܒܝܥܝܐ ܒܬܚܘܡܐ ܕܪ̈ܡܫܐ ܘܨܦܪܐ ܘܢܦܼܩ ܦܘܩܕܢܐ ܐܚܪ̈ܢܐ ܕܢܗܘܐ ܪ̈ܚܫܐ ܡ̣ܢ ܡܝ̈ܐ.
The fourth day ended, limited by an evening and a morning; and another command was produced: "Let there be reptiles from the water!"

### The creation of the fish and the birds
ܘܐܘܠܕܘ ܡܝ̈ܐ ܚܝܘ̈ܬܐ ܓܢܣܢ̈ܐ ܕܠܐ ܡܢܝܢܐ ܪ̈ܚܫܐ ܘܢܘܢ̈ܐ ܘܬܢܝܢ̈ܐ ܘܚܝܘ̈ܬܐ ܩܛܘܠܬ̈ܐ. ܐܝܘ ܚܝܠܐ ܕܦܫܝ̈ܩܢ ܠܗ ܥܣܩ̈ܬܐ ܐܝܟ ܨܒܝܢܗ ܕܡ̣ܢ ܡܝ̈ܐ ܪ̈ܟܼܒ ܘܥܼܒܕ ܓܢܣܢ̈ܐ ܕܠܐ ܡܢܝܢܐ. ܡ̣ܢ ܡܝ̈ܐ ܒܪܐ ܦܪ̈ܚܬܐ ܘܟܝܢܐ ܕܣܚܐ ܥܠ ܐܐܪ ܘܟܕ ܡ̣ܢ ܡܝ̈ܐ ܗܼܝ ܐܝܟ ܪ̈ܚܫܐ ܛܥܝܢ ܠܗ̇ ܐܐܪ ܘܡܗܠܟ. ܐܝܘ ܛܒܐ ܟܡܐ ܪܒ ܚܘܒܗ ܕܢܛܪ ܪܡܙܗ ܠܩܢܝܢܘ̈ܗܝ ܕܒܫܘܠܡ ܬܘܩܢܝ̈ܗܘܢ ܒܒܘܪ̈ܟܬܐ ܙܘܕ ܐܢܘܢ. ܐܘܠܕܘ ܠܡ ܘܣܓܘ ܒܐܪ̈ܥܐ ܒܝܡܐ ܘܝܒܫܐ ܫܘܝܐܝܬ ܟܕ ܡܬܢܛܪ̈ܝܢ ܓܢܣܝ̈ܟܘܢ ܒܝܘܒܠܐ ܚܕ ܒܬܪ ܚܕ. ܒܝܘܡܐ ܚܡܝܫܝܐ ܫܠܡ ܒܪ̈ܝܬܐ ܕܪ̈ܚܫܐ ܘܦܪ̈ܚܬܐ ܘܒܝܘܡܐ ܕܫܬܐ ܫܪܪ̈ܝ ܕܢܒܪܐ ܚܝܘܬܐ ܘܒܥܝܪܐ.
And the waters gave birth to animals of innumerable species: reptiles, fish, sea monsters, and deadly animals. O Power for whom difficulties are easy, according as it wills, which formed and made from the water innumerable species! He created the bird from the water, a nature that flies in the air, and although it comes from the water like the reptiles, the air carries it and makes it advance. O Good One, how great is Thy love, Thou whose sign guards Thy goods, which Thou hast provided with blessings, at the completion of their creation! "Be fruitful and multiply on the earth, in the sea and on the dry (land) at the same time, your species preserving themselves by succession from one to the other!" The fifth day, He finished the creation of the reptiles and the birds, and the sixth day, He undertook to create the wild and domestic animals.


[p. 596] {#po.34.p596}

### The creation of the wild and domestic animals
ܬܦܩ ܠܡ ܐܪ̈ܥܐ ܘܬܝܬܐ ܢܦܫܐ ܚܝܬܐ ܕܟܠ ܓܢܣܝ̈ܢ ܚܝܘܬ ܫܢܐ ܘܒܥܝܪ̈ܐ ܘܪ̈ܚܫܐ ܝܠܕܐ ܕܡ̣ܢ ܐܪ̈ܥܐ. ܐܝܘ ܟܝܢܐ ܚܪ̈ܫܐ ܕܐܦܼܪܥ ܚܝܘ̈ܬܐ ܡ̣ܢ ܓܘ ܥܘܒܗ ܕܟܕ ܡܕܪ̈ܐ ܗܼܘ ܫܝܛ ܒܚܙܬܐ ܐܘܠܕ ܫܘܦܪ̈ܐ ܕܟܠ ܓܢܣܝ̈ܢ. ܠܘ ܗܼܘ ܐܘܠܕ ܒܟܝܢܗ ܐܠܐ ܚܝܠܐ ܕܥܒܘܕܗ ܗܿܘ ܕܪܩܼܥ ܐܪܥܐ ܒܪ̈ܫܝܬ ܦܩܼܕ ܠܗ̇ ܘܝܠܕܬܼ ܚܝܘ̈ܬܐ. ܒܝܘܡܐ ܕܫܬܐ ܐܬܒܪܝܬ ܒܥܝܪ̈ܐ ܘܚܝܘܬܐ ܥܡ ܐܕܡ ܕܗܠܝܢ ܕܥܗ̈ܢܢ ܠܦܘܠܚܢܗ ܥܡܗ ܢܩ̈ܒܠܢ ܬܘܩܝܐ. ܒܝܘܡܐ ܕܫܬܐ ܐܫܬܠܡ ܬܘܩܢܐ ܕܟܠܡܐ ܕܗܘܐ ܘܚܘܝ ܚܝܠܗ ܒܪ̈ܘܝܐ ܒܥܒܕܘ̈ܗܝ ܕܐܡܼܪ ܘܗܘܘ.
"Let the earth bring forth and produce an animal of each species, wild beasts, domestic (animals), and reptiles, offspring of the earth!" O mute nature which, from thy bosom, produced animals; which, although thou art a mud despicable to behold, brought forth the beauty of each species! It is not she who brought (it) forth of herself, but it is the power of her Author, He who, in the beginning, stretched out the earth, (gave) it a command, and it gave birth to trees. The sixth day, the domestic and wild animals were created, as well as Adam, for what is suitable for his use receives (its) structure at the same time as him. The sixth day, the creation of all that exists was finished, and the Creator manifested His power through His works, for He spoke and they existed. The seventh day:
ܒܝܘܡܐ ܫܒܝܥܝܐ ܐܬܬܢܝܚܝ ܥܒܘܕܐ ܡ̣ܢ ܬܘܩܢܘ̈ܗܝ ܘܒܪܟܗ ܠܝܘܡܐ ܫܒܝܥܝܐ ܘܩܼܪܝܗܝ ܝܘܡܐ ܩܕܝܫܐ.
The seventh day, the Creator ceased His creations, and He blessed the seventh day, and called it "holy day."
ܠܐ ܓܝܪ ܠܐܝܐ ܗܘ ܚܝܠܐ ܘܠܐ ܡܬܥܘܟ ܨܒܝܢܗ ܗܼܘܐ ܡܕܡ ܡ̣ܢ ܠܐ ܡܕܡ ܠܫܬܐ ܝܘܡܝ̈ܢ ܟܠܡܕܡ ܠܐ ܗܼܘܐ ܕܥܣܩܐ ܠܨܒܝܢܗ ܠܐ ܒܪܐ ܡܚܕܐ ܟܠܡܕܡ ܕܒܚܕ ܝܘܡ ܘܒܣܓܝ̈ܐܐ ܫܘܐ ܠܗ ܕܢܥܒܕ ܟܠܡܕܡ.
Certainly, this Power does not toil and His will has no obstacles; things existed from nothing, all (existed) in six days. It is not because (it was) difficult for His will that He did not create all things instantaneously, for (it was) the same for Him to make all things in a single day or in several.
ܛܟܣܐ ܥܼܒܕ ܠܗ ܠܒܪܝܬܗ ܐܝܟܢܐ ܕܗܼܘ ܝܿܕܥ ܗܼܘܐ ܘܠܝܘܠܦܢܐ ܕܡܠܝ̈ܠܐ ܠܫܬܐ ܝܘܡܝ̈ܢ ܫܠܡ ܟܠ. ܚܙܝܢ ܗܼܘܘ ܓܝܪ ܬܘܩܢܗ ܡܠܐܟ̈ܐ ܘܡܬܒܩܝܢ ܒܗ ܘܡܐ ܕܐܡܼܪ ܡܠܬܐ ܘܗܘܬ ܩܥܘ ܠܗ ܫܘܒܚܐ ܪ̈ܘܚܢܐ. ܒܝܕܥܬܐ ܟܠ ܐܣܥܪ ܗܼܘܐ ܗܿܘ ܝܕܘܥܐ ܕܟܠ ܝܕܥ ܘܡ̣ܢ ܡܕܡ ܘܡ̣ܢ ܠܐ ܡܕܡ ܐܝܬܝ ܥܒܝ̈ܕܐ ܠܗܘܝܐ. ܠܢܘܗܪ̈ܐ ܒܪܝܗܝ ܡ̣ܢ ܠܐ ܡܕܡ ܕܢܚܘܐ ܥܠ ܪܒܘܬܗ ܘܐܦ ܠܢܦܫܢ ܡ̣ܢ ܠܐ ܡܕܡ ܕܢܚܘܐ ܕܒܟܠ ܫܠܝܛ. ܒܪܐ ܠܢܗܝܪ̈ܐ ܡ̣ܢ ܢܘܗܪܐ ܫܡܫܐ ܘܣܗܖ̈ܐ ܠܡܦܝ̈ܕܐ ܘܝܒܒܘ ܟܢܫ̈ܐ ܕܡܠܐܟ̈ܐ ܫܘܒܚܐ ܠܚܝܠܐ ܡܬܩܢ ܟܠ. ܒܗܠܝܢ ܦܘܪ̈ܣܐ ܚܙܐ ܗܼܘܐ ܒܪ̈ܘܝܐ ܟܕ ܡܬܩܢ ܟܠ ܘܡܬܗܐ ܗܼܘܐ ܠܥܒܕܘ̈ܗܝ ܕܢܠܦ ܚܝܠܗ ܠܡܠܝ̈ܠܐ.
He put order into His creation, as He (Himself) knew, and He finished everything in six days, for the instruction of the reasonable (beings). For the angels saw His creation and considered it, and each time He said a word and it was realized, the spiritual (beings) shouted a praise to Him. The Knower who knows everything realized everything by (His) science, and He brought the created (beings) into existence, from what is and what is not. || He created light from nothing, to manifest His greatness, and (He also created) our soul from nothing, to show that He is all-powerful. He created the luminaries from the light, the sun and the moon (as) lamps, and the assemblies of the angels sang a praise to the Power that constituted all things. The Creator saw these modalities while constituting all things, and He deferred His works, to teach His power to the reasonable (beings).


[p. 598] {#po.34.p598}
ܒܒܘܖ̈ܟܬܐ ܩܕܝܫ̈ܬܐ ܙܘܕܗ ܠܝܘܡܐ ܫܒܝܥܝܐ ܕܠܐ ܢܗܘܐ ܠܗ ܢܘܟܖܪ̈ܝܐ ܕܠܐ ܨܒܼܐ ܕܢܒܪܐ ܒܗ ܡܕܡ
He provided the seventh day with holy blessings, so that it would not be unusual that He did not wish to create anything on that (day).
ܝܘܡܐ ܗܼܘܐ ܐܦ ܗ̇ܘ ܝܘܡܐ ܡܢ ܝܘܡܬ̈ܐ ܩܕܡܝ̈ܐ ܘܕܠܐ ܗܼܘܐ ܠܗ ܬܘܩܢܐ ܩܼܪܝܗܝ ܩܕܝܫܐ ܕܠܐ ܢܣܠܝܘܗܝ. ܝܕܥܬܐ ܕܩܕܝܫܘܬܗ ܗܕܐ ܗܝ ܕܐܬܬܢܝܚ ܡ̣ܢ ܥܡܠܐ ܕܡܐ ܕܫܡܥܢܢ ܫܡ ܥܡܠܐ ܢܐܠܦ ܡܢܘ ܒܪܘܝܐ. ܥܬܝܕ ܗܼܘܐ ܕܢܗܘܐ ܗܢ ܝܘܡܐ ܝܘܠܦܢܐ ܠܙܪܥܗ ܕܐܒܪܡ ܕܡܐ ܕܢܛܪ̈ܝܢ ܠܗ ܡ̣ܢ ܥܡܠܐ ܢܐܠܦܘܢ ܥܠܬܗ ܕܡܢ ܐܝܬܝܗܿ.
That day was also one of the first days, and because no creation took place on it, He called it holy, for fear that we might despise it. The knowledge of its holiness is that He ceased (His) labor, so that hearing the word "labor," we might learn who the Creator is. This day was to be an instruction for the posterity of Abraham: when one preserves it from (all) labor, one will learn what the reason for it is.

### The originality of the creation of man
ܥܠ ܟܠ ܕܝܢ ܕܗܼܘܐ ܡܢܗ ܩܠܐ ܐܫܼܡܥ ܟܕ ܗ̇ܘܐ ܘܗܼܘ ܠܗ ܫܒܚ ܠܬܘܩܢܗ ܕܫܦܝܪ ܡܢܗ ܐܬܬܩܢ. ܥܕܡܐ ܠܗܪܟܐ ܫܘܝܘܬܐ ܕܚܕ ܩܠܐ ܥܠ ܟܠ ܕܗܼܘܐ ܘܒܫܘܠܡܐ ܕܬܘܩܢܘ̈ܗܝ ܩܠܐ ܚܕܬܐ ܐܬܡܠܠ. ܢܥܒܕ ܠܡ ܐܢܫܐ ܡܠܝܠܐ ܒܨܠܡܢ ܕܝܠܢ ܘܕܡܘܬܢ ܘܢܫܬܠܛ ܥܠ ܟܠ ܕܗܼܘܐ ܕܡܛܠܬܗ ܐܬܬܩܢ. ܐܝܘ ܐܘܡܢܐ ܟܡܐ ܚܟܝܡ ܐܝܘ ܛܒܐ ܟܡܐ ܫܦܝܥ ܚܘܒܗ ܕܒܪܐ ܘܨܒܬ ܟܠܡܕܡ ܘܗܝܕܝܢ ܓܒܠܗ ܠܝܪ̈ܘܬܐ. ܒܢܐ ܠܗ ܒܝܬܐ ܠܥܦܪ̈ܢܐ ܘܐܣܢ ܘܡܠܝܗܝ ܛܒ̈ܬܐ ܘܚܪ̈ܫܐ ܘܡܠܝ̈ܠܐ ܐܟܚܕ ܠܬܫܡܫܬܗ ܫܥܒܕ ܐܢܘܢ.
For all that existed by Him, He caused a word to be heard when (a thing) exists, and He Himself glorified His creation for having been created beautiful by Him. Until now, (there is) identity of a single word concerning all that was, and at the end of His creations, a new word was pronounced. "Let us make man reasonable in our own image and in our likeness, and let him dominate over all that exists, which has been constituted because of him!" O what a wise artisan! O Good One, how Thy love abounds, Thou who created and adorned all things, and then fashioned the heir! He built a dwelling for the (being) of dust, and He heaped goods there and filled it; and He subjected to his service both the mute and the endowed-with-reason (beings).
ܒܟܠܗܝܢ ܝܩܪ̈ܗ ܠܡܝܘܬܐ ܒܬܘܩܢܗ ܘܒܫܘܠܛܢܗ ܒܗ̇ܝ ܕܩܪܝܗܝ ܨܠܡܐ ܕܝܠܗ ܘܫܥܒܕ ܠܗ ܟܠܡܐ ܕܗܼܘܐ. ܒܟܠܗܝܢ ܕܗܘ̈ܝ ܘܐܬܬܩܢ ܩܠܐ ܒܠܚܘܕ ܡܫܡܥ ܗܼܘܐ ܘܗܪܟܐ ܒܬܘܩܢܗ ܕܐܕܡ ܡܠܟܐ ܘܚܘܫܒܐ ܚܕܬܐ. ܢܥܒܕ ܐܢܫܐ ܒܕܡܘܬܢ ܘܢܗܘܐ ܫܠܝܛ ܐܟܘܬܢ ܕܒܝܕ ܨܠܡܐ ܕܓܠܝܘܬܗ ܢܟܪܙ ܚܝܠܗ ܠܥܒܝ̈ܕܐ.
He honored the mortal in everything: in his constitution and in his power, in that He called him "His own image" and subjected to him all that exists. For all that exists and was constituted, He caused only one word to be heard, but now for the creation of Adam, (there is) a counsel and a new reflection. "Let us make man in our likeness and let him dominate as we do, so that, by his visible image, he may announce His power to the created (beings)!"
ܒܗ ܓܠܐ ܐܢܐ ܥܠ ܟܣܝܘܬܝ ܠܟܝܢ̈ܐ ܡܬܝܕܥܢ̈ܐ ܘܒܗ ܡܒܕܩ ܐܢܐ ܥܠ ܡܪܘܬܝ ܠܚܪ̈ܫܐ ܘܠܡܠܝ̈ܠܐ ܐܟܚܕ. ܐܝܟ ܨܠܡܐ ܣܐܡ ܐܢܐ ܠܗ ܠܥܒܝ̈ܕܐ ܕܢܬܒܩܘܢ ܒܗ ܕܒܝܕ ܚܘܒܐ ܕܠܘܬܗ ܒܗ ܟܠܢܫ ܠܝ ܢܫܬܘܕܥ. ܬܗܪܐ ܗܼܘ ܬܘܩܢܗ ܕܐܕܡ ܘܡܫܚܠܦ ܡ̣ܢ ܕܥܒܝ̈ܕܐ ܕܟܠ ܕܗܼܘܐ ܒܡܠܬܗ ܥܒܕܗ ܘܠܗ ܒܡܠܟܐ ܐܡܪ ܕܐܥܒܕ.
Through him, I reveal my hidden (nature) to the intelligent natures, and through him I announce my mastery, both to the mute and the endowed-with-speech (beings). I place him as an image for the creatures, so that they may consider him, so that by means of the love that he (will have) for Him, every man may know me." Wonderful is the constitution of Adam and different from that of the (other) created (beings), for He made all that exists by His word, but for him, He said with deliberation: "I will make..."


[p. 600] {#po.34.p600}
ܫܒܝܚܐ ܓܒܝܠܬܗ ܕܥܦܪ̈ܢܐ ܘܐܪ̈ܙܐ ܟܣܝ̈ܐ ܛܡܝܪ̈ܝܢ ܒܗܿ ܕܠܐ ܐܡܪ ܕܢܗܘܐ ܐܟܘܬܝ ܐܠܐ ܒܨܠܡܢ ܘܕܡܘܬܢ. ܐܝܘ ܡܝܘܬܐ ܕܟܣܝܢ ܒܗ̇ ܒܓܒܝܠܬܗ ܐܪ̈ܙܐ ܟܣܝ̈ܐ ܐܝܘ ܥܦܪܢܐ ܕܒܕܩ ܠܢ ܒܬܘܩܢܗ ܩܢܘܡ̈ܐ ܬܠܬܐ.
The formation of the (being) of dust is glorious and hidden mysteries are concealed therein, for He did not say: "Let him be like me!" but "in our image and our likeness"! O mortal, in the formation of whom secret mysteries are hidden! O (being) of dust who, by his constitution, reveals to us the three Persons!
ܒܬܘܩܢܐ ܕܨܠܡܗ ܕܐܕܡ ܐܝܟ ܕܒܐܪ̈ܙܐ ܐܠܦ ܠܢ ܗܝ ܕܒܨܠܡܢ ܘܕܡܘܬܢ ܩܢܘܡ̈ܐ ܬܠܬܐ ܫܘܕܥ ܠܢ
By the constitution of Adam, (his) image, (God) taught us as in a mystery, that (the expression) "in our image and our likeness" made known to us the three Persons.
ܐܝܬܘܬܐ ܕܠܐ ܫܘܪ̈ܝܐ ܕܐܒܐ ܘܒܪܐ ܘܪܘܚ ܩܘܕܫܐ ܒܨܠܡܗ ܕܐܕܡ ܐܬܝܩܪ ܐܝܟ ܕܒܐܪ̈ܙܐ ܟܠ ܕܗܼܘܐ. ܘܐܠܐ ܠܡܢܘ ܩܪܐ ܗܼܘܐ ܒܫܘܝܘܬܐ ܕܥܡ ܪ̈ܒܘܬܗ ܕܒܨܠܡܢ ܕܝܢ ܬܘ ܢܥܒܕ ܠܐ ܐܡܪ ܗܼܘܐ ܠܡܠܐܟ̈ܐ. ܒܗܕܐ ܕܨܠܡܢ ܐܠܦ ܠܢ ܥܠ ܦܘܪ̈ܫܢܐ ܕܩܢܘܡ̈ܐ ܘܒܗ̇ܝ ܕܕܡܘܬܐ ܕܝܠܢ ܥܠ ܫܘܝܘܬܗ ܕܟܝܢܐ. ܬܘ ܠܡ ܢܥܒܕ ܒܪܢܫܐ ܘܢܐܣܘܪ ܒܗ ܟܠܝܡܐ ܕܗܼܘܐ ܕܒܐܚܝܢܘܬܗܘܢ ܕܠܘܬܗ ܒܚܘܒܐ ܢܡܠܘܢ ܣܘܢܩܢܘ̈ܗܝ. ܘܥܒܕ ܐܠܗܐ ܒܨܠܡܗ ܠܐܕܡ ܥܦܪܐ ܡ̣ܢ ܐܪ̈ܥܐ ܘܢܦܚ ܒܗ ܢܦܫܐ ܠܚܝ̈ܐ ܟܝܢܐ ܚܝܐ ܘܡܠܝܠܐ. ܐܝܘ ܚܘܒܐ ܕܠܘܬ ܓܢܣܢ ܐܝܘ ܐܝܩܪܗ ܕܬܘܩܢܢ ܕܚܝܘܬܐ ܕܐܫܪܝ ܒܢ ܡܢ ܚܘܒܗ ܨܒܐ ܘܢܦܚ ܒܢ. ܐܝܘ ܟܝܢܐ ܕܠܐ ܪܘܟܒܐ ܕܐܝܬܘܬܗ ܠܐ ܡܬܡܫܚܐ ܕܟܕ ܡܬܬܩܢ ܠܗ ܠܟܝܢܢ ܒܐܝܕܝ̈ܐ ܘܦܘܡܐ ܥܒܕܢ. ܠܐ ܥܒܕܢ ܐܝܟ ܚܝܘ̈ܬܐ ܕܗܘܘ ܕܟܪ̈ܐ ܘܢܩܒ̈ܬܐ ܕܐܕܡ ܩܕܝܡ ܒܒܪ̈ܝܬܗ ܘܗܝܕܝܢ ܚܘܐ ܡܢ ܐܠܥܗ. ܬܩܢ ܠܐܕܡ ܩܕܡܝܬ ܘܗܝܕܝܢ ܠܐܢܬܬܐ ܡ̣ܢ ܐܕܡ ܕܢܐܠܦ ܚܝܠܐ ܕܥܒܘܕܗ ܕܗܘܝܘ ܨܒܬ ܬܘܩܢܗ.
The Essence without beginning of the Father, the Son, and the Holy Spirit was honored by the image of Adam as (by) all that exists, mysteriously. And otherwise, whom was He inviting to an equality with His greatness, for He certainly had not said to the angels: "Let us make in our image"? By this (expression) "our image," He taught us the distinction of the Persons, and by that of "our likeness," the equality of nature. "Come, let us make man and attach to him all that exists, so that (the creatures), by their affinity with him, may fulfill his needs with love!" And God made Adam, dust of the ground, in His image and He breathed into him a breath of life, a living and reasonable nature. O love for our race, O glory of our creation: the life that He introduced into us, He wished, by His love, to breathe into us! O uncompounded nature of Thy immeasurable Essence, who, in constituting our very nature, fashioned us with hands and mouth! He did not make us like the animals, which were male and female, for Adam (was) the first to be created, and Eve (was) then, from (one) of his ribs. He constituted Adam first, then the woman from Adam, so that he might learn that the power of Him who made him adorns His creation.
ܓܒܠ ܠܡ ܡܪܝܐ ܐܠܗܐ ܠܐܕܡ ܥܦܪܐ ܡ̣ܢ ܐܪܥܐ ܘܣܡܗ ܒܦܪ̈ܕܝܣܐ ܕܥܕܢ ܕܢܗܘܐ ܒܓܘܗ ܝܪ̈ܘܬܐ. ܒܥܘܡܪ̈ܐ ܦܐܝܐ ܘܙܓܝܓܐ ܣܡܗ ܠܐܕܡ ܐܝܟ ܨܠܡܗ ܕܥܒܝ̈ܕܐ ܒܝܕ ܩܪ̈ܝܒܐ ܒܚܘܒܐ ܢܩܪ̈ܘܢ ܥܒܘܕܐ. ܕܡܐ ܨܠܡܗ ܠܒܪܘܝܐ ܠܗ ܒܫܡܐ ܠܘ ܒܟܝܢܐ ܕܐܫܠܛܗ ܥܠ ܟܠ ܕܐܬܬܩܢ ܐܝܟ ܒܪܘܝܐ ܥܠ ܕܝܠܗ.
"The Lord God fashioned Adam, dust of the ground, and established him in the paradise of Eden to be the heir within it." He placed Adam, like His image, in a beautiful and desirable dwelling, so that the created (beings), through the intermediary of a nearby (being), might invoke the Creator with love. Definition of the image of God: His image is similar to the Creator, nominally but not by nature, for He made him dominate over all that He constituted, as the Creator (dominates) over that (which is) His.


[p. 602] {#po.34.p602}
ܗ̇ܘܐ ܐܒܐ ܡܐ ܕܐܘܠܕ ܘܝܠܕ ܝܠܕܐ ܕܕܡ̈ܐ ܠܗ ܐܝܟ ܐܒܐ ܕܐܘܠܕ ܠܒܪ̈ܐ ܒܟܝܢܗ ܕܠܐ ܫܘܖ̈ܝܐ. ܗܼܘ ܒܙܒܢܝܐ ܗ̇ܘܐ ܐܒܐ ܘܒܙܒܢܐ ܝܠܕ ܝܠܕܐ ܐܝܬܝܐ ܕܠܐ ܙܒܢܐ ܐܘܠܕ ܘܠܝܬ ܙܒܢܐ ܒܝܬ ܠܗ ܠܝܠܕܗ.
(God) becomes Father when He has begotten, and He begets the Begotten who is similar to Him like a father who has begotten a son, by His nature without beginning. It is in time that He becomes father, and in time that He begets a begotten (one), (but) the Being-by-Himself has begotten timelessly, and there is no time between Him and His Begotten.
ܥܒܕ ܡܕܡ ܡ̣ܢ ܡܕܡ ܘܟܕ ܠܐ ܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܡܬܩܢ ܠܗ ܐܝܟ ܥܒܘܕܐ ܕܐܝܬܝ ܟܠ ܠܗܘܝܐ ܡ̣ܢ ܠܐ ܡܕܡ.
He makes one thing with another, and when it does not exist, He constitutes it, as creator, who brought everything into existence from nothing.
ܥܒܕ ܡܕܡ ܡ̣ܢ ܡܕܡ ܘܡܿܬܕܡܐ ܠܗ ܠܒܪ̈ܘܝܐ ܟܠ ܕܗܼܘܐ ܒܐܪ̈ܥܐ ܚܳܐܖܪ ܘܕܝܠܗ ܚܝܪܗ ܒܫܡܝܐ. ܕܒܝܕ ܪܡܙܐ ܕܚܝܪ̈ܗ ܠܐܬܪܗ ܒ̇ܿܥܐ ܥܠܝܐ ܒܗܠܝܢ ܕܡܐ ܥܦܪ̈ܢܐ ܐܝܟ ܕܒܐܪ̈ܙܐ ܠܒܪ̈ܘܝܐ. ܕܟܕ ܬܘܒ ܒܡܠܬܐ ܘܚܝܘܬܐ ܦܩܿܕ ܐܦ ܗܼܘ ܠܟܝܢ̈ܐ ܒܫܡܐ ܕܨܠܡܐ ܫܐܝܠܐܝܬ ܟܢܝܗ ܠܐܕܡ ܩܕܡܝܐ. ܘܗܼܘܐ ܨܠܡܐ ܒܥܒ̇ܕܐ ܒܡܫܝܚܐ ܐܕܡ ܕܬܪ̈ܝܢ ܗܪ̈ܟܐ ܫܩܠܬܼ ܫܘܠܡܐ ܗܝ ܕܒܨܠܡܢ ܬܘ ܢܥܒܕ. ܕܫܩܠܗ ܠܨܠܡܗ ܒܪ̈ܘܝܐ ܘܥܒܕܗ ܥܘܡܪܐ ܠܐܝܩܪܗ ܒܡܫܝܚܐ ܗܘܘ ܒܥ̇ܒܕܐ ܡܘܠܟܢ̈ܐ ܕܠܘܬ ܐܕܡ ܕܩܼܪ̈ܝܗܝ ܨܠܡܗ ܘܐܬܚܒܠ ܗܦܼܟ ܘܐܬܚܕܬ ܒܡܫܝܚܐ. ܒܝܕ ܥܠܬܐ ܕܨܠܡܗ ܕܐܕܡ ܬܢܐ ܪ̈ܒܘܬܗ ܕܦܪ̈ܕܝܣܐ ܕܐܕܡ ܢܕܥ ܐܝܩܪܗ ܕܒܐܬܪܐ ܫܒܝܚܐ ܣܐܡ ܠܗ. ܥܒܕ ܠܡ ܡܖ̈ܝܐ ܐܠܗܐ ܦܪ̈ܕܝܣܐ ܒܥܕܢ ܠܘܩܕܡ ܘܐܘܥܝ ܒܓܘܗ ܐܝܠܢ̈ܐ ܕܥܗܢܝܢ ܣܓܝ ܠܡܐܟܘܠܬܐ. ܚܕ ܕܝܢ ܡܢܗܘܢ ܕܐܝܠܢ̈ܐ ܣܡ ܒܡܨܥܬܗ ܕܦܪ̈ܕܝܣܐ ܘܩܼܪܝܗܝ ܐܝܠܢܐ ܕܚܝ̈ܐ ܕܢܩܒܠ ܒܗ ܐܕܡ ܚܝ̈ܐ. ܢܨܒ ܕܝܢ ܐܝܠܢܐ ܐܚܪ̈ܢܐ ܘܩܼܪ̈ܝܗܝ ܝܕܥ ܕܛܒ ܘܒܝܫ ܕܝܕܥܬܐ ܕܟܣܝܐ ܒܐܕܡ ܒܦܐܪܐ ܕܡܢܗ ܡܬܓܠܝܐ. ܬܼܢܝܐ ܥܠ ܢܒܥܐ ܕܢ̇ܦܩ ܗܘܐ ܡ̣ܢ ܦܪ̈ܕܝܣܐ ܘܡܫܩܐ ܠܗ ܕܡܸܢ ܪ̈ܒܘܬܗ ܡܬܦܪ̈ܫܝܢ ܐܪ̈ܒܥܐ ܢܒܥ̈ܐ ܠܟܠ ܦܢܝ̈ܢ. ܢܗܪܐ ܩܕܡܝܐ ܦܝܫܘܢ ܕܚܿܕܪ ܠܐܪ̈ܥܐ ܕܚܘܝܠܐ ܕܬܡܢ ܕܗܒܐ ܘܒܪ̈ܘܠܐ ܘܟܐܦ̈ܐ ܘܛܒܥ̈ܐ ܢܗܝܪ̈ܐ. ܢܗܪܐ ܬܢܝܢܐ ܓܝܚܘܢ ܕܡܫܩܐ ܠܐܪ̈ܥܐ ܕܟܘܫܝ̈ܐ ܘܒܫܦܥܐ ܕܡܢܗ ܡܿܚܐ ܠܡܨܪ̈ܝܐ ܘܡܕܝܢܝ̈ܐ. ܢܗܪܐ ܕܬܠܬܐ ܕܩܠܬ ܕܥܒܝܕ ܐܝܙܓܕܐ ܠܐܬܘܪ ܘܡܬܒܤܡܝܢ ܡ̣ܢ ܚܠܝܘܬܗ ܟܠܗܘܢ ܥܡܡ̈ܐ ܕܚܕ̈ܪܘܗܝ.
He makes one thing with another, and it resembles the Creator Himself; all that exists looks toward the earth, but his gaze (Adam's) is toward heaven. Because, in the blink of an eye, he seeks the Most High in His place, by that the (being) of dust resembles the Creator as if mysteriously. For, as moreover, he too commands the natures, by word and vitality, (God) called the first Adam, by metaphor, by the name of image. And the image was realized in Christ, the second Adam; here this (word) has ended: "Come, let us make in our image..." For the Creator took His image and made it a dwelling for His glory; the promises (made) to Adam were realized in Christ. He whom He called His image and who had become corrupted, was restored in Christ. The trees and the rivers of Paradise: Because of the image, Adam, (Moses) described the greatness of Paradise, so that Adam might know that he is honored: (God) established him in a glorious place. "The Lord God first made a garden in Eden, and He made trees grow there that were edible." He placed one of them in the midst of Paradise, and called it "tree of life," so that through it Adam might acquire life. He planted another tree, and called it "knower of what is suitable," for the knowledge that (is) hidden in Adam will be manifested by the fruit that (comes) from it. He described the source that went out of Paradise and watered it, from which, due to its importance, four rivers separate on all sides. The first river is the Pishon, which winds around the land of Havilah, where there is gold, beryl, stones, and sparkling jewels. The second river is the Gihon, which waters the land of the Cushites, and makes live, by the flood that comes from it, the Egyptians and the Midianites. The third river is the Tigris, which was made messenger of Assur, and all the surrounding peoples delight in its sweetness.


[p. 604] {#po.34.p604}
ܦܪ̈ܬ ܕܝܢ ܢܗܪܐ ܪ̈ܒܝܥܝܐ ܕܥܒ̈ܪ ܒܡܨܥܬܗ̇ ܕܐܪ̈ܥܐ ܘܝܿܗܒ ܢܦܫܗ ܠܦܠܚ̈ܐ ܕܢܪ̈ܟܒܘܢ ܒܗ ܐܪ̈ܥܬܐ. ܠܘ ܕܢܫܒܚ ܦܪ̈ܕܝܣܐ ܐܫܬܥܝ ܢܒܝܐ ܗܠܝܢ ܘܠܘ ܕܢܩܠܣ ܪ̈ܒܘܬܐ ܕܢܗܪ̈ܘܬܐ ܕܒ̣ܓܘܗ.
The Euphrates is the fourth river, which passes through the midst of the earth, and gives itself to the cultivators so that they may make fields thanks to it. It is not to glorify Paradise that the prophet recounted these things, nor to praise the majesty of the rivers that are found there;
ܠܚܝܠܗ ܕܒܪ̈ܘܝܐ ܐܘܖ̈ܒ ܕܦܫܝ̈ܩܢ ܠܗ ܥܣܩ̈ܬܐ ܕܐܝܟܢ ܢܒܥܗ ܕܦܪ̈ܕܝܣܐ ܒܓܘ ܝܡܐ ܥܒܪ ܨܐܕܝܢ.
(But) he exalted the power of the Creator, for whom difficulties are easy, such as (making) the source of Paradise pass inside the sea to us.
ܥܒܪ̈ܝܢ ܡܝ̈ܐ ܒܓܘ ܝܡܡ̈ܐ ܘܠܐ ܚܠܛܝܢ ܡܝ̈ܐ ܒܡܝ̈ܐ ܘܣܗܕܐ ܗܕܐ ܠܖ̈ܒܘܬܗ ܕܚܝܠܗ ܕܐܬܼܩܢ ܟܠܡܕܡ. ܐܝܘ ܪܡܙܐ ܕܢ̇ܛܪ ܡܝ̈ܐ ܒܓܘ ܡܝ̈ܐ ܐܝܟ ܕܒܡܐܢܝܐ ܘܠܐ ܕܚܶܠܛ ܡܝ̈ܐ ܕܥܕܢ ܒܡܝ̈ܐ ܫܓܝ̈ܫܐ ܕܬܗܘܡܐ.
The water passes inside the seas, and it does not mix with the (other) waters, and this testifies to the greatness of him whose power constituted all things. O sign that preserves water inside (another) water, as in a vase, and which does not mix the water of Eden with the murky water of the abyss!
ܥܿܒ̈ܕܐ ܚܪ̈ܫܐ ܘܫܬܝܩ̈ܐ ܩܥܿܝܢ ܟܠܫܥ ܥܠ ܚܝܠܗ ܟܕ ܡܠܦܝܢ ܠܡܠܝܠܘܬܐ ܕܗܐ ܥܒܘܕܐ ܒܥܵܒ̈ܕܘܗܝ. ܝܘܠܦܢܐ ܕܡܠܐ ܚܝ̈ܐ ܓܠܼܐ ܠܢ ܫܪ̈ܒܗ ܕܦܪ̈ܕܝܣܐ ܘܬܫܥܝܬܐ ܕܥܠ ܐܝܠܢܐ ܠܡܠܝܠܘܬܐ ܩܪ̈ܒܬܼܢ.
Mute and silent works proclaim his power unceasingly, by teaching the intelligence of this Creator in his works. Recapitulation of the work of the six days. The history of Paradise has revealed to us a teaching full of life, and the story concerning the tree has brought us to reason.
ܒܥ̈ܠܠܬܐ ܨܒ̣ܼܐ ܕܢܚܟܡ ܒܪ̈ܘܝܐ ܠܡܠܝܠܘܬܢ ܘܐܝܟ ܕܠܫܒܪ̈ܐ ܒܠܘܚܡܐ ܠܚܐܪ̈ܘܬܐ ܕܒܢ ܐܘܕܥ ܠܢ. ܠܪ̈ܘ̇ܚܢܐ ܒܝܕ ܬܘܩܢܘ̈ܗܝ ܐܠܦ ܕܐܝܬܝܐ ܗܼܘ ܘܥܒܘܕܐ ܗܼܘ ܘܠܢ ܒܟܝܢܐ ܚܟܡ ܠ ܕܸܓܠܼܝܐ ܒܢ ܥܠ ܟܣܝܘܬܗ. ܒܪ̈ܫܝܬ ܠܐܪ̈ܥܐ ܘܠܫܡܝܐ ܬܩܢ ܟܕ ܠܐ ܐܝܬܝܗܘܢ ܘܥܡܗ ܕܚܝܠܐ ܕܒܪ̈ܫܝܬ ܗܘܘ ܚܝܠܐ ܡܬܝܕܥܢ̈ܐ. ܒܪܐ ܢܗܝܪ̈ܐ ܟܕ ܡܿܫܡܥ ܩܠܐ ܠܟܢܫ̈ܐ ܕܪ̈ܘܚܢܐ ܘܒܗܘܝܗ ܕܢܘܗܖ̈ܐ ܐܠܦ ܕܗܼܘ ܨܒܬ ܠܩܢܘܡܝ̈ܗܘܢ.
By pretexts the Creator wished to instruct our reason, and as to children, he made known to us through the threat, the freedom that (is) in us. To the spiritual (beings), by means of his creations, he taught that he is the Being-by-himself and the Creator, but us, he instructed in (our) nature, for he reveals through us his hidden (nature). In the beginning he constituted the heaven and the earth, while they did not exist, and at the same time as the power that he (deployed) in the beginning, the intelligent powers: He created the luminaries by making a word heard to the assemblies of the spiritual (beings), and by the existence of light, he taught (them) that it is he who had adorned them himself.
ܥܼܒܕ ܡ̣ܢ ܡܝ̈ܐ ܠܪ̈ܩܝܥܐ ܘܐܛܸܥܢ ܡܝ̈ܐ ܠܥܠ ܡܢܝܗ ܘܨܒܬܗ ܒܛܒܥ̈ܐ ܢܗܝܪ̈ܐ ܨܒܬܐ ܕܚܫܚܐ ܠܡܠܝܠܐ. ܟܢܫ ܡܝ̈ܐ ܡܸ̣ܢ ܐܪܥܐ ܠܩܒܝܐ ܕܝܡܡ̈ܐ ܘܬܗܘܡ̈ܐ ܘܨܒܬܗ̇ ܒܙܪ̈ܥܐ ܘܒܐܝܠܢ̈ܐ ܠܚܝ̈ܐ ܕܐܢܫܐ ܘܕܒܥܝܪ̈ܐ.
He made the firmament from water and made it support that which (is found) above it, and he decorated it with sparkling jewels, a decoration useful to the rational (beings). He gathered the waters of the earth into the reservoir of the oceans and the abysses, and he adorned (the earth) with seeds and trees for the life of man and cattle.


[p. 606] {#po.34.p606}
ܐܪܚܫܘ ܡܝ̈ܐ ܚܝܘ̈ܬܐ ܘܝܠܕܬܼ ܐܪܥܐ ܟܠ ܓܢܣܝ̈ܢ ܘܒܬܘܩܫ̈ܐ ܫܬܝܩ̈ܐ ܚܟܡ ܣܓܝ ܠܡܠܝܠܐ.
The water produced animals and the earth brought forth all species, and by silent creations, (God) instructed the rational (beings) much.
ܥܒܕ ܠܟܝܢܢ ܡ̣ܢ ܐܪ̈ܥܐ ܘܢ̣ܦܚ ܒܢ ܪ̈ܘܚܐ ܕܚܝ̈ܐ ܘܒܚܘܒܐ ܕܠܘܬ ܓܢܣܢ ܐܣܪ ܚܪ̈ܫܐ ܘܡܠܝ̈ܠܐ.
From the soil, he made our nature and he breathed into us a breath of life, and out of love for our race, he united the mute (beings) to the endowed-with-speech (beings).

### Motifs and purpose of creation
ܗܠܝܢ ܟܠܗܘܢ ܬܘܩܢ̈ܐ ܟܣܝܢ ܗܼܘܘ ܡܡܬܘܡ ܒܝܕܥܬܗ ܘܠܓܠܝܐ ܐܝܬܝ ܐܢܘܢ ܘܚܘܝ ܚܘܒܗ ܠܩܢܝܢܘܗܝ
All these creations were hidden since always in his Science, but he produced them in the open and he showed his love to his possessions.
ܩܕܝܡ ܗܼܘܐ ܕܝܢ ܨܒܝܢܗ ܠܬܘܩܢܘ̈ܗܝ ܥܡ ܐܝܬܘܬܗ ܘܝܿܕܥ ܗܼܘܐ ܥܕܠܐ ܢܒܪ̈ܐ ܕܟܡܐ ܟܝܢ̈ܐ ܥܒ̇ܕ ܗܼܘܐ.
Certainly, his will was prior to his creations, as well as his Essence, and he knew, before creating, how many natures he would make.
ܡܦܣ ܗܼܘܐ ܕܬܪ̈ܝܢ ܬܘܩܢ̈ܐ ܥܗܢܝܢ ܗܼܘܘ ܠܡܠܝܠܘܬܐ ܥܘܡܖ̈ܐ ܕܐܪ̈ܥܐ ܠܡܝܘܬ̈ܐ ܘܫܡܝܐ ܠܠܐ ܡܝܘܬ̈ܐ.
He knew that two creations were suitable for the rational (beings): the earthly dwelling for mortals, and heaven for the immortals.
ܝܿܕܥ ܗܼܘܐ ܚܘܫܒܝ̈ܗܘܢ ܕܡܠܝ̈ܠܐ ܥܕܠܐ ܢܗܘܘܢ ܘܓܠܝ̈ܢ ܗܼܘܝ̈ ܠܗ ܟܣܝ̈ܬܐ ܕܒܬܪ ܙܒܢܐ ܡܣܕܥܪ̈ܢ.
He knew the thoughts of the rational (beings) before they existed, and the hidden things that would be accomplished in time were manifest to him.
ܒܟܠ ܕܗܼܘܐ ܡܬܒܩܐ ܗܼܘܐ ܥܕܠܐ ܢܐܬܐ ܠܗܘܝܐ ܘܡܣܝܟܝܢ ܗܼܘܘ ܒܝܕܥܬܗ ܥܿܒ̈ܕܐ ܕܡܢܗ ܗܘܝܢ ܗܼܘܘ.
He considered everything that is, before it came into existence, and the works that proceeded from him were included in his Science.
ܠܘ ܣܘܢܩܢܗ ܐܬܿܡܠܝ ܕܐܝܬܝ ܒܖ̈ܝܬܐ ܠܗܘܝܐ ܨܒܝܢܗ ܟܣܝܐ ܚܘܝ ܕܟܡܐ ܪ̈ܚܶܡ ܠܩܢܝܢܘ̈ܗܝ.
It is not to realize what he needed that he brought creation into existence, (but) he showed his secret will, how much he loves his possessions!
ܗܼܘ ܢܒܥܐ ܗܼܘ ܕܡܠܐ ܚܝ̈ܐ ܕܩܢܐ ܗܼܘ ܚܝ̈ܐ ܒܟܝܢܗ ܘܡ̣ܢ ܠܐ ܡܕܡ ܬܩܢ ܟܠ ܘܝܗܼܒ ܚܝܘܬܐ ܒܡܠܝ̈ܠܐ.
It is he, the source full of life, who possesses life in himself, and from nothing he constituted all things and gave life to the rational (beings).
ܥܘܬܪ̈ܗ ܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܨܒܝܢܗ ܘܓܙܗ ܪܒܐ ܚܘܫܒܗ ܘܟܕ ܠܐ ܫܐܠܘܗܝ ܢܣܘܒ̈ܐ ܦܬܼܚ ܣܝܡܬ̈ܗ ܘܐܥܼܬܪ ܟܠ.
His will is his wealth, and his thought, his great treasure, and although the beneficiaries do not ask him for it, he opened his treasures and enriched them all.
ܦܪ̈ܝܣ ܚܘܒܗ ܥܠ ܒܪ̈ܝܬܗ ܘܣܥܿܪ ܟܠܝܘܡ ܩܢܝܢܘ̈ܗܝ ܘܢܛܪ ܪܡܙܗ ܠܟܝܢ̈ܐ ܡܠܝ̈ܠܐ ܘܚܪ̈ܫܐ ܫܘܝܐܝܬ.
His love spreads over his creatures and visits his possessions daily, and his sign preserves both the natures endowed with speech and the mute ones.
ܣܡ ܢܡܘܣܐ ܠܡܝܘ̈ܬܐ ܕܐܢ ܢܛܪ̈ܝܢ ܠܗ ܚܐܝܢ ܒܗ ܘܐܩܝܡ ܚܝܠܗ ܪܘܚܢܐ ܠܬܫܡܫܬܐ ܕܣܘܢܩܢܝ̈ܗܘܢ.
He fixed a law for mortals, such that by keeping it, they live by it, and he put his spiritual power at the service of their needs.
ܗܠܝܢ ܡܡܬܘܡ ܣܝ̈ܡܢ ܗܘܝ̈ ܕܡܣܬܥܪ̈ܢ ܐܝܟ ܨܒܝܢܗ ܘܒܝܕܥܬܐ ܕܠܐ ܡܬܕܪ̈ܟܐ ܬܚܡ ܙܒܢܐ ܠܥܒ̇ܕܘ̈ܗܝ.
What was realized according to his will was fixed since always, and by (his) incomprehensible Science, he limited the times for his works.
ܟܠܝܘܡ ܡܟܪܿܙ ܟܣܝܬܗ ܒܓܠܝ̈ܬܐ ܕܥܒ̇ܕ ܐܝܕܘ̈ܗܝ ܟܕ ܬܿܢܝܢ ܥܠ ܪ̈ܒܘܬܗ ܫܡܫܐ ܘܣܗܖ̈ܐ ܒܪ̈ܗܛܝܗܘܢ.
He proclaims his secrets every day in the manifestations of the work of his hands, when the sun and the moon, by their evolutions, retell his greatness.
ܠܗ ܚܝܒܝܢ ܬܘܩܢܘ̈ܗܝ ܬܘܕܝܬܐ ܥܠ ܡܘܗܒܬܗ ܕܒܪ̈ܚܡܐ ܨܒܬ ܐܢܘܢ ܒܚܘܒܐ ܢܘܕܘܢ ܠܐܝܬܘܬܗ.
His creatures owe him thanksgiving for what he gives (them); since he has adorned them out of love, let them render thanks with devotion to his Essence!


[p. 608] {#po.34.p608}
ܠܘ ܣܘܢܩܢܗ ܡܬܡܠܐ ܡ̣ܢ ܬܘܕܝܬܐ ܕܥܒܝ̈ܕܐ ܬܘܕܝܬܐ ܠܢ ܝܘܬܪ̈ܢܐ ܗܝ ܘܠܗ ܕܝܠܗ ܠܐ ܡܬܒܥܝܐ.
It is not so that what he needs may be realized that the created (beings) render thanks, (but) thanksgiving is a profit for us, and is not sought by himself.
ܡܢܘ ܩܠܣ ܠܟܝܢܗ ܟܕ ܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܗܼܘܐ ܡܬܘܡܐܝܬ ܘܡܿܢ ܩܪܒ ܠܗ ܬܫܒܘܚܬܐ ܥܕܠܐ ܢܒܪܐ ܒܪ̈ܝܬܐ.
Who praised his nature, while it existed eternally, and who presented a praise to him, before he created the creatures?
ܫܘܒܚܗ ܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܒܟܝܢܗ ܘܪ̈ܘܡܪ̈ܡܗ ܥܡ ܐܝܬܘܬܗ ܘܠܐ ܙܥܿܪ ܐܦܠܐ ܝܰܪܒ ܕܪܡ ܗܼܘ ܟܝܢܗ ܡ̣ܢ ܗܠܝܢ
His glory is in himself, and his majesty is with his Essence, and it neither decreases nor increases, for his nature is too high for that.
ܠܢ ܗܼܘ ܡܘܪܒ ܟܕ ܩܪܐ ܘܡܩܖ̈ܒ ܠܢ ܠܝܕܥܬܗ ܕܡܐ ܕܝܕܥܢܝܗܝ ܢܬܠ ܠܢ ܡܠܟܘܬܐ ܚܠܦ ܬܘܕܝܬܐ.
It is us he exalts, when he invites us and offers us to know him, so that, when we have known him, he may give us the kingdom in exchange for thanksgiving.
ܒܚܘܒܐ ܕܢܦܫܐ ܢܘܕܐ ܠܗ ܠܥܒܘܕܐ ܥܠ ܬܘܩܢܘܗܝ ܕܡܛܠܬܢ ܬܩܢ ܟܠ ܠܗ ܬܘܕܝܬܐ ܡ̣ܢ ܕܝܠܗ܀
Let us render thanks, with a loving soul, to the Creator for his works! To him, who constituted all things for us, the thanksgiving of those who belong to him!

### IV
ܥܠ ܬܘܩܢܐ ܕܐܕܡ ܘܚܘܐ ܘܥܠ ܥܒܪ ܦܘܩܕܢܐ
ON THE CONSTITUTION OF ADAM AND EVE AND ON THE TRANSGRESSION OF THE COMMANDMENT

### The creation of Adam and Eve
ܡܠܟܐ ܒܨܠܡܗ ܡܬܝܩܪ ܟܕ ܪ̈ܚܝܩܐ ܐܝܟ ܩܪ̈ܝܒܐ ܒܪ̈ܘܝܐ ܒܨܠܡܗ ܕܐܕܡ ܨܒܼܐ ܕܢܚܟܡ ܠܡܠܝܠܐ.
Whether it be far or near, a king is honored by his image; through Adam, (his) image, the Creator wished to instruct the rational (beings).
ܒܥܠܡܐ ܡܕܝܢܬ ܡܠܟܘܬܐ ܣܡܗ ܠܨܠܡܗ ܒܪ̈ܘܝܐ ܘܒܨܠܡܐ ܡܬܚܙܝܢܐ ܡܘܕܥ ܚܝܠܐ ܕܟܣܝܘܬܗ. ܒܨܠܡܗ ܙܝ̈ܢܗ̇ ܒܪ̈ܘܝܐ ܠܒܪ̈ܝܬܐ ܟܕ ܡܬܩܢ ܠܗ̇ ܕܒܚܘܒܐ ܕܠܘܬ ܐܕܡ ܒܚܘܒܐ ܕܝܠܗ ܢܬܕܡܘܢ.܀ ܥܒܕܗ ܐܚܝܢܐ ܠܡܠܐܟ̈ܐ ܒܝܕ ܢܦܫܐ ܪ̈ܘܚܢܝܬܐ ܘܐܩܦ ܠܗ ܟܝܢ̈ܐ ܚܪ̈ܫܐ ܒܝܕ ܪ̈ܘܟܒܐ ܕܗܕܡܘ̈ܗܝ. ܓܒܼܠ ܠܡ ܡܪܝܐ ܐܠܗܐ ܠܐܕܡ ܥܦܪ̈ܐ ܡ̣ܢ ܐܪܥܐ ܘܢܦܼܚ ܒܗ ܪ̈ܘܚܐ ܕܚܝ̈ܐ ܘܥܒܕܗ ܚܝܐ ܘܡܠܝܠܐ. ܚܼܙܐ ܠܡ ܡܪܝܐ ܐܠܗܐ ܘܗܐ ܟܠ ܕܥܒܼܕ ܐܫܬܡܠܝ ܚܘܐ ܒܠܚܘܕ ܚܣܝܪܐ ܡܥܕܪ̈ܢܝܐ ܕܬܗܘܐ ܠܐܕܡ.
In the world, a royal city, the Creator placed his image, and by a visible image, he made known the power of his hidden (nature). In constituting it, the Creator endowed creation with his image, so that through love toward Adam, they might imitate his own love. By the spiritual soul, he made him a close relative of the angels, and by assembling members in him, he aggregated him to the mute natures. "The Lord God modeled Adam from the dust of the soil, and he breathed into him a breath of life, and made him living and rational." "The Lord God looked: and behold, everything he made was finished; Eve alone was missing, who would be a help for Adam."


[p. 610] {#po.34.p610}
ܐܪ̈ܡܝ ܡܪܝܐ ܐܠܗܐ ܫܠܝܐ ܥܠ ܐܕܡ ܘܕܡܼܟ ܘܢܣܼܒ ܐܠܥܐ ܡ̣ܢ ܐܠܥܘ̈ܗܝ ܘܬܩܢ ܠܐܢܬܬܐ ܒܕܡܘܬܗ. ܫܩܼܠ ܗܕܡܐ ܡ̣ܢ ܐܕܡ ܘܥܒܼܕ ܐܢܫܐ ܐܝܟ ܐܕܡ ܘܡܠܝ ܠܐܕܡ ܗܕܡܘ̈ܗܝ ܕܠܐ ܢܗܘܐ ܠܗ ܚܣܝܪܐ.
The Lord God made a (deep) sleep fall upon Adam, and he slept; and he took a rib, from his ribs, and constituted a woman in his likeness. He removed a member from Adam, and made a human (being) like Adam, and he completed the members of Adam, for fear that he might lack any.
ܚܼܙܐ ܐܕܡ ܬܗܪܐ ܚܕܬܐ ܐܢܫܐ ܕܗܼܘܐ ܡ̣ܢ ܐܢܫܐ ܕܡܫܡܠܝܐ ܗܼܘܐ ܒܟܠܡܕܡ ܒܦܓܪ̈ܐ ܘܢܦܫܐ ܐܟܘܬܗ. ܐܪ̈ܚܫ ܒܗ ܕܝܢ ܥܒܘܕܐ ܓܠܝܢܐ ܐܝܟ ܕܡܨܐ ܗܼܘܐ ܘܥܒܕܗ ܕܢܕܥ ܬܘܩܢܗ̇ ܕܚܘܐ ܡܢܬܐ ܗ݈ܝ ܡ̣ܢ ܒܣܪ̈ܗ.
Adam saw a new wonder: a human (being) who was (made) from another, who was perfect in everything, of body and soul, similar to him. The Creator suggested the revelation (of it) to him, as he could, and he made him know that the constitution of Eve was a part of his flesh.
ܐܬܢܒܝ ܐܕܡ ܘܐܡܼܪ ܕܚܘܐ ܓܪ̈ܡܐ ܗܼܝ ܡ̣ܢ ܓܪ̈ܡܝ ܗܕܐ ܬܬܟܢܐ ܐܢܬܬܐ ܕܡܩܝܡܐ ܥܘܡܪܐ ܕܙܒܢܝܐ. ܒܬܘܩܢܗ ܕܐܕܡ ܚܘܝ ܕܠܐܕܡ ܪܫܐ ܣܐܡ ܠܗ ܘܚܘܐ ܒܛܟܣܐ ܬܪ̈ܝܢܐ ܡܡܠܝܐ ܠܓܒܪ̈ܐ ܬܫܡܫܬܗ. ܫܘܝܐ ܗ݈ܝ ܚܘܐ ܥܡ ܐܕܡ ܒܟܝܢܐ ܘܒܬܘܩܢܝܐ ܒܨܝܪܐ ܗ݈ܝ ܡܢܗ ܒܫܘܠܛܢܐ ܕܐܕܡ ܪ̈ܫܐ ܘܗܼܝ ܪ̈ܓܠܐ.
Adam prophesied and said: "Eve is the bone of my bones; this one shall be called woman, for she founds temporal existence." In constituting Adam, he showed that he established him as the head, and that Eve, in the second rank, would accomplish the service of man. Eve, by nature and by constitution, is the equal of Adam; (but) she is inferior to him in power, for Adam is the head, and she, the feet.


[p. 612] {#po.34.p612}
ܠܒܪ ܡ̣ܢ ܥܘܡܪܐ ܕܦܪ̈ܕܝܣܐ ܬܩܢ ܐܢܘܢ ܒܪ̈ܘܝܐ ܘܒܐܬܪ̈ܐ ܕܥܕܢ ܣܡ ܐܢܘܢ ܐܝܟܐ ܕܕܢܚܝܢ ܢܗܝܪ̈ܐ.
The Creator constituted them outside the dwelling of Paradise and placed them in the place of Eden, where the stars rise.
ܕܒܼܪ ܠܡ ܡܪܝܐ ܐܠܗܐ ܠܐܕܡ ܨܠܡܐ ܕܐܝܩܪܗ ܘܣܡܗ ܒܦܪ̈ܕܝܣܐ ܕܥܕܢ ܕܢܗܘܐ ܒܓܘܗ ܝܪ̈ܘܬܐ.
The Lord God led Adam, the image of his glory, and established him in the paradise of Eden to be its heir within.
ܣܡ ܠܗ ܕܝܢ ܐܦ ܦܘܩܕܢܐ ܕܚܿܫܚ ܗܼܘܐ ܠܗ ܒܗ̇ܘ ܙܒܢܝܐ ܕܢܕܥ ܚܝܠܐ ܕܟܝܢܗ ܘܢܐܠܦ ܫܘܠܛܢ ܥܒܘܕܐ.
He also fixed a commandment for him, which was useful at that moment, so that he might know the power of his nature and be informed of the sovereignty of the Creator.
ܦܼܩܕ ܠܡ ܡܪܝܐ ܠܐܕܡ ܘܣܡ ܠܗ ܬܚܘܡܐ ܒܐܝܠܢܐ ܕܒܝܕ ܗܕܐ ܬܬܝܕܥ ܦܪ̈ܘܫܘܬܐ ܕܟܝܢܗ. ܟܠܗܘܢ ܦܐܪ̈ܐ ܕܦܪ̈ܕܝܣܐ ܠܒܘܣܡܟ ܝܗܿܒܬ ܐܢܘܢ ܡ̣ܢ ܚܕ ܒܠܚܘܕ ܠܐ ܬܐܟܘܠ ܕܡܘܬܐ ܡܘܠܕ ܡ̣ܢ ܦܐܪ̈ܘܗܝ. ܐܢ ܕܝܢ ܬܛܪ ܦܘܩܕܢܝ̈ ܘܠܐ ܬܡܪ̈ܚ ܥܠ ܐܝܠܢܐ ܦܐܪ̈ܐ ܕܚܝ̈ܐ ܐܬܠ ܠܟ ܡܐ ܕܒܩܝܬܗ̇ ܠܚܐܪܘܬܟ. ܠܬܪ̈ܝܗܘܢ ܕܝܢ ܫܘܝܐܝܬ ܣܡܗ ܠܗܢܐ ܢܡܘܣܐ ܕܠܐ ܢܬܥܕܠܘܢ ܒܚܕܕ̈ܐ ܡܐ ܕܥܒܪ̈ܝܢ ܥܠ ܦܘܩܕܢܐ.
"The Lord gave an order to Adam and fixed a limit for him with a tree, so that by means of it, his natural discernment might be known (to him)." "All the fruits of Paradise, I have given (to you) for your enjoyment; of one you shall not eat, for by its fruits it begets death!" "If you keep my commandments, and do not venture to the tree, I will give you the fruit of life, when you have tested your freedom." For both of them equally he established this law, so that they would not accuse one another when they transgressed the commandment.

### The original mortality of the first man
ܐܘ ܢܒܥܐ ܕܟܠ ܚܟ̈ܡܬܐ ܕܝ̇ܕܥ ܟܠ ܥܕܠܐ ܢܗܘܐ ܕܟܕ ܝܕܥ ܠܗ ܠܬܘܩܢܗ ܣܐܡ ܠܗ ܐܝܟ ܠܐ ܝܕܥ. ܒܥܬܐ ܕܗܠܝܢ ܓܕܪܓܬܢܝ ܕܐܬܢܐ ܥܠܬܗ ܕܐܝܠܢܐ ܕܐܝܟܢ ܡܫܟܚ ܫܬܝܩܐ ܕܢܗܘܐ ܝ̇ܕܥ ܚܟ̈ܡܬܐ. ܘܐܢ ܝ̇ܕܥ ܗܼܘܐ ܕܠܐ ܛܪ ܢܡܘܣܐ ܕܣܐܡ ܗܼܘܐ ܠܗ ܥܠ ܡܶܢ ܥܒܕܗ ܡܝܘܬܐ ܠܡܝܘܬܐ ܕܠܐ ܒܨܒܝܢܗ. ܘܐܢ ܠܘ ܡܝܘܬܐ ܒܪܝܗܝ ܗܼܘܐ ܠܐܕܡ ܪ̈ܫܗ ܕܟܝܢܢ ܗܘ ܝܕܘܥܐ ܕܟܠ ܝܕܥ ܥܠ ܡܢ ܠܐ ܟܠܝܗܝ ܡ̣ܢ ܗܕܐ. ܫܡܥܐ ܕܡܠܬܐ ܕܩܪ̈ܝܢܐ ܕܘܕ ܣܓܝ ܠܦܫܝܛ̈ܐ ܕܥܒܪ ܦܘܩܕܢܗ ܕܐܕܡ ܥܒܕܗ ܠܓܢܣܢ ܡܝܘܬܐ. ܘܐܢܗܘ ܕܣܟܠܘܬܗ ܕܐܕܡ ܚܝܒܬ ܠܐܕܡ ܘܠܝܠܕܘ̈ܗܝ ܗܘ ܝܕܘܥܐ ܕܟܠ ܝܕܥ ܥܠ ܡܢܐ ܥܒܪܬܗ ܗܕܐ. ܠܡܿܢ ܬܩܢ ܗܼܘܐ ܒܪ̈ܝܬܐ ܕܚܫ̈ܚܢ ܣܓܝ ܠܡܝܘܬ̈ܐ ܘܥܠ ܡܿܢ ܕܟܪ̈ܐ ܘܢܩ̈ܒܬܐ ܒܟܝܢܐ ܠܐܡܝܢܘܬܐ. ܚܣ ܕܝܢ ܠܐ ܦܐܝܐ ܕܢܐܡܪ ܗܕܐ ܠܨܒܝܢ ܒܪܘܝܐ ܕܠܐ ܝ̇ܕܥ ܗܼܘܐ ܕܚܛܝܢܢ ܘܪ̈ܘܓܙܐ ܫܚܠܦ ܨܒܝܢܗ. ܝܕܥ ܗܼܘܐ ܟܕ ܡܬܩܢ ܠܢ ܕܡܝܘ̈ܬܐ ܥܒܕ ܗܼܘܐ ܠܢ ܘܣܗܕܐ ܒܪ̈ܝܬܗ ܠܝܕܥܬܗ ܕܠܡܝܘ̈ܬܐ ܐܬܒܪܝܬ.
O source of all wisdoms, who knowest all things before they are, who, while knowing thy very creation, establishest it as if thou didst not know it! The search for these things has pushed me to restate the reason (for being) of the tree: how can a silent (being) be instructed in wisdoms? And if (God) knew that (man) would not observe the law that He had fixed for him, why did He make (man) mortal against his will? And if He had not created Adam mortal, the head of our nature, why did the Knower, who knows everything, not prevent him from it? The hearing of the word of the narrative has greatly troubled the simple: the transgression of the commandment by Adam has made our race mortal! And if the fault of Adam condemned Adam and his children, why did this one overcome the Knower who knew everything? Why had He constituted creatures that (would be) very useful for mortals, and why males and females by nature, for perpetuation? It is not fitting, certainly not, that we say this of the will of the Creator: He did not know that we would sin and anger changed his will. He knew, in constituting us, that He was making us mortal; and his creation testifies that He knew it had been created for mortals.


[p. 614] {#po.34.p614}

### The role of the tree of the knowledge of good and evil
ܡܠܝ̈ܠܐ ܘܦܪ̈ܘܫܐ ܥܒ̣ܕܢ ܘܐܣܢ ܘܡܠܢ ܚܟ̈ܡܬܗ ܘܨܒܐ ܕܢܝܬܐ ܒܢܣܝܢܐ ܠܓܠܝܘܬܐ ܦܪ̈ܘܫܘܬܢ.
He made us (endowed) with reason and discernment, and He accumulated (in us) his wisdoms and we (in it) and He wanted to bring our discernment to be revealed through a test
ܓܒܝܠ ܗܼܘܐ ܐܕܡ ܘܡܨܒܬ ܒܦܓܪܐ ܘܢܦܫܐ ܫܘܝܐܝܬ ܘܟܣܝܐ ܗܼܘܬ ܦܪ̈ܘܫܘܬܗ ܥܕܡܐ ܠܥܒܪ ܦܘܩܕܢܐ. ܗܘܼܐ ܦܘܩܕܢܐ ܐܝܟ ܟܘܪ̈ܐ ܘܐܥܠܗ ܠܐܕܡ ܠܒܘܚܪ̈ܢܐ ܘܐܬܓܠܝܬ ܦܪܘܫܘܬܗ ܕܠܘ ܚܣܝܪ̈ܐ ܗܼܘ ܒܟܝܢܗ. ܩܼܪܝܗܝ ܐܝܠܢܐ ܕܝܕܥܬܐ ܟܕ ܚܪ̈ܫܐ ܗܼܘܐ ܒܟܝܢܗ ܕܝܕܥܬܐ ܕܟܣܝܐ ܒܐܕܡ ܒܦܐܪܐ ܕܡܢܗ ܐܬܓܠܝܬ. ܕܗܒܐ ܗܼܘܐ ܫܚܝܡܐ ܐܕܡ ܩܕܡ ܡܐܟܘܠܬܗ ܕܐܝܠܢܐ ܘܡ̣ܢ ܒܬܪ ܕܐܟܼܠ ܦܐܪܐ ܚܘܝ ܫܘܦܪ̈ܐ ܕܨܘܪ̈ܬܗ.
Adam was fashioned and adorned, both in (his) body and in (his) soul, but his discernment was hidden until the transgression of the commandment. The commandment was like a crucible, and (God) made Adam enter into a test, and his discernment appeared, which was not lacking in his nature. He called it "tree of knowledge," although it is mute by its nature, for the knowledge, hidden in Adam, was manifested by the fruit that (comes) from it. Adam was ordinary gold, before the eating of the tree, but after he had eaten the fruit, he showed the beauty of his features.
ܗܼܘܐ ܦܐܪ̈ܐ ܒܕܡܘܬ ܟܘܪܐ ܘܐܕܡ ܒܓܘܗ ܐܝܟ ܕܗܒܐ ܘܢܦܼܚ ܒܗ ܚܝܠ ܡܐܟܘܠܬܐ ܘܩܒܠ ܛܥܡܐ ܕܝܕܥܬܐ. ܬܘܩܢܐ ܕܥܠܡܐ ܕܥܬܝܕ ܒܐܝܠܢ ܚܝ̈ܐ ܒܕܩ ܠܢ ܘܣܡܗ ܒܕܘܟܬ ܦܘܪ̈ܥܢܐ ܠܡܝܬܪ̈ܘܬܐ ܕܨܒܝܢܗ. ܕ̈ܓܬܐ ܕܦܐܪ̈ܐ ܕܐܝܠܢܐ ܓܙܲܓܬ̣ܼ ܠܫܒܪ̈ܘܬܗ ܕܐܕܡ ܕܡܐ ܕܐܟܠܬܼ ܢܦܫܗ ܡܢܗ ܡܚܕܬ ܠܗ ܦܐܪ̈ܐ ܕܚܝ̈ܐ. ܒܫܡܥܐ ܕܬܪ̈ܝܢ ܐܝܠܢ̈ܐ ܥܠܡ̈ܐ ܬܪ̈ܝܗܘܢ ܐܡܬܠ ܠܢ ܘܐܝܟ ܕܠܫܒܪ̈ܐ ܒܝܕ ܐܪ̈ܙܐ ܨܒ̣ܐ ܕܢܒܕܩ ܟܣܝܘܬܗ.
The fruit was like a crucible, and Adam is in it like gold, and He breathed into him the strength to eat, and (Adam) received the taste of knowledge By the tree of life, (God) announced to us the constitution of the world to come; and in his very good will, He placed it in the place of retribution. The desire for the fruit of the tree pressed the young Adam: when he himself has eaten of it, the fruit of life will renew him. By the narrative of the two trees, (God) has figured for us the two worlds; and as to children, He wanted to reveal his hidden (nature) by means of a symbol.
ܒܐܝܠܢܐ ܕܝܢ ܕܝܕܥܬܐ ܒܥܘܬܪ̈ܐ ܐܪ̈ܥܢܐ ܐܣܪܗ ܘܒܐܝܠܢ ܚܝ̈ܐ ܒܕܩ ܠܗ ܥܠ ܗܿܘ ܥܘܬܪܐ ܫܡܝܢܐ. ܠܐ ܡܨܐ ܗܼܘܐ ܕܢܐܠܦ ܗܕܐ ܐܕܡ ܫܒܪܐ ܒܗܿܘ ܙܒܢܐ ܘܥܠ ܗܿܝ ܓܙ̈ܓܗ ܒܐܝܠܢܐ ܕܢܗܘܐ ܣܦܿܩ ܠܝܕܥܬܐ. ܝܿܕܥ ܗܼܘܐ ܒܟܠܗܝܢ ܕܐܕܡ ܗܽܘ ܕܒܪ̈ܝܗܝ ܠܐܕܡ ܘܠܟܠ ܘܒܬܘܩܢܗ ܕܡܝܘܬܐ ܨܒ̣ܐ ܕܢܒܕܩ ܚܟܡܬܗ. ܬܚܡܗ ܡ̣ܢ ܚܕ ܐܝܠܢܐ ܘܓܙ̈ܓܗ ܠܪ̈ܓܬܐ ܕܐܚܪܢܐ ܘܕܢܐܟܘܠ ܘܕܠܐ ܢܐܟܘܠ ܒܨܒܝܢ ܐܕܡ ܣܝܡܐ ܗܼܘܬ.
By the tree of knowledge, He bound (Adam) to earthly wealth, and by the tree of life, He announced to him heavenly wealth. The young Adam, at that moment, could not be informed of it, and that is why He pushed him, through the tree, to become capable of knowing He who created Adam and all things, knew everything about Adam, and in constituting the mortal, He wanted to announce his sciences. He forbade him one tree, and pushed him to desire the other, and (that) was placed in the will of Adam, to eat or not to eat.

### The installation in Paradise
ܒܥܘܡܪܐ ܕܥܕܢ ܣܡ ܐܢܘܢ ܠܦܫܝܛ̈ܐ ܘܝܩܪ ܐܢܘܢ ܘܫܥܒܕ ܟܠ ܕܗܼܘܐ ܡܢܗ ܠܫܘܠܛܢܐ ܕܨܠܡܗ ܕܐܕܡ. ܒܪܐ ܠܡ ܡܪ̈ܝܐ ܐܠܗܐ ܠܒܥܝܪ̈ܐ ܘܠܚܝܘ̈ܬܐ ܘܐܥܼܒܪ ܐܢܘܢ ܩܕܡ ܐܕܡ ܕܢܩܪ̈ܐ ܫܡܗ̈ܐ ܠܩܢܝܢܘ̈ܗܝ. ܘܝܗܼܒ ܒܗ ܚܝܠܐ ܥܒܘܕܗ ܘܩܼܪ̈ܐ ܫܡܗ̈ܐ ܟܡܐ ܕܣܦܼܩ ܟܕ ܡܿܠܦ ܠܗ ܒܝܕ ܗܕܐ ܕܡܪܐ ܕܟܠ ܕܗܼܘܐ ܣܡܗ.
He established the simple ones in the dwelling of Eden, and He honored them; and He subjected everything that existed through Him under the dominion of Adam, (his) image. "The Lord God created the domestic and wild animals; and He made them pass before Adam, so that he might give names to his possessions. And his creator conferred power upon him and he gave names, as much as he could, teaching him thereby that He was establishing him master of everything that existed.


[p. 616] {#po.34.p616}
ܬܗܖܪ̈ܐ ܗܼܘ ܨܠܡܗ ܕܒܪ̈ܘܝܐ ܕܟܡܐ ܡܝܬܪ ܬܘܩܢܗ ܕܡܪܘܬܗ ܥܠ ܟܠ ܡܬܚܬܼ ܘܠܗ ܐܫܬܥܒܕܘ ܟܠ ܕܗܘܘ. ܒܗ ܐܣܝܪ̈ܝܢ ܫܘܝܐܝܬ ܚܝ̈ܐ ܚܪ̈ܫܐ ܘܡܠܝܠ̈ܐ ܘܒܐܚܝܢܘܬܘܢ ܕܠܘܬܗ ܡܙܝܚܝܢ ܠܗ ܐܝܟ ܕܠܡܠܟܐ. ܪܘܡܐ ܘܥܘܡܩܐ ܠܕܘܝܪ̈ܘܗܝ ܝܡܐ ܘܝܒܫܐ ܠܦܘܪ̈ܢܣܗ ܪ̈ܘܚܐ ܘܡܫܒ̈ܐ ܠܚܝܘܬܗ ܩܝܛܐ ܘܣܬܘܐ ܠܢܝܚܗ. ܦܨܝ̈ܚܢ ܗܘܼܝ ܒܗ ܒܪ̈ܝܬܐ ܘܫܡ̈ܥܢ ܠܗ ܐܝܟ ܕܠܡܪ̈ܐ ܘܡܕܝܠܝܢ ܗܼܘܘ ܡܠܐܟ̈ܐ ܒܬܫܡܫܬܗ ܟܕ ܚܕܝܢ ܗܼܘܘ. ܒܚܘܒܐ ܠܘܬܗ ܟܢܫܝܢ ܗܼܘܘ ܟܝܢ̈ܐ ܚܪ̈ܫܐ ܘܡܠܝ̈ܠܐ ܘܚܕܝܢ ܗܼܘܘ ܕܡܫܘܬܦ ܗܼܘ ܟܝܢܐ ܕܟܠܗܘܢ ܒܐܝܩܪܗ.
The image of the Creator is admirable, so remarkable is his constitution, so that his mastery extended over all things, and everything that existed was subjected to him. The living (beings), mute and endowed with reason, both, are linked to him, and because of their affinity with him, they honor him as a king. He has for dwellings the (world) above and below, for his sustenance, the sea and the dry land; for his life, the winds and the breezes; for his rest, summer and winter. The creatures rejoiced in him, and listened to him as a master; and the angels went about, joyful, in his service. The mute and rational natures gathered with love near him, and rejoiced that their nature, all of them, was associated with his glory.

### The jealousy of Satan
ܚܕ ܒܠܚܘܕ ܠܐ ܟܼܕܢ ܢܦܫܗ ܒܡܪ̈ܘܬܐ ܕܚܘܒܗ ܕܐܕܡ ܘܠܐ ܒܣܡܬܼ ܠܗ ܐܘܝܘܬܐ ܕܒܓܐܪܐ ܒܠܥ ܕܚܣܡܐ. ܠܐ ܗܼܘܐ ܩܛܝܪ̈ܐ ܕܐܚܪ̈ܢܐ ܥܼܨܝܗܝ ܗܼܘܐ ܕܢܗܘܐ ܕܠܩܘܒܠܐ ܕܒܟܝܢܗ ܛܒܐ ܒܪ̈ܐ ܗܼܘܐ ܐܝܟ ܡܠܐܟ̈ܐ ܒܢܝ̈ ܬܓܡܗ. ܠܐ ܗܘܬ ܥܠܬܐ ܠܚܣܡܗ ܘܠܐ ܐܬܪܐ ܠܡܪ̈ܝܪܘܬܗ ܐܠܐ ܪܒܘܬܗ ܕܐܕܡ ܕܚܼܙܝܗܝ ܕܡ̣ܢ ܟܠ ܡܬܝܩܪ. ܕܠܚܬܼܗ ܡܪܬܗ ܠܚܣܡܐ ܘܗܼܘܐ ܟܠܗ ܕܠܩܘܒܠܐ ܘܐܬܚܡܬ ܥܠ ܒܪ̈ܘܝܐ ܒܗ̇ܝ ܕܝܩܪ ܨܠܡܗ ܕܐܕܡ. ܗܼܘ ܒܚܘܫܒܘ̈ܗܝ ܝܩܕ ܗܼܘܐ ܒܙ̈ܓܬܐ ܒܝܫܬܐ ܕܕܠܩܬܼ ܒܗ ܘܟܠ ܦܘܪ̈ܣܝܢ ܡܨܛܢܥ ܗܼܘܐ ܕܢܝܬܐ ܠܓܠܝܐ ܨܢܥܬܗ. ܙ̈ܓܬܗ ܣܢܝܬܐ ܗܕܐ ܗܼܘܬ ܕܥܠ ܡܢܐ ܐܕܡ ܡܬܝܩܪ ܘܥܠ ܡܢܐ ܒܚܘܒܗ ܟܕܝܢܝܢ ܟܝܢ̈ܐ ܚܪ̈ܫܐ ܘܡܠܝܠ̈ܐ. ܠܝ ܕܐܝܬܝ ܪܝܫܐ ܕܐܐܪ ܘܟܠܗܘܢ ܬܓܡ̈ܐ ܪ̈ܘܚܢܐ ܐܦܠܐ ܒܡܠܬܐ ܝܩܪ ܠܢ ܟܕ ܡܬܩܢ ܠܗ ܠܟܝܢܢ.
Only one did not submit to the mastery of love for Adam; and the agreement did not please him, for he was struck by an arrow of jealousy. It was not the constraint of others that forced him to become the Adversary, for he had been created good by nature like the angels his companions. There was no reason for his jealousy, nor place for his bitterness, if not the greatness of Adam, whom he saw honored by all. His bitterness troubled the jealous one, and he became the total adversary, and he grew hot against the Creator because He had honored Adam (his) image. He, in his thoughts, burned with an evil desire that consumed him, and he imagined all the means to manifest his machinations. Such was his odious desire; why is Adam honored, and why are the mute and rational natures subjected to his love? I, the Prince of the air and all the spiritual legions, He did not even honor us with a word, in constituting our nature.
ܒܬܘܩܢܗ ܕܗܢܐ ܫܝܛܐ ܥܦܪܐ ܕܚܝܚܐ ܕܐܪ̈ܥܐ ܢܥܒܕ ܠܡ ܐܢܫܐ ܒܨܠܡܢ ܘܢܗܘܐ ܝܩܝܪ̈ܐ ܒܕܡܘܬܢ.
(But) in constituting this vile fine dust of the ground, (He said): "Let us make man in our image, and let him be honored in our likeness!"
ܥܒܕܗ ܐܝܟ ܡܪ̈ܐ ܒܐܪ̈ܥܐ ܘܩܪܝܗܝ ܨܠܡܐ ܕܟܣܝܘܬܗ ܘܡܬܝܩܪ ܡ̣ܢ ܟܠ ܕܗܘܘ ܐܝܟ ܒܪ̈ܘܝܐ ܒܒܪܝܬܗ.
He made him like a master on earth, and called him the image of his hidden (nature); and he is honored by everything that exists, like the Creator by his creation.
ܐܠܐ ܫܥܒܕ ܠܗ ܠܐܝܩܪܗ ܫܡܝܢ̈ܐ ܘܐܪ̈ܥܢܝܐ... ܓܒܝܠ ܡ̣ܢ ܐܪܥܐ ܢܐ ܡܝܘܬܐ ܘܫܝܛ ܒܟܝܢܗ ܐܝܟ ܐܪܥܐ
But He has subjected the heavenly and earthly (beings) to honor him.
ܘܫܘܠܛܢܐ ܕܡܠܬܗ ܡܡܠܟ ܥܠ ܥܠܝ̈ܐ ܘܬܚܬܝ̈ܐ. ܣܦܬ ܢܘܪܐ ܕܚܣܡܗ ܘܕܠܩ ܪ̈ܘܓܙܐ ܕܛܢܢܗ ܘܒܥܐ ܕܢܕܝܩ ܨܐܕܝܗܘܢ ܘܢܝܬܐ ܠܓܠܝܐ ܚܘܫܒܘ̈ܗܝ.
The mortal was fashioned from earth, and like the earth, he is vile by his nature, but the authority of his word reigns over the superior and inferior (beings). The fire of his jealousy burned, and the anger of his envy flamed, and he sought to appear before them and to manifest his thoughts.


[p. 618] {#po.34.p618}
ܠܐ ܚܘܝ ܠܗܘܢ ܨܢܥܬܗ ܐܝܟ ܕܡܥܕ ܕܢܥܒܕ ܗܫܐ ܕܠܐ ܫܠܝܛ ܗܼܘܐ ܒܗ̇ܘ ܙܒܢܐ ܕܢܬܕܡܐ ܘܐ ܒܐܣܟܝܡ ܐܢܫܐ.
He did not show them his machinations, as he is accustomed to doing currently, for he did not have the power at that moment to imitate a human form.

### The seduction of the serpent
ܚܘܝܐ ܥܒܕ ܠܗ ܟܘܬܝܢܐ ܘܠܒܫ ܘܟܣܝ ܣܢܝܘܬܗ ܕܠܐ ܢܬܪ̈ܪܗܒܘܢ ܡ̣ܢ ܫܠܝܐ ܒܚܙܬܐ ܕܠܐ ܡܥܕܝܢ ܒܗܿ.
He made himself a tunic from the serpent, put (it) on and hid his hatred (in it), for fear that they might suddenly be frightened by a spectacle to which they were not accustomed.
ܒܗܝܠܝܢ ܘܫܠܝܢ ܬܡܝ̈ܡܐ ܘܢܛܪ̈ܝܢ ܛܟܣܗ ܕܥܒܘܕܐ ܘܦܐܝܐ ܚܙܬܗܘܢ ܠܚܕ̈ܕܐ ܒܢܟܦܘܬܐ ܕܪ̈ܥܝܢܝܗܘܢ.
The innocent ones were calm and tranquil, and maintained the order of the Creator, and it (was) pleasant for them to look at one another, in the purity of their consciences.
ܫܪܝ ܡܡܠܠ ܘܐ ܙܐܦܢܐ ܒܟܕܢܪ̈ܐ ܕܒܣܪܐ ܒܩܠܐ ܕܡܢܐ ܗܝ ܥܠܬܐ ܕܟܠܝܢ ܐܢܬܘܢ ܡ̣ܢ ܡܐܟܘܠܬܐ ܕܟܠ ܦܐܪ̈ܝܢ.
The dissimulator began to speak, with a carnal cithara, the voice: "What is the reason for which it has been forbidden to you to eat of all the fruits?"
ܠܗܘܢ ܫܐܠ ܗܼܘܐ ܩܕܡܝܬ ܐܝܟ ܕܢܐܠܦ ܫܪܝܪܐܝܬ ܕܡܦܣ ܗܼܘܐ ܒܗ ܒܟܝܢܗ ܕܠܐ ܡܨܐ ܝܕܥ ܟܣܝ̈ܬܐ.
He questioned them first, as if to inform himself truly, for he knew from himself that he could not know the hidden (things).
ܠܐ ܫܡܝܥ ܗܼܘܐ ܠܗ ܦܘܩܕܢܐ ܕܣܡ ܒܪ̈ܘܝܐ ܠܒܝܬ ܐܕܡ ܥܠ ܗܕܐ ܫܐܠ ܐܢܘܢ ܕܢܐܠܦ ܡܠܬܐ ܘܟܢ ܢܛܥܐ.
He had not heard the commandment that the Creator had fixed at the Adamic hearth, so he questioned them, in order to be informed of the word, and then to lead (them) astray.
ܚܘܝܐ ܠܚܘܐ ܐܡܪ̈ ܗܼܘܐ ܨܢܝܥܐ ܠܫܦܝܬ ܪ̈ܥܝܢܐ ܕܕܠܡܐ ܘܐ ܒܪ̈ܘܝܐ ܟܠܟܘܢ ܡ̣ܢ ܦܐܪ̈ܐ ܕܕ̈ܓܝܓܐ ܕܥܕܢ.
The serpent had said to Eve, the cunning one to the simple-minded: "Has the Creator forbidden you the delicious fruits of Eden?"
ܫܒܪ̈ܬܐ ܠܢܟܝܠܐ ܐܡܪܬ ܕܠܐ ܟܠܝܢܢ ܡ̣ܢ ܐܝܠܢ̈ܐ ܡ̣ܢ ܚܕ ܐܝܠܢܐ ܬܚܡܢ ܕܡܘܬܐ ܟܣܐ ܒܗ̇ ܒܡܐܟܘܠܬܗ.
The young (woman) said to the seducer: "The trees are not forbidden to us, (but) He has prohibited one tree to us, for death is hidden in the very fact of eating of it."
ܐܫܟܚ ܐܬܪ̈ܐ ܙܐܦܢܐ ܘܫܪܝ ܕܢܙܪ̈ܘܥ ܙܝܙܢܘ̈ܗܝ ܘܐܘܥܝ ܦܐܪܐ ܐ ܕܨܒܝܢܗ ܕܓܠܘܬܐ ܒܫܘܪ̈ܝܐ.
The dissimulator took occasion from it and began to sow his tares, and he produced the fruit of his will, the lie at the beginning.
ܠܐ ܠܡ ܡܘܬܐ ܡܝܬܝܢ ܐܢܬܘܢ ܚܘܝܐ ܠܚܘܐ ܡܛܥܐ ܗܼܘܐ ܐܝܟ ܐܠܗ̈ܐ ܗܘܝܢ ܐܢܬܘܢ ܝܕܘܥ̈ܐ ܕܣܬܝܪ̈ܬܐ.
"You will not die of death," the serpent was leading Eve astray, "you will be like gods, knowing the hidden (things)."
ܐܘܗ ܡ̣ܢ ܣܢܐܐ ܕܐܢܫܘܬܐ ܕܕܠܚܗ ܠܫܝܢܐ ܕܒܪ̈ܝܬܐ ܕܠܐ ܣܦܩܬ ܕܐܛܥܝ ܒܠܚܘܕ ܐܠܐ ܕܐܒܥ ܐܠܗ̈ܐ.
Oh! hatred of humanity that troubled the peace of creation, for it is not enough for him only to have led astray, but to produce gods!
ܗܪ̈ܟܐ ܕܢܚܬ ܒܝܫܘܬܗ ܘܗܼܘ ܒܡܠܘ̈ܗܝ ܩܛܪܓ ܢܦܫܗ ܕܚܣܡ ܒܐܕܡ ܘܒܝܠܕܘ̈ܗܝ ܘܐܥܠ ܐܝܬܝ̈ܐ ܣܓܝ̈ܐܐ.
Here appeared his perversity, and it is by his words that he accused himself of being jealous of Adam and his children, and of having introduced numerous Beings:
ܐܠܗ̈ܐ ܠܡ ܗܘܝܢ ܐܢܬܘܢ ܘܠܘ ܘܐ ܚܕ ܐܠܗܐ ܒܠܚܘܕ ܘܥܠ ܗܕܐ ܟܠܟܘܢ ܡܢܗ ܕܐܝܠܢܐ ܘܩܪܝܗܝ ܡܘܬܐ.
"You will be gods, and there (will) not be only one unique God, and that is why He has forbidden you the tree and called it Death.
ܩܪ̈ܘܒܘ ܐܬܒܣܣܡܘ ܡ̣ܢ ܦܐܪ̈ܘܗܝ ܘܗܐ ܡܬܦܬܚ̈ܢ ܥܝܢܝ̈ܟܘܢ ܕܚܝ̈ܐ ܟܣܝܢ ܒܗ̇ ܒܡܐܟܘܠܬܐ ܕܬܥܒܕ ܐܢܫܐ ܠܐ ܐܠܗ̈ܐ.
Approach, delight in its fruits, and behold your eyes will open, for life is hidden in this very eating, which will make men into gods."

### The transgression of the commandment
ܚܙܬ ܠܡ ܐܢܬܬܐ ܠܐܝܠܢܐ ܕܕ̈ܓܬܐ ܗܼܘ ܟܠܗ ܘܚܙܬܐ ܗܼܘ ܘܢܣܒܬ ܡ̣ܢ ܦܐܪ̈ܘܗܝ ܘܐܟܠܬ ܘܝܗܒܬ ܥܡܗ̇ ܠܫܘܬܦܗ̇.
"The woman saw that the tree was entirely (an object) of desire and of sight; and she took of its fruits, ate, and gave to her companion as well as to herself.


[p. 620] {#po.34.p620}
ܗܝ ܠܘܩܕܡ ܐܡܼܖ ܕܐܟܠܬܼ ܕܬܩܛܘܦ ܠܗ̇ ܫܡ ܐܠܗܐ ܒܗ̇ܝ ܕܐܬܕܒܪܬ ܠܦܝܣܐ ܕܐܝܟ ܐܠܗ̈ܐ ܗ̇ܘܝܢ ܐܢܬܘܢ. ܠܒܥܠܗܿ ܐܚܪܖܝܬ ܝܗܒܬܼ ܠܗ ܕܗܝ ܠܘܩܕܡ ܬܗܘܐ ܐܠܗ ܕܣܒܪ̈ܐ ܣܪ̈ܝܩܐ ܐܛܥܝ ܒܗ̇ܿ ܕܫܘܘܕܝܗ ܕܐܟܠܩܪ̈ܨܐ.
(Moses) says that it is she who ate first, in order to obtain for herself the name of god, because she had been led to believe: "You will be like gods." To her husband she gave some then, so that she, before (him), would be god; for the vain hope of the promise of the slanderer had led her astray.

### The consequences of sin
ܐܬܦܼܬܚ ܠܡ ܥܝܢܝ̈ܗܘܢ ܒܡܐܟܘܠܬܐ ܕܦܐܪ̈ܐ ܘܚܙܘ ܘܐܿܬܥܕܠܘ ܒܩܢܘܡܝ̈ܗܘܢ ܚܙܬܐ ܕܡܠܝܐ ܫܘܚܪܐ. ܠܐ ܝܕܥܝܢ ܗܼܘܘ ܟܘܐܪ̈ܐ ܩܕܡ ܡܐܟܘܠܬܐ ܕܐܝܠܢܝܐ ܘܡ̣ܢ ܕܥܒܪܘ ܥܠ ܦܘܩܕܢܐ ܚܙܘ ܕܫܠܝܚܝܢ ܘܡܦܪܣܝܢ. ܕܒܩܘ ܕܝܢ ܛܪ̈ܦܐ ܕܬܐܢ̈ܐ ܡ̣ܢ ܟܘܚܕܐ ܕܚܕ̈ܕܐ ܕܚܛܝܬܐ ܥܒܕܬܼ ܐܢܘܢ ܦܘܪ̈ܣܝܐ ܘܨܥܪܐ ܡܫܫܠܝ.
"By eating the fruit, their eyes opened and they saw; and they reproached one another in the face a spectacle full of darkness." They did not know shame, before eating of the tree, but after having transgressed the commandment, they saw that they were naked and exposed. Then they assembled fig leaves, as a result of (their) reciprocal modesty, for sin made them suddenly naked and ashamed.
ܟܣܝܐ ܗܼܘܬ ܦܪ̈ܘܫܘܬܐ ܒܪ̈ܥܝܢܝܗܘܢ ܕܦܫܝܛ̈ܐ ܘܡ̣ܢ ܕܐܟܠܘ ܡ̣ܢ ܐܝܠܝܐ ܗܘܘ ܦܪ̈ܘܫܐ ܕܘܠܝܬ̈ܐ. ܥܠ ܗܕܐ ܩܼܪܝܗܝ ܕܝܕܥܬܐ ܠܐܝܠܢܐ ܕܐܟܠܘ ܡܢܗ ܕܝܕܥܬܐ ܕܪ̈ܥܝܢܝܗܘܢ ܒܡܐܟܘܠܬܐ ܕܡܢܗ ܕܢܚܬܼ.
Discernment was hidden in their simple minds, but after having eaten of the tree, they discerned (their) obligations. That is why the tree of which they ate, He called "of knowledge," because the knowledge of their minds was manifested by eating of it.
ܠܘ ܕܝܕܥܬܐ ܝܿܗܒ ܗܼܘܐ ܕܗܕܐ ܠܐ ܩܢܐ ܒܟܝܢܗ ܐܠܐ ܕܒܥܠܬܗ ܕܝܠܗ ܝܠܦܘܗܝ ܠܥܒܪ ܦܘܩܕܢܐ. ܥܒܕܘ ܬܟܣܝܬܐ ܠܓܘܫܡܝ̈ܗܘܢ ܛܪ̈ܦܘܗܝ ܕܝܠܗ ܕܐܝܠܝܐ ܕܒܥܠܬܗ ܩܢܘ ܝܕܥܬܐ ܒܛܪ̈ܦܘܗܝ ܟܣܝܘ ܦܓܪ̈ܝܗܘܢ.
Not that it gave knowledge, for this one does not possess (it) in itself, but because of it precisely, they learned to transgress the commandment. They made a garment for their body, from the leaves of the tree itself because of which they acquired knowledge, (and) with its leaves they covered their body.

### The intervention of God
ܗܿܘ ܕܝܢ ܝܕܥ ܟܣܝ̈ܬܐ ܠܐ ܐܗܡܝ ܡ̣ܢ ܬܘܩܢܗ ܕܐܦ ܟܕ ܣܡ ܠܗ ܦܘܩܕܢܐ ܝ̇ܕܥ ܗܼܘܐ ܕܡܚܼܛܐ ܚ̇ܛܐ. ܠܦܢܝܗ ܕܝܘܡܐ ܐܫܼܡܥ ܗܼܘܐ ܩܠܐ ܕܪ̈ܓܠܘܗܝ ܒܪ̈ܘܝܐ ܟܕ ܐܬܼܐ ܕܢܬܒܥ ܐܢܘܢ ܥܝܪ̈ܬܐ ܕܥܒܪ ܦܘܩܕܢܐ. ܐܘ ܟܝܢܐ ܕܠܐ ܪܘܟܒܐ ܕܠܐ ܡܣܬܝܟ ܒܥܒ̈ܕܘܗܝ ܕܐܩܢܝ ܩܠܐ ܠܗܠܟ̈ܬܗ ܟܕ ܐܿܬܐ ܕܢܟܤܣ ܠܐܕܡ. ܐܘ ܢܒܥܐ ܕܟܠ ܚܟܡܬ̈ܐ ܕܐܚܝܕ ܣܟܐ ܕܟܠܡܕܡ ܕܡܫܐܠ ܐܝܟ ܠܐ ܝܕܥ ܕܐܝܟܐ ܡܣܬܪ ܒܪ ܒܝܬܗ. ܐܫܡܥ ܩܠܐً ܕܗܠܟ̈ܬܗ ܕܢܪܗܒ ܐܢܘܢ ܥܠ ܕܚܛܘ ܡܫܐܠ ܗܼܘܐ ܕܐܝܟܐ ܐܢܬ ܐܕܡ ܘܡܚܘܐ ܕܝܿܕܥ ܕܐܝܟܐ ܗܼܘ. ܐܝܟܐ ܐܢܬ ܐܕܡ ܐܡܼܖ ܗܼܘܐ ܥܒܘܕܐ ܠܨܠܡܐ ® ܕܐܬܩܢ ܐܝܟܐ ܐܢܬ ܝܪ̈ܬܗ ܕܦܪ̈ܕܝܣܐ ܠܡܢܐ ܡܛܫܐ ܐܢܬ ܣܘܪ̈ܚܢܝܟ. ܐܘ ܛܒܐ ܟܡܐ ܡܚܒ ܠܗ ܠܨܠܡܐ ܕܐܕܡ ܥܦܖ̈ܢܐ ܕܒܢܥܡ̈ܬܐ ܚܢܝܓܬ̈ܐ ܒܥܐ ܠܗ̇ ܠܐܒܝܕܘܬܗ.
Now He who knows hidden things did not turn away from his creation, for even when He fixed a commandment for him, He knew that he would indeed sin. At the decline of the day, the Creator made the sound of his footsteps heard, coming to claim from them vengeance for the transgressed commandment. O uncomposed nature, which cannot be limited by thy works, who conferred sound upon thy footsteps, in coming to confound Adam! O source of all wisdoms, who holdest the limit of all things, who askest, as if thou didst not know, where thy familiar is hiding! He made the sound of his footsteps heard, to frighten them because they had sinned; He was asking: "Where are you, Adam?", and showing that He knew where he was. "Where are you, Adam," said the Creator to the image that He had constituted, "where are you, heir of paradise? Why do you hide your faults?" O Good One, how thou lovest the image of Adam, (made) of dust, thou who, with plaintive accents, questionest him who is lost!


[p. 622] {#po.34.p622}
