ܒܗ ܒܬܘܩܢܗܘܢ ܡܩܬ ܗܘܢܐ ܥܝܪ̈ܐ ܕܒܗܘܢ ܠܐ ܝܗܒܝܢ ܠܗ ܕܢܫܠܐ ܘܢܫܟܬ ܡ̣ܢ ܬܫܒܘܚܬܐ. ܗܘܢܐ ܥܝܪ̈ܐ ܩܢܝܢ ܒܟܝܢܐ ܕܡܟܝܢܝܢ ܒܗ ܘܡܛܠ ܗܕܐ ܟܢܝ ܐܢܘܢ ܟܬܒܐ ܥܝܪ̈ܐ. ܥܝܪ̈ܐ ܐܢܘܢ ܕܠܐ ܕܡܟܘ ܡܡܬܘܡ ܘܠܐ ܬܘܒ ܕܡܟܝܢ ܘܒܥܝܪ̈ܘܬܗܘܢ ܡܥܝܪ̈ܝܢ ܐܢܫܐ ܠܘܬ ܝܘܠܦܢܐ. ܒܒܝܬ ܝܘܠܦܢܝܐ ܡܩܕܡܝܢ ܩܝܡܝܢ ܐܝܟ ܪ̈ܒܢܐ ܘܡܚܟܡܝܢ ܠܗ̇ ܠܫܒܪܘܬ ܐܢܫܐ ܒܝܘܠܦܢܝ̈ܗܘܢ.
The watching intellect that (is) in them scrutinizes their very creation; they do not allow it to stop nor to cease (rendering) glory. They possess a watching intellect in the nature in which they were constituted, and that is why Scripture called them "watchers." They are watchers because they have never slept and will never sleep either; and thanks to their watching, they awaken men to teach (them). Like masters, they stand at the school in advance, and instruct men in their childhood by their teachings.
ܡ̣ܢ ܗ̇ܘ ܪܒܐ ܕܚܟܡ ܐܢܘܢ ܩܢܘ ܚܟܡܬ̈ܐ ܘܠܗ̇ܘ ܢܝܫܐ ܕܪ̈ܒܢܘܬܗ ܚܢܝܢ ܟܠܫܥ. ܒܗܓܝܢ ܡܠܝܬܗ ܥܢܝܢ ܘܥܡܝܠܝܢ ܠܠܝ ܐܝܡܡ ܘܥܡܗܘܢ ܕܙܒܢ̈ܐ ܪܗܛܝܢ ܒܥܝܢ ܒܥܬ ܥܢܝܢܗ. ܥܢܝܢ ܚܘܒܐ ܐܠܗܝܐ ܪ̈ܚܝܡ ܥܠ ܥܝܪ̈ܐ ܬܘ ܢܗܘܐ ܠܗܘܢ ܚܒܪ̈ܐ ܒܗܢ̈ܓܐ ܕܐܡܝܢܝܢ ܒܗ. ܠܨܒܝܢ ܟܣܝܐ ܡܫܡܫܝܢ ܟܠܝܘܡ ܕܠܐ ܩܘܛܥܐ ܬܘ ܢܬܐܡܢ ܒܐܡܝܢܘܬܐ ܕܨܝܕ ܥܒܘܕܢ.
From this master who instructed them, they acquired the sciences, and they tend ceaselessly toward the goal of his teaching. They are occupied and work night and day meditating on his word, and they run with the times, in search of his intimacy. The intimacy of divine love pleases the angels; let us be their companions in the meditation in which they are constantly! They (are) daily in the service of the will of the hidden (God), without slackness; let us persevere with the perseverance that they (have) before our Creator!
ܚܠܦ ܦܘܪ̈ܩܢܢ ܫܚܝܩܝܢ ܘܠܐܝܢ ܗܐ ܡ̣ܢ ܕܗܘܘ ܢܥܕܪ ܐܢܘܢ ܐܦܢ ܩܠܝܠ ܒܥܡܠܐ ܕܚܝܢ̈ܢ. ܡܛܠܬܢ ܡܙܝܥܝܢ ܠܐܐܪ ܒܟܠ ܫܘܚܠܦܝ̈ܢ ܢܙܝܥ ܡܚܫܒܬܢ ܠܡܕܥ ܛܟܣܐ ܕܫܘܚܠܦܝ̈ܗܘܢ.
They expend themselves and tire themselves for our salvation since they existed; let us help them ever so little in the labor of our life! They move the atmosphere for us in all seasons; let us apply our reflection to knowing the order of their changes!
ܚܠܦܝܢ ܪܗܛܝܢ ܒܐܣܣܛܕܝܘܢ ܪܘܚܢܝܬܐ ܢܩܠܣ ܐܢܘܢ ܕܢܫܘܐ ܠܐܓܪ̈ܐ ܕܟܠܝܠܝ̈ܗܘܢ.
They run for us in the spiritual stadium; let us praise them in order to merit the reward of their crowns!
ܠܥܘܕܪ̈ܢ ܢܦܫܢ ܕܡܝܢ ܒܐܓܘܢܐ ܥܡ ܚܒܪ̈ܝܗܘܢ ܢܠܒܒ ܐܢܘܢ ܕܬܘܒ ܢܬܟܫܪ̈ܘܢ ܥܠ ܙܟܘܬܢ.
For the help of our soul, they launch themselves into combat against their companions; let us encourage them so that we may also work as diligently for our victory!


[p. 670] {#po.34.p670}
ܩܪ̈ܒܐ ܕܫܐ̈ܕܐ ܡܫܠܝܢ ܡܢܢ ܒܐܓܘܢܝ̈ܗܘܢ ܢܦܪܘܥ ܛܝܒܘ ܠܡܪܐ ܛܒܐ ܕܙܝܢ ܐܢܘܢ.
By their combats, they distance from us the war of the demons; let us give thanks to the beneficent Master who armed them!
ܠܕܝܘ̈ܐ ܪ̈ܕܦܝܢ ܡ̣ܢ ܡܫܪ̈ܝܬܢ ܕܠܐ ܢܟܘܢܢ ܢܫܒܚ ܠܫܡܐ ܕܐܣܪ̈ ܐܢܘܢ ܒܪ̈ܚܡܬ ܢܦܫܢ.
They chase the demons far from our camp so that they do not harm us; let us glorify the name that attached them to the love of our soul!


[p. 672] {#po.34.p672}

### VI
ܥܠ ܕܘܒܪ̈ܐ ܕܡܠܐܟ̈ܐ
ON THE GOVERNMENT OF THE ANGELS

### The world, object of questioning
ܥܠܡܐ ܕܙܒܢܐ ܚܙܝܬ ܟܕ ܪܗܛ ܒܐܡܝܢܘܬܐ ܘܨܒܝܬ ܕܐܠܦ ܕܡܢ ܡܪܗܛ ܠܗ̇ ܠܫܬܝܩܘܬܗ.
I saw the temporal world in its continuous course, and I wanted to learn who makes its silent (nature) run.
ܪܗܛܗ ܘܫܬܝܩܘܬܗ ܚܙܝܬ ܕܡܛܟܣ ܚܟܝܡܐܝܬ ܘܐܡܪܬ ܐܢܐ ܕܡܢ ܟܝ ܚܟܡܗ ܠܡܪ̈ܕܐ ܒܛܟܣܐ.
I saw that its silent course was ordered with wisdom; and I said: "Who then taught it to walk with order?"
ܒܫܘܚܠܦ ܙܒܢܘ̈ܗܝ ܬܗܪܬ ܣܓܝ ܕܟܡܐ ܫܚܝܩܝܢ ܘܒܥܝܬ ܕܐܕܥ ܕܡܢ ܫܚܩ ܠܗ ܘܡܢܛܪ ܠܗ.
As for the changing of its seasons, I have marveled greatly (seeing) how they are pressed; and I sought to know who presses and maintains it.
ܒܝܬ ܫܘܚܠܦܘ̈ܗܝ ܥܠܬ ܩܡܬ ܐܝܟ ܕܒܐܓܘܢܐ ܕܐܚܘܪ ܫܦܝܪ ܕܐܝܟܢ ܥܠܒܝܢ ܐܦ ܡܬܥܠܒܝܢ.
I penetrated into its variations and stood there as if in battle, to see well how they triumph and are also defeated.
ܥܡ ܡܪ̈ܕܝܬܗ ܨܒܘ ܚܘܫܒܝ̈ ܠܡܗܠܟܘ ܕܐܕܥ ܕܐܝܢܘ ܫܘܪ̈ܝ ܐܘܪ̈ܚܗ ܘܫܘܠܡ ܪܗܛܗ.
My thoughts wished to travel at the same time as it advances, in order to know what is the beginning of its path and the end of its evolution.
ܪܗܛܗ ܘܛܟܣܗ ܥܒܕܘܢܝ ܕܐܒܥܐ ܒܥܬܐ ܕܥܠܘܗܝ ܕܡܐ ܕܒܥܝܬܗ ܐܕܥ ܘܐܘܕܥ ܠܕܒܥܝܢ ܠܗ.
Its evolution and its order made me search for what concerns it, in order to know it when I have searched for it, and to make it known to those who search for it.
ܒܥܬܗ ܫܚܩܬܢܝ ܐܝܟ ܕܠܟܦܢܐ ܐܒܪ̈ܓܬ ܠܚܡܐ ܘܡܬܝܐܒ ܐܢܐ ܕܐܡܠܐ ܪ̈ܥܝܢܝ ܡ̣ܢ ܦܪ̈ܟܘܟܘܗܝ.
Searching for it has tormented me, as the desire for bread does for a hungry man, and I desire to satisfy my mind with its crumbs.
ܫܘܦܪܗ ܓܙ̈ܓܢܝ ܕܐܩܪܘܒ ܨܐܕܘܗܝ ܘܐܬܒܩܐ ܒܗ ܕܛܟ ܬܫܦܪ ܒܗ ܣܢܝܘܬ ܡܠܬܝ ܡܠܝܬ ܚܫ̈ܐ.
Its beauty incited me to approach it and to consider it, (thinking) that the unworthiness of my speech, full of passions, might perhaps please it.
ܒܡܠܐ ܕܥܠܘܗܝ ܕܓܝܓ ܐܢܐ ܕܐܗܘܐ ܚܒܪܐ ܒܫܘܦܪܗ ܘܡܛܝܒ ܐܢܐ ܕܐܝܬܐ ܠܓܠܝܐ ܙܓܬ ܪ̈ܥܝܢܝ.
By discoursing about it, I desire to become associated with its beauty, and I am ready to manifest the desire of my mind.
ܪ̈ܓܬܗ ܕܥܠܡܐ ܫܚܩܬܗ ܠܪ̈ܥܝܢܝ ܠܒܪ ܡ̣ܢ ܛܟܣܐ ܘܗܐ ܥܨܝܐ ܠܝ ܕܐܘܠܕ ܡܠܐ ܒܕܡܘܬ ܥܘܠ̈ܐ.
The desire of the world has extraordinarily troubled my mind, and behold, it forces me to give birth to discourses like newborns.
ܐܝܟ ܝܠܕܬܐ ܡܚܒܠ ܡܕܥܝ ܒܥܠܬܐ ܕܥܠܘܗܝ ܕܐܡܬܝ ܢܘܠܕ ܥܘܠ̈ܐ ܕܡܐܡܪ̈ܐ ܩܕܡ ܫܡܘܥ̈ܐ. ܥܠܬ ܥܠܡܐ ܐܝܬܝܗ̇ ܥܠܬܐ ܕܝܠܕܬ ܬܪܥܝܬܝ ܕܡܢܘ ܡܙܝܥ ܠܗ ܘܡܫܚܠܦ ܠܗ ܟܕ ܗܼܘ ܚܪ̈ܫܐ ܗܼܘ. ܡܢܘ ܡܫܚܠܦ ܩܝܛܐ ܠܣܬܘܐ ܘܣܬܘܐ ܠܩܝܛܐ ܘܡܢܘ ܗܦܟ ܩܪܝܪܘܬܗ ܠܚܡܝܡܘܬܐ.
Like one who gives birth, my mind is in labor, (seeking) the cause that concerns it, (wondering) when it will give birth to the newborns of the homily in the presence of the listeners. The cause of the world is the reason why my thought has given birth. Who is it that moves it and transforms it since it is a mute (being)? What is the cause of all cosmic phenomena? Who substitutes summer for winter and winter for summer, and who changes its coldness into heat?


[p. 674] {#po.34.p674}
ܡܶܢ ܡܕܢܚ ܠܗ ܠܐܣܦܝܪ ܫܡܫܐ ܒܥܕܢ ܨܦܪ̈ܐ ܘܡܢܘ ܡܛܲܫܐ ܦܐܝܘܬ ܢܘܗܖܪ̈ܗ ܒܥܕܢ ܪ̈ܡܫܐ. ܡܶܢ ܡܪܗܛ ܠܗ ܒܐܦܝ̈ ܕܘܡܐ ܩܠܝܠܐܝܬ ܘܡܶܢ ܐܚܝܕ ܠܗ ܕܠܐ ܢܬܪ̈ܦܐ ܘܢܦܠ ܒܐܪ̈ܥܐ. ܡܰܢ ܥܿܒܕ ܠܗ ܠܣܗܖ̈ܐ ܙܥܘܪ̈ܐ ܒܪ ܚܕ ܝܘܡܐ ܘܡܿܢ ܡܣܩ ܠܗ ܒܦܠܓܘܬ ܝܪ̈ܚܐ ܠܥܠܝܡܘܬܐ. ܡܶܢ ܡܪܡܐ ܒܗ̇ ܒܫܬܝܩܘܬܗ ܦܓܘ̈ܕܐ ܕܛܟܣܐ ܕܠܐ ܢܬܕܘܕ ܛܟܣܐ ܪܒܐ ܕܠܠܝ ܐܝܡܡ. ܡܢܘ ܡܪܗܛ ܟܘܟܒ̈ܐ ܒܛܟܣܐ ܚܕ ܒܬܪ ܚܕ ܘܡܚܘܐ ܠܗܘܢ ܫܒܝܠ ܡܪ̈ܕܝܬܐ ܕܠܐ ܡܬܦܣܩܐ. ܡܢܘ ܡܙܝܥ ܠܗ ܠܐܐܪ ܚܪ̈ܫܐ ܠܘܬ ܬܫܡܫܬܢ ܘܡܿܢ ܡܿܩܢܐ ܠܗ̇ ܠܫܬܝܩܘܬܗ ܩܠ̈ܐ ܓܫܝܡܹ̈ܐ. ܡܢܘ ܡܩܿܫܐ ܘܡܥܿܒܐ ܠܗ̈ ܠܩܛܝܢܘܬܗ ܘܡܿܢ ܡܿܛܥܢ ܠܗ ܫܦܥ̈ܐ ܕܡܝ̈ܐ ܘܪ̈ܐ̈ܣ ܒܐܪ̈ܥܐ. ܡܢܘ ܡܪ̈ܝܡ ܠܗ ܐܝܟ ܕܠܫܒܛܐ ܠܥܠ ܡܢ ܝܡܐ ܘܡܬܚܬܐ ܠܗ ܠܝܨܪܗ ܪ̈ܓܝܐ ܒܝܕ ܥܠܥܠܘ̈ܗܝ. ܡܢܘ ܡܫ̈ܠܐ ܠܟܬܝܫܘܬܗ ܡܐ ܕܡܬܒܥܖܪ ܘܡܢܘ ܦܢܐ ܘܡܒܥܪܖܪ̈ ܠܗ ܡܐ ܕܡܫܬܝܢ. ܐܝܟܐ ܕܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܡܫܒܗ ܩܫܝܐ ܡܐ ܕܠܐ ܢܫ̇ܒ ܘܡ̣ܢ ܐܝܡܟܐ ܐܿܬܐ ܡܫܫܠܝ ܒܚܐܦܐ ܩܫܝܐ. ܡܢܘ ܡܣܦ ܢܘܪܐ ܕܒܪ̈ܩܐ ܡ̣ܢ ܪܥܕܘܬܗ ܘܡܢܘ ܕܡܫܡܥ ܩܠ̈ܐ ܕܪ̈ܥܡܐ ܡ̣ܢ ܫܠܝܘܬܗ. ܡܢܘ ܡܡܙܓ ܩܪ̈ܝܪ̈ܘܬܗ ܒܚܡܝܡܘܬܐ ܕܢܗܘܐ ܗܢܝ ܫܩܝܐ ܕܡܢܗ ܠܦܐܪ̈ܐ ܕܐܪ̈ܥܐ. ܡܿܢ ܢܿܛܪ ܠܗ ܡܐ ܕܡܿܬܚܠܛ ܒܓܘܫܡ̈ܐ ܥܒܝ̈ܐ ܕܠܐ ܢܬܛܠܩ ܡ̣ܢ ܐܣܛܘܟܣ̈ܐ ܕܢܘܟܪ̈ܝܝܢ ܠܗ. ܗܠܝܢ ܥܒܕܘܢܝ ܕܐܒܥܐ ܒܥܬܐ ܕܫܘܚܠܦܝ̈ܗܘܢ ܕܕܝܠܗܘܢ ܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܚܝܠ ܫܘܚܠܦܐ ܐܘ ܕܐܚܪ̈ܢܐ. ܗܢܘܢ ܚܪ̈ܫܐ ܩܢܝܢ ܗܼܘܘ ܡܠܬܐ ܒܟܝܢܝ̈ܗܘܢ ܐܘ ܕܠܡܐ ܟܝ ܡܠܝ̈ܠܐ ܡܙܝܥܝܢ ܠܕܠܐ ܡܠܬܐ. ܩܢܝܝ̈ ܡܠܬܐ ܩܢܝܢ ܟܝ ܚܝܠܐ ܠܡܣܥ ܗܕܐ ܐܘ ܕܠܡܐ ܬܘܒ ܒܚܝܠ ܒܪ̈ܘܝܐ ܡܨܝܢ ܡܬܚܝܠܝܢ. ܪ̈ܢܝܐ ܕܗܠܝܢ ܐܪ̈ܡܝ ܒܪ̈ܥܝܢܝ ܪ̈ܢܝܚܐ ܕܗܠܝܢ ܘܪ̈ܢܝܬ ܕܚܕ ܗܼܘ ܣܿܥܪ ܗܠܝܢ ܒܚܝܠ ܨܒܝܢܗ.
Who makes the sphere of the sun rise at the time of morning, and who hides its splendid light at the time of evening? Who propagates it rapidly in the world above, and who holds it back for fear that it might weaken and fall upon the earth? Who makes the little moon ephemeral, and who makes it reach its fullness in the middle of the month? Who places upon it, the silent one, the reins of order, so that the great order of night and day is not disturbed? Who makes the stars evolve in order, one after the other, and indicates to them the path of an uninterrupted journey? Who moves the mute atmosphere, for our service, and who confers material sounds upon its silence? Who hardens and solidifies its fluidity, and who makes it carry masses of water and distills it upon the earth? Who raises it, like a scepter, above the sea, and lowers it, (it which is) inclined to be humid, by means of its disturbances? Who calms its agitation when it rages, and who unleashes it again when it has calmed down? Where is its violent breath, when it does not blow, and from where does it come suddenly with a violent impetuosity? Who lights the fire of lightning, from its suppleness, and who makes the sounds of thunder resound, from its silence? Who tempers it with heat, (it which is) cold, so that the rain, which (comes) from it, may be useful to the fruits of the earth? Who preserves it when it is mixed with heavy bodies, so that it is not consumed by elements that are foreign to it? These (phenomena) made me search for the cause of their variations, (wondering) if the power to change belongs to them or (belongs) to others. Were they, the mute (beings), endowed with reason in themselves, or did the (beings) endowed with reason truly move those who (are) without reason? Do those who are endowed with reason truly possess the power to do so, or, furthermore, is it not by the power of the Creator that they can have this power? Reflection on the one led my mind to reflect on the others, and I thought that He is unique, He who accomplishes these things by the power of His will.


[p. 676] {#po.34.p676}

### The angels, appointed to instruct and govern the world
ܚܕ ܗܼܘ ܚܝܠܐ ܕܣ̈ܥܪ ܚܝܠܗ ܒܝܕ ܪ̈ܘܚܢܐ ܘܗܘܝܘ ܡܛܟܣ ܠܥ̈ܒܕ ܐܝܕܘ̈ܗܝ ܒܥܿܒܕ ܐܝܕܘ̈ܗܝ. ܒܝܕ ܡܠܠ̈ܐ ܡܙܝܥ ܠܟܝܢ̈ܐ ܠܐ ܕ̈ܓܘܫܐ ܘܒܪ̈ܘܚܢܐ ܓܿܡܪ ܚܘܒܗ ܨܝܕ ܒܪ̈ܝܬܐ. ܠܡܠܝܠܘܬܗܘܢ ܐܓܥܠ ܥܠܡܐ ܡ̣ܢ ܫܘܪ̈ܝܐ ܕܢܗܘܘܢ ܢܛܪ̈ܝܢ ܫܘܚܠܦ ܙܒܢ̈ܐ ܕܠܐ ܕܘܘܕܐ. ܠܗܘܢ ܠܡܠܐܟ̈ܐ ܥܒܼܕ ܓܐܙܒܪ̈ܐ ܕܒܤܝܡܘܬܗ ܕܢܦܠܓܘܢܝܗܝ ܠܓܙܐ ܕܪ̈ܚܡܘܗܝ ܠܐܪ̈ܥܢܝܐ. ܠܗܘܢ ܗܼܘ ܐܩܝܡ ܐܦܛܪ̈ܘܦܐ ܥܠ ܬܫܡܫܬܗ ܕܢܗܘܘܢ ܝܗܒܝܢ ܦܪܣܐ ܒܙܒܢܗ ܠܒܢܝ̈ ܒܝܬܗ. ܠܗܘܢ ܗܼܘ ܝܗܒܗ ܥܠܡܐ ܫܒܪܐ ܐܝܟ ܕܠܪܒܐ ܕܢܚܟܡܘܢܝܗܝ ܡ̣ܢ ܫܒܪܪܘܬܐ ܠܓܡܝܪ̈ܘܬܐ. ܠܪ̈ܒܢܘܬܗܘܢ ܐܫܠܼܡ ܪܗܛܐ ܕܩܠܝܠܘܬܗ ܕܢܛܟܣܘܢܝܗܝ ܡ̣ܢ ܣܘܥܪ̈ܢܐ ܕܙܠܝܠܘܬܐ. ܦܕܓܘ̈ܓܐ ܡܗܝܪ̈ܝ ܗܘܢܐ ܥܒܕ ܙܐ ܠܗ ܠܣܟܠܐ ܕܠܐ ܢܬܠܘܢ ܠܗ ܠܡܗܠܟܘ ܐܝܟ ܨܒܝܢܗ. ܣܟܲܠ ܗܼܘܐ ܣܓܝ ܡ̣ܢ ܒܘܝܢܝܐ ܐܿܟܡܐ ܕܣܟܼܠ ܥܠ ܗܝ ܐܩܝܡ ܥܪ̈ܝܡܝ ܗܘܢܐ ܥܠ ܬܫܡܫܬܗ. ܠܫܘܠܛܢܝ̈ܗܘܢ ܐܓܥܠ ܥܘܬܪܐ ܐܐ ܕܚ̈ܫܚ ܗܼܘܐ ܠܗ ܕܢܗܘܘܢ ܝܨܦܝܢ ܕܣܢܝܩܘܬܗ ܝܘܡܐ ܒܝܘܡܐ. ܐܣܘܛܐܝܬ ܙ̈ܓܝܓ ܗܼܘܐ ܝܥܢܐ ܕܢܐܟܘܠ ܘܢܡܘܬ ܘܣܡܗ ܠܐܘܟܠܐ ܒܐܝܕܐ ܕܬܩܢܥ̈ܐ ܕܢܦܪ̈ܣܘܢܝܗܝ. ܒܐܝܕܐ ܕܬܩܢܼܐ ܨܒܼܐ ܕܢܛܟܣ ܠܐܣܘܛܘܬܗ ܕܠܐ ܡܐ ܕܐܟܠ ܐܣܘܛܐܝܬ ܢܡܘܬ ܒܝܫܐܝܬ. ܚܟܝܡ ܛܒܐ ܡܪܗ ܕܥܠܡܐ ܒܥܪ̈ܝܪ̈ܝܐ ܘܚܟܝܡܐܝܬ ܚܟܡܗ ܒܡܠܬܐ ܕܒܡܠܠ̈ܐ. ܡܠܝ̈ܠܐ ܐܩܝܡ ܥܠ ܬܫܡܫܬܗ ܕܠܐ ܡܠܠܐ ܕܢܚܟܡܘܢܗ̇ ܠܒܪ̈ܝܪ̈ܘܬܗ ܒܥܪ̈ܝܡܘܬܗܘܢ. ܒܗܘܢ ܒܡܠܝܠ̈ܐ ܡܕܒܪ ܟܠ ܡܬܩܢ ܟܠܐ ܘܠܝܬ ܐ ܒܥܒܝܼܕܐ ܕܠܘ ܒܐܝܕܝ̈ܗܘܢ ܡܿܫܠܡ ܪܗܛܗ. ܠܗܘܢ ܗܼܘ ܡܿܫܠܡ ܪ̈ܗܛܐ ܕܚܪ̈ܫܐ ܘܕܡܠܠ̈ܐ ܘܐܝܟ ܡܠܬܗܘܢ ܡܬܪܓܡ ܟܣܝܐ ܥܡ ܬܘܩܢܘ̈ܗܝ. ܒܡܨܥܝܘܬܗܘܢ ܡܚܘܐ ܚܘܒܗ ܐܦ ܠܘܚܡܗ ܘܝܿܗܒ ܛܘܒ̈ܐ ܘܡ̇ܿܣܪܚ ܢܓ̈ܕܐ ܠܡܬܢܓܕܢ̈ܐ.
It is unique, the Power whose authority acts through the intermediary of the spiritual (beings), and it is it that orders the work of His hands by the work of His hands. He moves the insensible natures by means of the (beings) endowed with reason, and He realizes His love for the creatures through the spiritual (beings). From the beginning, He entrusted the world to their reason so that they might watch over the change of seasons, without disorder. He made the angels themselves the treasurers of His delights, so that they might distribute to the earthly (beings) the treasure of His mercy. It is they whom He appointed as stewards in His service, so that they might give food in (due) time to the people of His house. It is to them that He gave the world (still) as a child, as to a master, so that they might instruct it, from childhood to maturity. He handed over to their magisterium its rapid evolution, so that they might order it, far from vile actions. He made them pedagogues with keen intelligence, for the senseless (world), so that they would not let it go according to its (own) pleasure. It was much too senseless to understand (it), senseless as it is: thus He appointed to its service those who have an advised mind. He entrusted to their powers the wealth that was useful to it, so that they might provide for what it needs day after day. The greedy (world) desired immoderately to eat and die, but (God) put the food in the hand of the firm (angels) so that they might distribute it. Through the intermediary of the firm (angels), He wished to order its intemperance, so that, when it eats immoderately, it does not die miserably. The master of the unleashed world is wise and good, and He instructed it with wisdom, thanks to the reason that (is) in the reasonable (beings). He appointed reasonable (beings) to the service of the one that is not, so that they might instruct its ignorance thanks to their sagacity. He who constituted all things directs everything by the reasonable (beings) themselves, and among the created (beings), there is none whose life He has not placed in their hands. It is to them that He hands over the life of the mute and speech-endowed (beings), and according to their reason, the hidden (Being) interprets it to His creatures. Through their mediation, He manifests as much His love as His reprobation, and He grants benefits and inflicts punishments upon those who must be punished.


[p. 678] {#po.34.p678}
ܒܐܝܙܓܕܘܬܗܘܢ ܣܥܿܪ ܟܠ ܗܼܘ ܡܪܟܠ ܘܓܿܒܐ ܘܡܿ̈ܣܠܐ ܐܝܟ ܡܐ ܕܚܙܐ ܠܪܡܙܗ ܡܡܬܘܡ. ܠܗܘܢ ܗܼܘ ܡܫܕܪ ܒܐܝܙܓܕܘܬܐ ܠܘܬ ܐܪ̈ܥܢܝܐ ܘܗܢܘܢ ܦ̈ܢܝܢ ܘܡܚܘܝܢ ܠܗ ܐܝܟ ܡܐ ܕܝܿܕܥ. ܡܢܗܘܢ ܬܿܒܥ ܡܢܝܢ ܐܢܫܐ ܘܫܘܚܠܦ ܙܒܢ̈ܐ ܘܚܦܝܛܝܢ ܛܒ ܠܡܛܪ ܛܟܣܐ ܕܣܝܡ ܒܐܝܕܝ̈ܗܘܢ.
The Master of all things visits all things through their intervention and He chooses and rejects, as His Sign has seen since always. It is they whom He sends on an embassy to the earthly (beings), and they in return designate Him as "The One who knows". He asks them to count men and to make the times vary, and they take great care to maintain the order that has been deposited in their hands.
ܒܗܘܢ ܗܘ ܪܒܐ ܣܥܿܪ ܣܥܘܪ̈ܘܬܐ ܕܣܥܘܪܘܬܗ ܘܒܙܒܢ ܪ̈ܕܐ ܘܒܙܒܢ ܩܿܐܡ ܐܝܟ ܕܢܥܕܪ. ܒܗܘܢ ܗܼܘ ܦܿܪܩ ܠܪ̈ܚܡܝ ܚܘܒܗ ܡܐ ܕܡܬܐܠܨܝܢ ܘܒܗܘܢ ܪ̈ܕܐ ܠܡܒܙܚ̈ܢܐ ܡܐ ܕܡܫܬܥܠܝܢ. ܒܗܘܢ ܗܼܘ ܪܒܐ ܡܕܒܪ ܫܘܠܛܢ ܡܠܟ̈ܐ ܘܦܘܠܓ ܥܡܡ̈ܐ ܘܒܚܠܝܨܘܬܗܘܢ ܣܿܕܪ ܩܪ̈ܒܐ ܘܡܥܒܪ ܩܪ̈ܒܐ.
It is through them that He realizes His sublime activity, and sometimes He punishes, sometimes He assumes to help. It is through them that He delivers those who cherish His love, when they are oppressed, and through them that He punishes the mockers when they pride themselves. It is through them that He guides the power of kings and the division of peoples, and thanks to their courage that He organizes wars and makes others cease.

### The image of the spiritual chariot
ܐܝܟ ܗܢܝܘܟܐ ܪ̈ܟܝܒ ܦܘܩܕܢܗ ܥܠ ܙܘܥܝ̈ܗܘܢ ܘܒܕܡܘܬ ܪ̈ܟܫܐ ܟܕܝܢܝܢ ܗܢܘܢ ܘܦܪ̈ܚܝܢ ܒܐܐܪ. ܐܝܘ ܡܪ̈ܟܒܬܐ ܪ̈ܘܚܢܝܬܐ ܕܐܠܗܐ ܟܠ ܕܟܕܝܢܝܢ ܒܗ̇ ܪ̈ܟܫܐ ܕܪ̈ܘܚܐ ܒܥܪ̈ܩܐ ܕܪ̈ܘܚܐ. ܐܝܘ ܗܢܝܘܟܐ ܕܐܚܝܕ ܥܠܡܐ ܠܐ ܒܐܝܕܝ̈ܐ ܕܡܪܗܛ ܟܢܫ̈ܐ ܕܫܡܝ̈ܢܐ ܒܐܦܝ̈ ܪܘܡܐ. ܐܝܘ ܩܪ̈ܘܟܐ ܕܪܡ ܪ̈ܘܟܒܗ ܡܢ ܪ̈ܘܟܒܐ ܕܡܪܗܛ ܪܗܛܐ ܕܠܐ ܗܕܡ̈ܐ ܡܬܚܙܝܝ̈ܐ. ܡܢܘ ܡܡܬܘܡ ܪ̈ܟܒ ܥܠܝ ܡܕܡ ܕܠܐ ܡܬܪܟܒ ܘܟܕܝܢ ܬܚܘܬܘܗܝ ܟܝܢܐ ܕܠܐ ܟܕܝܢ ܬܚܬܝ ܕ̈ܥܫܐ. ܡܿܢ ܠܐ ܢܬܗܪ ܒܪܗܛܐ ܪܒܐ ܕܛܘܪ̈ܐ ܪܒܐ ܕܗܐ ܒܨܝܪ̈ ܩܠܝܠ ܐܠܦ̈ܐ ܐܫܬܐ ܕܚܕ ܗܼܘ ܪ̈ܗܛܗ.
Like a charioteer, His command rides their faculties, and they are harnessed like horses and they fly in the air. O spiritual chariot of the God of all things, to which are harnessed spiritual horses with spiritual reins! O charioteer who holds the world, not in (his) hands, for He makes the assemblies of the heavenly (beings) dart in the world above! O carriage whose arrangement is too sublime to be manufactured, for it darts well without apparent structures! Who is the one who has ever ridden something that one cannot ride, and under which is harnessed a nature that is not harnessed to the senses? Who would not marvel at the great race, at the great distance, for behold, it is nearly six thousand years that its race has been the same?
ܡܶܢ ܠܐ ܢܐܡܪ ܕܗܿܘ ܕܒܪܐ ܟܠ ܗܼܘ ܡܪܗܛ ܠܗ ܘܚܝܠܗ ܡܚܝܠ ܠܡܚܝܠܘܬܐ ܕܥܒܕ ܐܝܕܘ̈ܗܝ. ܚܝܠܗ ܡܪܗܛ ܠܫܡܝܢ̈ܐ ܬܚܝܬ ܦܘܩܕܢܗ ܘܒܗ ܒܦܘܩܕܢܗ ܡܪܗܛ ܥܠܡܐ ܬܚܝܬ ܫܡܫܘ̈ܗܝ. ܐܘ ܠܬܕܡܘܪ̈ܬܐ ܕܒܬܕܡܘܪ̈ܬܐ ܥܦܝܦܬ ܬܗܪܐ ܕܟܕܝܢ ܥܠܡܐ ܬܚܝܬ ܒܪ̈ܘܝܐ ܘܟܢܘ̈ܬܗ. ܟܕܝܢܝܢ ܥܝܪ̈ܐ ܬܚܝܬ ܗܘ ܪܡܙܐ ܕܐܬܩܢ ܐܢܘܢ ܘܟܕܝܢ ܥܠܡܐ ܬܚܝܬ ܫܘܠܛܢܐ ܕܐܫܠܛ ܐܢܘܢ.
Who would not say that He who created everything is the one who makes it dart, and that His power strengthens the weakness of the work of His hands? Under His command, His power makes the heavenly (beings) dart, and by His command itself, it makes the world dart under (the order) of His servants. O wonder, which as a result of (another) wonder is doubly wonderful: the world is harnessed to the Creator and to His companions! The angels are subject to this Sign that constituted them, and the world is subject to the authority that He conferred upon them.


[p. 680] {#po.34.p680}
ܒܢܝܪ̈ܐ ܕܚܘܒܗ ܟܕܝܢܐ ܒܪ̈ܝܬܐ ܬܚܝܬ ܪ̈ܘܚܝܐ ܘܫܘܠܛܢܝ̈ܗܘܢ ܠܥܠ ܡܢ ܚܨܗ̇ ܐܝܟ ܗܢܝܘܟܐ. ܒܥܪ̈ܩܐ ܕܪ̈ܘܚܐ ܠܒܝܟܝܢ ܠܐܐܪ ܠܐ ܡܓܫܡܬܐ ܘܡܕܒܪ̈ܝܢ ܠܗ̇ ܐܝܟ ܨܒܝܢܐ ܕܡܕܒܪ ܟܠ. ܟܠ ܥܡ ܟܠ ܟܕܝܢ ܘܡܫܥܒܕ ܠܫܘܠܛܢܝ̈ܗܘܢ ܘܥܡ ܟܠ ܪ̈ܗܛܝܢ ܘܠܟܠ ܢܛܪ̈ܝܢ ܕܠܐ ܢܟܝܚ̈ܐ.
Creation is harnessed to the spiritual (beings) by the yoke of His love, and their prerogatives (are) above its expanse, like a charioteer. With spiritual reins, they hold the incorporeal air, and they direct it according to the will of the One who directs all things. Everything, absolutely everything, is subject and subordinate to their prerogatives, and they evolve at the same time as all things, and maintain them all without damage.

### The diverse tasks of the angels: directing the stars
ܒܡܢܘܬ̈ܐ ܕܫܘܠܛܢܝ̈ܗܘܢ ܦܠܓܗ ܠܥܠܡܐ ܘܟܠܚܕ ܡܢܗܘܢ ܙܗܝܪ ܒܥܒܕܐ ܕܡܓܥܠ ܒܐܝܕ̈ܘܗܝ. ܐܝܬ ܕܠܫܡܫܐ ܠܒܝܟ ܒܐܝܕܝ̈ܐ ܕܠܐ ܪ̈ܘܟܒܐ ܘܡܚܘܐ ܠܗ ܫܒܝܠ ܡܪܕܝܬܐ ܕܠܐ ܕܘܪ̈ܟܬܐ.
(God) divided the world into portions (subject) to their prerogatives, and each of them takes care of the work that (God) has placed in his hands. There is one who holds the sun by uncomposed hands, and indicates to it the path to travel (which is) without traces.
ܥܡܗ ܕܚܪ̈ܫܐ ܕܗܿܛ ܥܝܪ̈ܐ ܩܠܝܠܐܝܬ ܘܡܛܟܣ ܠܗ ܕܠܐ ܢܬܕܘܕ ܫܒܝܠ ܡܪ̈ܕܝܬܗ. ܥܡ ܫܪ̈ܝܘܬܗ ܐܡܝܢ ܦܪܚ ܩܠܝܠ ܗܘܢܝܐ ܘܡܣܝܟ ܠܗ ܠܚܘܕܪܐ ܕܐܪܥܐ ܠܠܝܐ ܐܝܡܡ. ܐܝܬ ܕܠܣܗܪܐ ܛܥܝܢ ܥܠ ܟܬܦܗ ܠܐ ܡܓܫܡܬܐ ܘܡܕܒܪ ܠܗ ܒܝܕ ܫܘܚܠܦ̈ܐ ܐܝܟ ܕܠܫܒܪ̈ܐ.
The angel evolves rapidly with the mute (being), and he disciplines it so that the path it travels is not disturbed. The one whose intellect is subtle flies constantly with this free (being), and he contains it around the earth night and day. There is (another) who carries the moon on his incorporeal shoulder, and leads it by means of (its) phases, like a child.
ܒܗܘܢܐ ܛܥܝܢ ܠܗ ܡܗܘܢ ܗܘܢܐ ܠܠܐ ܦܪܘܫܐ ܘܡܕܒܪ ܠܗ ܕܠܐ ܢܬܕܒܪ ܒܥܪ̈ܝܪ̈ܐܝܬ.
Thanks to his intellect, the (angel) endowed with a rational intellect carries (the moon) which is without discernment and he leads it so that it does not conduct itself in an unleashed way.
ܛܟܣ̈ܐ ܘܕܪ̈ܓܐ ܣܿܐܡ ܩܘܕܡܘܗܝ ܐܝܟ ܝܠܘܦܐ ܕܢܗܘܐ ܝܿܕܥ ܕܟܡܐ ܘܟܡܐ ܢܚܶܬ ܘܣܿܠܩ. ܐܝܬ ܕܠܨܡܚܐ ܕܟܘܟܒ̈ܐ ܠܒܝܟܝܢ ܐܝܟ ܠܡܦܝ̈ܕܐ ܘܒܨܡܚܢ̈ܝܗܘܢ ܪ̈ܕܦܝܢ ܚܫܟܐ ܕܠܝܠܘ̈ܬܐ. ܐܘܪ̈ܚܐ ܫܘܝܬܐ ܡܫ̇ܘܝܢ ܒܐܐܪ̈ ܡܠܝ̈ܠܐ ܠܚܪ̈ܫܐ ܘܐܡܝܢܝܢ ܒܗ̇ ܘܠܐ ܡܬܪ̈ܦܝܢ ܡܢ ܡܪ̈ܕܝܬܗܿ.
He places before it, like (before) one who is learning, rules and degrees, so that it knows how many times it (must) descend and ascend. There are those who hold like lamps the brilliant stars, and who chase away by their glows the darkness of the nights. The reasonable (beings) flatten in the air a smooth path for the mute (beings), and they are constantly there and do not stop traveling it.
ܣܕܪ̈ܝܢ ܣܕܪ̈ܝܢ ܪ̈ܕܝܢ ܒܐܘܪܚܐ ܠܐ ܕ̈ܓܥܘܫܐ ܘܫܡܝܢ̈ܐ ܒܪ̈ܫ ܣܕܪ̈ܝܗܘܢ ܐܝܟ ܗܕܝ̈ܐܐ. ܠܡܫܪܝܬ ܡܠܟܐ ܕܡܿܝܢ ܛܟܣ̈ܐ ܘܛܘܟܣܝ̈ܗܘܢ ܘܪ̈ܘܚܢܝܐ ܪ̈ܒܝ ܚܝ̈ܠܐ ܕܠܐ ܡܣܬܝܟܝܢ.
The insensible (beings) advance on (this) path, groups by groups, as well as the heavenly (beings), like guides, at the head of their groups. The ranks and their dispositions resemble the army of the king, and the spiritual (beings) are chiefs of immense armies.

### Atmospheric phenomena
ܐܝܬ ܕܥܠ ܐܐܪ̈ ܡܬܬܙܝܥܢܐ ܦܩܝܕܝܢ ܠܡܩܡ ܘܡܙܝܥܝܢ ܠܗ ܒܙܘܥ̈ܐ ܟܣܝ̈ܐ ܕܪ̈ܥܝܢܝܗܘܢ. ܒܙܘܥܐ ܡܙܝܥܝܢ ܠܐ ܦܓܪ̈ܢܐ ܠܠܐ ܦܓܪ̈ܢܐ ܘܡܪ̈ܟܒܝܢ ܠܗ ܟܕ ܗܼܘ ܟܝܢܗ ܕܠܐ ܪ̈ܘܟܒܐ ܗܼܘ.
There are those who have received the order to stand above the mobile air, and who agitate it thanks to the hidden faculties of their spirits. By a faculty, the incorporeal (beings) agitate the incorporeal (air), and they make of it a compound, even though it is its nature to be uncomposed.


[p. 682] {#po.34.p682}
ܠܐ ܓܘܫܡܢ̈ܐ ܡܬܬܠܝܢ ܒܗ ܒܠܐ ܓܘܫܡܢܝܐ ܘܡܬܠܚܝܢ ܠܗ ܒܡܢܘܬ̈ܐ ܐܝܟ ܕܠܓܫܝܡܐ. ܘܡ̣ܢ ܠܐ ܓܫܝܡܐ ܥܒܕܝܢ ܓܘܫܡܐ ܠܐ ܓܘܫܡܢ̈ܐ ܕܒܓܫܝܡܘܬܗ ܢܛܥܢ ܓܘܫܡܐ ܕܡܝ̈ܐ ܓܫܝܡ̈ܐ. ܢܚܬ̈ܐ ܠܒܝ̈ܕܐ ܙܩܪ̈ܝܢ ܡܢܗ̇ ܕܩܛܝܢܘܬܗ ܕܒܠܒܝܕܘܬܗܘܢ ܢܐܚܕܘܢ ܡܥ̈ܐ ܡܬܐܫܕܢ̈ܐ. ܥܢܢ̈ܐ ܦܪ̈ܣܝܢ ܒܡܨܥܬ ܚܘܕܪ̈ܐ ܕܪ̈ܘܡܐ ܘܥܘܡܩܐ ܘܠܥܠ ܡܢܗܝܢ ܟ̇ܫܝܢ ܡܝ̈ܐ ܐܝܟ ܬܛܠܝܠܐ. ܒܡܝ̈ܐ ܘܪ̈ܘܚܐ ܡܛܠܠܝܢ ܒܝܬܐ ܕܣܘܢܩܢ ܚܝܝ̈ܢ ܘܡ̣ܢ ܬܛܠܝܠܗ ܡܚܬܝܢ ܡܝ̈ܐ ܘܪ̈ܣܝܢ ܒܐܪ̈ܥܐ. ܐܝܬ ܕܠܪ̈ܥܡܐ ܡ̇ܩܢܝܢ ܚܝܠܐ ܕܕܚܝܠ ܩܠܗ ܘܡܙܝܥܝܢ ܠܗ̇ ܠܐܪ̈ܥܐ ܒܩܠܐ ܕܩܠܝܠܘܬܗ. ܒܗܼܘ ܒܐܐܪ̈ ܗܢܘܢ ܥܒܕܝܢ ܓܘܒ̈ܐ ܕܡܝ̈ܐ ܘܝܗܒܝܢ ܩܠܐ ܡܐ ܕܡܬܢܣܟܝܢ ܡܝ̈ܐ ܒܡܝ̈ܐ. ܩܠܐ ܕܪ̈ܘܚܐ ܥܒ̇ܪ ܡܢܫܠܝ ܒܩܒܝܐ ܕܡܝ̈ܐ ܘܡܒܕܪ ܠܗ ܠܟܢܫܐ ܕܡܝ̈ܐ ܕܐܝܬ ܒܥܢܢܐ. ܡܝ̈ܐ ܫܪ̈ܝܐ ܡܠܝܐ ܥܢܢܐ ܙܩܐ ܕܪ̈ܘܚܐ ܘܡܐ ܕܥܒܪ ܒܗ̇ ܩܠܐ ܕܪܥܡܐ ܨܪܬ̣ ܘܐܬܐܫܕܬܼ. ܐܝܘ ܬܟܬܘܫܐ ܕܬܪ̈ܝܢ ܚܪ̈ܫܐ ܘܚܕ ܡܠܠܐ ܕܟܣܝܐ ܒܓܘܗ ܥܠܬ ܫܝܢܐ ܕܥܝܪ̈ܐ ܘܐܢܫ̈ܐ. ܠܥܠܬ ܫܝܢܐ ܡܠܦܝܢ ܥܝܪ̈ܐ ܩܪ̈ܒܐ ܠܚܪ̈ܫܐ ܕܠܐ ܫܘܝܘܬܗܘܢ ܬܚܒܠ ܫܝܢܐ ܕܦܐܪ̈ܐ ܘܙܪ̈ܥܐ. ܐܘܡܢܐܝܬ ܡܠܦܝܢ ܩܐܪ̈ܣܐ ܡܠܝ̈ܠܐ ܠܚܪ̈ܫܐ ܘܡܢܗ ܕܐܐܪ ܚܫܠܝܢ ܙܝܢܐ ܕܡܙܕܝܢܝܢ ܒܗ. ܓܐܪ̈ܐ ܕܢܘܪ̈ܐ ܚܫܠܝܢ ܡܢܗ ܡܐ ܕܡܬܥܒܐ ܘܡܫܕܪ̈ܝܢ ܠܗܘܢ ܒܐܦܝ̈ ܪܘܡܐ ܒܚܐܦܐ ܩܫܝܐ. ܐܝܟ ܕܠܦܪܙܠܐ ܢܩܫܝܢ ܚܐܦܘ̈ܗܝ ܒܚܐܦܐ ܩܫܝܐ ܘܢܦܩܝܢ ܡܢܗ ܒܠܨܘܨܝ̈ܬܐ ܢܗܝܪ̈ܬܐ. ܢܗܝܪܐܝܬ ܡܢܗܪ ܡܢܫܠܝ ܒܡܨܥܬ ܢܘܗܪ̈ܗ ܘܩܠܝܠܐܝܬ ܡܚܘܐ ܢܘܗܪܗ ܘܡܛܫܐ ܠܗ܆ ܬܗܪ̈ܐ ܡܠܝܐ ܚܙܬܐ ܕܢܘܗܪܗ ܠܕܚܐܪ ܒܗ ܕܥܡ ܫܘܪ̈ܝܗ ܐܦ ܫܘܠܡܗ ܕܠܐ ܬܘܗܝܐ. ܠܐ ܡܪܦܐ ܠܗ ܠܕܚܐܪ ܒܗ ܕܢܚܘܪ ܫܦܝܪ ܕܥܕ ܚܐܪ ܒܗ ܩܦܣܗ ܠܢܘܗܪܗ ܡ̣ܢ ܒܒ̈ܬܗ.
The incorporeal (beings) are suspended from the incorporeal (air), and they split it into (different) portions as one splits what is corporeal. And with that (which is) incorporeal, the incorporeal (beings) make a body, so that, thanks to its corporeality, it (can) carry the body of corporeal water. They weave thick garments from its fluidity, so that, thanks to their thickness, they (can) contain liquid water. They extend the clouds in the middle of the spherical (space) above and below, and above them, they accumulate the water, like a roof. They cover the dwelling, which our life needs, with water and wind, and from its roof, they make the water descend and it waters the earth. There are those who confer upon the thunders the power of a terrible rumble, and they shake the earth itself by its sudden rumble. It is in the air that they make reservoirs of water, and the water resounds when it is poured into (another) water. The sound of the wind suddenly crosses the cistern of water, and it disperses the water that has gathered in the cloud. The cloud, the wineskin of the wind, is full of fluid water, and when the rumble of thunder crosses it, it tears and pours out. O struggle between two mute (beings) and one reasonable (being), in which is hidden the cause of peace for angels and men! It is for the cause of peace that the angels teach the mute (beings) to wage war, for fear that their agreement might destroy the peace of fruits and seeds. With skill, the reasonable (beings) teach the mute (beings) combat, and from the air, they forge a weapon with which they will be equipped. They forge arrows of fire with (the air), when it is dense, and they send them into the world above with an impetuous force. As for iron, they clash with an impetuous force, and luminous sparks come out of it. (The lightning) illuminates suddenly with clarity in the middle of its light, and suddenly it shows its light and hides it. The spectacle of its light fills with wonder the one who contemplates it, for at the same time as it begins, it finishes (shining) without waiting. It does not allow the one who contemplates it to contemplate it well, for, before he contemplates it, it withdraws its light from his pupils.


[p. 684] {#po.34.p684}
ܐܝܘ ܩܠܝܠܐ ܕܠܗ ܠܐ ܚܙܝܐ ܩܠܝܠܘܬܐ ܘܠܢ ܕܚܙܝܢܝܗܝ ܠܐ ܡܪܦܐ ܠܢ ܕܢܚܘܪ ܫܦܝܪ. ܫܒܘܩ ܠܢ ܚܪ̈ܫܐ ܕܢܚܘܪ ܒܫܘܦܪܐ ܕܫܬܝܩܘܬܟ ܠܐ ܬܚܬܚܬ ܠܢ ܒܩܠܝܠܘܬܟ ܕܠܘ ܕܝܠܟ ܗܼܝ. ܠܘ ܕܝܠܗ ܗܼܝ ܕܒܪ̈ܩܐ ܕܢܪ̈ܗܛ ܩܠܝܠܐܝܬ ܡܠܝܠܐ ܗܼܘ ܕܡܪܗܛ ܫܬܝܩܘܬܗ ܟܕ ܗܼܘ ܠܐ ܕ̈ܓܝܫ. ܥܝܪ̈ܐ ܗܼܘ ܕܡܪ̈ܟܢ ܥܢܢ̈ܐ ܠܝܡܐ ܩܠܝܠܐܝܬ ܘܛܥܢ̈ܢ ܡܝ̈ܐ ܘܠܐ ܡܬܛܒܥ̈ܢ ܒܝܘܩܪ̈ܐ ܕܡܝ̈ܐ. ܒܕܡܘܬ ܐܠܦܐ ܛܥܝܢܐ ܥܢܢܐ ܓܙܐ ܕܡܝ̈ܐ ܘܣܠܩܐ ܩܝܡܐ ܒܐܦܝ̈ ܪ̈ܘܡܐ ܘܟܢ ܡܬܦܬܚܐ. ܠܝܩ̈ܐ ܕܪܘܚܐ ܠܒܝܟ ܪܘܚܝܐ ܘܡܕܒܪ̈ ܠܗ̇ ܕܠܐ ܬܬܛܒܥ ܒܝܘܩܪ̈ܐ ܕܡܝ̈ܐ ܕܩܒܐ ܒܓܘܗ̇. ܐܝܘ ܡܠܚܐ ܕܡܪܗܛ ܐܠܦܗ ܕܐܐܪ̈ ܒܐܐܪ ܘܚܠܦ ܠܩ̈ܐ ܛܥܝܢ ܠܗܿ ܒܙܘܥ̈ܐ ܪ̈ܘܚܢܝܐ. ܒܙܘܥ̈ܐ ܛܥܝܢ ܠܗܿ ܪ̈ܘܚܢܝܐ ܠܪ̈ܘܚܐ ܕܐܐܪ ܘܡܐ ܕܡܙܕܥܙܥܐ ܝܗܒܐ ܩܠܐ ܕܡܙܝܥ ܠܐܪ̈ܥܐ. ܐܝܟ ܕܠܢܚܬܐ ܐܚܝܕ ܠܟܢܦ̈ܐ ܕܩܠܝܠܘܬܗ̇ ܘܡܬܚܬܐ ܠܗ̇ ܠܐܦܝ̈ ܐܪܥܐ ܟܕ ܠܐ ܨܒܝܐ. ܐܘ ܩܠܝܠܐ ܕܠܐ ܙܓܐ ܠܗ ܕܢܦܘܫ ܒܐܪ̈ܥܐ ܕܐܡܝܢ ܪܕܐ ܒܐܦܝ̈ ܪܘܡܐ ܐܝܟ ܝܩܝܪܐ. ܐܝܘ ܟܡܐ ܪܒܐ ܒܛܝܠܘܬ ܛܒܐ ܕܠܘܬ ܓܢܣܢ ܕܥܨܐ ܟܝܢ̈ܐ ܡܛܠܬܢ ܠܒܪ ܡ̣ܢ ܛܟܣܐ. ܟܝܢܗ ܕܐܐܪ ܦܪܘܚܘܬܐ ܩܢܐ ܒܟܝܢܗ ܘܗܢܘ ܬܗܪܐ ܕܣܠܩ ܠܪܘܡܐ ܘܢܚܬ ܨܐܕܝܢ. ܚܝܠ ܡܠܐܟܐ ܥܨܐ ܠܚܝܠܐ ܕܦܪܘܚܘܬܗ ܘܡܬܚܬܐ ܠܗ ܠܪ̈ܗܛܐ ܪܡܐ ܠܘܬ ܡܟܬ̈ܢ. ܚܕ ܡܠܐܟܐ ܥܒܝܕ ܪ̈ܒܚܝܠܐ ܥܠ ܡܫܪܝܬܗ ܘܡܕܒܪ ܠܗ̇ ܒܝܕ ܫܘܚܠܦ̈ܐ ܕܟܠ ܐܣܟܝܡܝ̈ܢ. ܚܕ ܗܼܘ ܕܥܒܕ ܪ̈ܫܢܘܬܐ ܥܠ ܫܘܚܠܦܘ̈ܗܝ ܟܕ ܐܝܬ ܥܡܗ ܒܢܝ̈ ܦܠܚܘܬܐ ܕܡܫܬܡܥܝܢ ܠܗ. ܚܠܦ ܣܛܢܐ ܕܣܛܐ ܘܢܦܠ ܥܒܝܕ ܩܕܝܫܐ ܘܦܠܚ̈ܐ ܕܥܡܗ ܚܠܦ ܠܓܝܘܢ̈ܐ ܕܫܐ̈ܕܐ ܛܪ̈ܘܢܐ. ܗܼܘ ܘܕܥܡܗ ܡܛܟܣܝܢ ܠܐܐܪ̈ ܒܥܪ̈ܝܪ̈ܝܐ ܘܟܢܫ̈ܐ ܐܚܪ̈ܢܐ ܡܙܕܒܪ̈ܝܢ ܡܕܡ ܕܦܩܝܕܝܢ ܒܗ.
O rapid (light) that does not see its rapidity, and does not allow us, who have seen it, to contemplate it well! Mute (being), let us contemplate the beauty of your silence! Do not seduce us by your rapidity, for it does not belong to you! It does not belong to the lightning to spring forth suddenly, (but) it is a reasonable (being) who makes it spring forth, he, the silent (being), without its knowledge. It is an angel who inclines the clouds rapidly toward the sea, and they carry the water (but) do not sink under the weight of the water. The cloud carries, like a boat, the treasure of water; and it rises and remains in the world above, then it opens. The spiritual (being) holds the oars of the wind, and directs (the cloud), for fear that it might sink under the weight of the water that is enclosed in it. O sailor who makes the boat of the air dart in the air, and who carries it by (his) spiritual faculties, by way of oars! By (his) faculties, the spiritual (being) carries the wind of the atmosphere, and when it is violently agitated, it produces a sound that shakes the earth. He holds his light wings like a garment, and inclines it toward the surface of the earth against its will. O light (wind), to which it does not please to remain on earth, which travels continually in the world above like a heavy (being)! How great is the foresight of good (God) with regard to our race, since He pushes natures out of (their) order because of us! The nature of the air possesses in itself the capacity to fly, and it is a wonder that it rises in the world above and descends toward us. The power of the angel constrains its power to fly, and it lowers (its) high evolution toward our low (affairs). A chief has been constituted chief of his army, and he leads it by changes of all kinds. He is alone, the one who exercises authority over its changes, although there are with him workers who obey him. He was made holy in the place of Satan who turned away (from his task) and was fallen, and the workers who accompany him replace the legions of tyrannical demons. The angel and those who accompany him discipline the unleashed air, and other assemblies (of angels) direct what they have been ordered (to direct). The powers of the chiefs have been hierarchized and they advance in order on the path of their activities.


[p. 686] {#po.34.p686}

### The activities of the angels near man
ܘܡܛܟܣܐܝܬ ܪܝܢ ܒܐܘܪܚܐ ܕܣܘܥܪ̈ܢܝܗܘܢ.܀ ܐܝܬ ܕܠܡܠܟ̈ܐ ܘܐܘܚܕܢܝ̈ܗܘܢ ܦܩܝܕ ܕܢܛܟܣ ܘܒܙܒܢ ܡܿܡܠܟ ܘܒܙܒܢ ܫ̇ܪܐ ܡ̣ܢ ܫܘܠܛܢܐ. ܐܝܬ ܕܩܪ̈ܒܐ ܡܦܠܓ ܝܗܒ ܠܩܪ̈ܒܬܢܝܐ ܘܐܝܟ ܒܘܚܪܢܗ ܡܙܟܐ ܠܛܒ̈ܐ ܘܪ̈ܕܐ ܠܒܝܫ̈ܐ. ܐܝܬ ܕܠܐܢܫ̈ܐ ܢܛܪ̈ܝܢ ܟܠܝܘܡ ܡ̣ܢ ܢܟܝܢ̈ܐ ܘܪ̈ܕܦܝܢ ܡܢܗܘܢ ܚܝܠܘܬ̈ܐ ܕܫܐ̈ܕܐ ܛܪ̈ܘܢܐ. ܚܕ ܡܠܐܟܐ ܠܘܐ ܠܟܠ ܒܪ̈ܢܫ ܠܘܝܬܐ ܕܚܘܒܐ ܘܥܡܗ ܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܡ̣ܢ ܝܘܡ ܝܠܕܗ ܥܕܡܐ ܠܡܘܬܗ. ܥܡ ܚܝܘܬܗ ܕܟܪ̈ܐ ܚܝ̈ܐ ܕܗ̇ܛ ܚܝܐ ܘܥܠ ܓܢܒ ܥܪ̈ܣܗ ܐܡܝܢ ܩܿܐܡ ܒܝܘܡ ܥܘܢܕܢܗ. ܗܼܘ ܦܪܫ ܠܗܿ ܠܢܦܫܐ ܐܚܝܢܬܗ ܡ̣ܢ ܗܕܡ̈ܐ ܕܗܼܘ ܡܘܒܠ ܠܗ̇ ܠܐܝܟܐ ܕܦܩܝܕ ܡ̣ܢ ܒܪ̈ܘܝܐ. ܬܚܝܬ ܦܘܩܕܢܐ ܕܒܪ̈ܘܝܘܬܐ ܟܕܝܢ ܘܡܫܥܒܕ ܘܠܘ ܕܝܠܗ ܗܝ ܪܫܝܬܐ ܠܡܸܣܥܪ ܗܕܐ ܕܡܣܬܥܖܿܐ ܒܗ. ܠܘ ܗܼܘ ܝܗܒ ܡܬܚܐ ܕܚܝ̈ܐ ܘܙܒܢܐ ܟܪ̈ܝܐ ܘܠܐ ܫܠܝܛ ܠܗ ܕܢܘܣܦ ܫܥܐ ܘܢܒܨܪ ܫܥܐ. ܘܐܦ ܠܗ ܠܐܢܫܐ ܠܘ ܒܩܛܝܪ̈ܐ ܡܕܒܪ̈ܝܢ ܚܝ̈ܘܗܝ ܘܠܐ ܡܨܐ ܕܢܥܨܝܘܗܝ ܠܐ ܠܡܛܐܒܘ ܘܠܐ ܠܡܒܐܫܘ. ܚܐܖ̈ܘܬ ܢܦܫܐ ܩܢܐ ܒܪ̈ܢܫܐ ܒܕܘܡܝܐ ܕܝܠܗ ܘܡܨܐ ܕܢܝܚܛܐ ܘܐܦ ܕܢܙܕܕܩ ܐܝܟ ܦܪ̈ܘܫܐ. ܠܐ ܡܬܥܨܝܐ ܚܐܪ̈ܘܬ ܢܦܫܐ ܕܒܒܪ̈ܢܫܐ ܘܠܘ ܡܬܕܒܪܐ ܠܡܸܤܥܪ ܡܕܡ ܟܕ ܠܐ ܡܢܚܐ. ܚܕ ܗܼܘ ܟܝܢܐ ܕܢܦܫܐ ܕܐܢܫܐ ܘܕܪ̈ܘܚܝܐ ܘܠܐ ܡܬܚܣܢܐ ܐܠܐ ܚܣܢܬܼܗ ܗܝ ܠܩܢܘܡܗܿ. ܩܢܘܡܐ ܓܡܝܪ̈ܐ ܩܢܝܐ ܒܝܬܗ̇ ܡ̣ܢ ܫܘܪ̈ܝܐ ܐܝܟ ܡܐ ܕܓܡܝܪ ܐܦ ܬܘܩܢܐ ܕܫܡܝܢ̈ܐ. ܐܚܝܢܝ̈ܗ̇ ܐܢܘܢ ܘܡܢܗ ܕܛܘܗܡܐ ܕܝܠܗܘܢ ܐܝܬܝܗ̇ ܘܠܐܚܝܢܘܬܗ̇ ܡܚܒܒܝܢ ܟܠܫܥ ܒܣܘܥܪ̈ܢܝܗܘܢ. ܠܗ̇ܝ ܒܪܬ ܩܠܐ ܕܩܪܬܼ ܠܐܢܫܐ ܒܫܡ ܟܣܝܘܬܐ ܠܗ̇ ܗܼܘ ܡܝܩܪ̈ܝܢ ܒܝܕ ܐܝܩܪ̈ܐ ܕܨܝܕ ܒܪ̈ܢܫܐ. ܒܐܢܫܐ ܡܚܘܝܢ ܚܘܒܗܘܢ ܪ̈ܒܐ ܕܨܝܕ ܒܪ̈ܘܝܐ ܥܠ ܗܺܝ ܠܐܝܢ ܒܡܙܘܢ ܚܝܝ̈ܢ ܘܒܬܫܡܫܬܢ.
There is (one) to whom (God) has ordered to organize kings and their possessions, and sometimes he makes king, sometimes he dismisses from power. There is one who distributes war and gives it to the combatants, and according to his discrimination, he exonerates the good and punishes the wicked. There are those who preserve men daily from what harms, and chase far from them the armies of tyrannical demons. An angel accompanies each man with (his) affectionate company, and he is with him from the day of his birth until his death. The living (angel) evolves at the same time as the man lives whose existence is brief, and he stands continually at the head of his bed, on the day of his death. It is he who separates the soul itself, his relative, from the members, for it is he who leads it to the place ordered by the Creator. He is subject and obedient to what the creative power (of God) orders (him), and it does not belong to him in his own right to do what he does. It is not he who grants a long life or a brief time, and he does not have the power to add an hour or to subtract one. And as for the man himself, (the angel) does not direct his life under constraint, and he cannot force him either to do good or to do evil. Identity of the human soul and the angelic nature. Man possesses the freedom of soul, in the likeness of (the angel), and he can sin but also remain just, as an (being) endowed with discernment. The freedom of the soul that (is) in man cannot be forced, nor led to do something while it does not consent to it. The nature of the soul of man and of the spiritual (beings) is one, and it cannot be dominated but it dominates itself. From the origin, it possesses in itself a perfect substance, as is perfect also the constitution of the heavenly (beings). They are of his kinship, and she is of their own race, and by their acts, they cherish her ceaselessly, her, their relative. This word that called man, by the name of the hidden (Nature), it is it that they honor, by the honor that they manifest toward man. Through man they manifest their great love toward the Creator; thus they toil for the conservation of our life and in our service.

### The activities of the angels are for man
ܡܛܠܬܢ ܫܚܝܩܝܢ ܣܓܝ ܐܦ ܡܫܬܚܩܝܢ ܘܠܐ ܡܬܬܢܝܚܝܢ ܥܕܡܐ ܕܢܝܚܝܢ ܒܠܡܐܝܢ ܚܘܒܐ.
It is for us that they have tired themselves much and are still tiring themselves, and they will not rest before they rest in the harbor of love.


[p. 688] {#po.34.p688}
ܡܛܠܬܢ ܕܪܡܝܢ ܒܐܓܘܢܐ ܕܫܘܚܠܦ ܙܒܢ̈ܐ ܕܒܫܘܚܠܦܗܘܢ ܢܣܒܪ ܚܝ̈ܐ ܕܠܐ ܫܘܠܡܐ. ܡܛܠܬܢ ܠܝܐܝܢ ܘܥܡܝܠܝܢ ܒܥܡܝܠ̈ܬܐ ܘܟܕ ܠܐ ܠܝܐܝܢ ܠܝܐܝܢ ܥܡܢ ܕܚܢܢ ܢܬܬܢܝܚ. ܡܛܠܬܢ ܡܙܝܥܝܢ ܠܐܐܪ̈ ܒܙܘܥܐ ܕܒܗܘܢ ܕܒܝܕ ܡܫܒ̈ܘܗܝ ܢܩܝܡܘܢ ܙܪ̈ܥܐ ܕܩܘܝܡ ܚܝ̈ܢܢ. ܡܛܠ ܚܝ̈ܝܢ ܡܛܟܣܝܢ ܪܗܛܐ ܕܝܪ̈ܚܝ ܫܢܬܐ ܕܠܐ ܢܬܕܘܕ ܘܢܛܥܐ ܛܟܣܐ ܕܦܪ̈ܘܫܘܬܢ. ܡܛܠܬܢ ܡܚܘܝܢ ܐܘܪ̈ܚܐ ܠܫܡܫܐ ܘܣܗܪ̈ܐ ܕܠܐ ܬܘܒ ܢܛܥܐ ܫܒܝܠ ܡܪ̈ܕܝܬܢ ܕܨܝܕ ܒܪ̈ܘܝܐ.
It is for us that they launch themselves into combat for the change of seasons, so that, as a result of their change, we may await the life that (has) no end. It is for us that they toil and tire themselves in painful (works), and although they do not toil, they toil with us so that we may rest. It is for us that they agitate the air by the faculty that (is) in them, to make the seeds rise for our vital subsistence, by means of the breezes (of the air). It is for our life that they order the course of the months of the year, for fear that our (faculty) of discernment may be troubled and go astray. It is for us that they indicate to the sun and to the moon (their) path, so that we do not lose either the path of our walk toward the Creator.
ܡܛܠܬܢ ܡܦܠܓܝܢ ܫܥ̈ܐ ܠܢܘܗܪܐ ܘܚܫܟܐ ܕܠܐ ܢܬܦܠܓܘܢ ܥܠ ܒܪܘܝܐ ܕܒܪܐ ܐܢܘܢ. ܡܛܠ ܕܙܪ̈ܓܗ ܩܢܘ ܫܘܠܛܢܐ ܕܕܪ̈ܓܐ ܘܛܟܣ̈ܐ ܘܚܘܝܘ ܕܦܩܕܝܢ ܐܦ ܡܬܦܩܕܝܢ ܕܕܪ̈ܓܗ ܢܬܝܩܪ.
It is for us that they distribute the hours to the light and to the darkness, for fear that we may doubt concerning the Creator who created them. It is because of His rank that they have acquired authority over the ranks and the orders, and they show that they command and are commanded, so that His rank may be exalted.
ܠܝܩܝܪ̈ܘܬܗ ܡܝܩܪ̈ܝܢ ܛܟܣ̈ܐ ܕܫܘܠܛܢܝ̈ܗܘܢ ܘܡܛܠ ܛܟܣܗ ܡܡܠܟ ܛܟܣܐ ܒܝܬ ܣܕܪ̈ܝܗܘܢ. ܛܟܣܐ ܪܒܐ ܫܪܐ ܒܛܟܣܝ̈ܗܘܢ ܕܡܛܟܣܢ̈ܐ ܘܟܕ ܚܕ ܐܢܘܢ ܐܝܬ ܗܘ ܕܦܩܕܝܢ ܐܦ ܡܬܦܩܕܝܢ.
The degrees of their powers honor His dignity, and because of His order, He makes order reign among their groups. A great order remains among the ranks of those who put in order, and although they are one, there are those who command, and (others) who are commanded.
ܒܦܩܘܕܘܬܗܘܢ ܚܙܐ ܟܪ̈ܘܙܐ ܪܒܐ ܕܥܡܡ̈ܐ ܘܟܢܝ ܐܢܘܢ ܒܫܡ ܫܘܠܛܢܝܐ ܕܫܘܠܛܢܝ̈ܗܘܢ. ܘܡܛܠ ܚܫܚܬܢ ܟܬܒ ܕܡܬܬܢܝܚܝܢ ܘܐܦ ܕܡܚܒܠܝܢ ܕܐܡܬܝ ܢܫܠܚ ܚܫܘܫܘܬܐ ܘܢܠܒܫ ܫܘܒܚܐ. ܟܠܗ̇ ܒܪ̈ܝܬܐ ܡܚܒܠܐ ܥܡܗܘܢ ܘܗܢܘܢ ܥܡܗ̇ ܕܐܡܬܝ ܢܐܬܐ ܝܘܡ ܡܘܠܕܐ ܕܡܢ ܒܝܬ ܡܝܬ̈ܐ. ܥܡ ܒܪ̈ܝܬܐ ܫܬܝܩ̈ܬܐ ܥܢܝܢ ܘܥܡܝܠܝܢ ܥܠ ܗܿܘ ܣܒܪܐ ܕܐܝܬ ܗܘ ܣܒܪܐ ܠܐܒܝܕܘܬܗ. ܒܣܒܪ̈ܐ ܕܣܒܪܗ ܬܠܝܐ ܒܪ̈ܝܬܐ ܘܝܬܝܪ̈ ܥܝܪ̈ܐ ܒܗܿܝ ܕܡܦܣܝܢ ܒܪ̈ܒܘܬ ܣܒܪ̈ܐ ܕܡܛܝܒ ܠܗܿ.
The great herald of the peoples saw that they commanded, and called them by the name of the power of their charges. And he wrote for our use, that they rest and also, that they will work, when we abandon passibility and put on glory. All creation will be with them in labor, and they with it, when the day of the nativity from among the dead comes. They will be occupied and will toil with the mute creatures, in this hope, which is hope of its destruction. Creation and especially the angels cling to their supreme hope, because they know the greatness of the hope that is prepared for it.
ܒܣܒܪܐ ܛܒܐ ܕܦܘܪ̈ܩܢ ܚܝܝ̈ܢ ܡܣܟܝܢ ܟܠ ܝܘܡ ܘܫܪܝܪ̈ܐ ܠܗܘܢ ܕܡܩܒܠܝܢ ܠܗ ܒܝܕ ܣܘܥܪ̈ܢܐ.
They wait every day, with the good hope that our life will be saved, and they are sure of obtaining it by means of (their) actions.


[p. 690] {#po.34.p690}
ܡܛܠ ܣܒܪܗ ܟܬܒ ܕܐܫܬܥܒܕܘ ܠܣܪ̈ܝܩܘܬܐ ܘܩܒܠ ܥܠܡܐ ܬܢܘܝ ܕܐܓܪ̈ܐ ܒܝܘܡ ܚܘܕܬܗ. ܫܪܝܪ ܥܡܠܐ ܕܥܡܠܝܢ ܥܝܪ̈ܐ ܚܠܦ ܒܪ̈ܢܫܐ ܘܗܐ ܬܚܘܝܬܐ ܕܫܪ̈ܝܪܘܬܗ ܒܫܘܚܠܦ ܙܒܢ̈ܐ.
It is because of His hope, he wrote, that they were subjected to vanity, and the world has received a contract of wages on the day of its restoration. The labor in which the angels toil because of man, is true; and here is the proof of its veracity: in the change of times.
ܙܒܢ̈ܐ ܣܗܕܝܢ ܥܠ ܥܡܠܝ̈ܗܘܢ ܕܫܡܝܢ̈ܐ ܘܚܠܦ ܦܘܡܐ ܒܫܬܩܐ ܩܥܝܢ ܕܟܡܐ ܫܚܝܩܝܢ.
The times testify to the labors of the heavenly (beings), and they proclaim in silence, instead (of it being) orally, how much they tire themselves.
ܒܫܬܩܐ ܩܥܝܢ ܟܝܢ̈ܐ ܚܪ̈ܫܐ ܥܡ ܒܪ̈ܢܫܐ ܕܚܘܪ ܒܪ̈ܢܫܐ ܡܛܠܬܟ ܫܚܝܩܝܢ ܥܝܪ̈ܐ.
The mute natures cry out in silence to man: "Look, man! The angels are wearying themselves for you!"

### Man must be grateful to the angels
ܡܛܠܬܟ ܪܡܝܢ ܒܫܚܩܐ ܡܠܝ̈ܠܐ ܘܚܪ̈ܫܐ ܘܗܘ ܒܪ̈ܢܫܐ ܠܐ ܪܓܝܫ ܕܟܡܐ ܫܚܝܩܝܢ ܚܠܦܘܗܝ. ܚܘܪ ܒܪ̈ܢܫܐ ܒܥܡܝܠܘܬܗܘܢ ܐܦܢ ܩܠܝܠ ܘܐܥܡܠ ܢܦܫܟ ܒܗܕ̈ܓܐ ܛܒܐ ܕܥܝܪ̈ܐ ܐܚܝܢܝ̈ܟ. ܚܙܝ ܕܚܠܦܝܟ ܫܚܝܩܝܢ ܣܓܝ ܘܐܦ ܡܫܬܚܩܝܢ ܘܥܡ ܬܪ̈ܒܝܬܟ ܡܪ̈ܒܝܢ ܦܐܪ̈ܐ ܠܣܢܝܩܘܬܟ.
"For your sake, the rational and the mute (beings) are plunged into toil; and it is man who does not perceive how much they weary themselves for him." "Man, consider their labor even a little, and toil yourself, in a good meditation on the angels, your kin!" "See how much they have wearied themselves for you, and are still wearying themselves, and at the same time as you grow, they make the fruits grow for your needs."
ܕܝܠܗܘܢ ܐܝܬܝܗ̇ ܬܪܒܝܬ ܦܐܪ̈ܐ ܘܡܘܥܝܬ ܙܪ̈ܥܐ ܘܒܡܠܬܗܘܢ ܡܛܟܣ ܪܗܛܐ ܕܝܪ̈ܚܝ ܫܢܬܐ. ܩܠܝܠܠܐܝܬ ܪ̈ܗܛܝܢ ܒܐܐܪ ܥܡ ܙܘܥܝ̈ܗܘܢ ܘܗܢܝܐܐ ܠܗܘܢ ܝܘܬܪ̈ܘܬܐ ܕܒܥܡܠܝ̈ܗܘܢ.
The growth of fruits and the germination of seeds belong to them, and the course of the months of the year is ordered by their word. They run through the air rapidly, by their faculties, and the burden of their labors is pleasant to them.
ܗܢܝܐܝܬ ܣܥܪ̈ܝܢ ܟܠܐ ܘܢܛܪ̈ܝܢ ܟܠܐ ܘܒܣܘܥܪ̈ܢܗܘܢ ܦܪ̈ܥܝܢ ܫܘܒܚܐ ܠܡܢ ܕܩܢܐ ܟܠܐ. ܫܘܒܚܐ ܩܥܝܢ ܡܐ ܕܡܫܬܠܚܝܢ ܒܣܘܥܪ̈ܢܝܗܘܢ ܘܗܢܘ ܬܗܪܐ ܕܥܡ ܣܘܥܪ̈ܢܐ ܐܦ ܬܫܒܘܚܬܐ. ܬܪ̈ܬܝܢ ܒܚܕܐ ܓܡܪ̈ܝܢ ܟܠܫܥ ܕܠܐ ܬܘܗܝܐ ܣܥܘܪ̈ܘܬܐ ܕܚܠܦ ܐܢܫܐ ܘܫܘܒܚܐ ܠܟܣܝܐ.
They visit all things and watch over all things with pleasure, and by their activity, they render glory to the one who created everything. They proclaim praise when they are sent to their activities, and it is a marvel that with (their) activity, there is also glorification. They do two things at once, ceaselessly (and) without delay, an activity that (is) for man, and praise to the Hidden (Being).
ܐܝܘ ܣܥܘܪ̈ܐ ܕܣܥܘܪ̈ܘܬܐ ܐܠܗܝܬܐ ܕܠܐ ܡܬܥܘܟܝܢ ܠܡܡܠܝܘ ܬܪ̈ܬܝܢ ܒܚܕܐ. ܐܝܘ ܡܫܡܫܢ̈ܐ ܡܫܡܫܝ̈ ܟܠܐ ܕܡܪܐ ܟܠܐ ܕܓܡܝܪ ܚܘܒܗܘܢ ܨܝܕ ܒܪ̈ܘܝܐ ܘܨܝܕ ܒܪ̈ܝܬܗ. ܐܝܘ ܐܝܙ̈ܓܕܐ ܕܡܠܟ ܡܠܟ̈ܐ ܠܐ ܡܝܘܬܐ ܕܒܐܝܙܓܕܘܬܗܘܢ ܢܛܪ̈ܝܢ ܚܝ̈ܐ ܕܡܝܘܬܘܬܐ. ܐܝܘ ܡܫܝܢܢ̈ܐ ܘܪ̈ܚܡܝ ܫܝܢܐ ܕܕܟܠ ܘܒܟܠ ܕܡܚܝܨܝܢ ܠܗ ܠܫܝܢܐ ܕܐܢܫܐ ܒܚܘܒܐ ܕܒܗܘܢ. ܐܝܘ ܟܪ̈ܘܙܐ ܕܚܝܠ ܒܪ̈ܘܝܐ ܕܨܝܕ ܒܪ̈ܝܬܗ ܕܕܠܐ ܩܠܐ ܨܝܬܝܢ ܩܠܐ ܟܝܢ̈ܐ ܚܪ̈ܫܐ.
O agents of divine activity, who are not embarrassed to accomplish two things at once! O ministers who realize all the (orders) of the Master of all things, whose love toward the Creator and his creatures is perfect! O messengers of the immortal king of kings, who maintain mortal life by your intercession! O peaceful ones and friends of peace, of the one who (is) all and in all, who strengthen the peace of man by the love that (is) in you! O heralds of the power of the Creator to his creatures, for without a voice, the mute natures hear (his) voice!


[p. 692] {#po.34.p692}
ܐܝܘ ܟܡܐ ܪܒܐ ܡ̣ܢ ܬܫܥܝܬܐ ܡܠܬܐ ܕܒܗܘܢ ܕܐܦ ܫܬܝܩ̈ܐ ܨ̈ܝܬܝܢ ܩܠܐ ܕܬܘܪ̈ܓܡܝܗܘܢ.
Oh! How the faculty of speech that (is) in them is too great to be explained, for even the silent (beings) hear the words of their conversations!
ܕܠܐ ܒܪܬ ܩܠܐ ܠܥܙܝܢ ܟܠܫܥ ܥܡ ܫܬܝܩ̈ܐ ܘܟܕ ܠܝܬ ܡܠܬܐ ܓܡܪ̈ܝܢ ܡܠܐ ܒܝܕ ܣܘܥܪ̈ܢܐ.
Without speech, they whisper ceaselessly with the silent (beings), and although there is no (expressed) speech, they carry out conversations by means of (their) acts!
ܒܟܣܝܐ ܠܥܙܝܢ ܥܡ ܐܣܛܘܟܣ̈ܐ ܠܐ ܪ̈ܓܘܫܐ ܘܝܗܒܝܢ ܩܠܐ ܡܬܪ̈ܓܫܢܐ ܐܝܟ ܪ̈ܓܘܫܐ.
The (beings) not endowed with sense whisper in secret with the elements, and emit perceptible noises, like those endowed with sense.
ܘܐܝܟ ܕܒܐܝ̈ܕܝܐ ܠܒܝܟܝܢ ܠܐܐ ܪܘܚܐ ܒܐܪ̈ܒܥ ܟܢܦܘ̈ܗܝ ܘܦܪ̈ܣܝܢ ܚܐܦܐ ܕܩܠܝܠܘܬܗ ܠܐܪ̈ܒܥ ܦܢܝ̈ܢ.
And they seize the air, as if with hands, by its four edges, and they diffuse it, (it whose) speed is impetuous, to the four extremities (of the world).
ܒܡܫܒܘ̈ܗܝ ܢܝ̈ܚܐ ܡܢܝܚܝܢ ܐܢܫܐ ܘܐܦ ܚܝܘܬ̈ܐ ܘܒܪܲܓܝܘܬܗ ܡܪ̈ܓܝܢ ܠܐܪ̈ܥܐ ܕܬܬܠ ܦܐܪ̈ܐ.
By its breezes, the calm (beings) give rest to man and to animals, and by its humidity, they make the earth moist, so that it may give fruits.
ܐܘܡܢܐܝܬ ܥܨܒܝܢ ܟܐܒܘ̈ܗܝ ܕܥܠܡܐ ܟܪ̈ܝܗܐ ܒܩܪ̈ܝܪ̈ܘܬܐ ܘܚܡܝܡܘܬܐ ܬܪ̈ܝܢ ܕܠܩܘܒ̈ܥܠܐ. ܢ
They bind with skill the wounds of the sick world, with cold and heat, two things that (are) contrary.
ܠܩܪܝܪ̈ܘܬܗ ܡܡܕܟܝܢ ܟܠܫܥ ܒܚܡܝܡܘܬܐ ܘܟܢ ܣܝܡܝܢ ܠܗ ܐܝܟ ܣܡܡܢ̈ܐ ܠܟܐܒ̈ܐ ܢܣܝ̈ܣܐ. ܐܝܘ ܠܐܣܝܘܬܐ ܠܐ ܡܓܫܡܬܐ ܕܠܐ ܓܘܫܡܢ̈ܐ ܕܡܡܕܟܝܢ ܠܗܿ ܪ̈ܘܚܢܝܐ ܘܚܫܚܐ ܠܓܘܫܡܢ.
They mix the cold (air) ceaselessly with the heat, then they place it, like medicines, on the open wounds. O immaterial medicine of the immaterial (beings), which the spiritual (beings) prepare and (which) serves our body!
ܠܐܘܡܢܘܬܗܘܢ ܝܗܒܗ ܠܥܠܡܐ ܡܪܗ ܕܥܠܡܐ ܢ ܕܚܙܐ ܕܝܕܥܝܢ ܠܡܥܨܒ ܟܐܒ̈ܐ ܕܢܣܝܣܘܬܗ.
The master of the world entrusted the world to their art, because he saw that they knew how to bind its open wounds.
ܚܝܠ ܨܒܝܢܗ ܣܡ ܒܟܝܢܗܘܢ ܡ̣ܢ ܫܘܪ̈ܝܐ ܕܢܗܘܘܢ ܣܦܩܝܢ ܠܡܣܥܪ ܥܠܡܐ ܟܪ̈ܝܗ ܒܚܛܝܬܐ.
From the beginning, he placed in their nature the strength of his will, so that they might be capable of visiting the world sick because of sin.
ܡܢܗ ܝ̇ܠܦܘܗ̇ ܠܐܘܡܢܘܬܐ ܘܟܢ ܐܬܢܨܚܘ ܘܚܝܠ ܥܘܕܪ̈ܢܗ ܕܪܗܛ ܥܡܗܘܢ ܥܕܡܐ ܠܚܪ̈ܬܐ.
They learned this art from him, then they excelled (in it), and his helping strength will evolve with them until the end.
ܥܡܗܘܢ ܪܗܛ ܚܝܠ ܒܪ̈ܘܝܐ ܩܠܝܠܐܝܬ ܘܥܡܗ ܪܕܝܢ ܕܠܐ ܡܘܫܥ̈ܬܐ ܕܦܣܥ̈ܬܐ.
The power of the Creator evolves rapidly with them, and with immeasurable steps, they advance with it.

### The type of angelic knowledge
ܩܛܝܢܐܝܬ ܕܡܙܗ ܕܠܐ ܒܪܬ ܩܠܐ ܘܨܝܬܝܢ ܘܠܗ ܕܠܐ ܡܫܡܥܬܐ ܓܘܫܡܢܝܬܐ.
His Sign, gives (them) subtle indications without speech, and they hear his voice without (there being) a material noise.
ܠܦܘܬ ܡܫܡܥܬܐ ܕܐܩܢܝ ܐܢܘܢ ܡܬܪܓܫ ܥܡܗܘܢ ܕܢܗܘܘܢ ܣܦܩܝܢ ܠܡܨܬ ܒܗܘܢܐ ܩܠܐ ܟܣܝܐ.
He converses with them, according to the (type of) hearing that he gave them, so that they might be capable of hearing (his) hidden voice in spirit.
ܒܗܘܢܐ ܨܝܬܝܢ ܠܗ̇ܝ ܒܪܬ ܩܠܐ ܕܟܠܗ̇ ܗܕܐ ܗܝ ܘܒܒܘܝܢܐ ܡܬܒܝܢܝܢ ܒܗ̇ܿ ܟܕ ܬܗܝܪ̈ܝܢ.
They hear in spirit this word which is all that (they hear) and they understand it by the intelligence while marveling at it.
ܐܝܘ ܟܡܐ ܪ̈ܒܐ ܪܒܘܬ ܬܗܪ̈ܐ ܕܒܪ̈ܘܚܝܐ ܘܙܥܘܪ ܦܘܡܐ ܕܟܠܗ̇ ܒܪ̈ܝܬܐ ܕܢܬܢܐ ܬܗܪ̈ܗܿ.
How great it is, the great marvel that (is) in the spiritual (beings), but (how) the mouth of universal creation is too weak to tell (in its turn) its marvel.
ܐܝܘ ܟܡܐ ܚܪ̈ܝܦ ܗܘܢܐ ܕܩܢܝܢ ܩܢܝܝ̈ ܗܘܢܝܐ ܕܣܦܩ ܠܡܨܬ ܩܠܐ ܕܩܛܝܢ ܡ̣ܢ ܟܠ ܗܘܢܝ̈ܢ.
How penetrating it is, the spirit that those who possess a spirit possess, for it is capable of hearing a voice that is more subtle than any spirit!


[p. 694] {#po.34.p694}
ܐܝܘ ܟܡܐ ܩܠܝܠ ܗܿܘ ܒܘܝܢܐ ܕܡܬܒܝܢܝܢ ܒܗ ܕܟܕ ܠܝܬ ܡܕܡ ܡܬܚܙܝܢܐ ܠܗܘܢ ܫܪ̈ܝܪ̈ܐ ܗܼܘ.
How lively it is, the intelligence by which they understand, for although there is nothing visible, (it) is real for them!
ܐܝܘ ܟܡܐ ܟܣܝܐ ܗܿܝ ܟܣܝܘܬܐ ܕܠܥܠ ܡܢܗܘܢ ܘܟܡܐ ܓܠܝܐ ܣܥܘܪ̈ܘܬܐ ܩܕܡ ܥܝܢܝ̈ܗܘܢ.
How hidden it is, this hidden (nature) that is above them, and how the (divine) operation is visible to their eyes!
ܐܝܘ ܟܡܐ ܫܪ̈ܝܪ ܫܪ̈ܪܐ ܟܣܝܐ ܕܪ̈ܥܝܢܝܗܘܢ ܘܠܐ ܡܬܦܠܓܝܢ ܠܡܒܥܐ ܒܥܬܐ ܕܗ̇ܝ ܟܣܝܘܬܐ.
How true it is, the hidden truth of their spirits, and they do not hesitate to inquire about this hidden (nature)!
ܟܣܝܐ ܡܢܗܘܢ ܐܝܟ ܡܐ ܕܟܣܝܐ ܡܢ ܟܠ ܒܪ̈ܝܢ ܘܗܢܘ ܬܗܪܐ ܕܟܡܐ ܕܟܣܝܐ ܠܗܘܢ ܐܝܟ ܓܠܝܐ ܗܼܝ.
It is hidden from them, as it is hidden from all creatures, and it is a marvel that it is as well hidden from them as it is visible!

### The faith of the angels
ܒܗܝܡܢܘܬܐ ܚܙܝܢ ܚܙܬܐ ܕܠܐ ܡܬܚܙܝܐ ܘܒܠܐ ܚܙܬܐ ܨܕܝܕ ܗܘܢܐ ܕܪ̈ܥܝܢܝܗܘܢ.
They contemplate in faith a vision that (is) invisible, and the intellect of their spirits scrutinizes what is not (an object) of vision.
ܒܗܝܡܢܘܬܐ ܨܝܬܝܢ ܠܐ ܕܠܐ ܒܪܬ ܩܠܐ ܘܡܩܿܒܠܝܢ ܠܗ ܒܠܥܕ ܪ̈ܓܫܐ ܡܬܪ̈ܓܫܢܐ.
They listen in faith to a voice without speech, and they perceive it without sensory organs.
ܒܗܝܡܢܘܬܐ ܫܠܡܘ ܪܗܛܐ ܕܗܝܡܢܘܬܐ ܘܗܝܡܢܘ ܕܡܨܝܐ ܠܡܗܝܡܢܘ ܒܫܡ ܒܪ̈ܘܝܐ.
They have realized in faith the course of faith, and they have believed that it is possible to believe in the name of the Creator.
ܒܗܝܡܢܘܬܐ ܩܡܘ ܕܢܛܟܣܘܢ ܚܝܠ ܐܣܛܘܟܣ̈ܐ ܘܒܗ̇ ܗܼܘ ܚܣܢܘܗܝ ܠܚܝܠܐ ܩܫܝܐ ܕܟܬܝܫܘܬܗܘܢ.
They have stood in faith, to discipline the strength of the elements, and it is thanks to it that they triumphed over the harsh strength of their agitation.
ܒܗܝܡܢܘܬܐ ܩܢܘ ܬܫܡܫܬܐ ܕܨܝܕ ܒܪ̈ܢܫܐ ܘܠܐ ܐܬܦܠܓܘ ܥܠ ܗ̇ܘ ܩܠܐ ܕܩܪܝܗܝ ܨܠܡܗ.
They have acquired through faith a ministry to man, and they did not doubt concerning this voice that called him "his image."
ܗܝܡܢܘܬܐ ܟܕܢܬܼ ܐܢܘܢ ܒܥܡܠܐ ܕܚܝܘ̈ܗܝ ܘܡܬܪ̈ܣܝܢ ܠܗ ܕܠܐ ܢܫܘܛܘܢܗ̇ ܠܗܝܡܢܘܬܐ. ܗܝܡܢܘܬܐ ܫܕܪ̈ܬ̣ ܐܢܘܢ ܠܐܚܝܢܘܬܗ ܘܒܗ̇ ܐ̇ܬܒܩܝܘ ܒܟܣܝܘܬ ܢܦܫܗ ܪ̈ܘܚܢܝܬܐ. ܪ̈ܘܚܢܝܬܐ ܘܠܐ ܡܝܘܬܬܐ ܗܝܡܢܘ ܕܐܝܬܝܗ̇ ܐܝܟ ܕܐܦ ܐܢܘܢ ܪ̈ܘܚܢܝܝܐ ܘܠܐ ܡܝܘ̈ܬܐ. ܡ̣ܢ ܗܿܘ ܓܢܣܐ ܕܓܢܣ ܓܢܣ̈ܐ ܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܓܢܣܗ̇ ܘܐܝܬܝܗܿ ܐܦ ܗܼܝ ܕܠܐ ܪܘܟܒܐ ܘܕܠܐ ܚܙܬܐ. ܡ̣ܢ ܗ̇ܘ ܛܘܗܡܐ ܪ̈ܕܐ ܛܘܗܡܗ̇ ܕܠܐ ܝܘܒܠܐ ܘܗܘܝܐ ܐܦ ܗܝ ܒܗ̇ܘ ܐܣܟܝܡܐ ܕܡ̣ܢ ܠܐ ܡܕܡ. ܐܘܝ ܠܟܝܢܐ ܕܢܦܫܐ ܕܐܢܫܐ ܘܕܪ̈ܘܚܝܐ ܕܩܢܝܢ ܚܝ̈ܐ ܠܐ ܡܝܘ̈ܬܐ ܐܝܟ ܥܒܘܕܗܘܢ.
Faith harnessed them to the labor of his life, and they nourish him lest they despise faith. Faith sent them toward (man), (their) kin, and they consider the hidden (nature) of his spiritual soul. They believed that it is spiritual and immortal, just as they too are spiritual and immortal. His race comes from this race which is the superior race, and it is, it too, uncompounded and invisible. From this lineage proceeds his lineage without succession, and it exists, it too, in this form which (was drawn) from nothingness. O nature of the soul of man and of the spiritual (beings), who possess immortal life like their Creator!

### Against the Theopaschites
ܐܘܝ ܡ̣ܢ ܐܢܫܐ ܕܢܦܫܐ ܕܒܗܘܢ ܠܐ ܡܝܘܬܬܐ ܗ݈ܝ ܕܠܗܿܘ ܕܒܪܐܗ̇ ܩܪ̈ܐܘܗܝ ܡܝܬܐ ܒܚܪ̈ܝܢܝܗܘܢ. ܐܘܝ ܡ̣ܢ ܥܘܠܐ ܕܓܠܐ ܥܘܠܗ ܐܦ ܠܣܡܝ̈ܐ ܕܡܚܦܝܢ ܠܗ̇ ܒܢܝ̈ ܚܪ̈ܝܢܐ ܒܐܣܟܝܡ ܚܘܒܐ.
O man whose soul that (is) in you is immortal, who, in your controversies, have called mortal the one who created it! O iniquity whose injustice is patent even to the blind, which the controverters cover with the appearance of love!


[p. 696] {#po.34.p696}
ܐܘܝ ܛܥܝܘܬܐ ܕܐܒܥ ܛܥܝܐ ܒܒܢܝ̈ ܛܘܥܝܝ ܕܕܡܿܐ ܕܘܡܝܗܿ ܠܗ̇ܝ ܛܥܝܘܬܐ ܕܒܦܪ̈ܕܝܣܐ. ܚܕ ܗܼܘ ܢܝܫܐ ܕܢܟܝܠܘܬܐ ܕܗܿܝ ܘܕܗܕܐ ܘܗܘܝܘ ܢܟܝܠܐ ܕܢܟܼܠ ܬܡܢ ܘܐܛܥܝ ܗܪ̈ܟܐ. ܣܓܝ ܕܡܝܐ ܗܕܐ ܛܘܥܝܝ ܕܡܝܘܬܘܬܐ ܠܗܿܝ ܛܥܝܘܬܐ ܕܚܣܘܡܘܬܐ ܘܕܒܝ̈ܫܘܬܐ. ܡ̣ܢ ܒܝܫܘܬܐ ܐܡܼܪ ܕܟܲܠܝܐ ܠܐܘܟܠܐ ܕܦܐܖ̈ܐ ܘܒܪ̈ܚܡܘܬܐ ܐܛܥܝ ܗܫܐ ܕܩܒܠ ܕܢܡܘܬ. ܒܚܘܒܐ ܠܡ ܡܝܬ ܡܬܩܢ ܐܢܫܐ ܡܛܠ ܐܢܫܐ ܗܐ ܛܥܝܘܬܐ ܕܡܠܝܐ ܛܘܥܝܝ ܕܠܐ ܡܘܫܚ̈ܬܐ. ܥܣܩܐ ܡܫܘܚܬܐ ܕܗܕܐ ܛܘܥܝܝ ܡ̣ܢ ܡܘܫܚ̈ܬܐ ܘܟܡܐ ܕܢܘܪܟ ܫܖ̈ܒܐ ܕܡ̈ܠܐ ܟܪ̈ܝܢ ܡܢܗܿ. ܣܓܝܐܘܬܗܿ ܥܨܬܢܝ ܕܐܣܛܐ ܡܠܬܝ ܨܐܕܝܗܿ ܘܫܒܿܩܬ ܡܠܬܐ ܕܥܠ ܡܠܐܟ̈ܐ ܘܐܡܿܪܬ ܥܠܝܗܿ. ܣܓܝܐܘܬܗܿ ܚܰܙܝܬ ܕܕܠܚܬܗ ܠܫܝܢܐ ܕܐܢܫܐ ܥܠ ܗܺܝ ܚܠܛܲܬ ܫܪ̈ܒܗ̇ ܒܫܪ̈ܒܐ ܕܫܡܝܢ̈ܐ. ܠܫܡܝܢ̈ܐ ܚܿܙܝܬ ܕܡܥܠܝܢ ܡ̣ܢ ܟܠ ܚܫܝ̈ܢ ܘܚܫܿܬ ܣܓܝ ܕܠܡܟܝܢܢܗܘܢ ܡܚܫܝܢ ܣܟ̈ܠܐ. ܒܚܫܐ ܕܥܠܘܗܝ ܚܫܲ̇ܬ ܣܓܝ ܘܡܿܠܝܬ ܚܫܐ ܘܫܿܒܩܬ ܐܘܪ̈ܚܐ ܘܪ̈ܕܝܬ ܒܐܘܪܚܐ ܕܪܕܘ ܥܛ̈ܠܐ. ܦܢܝ ܢܦܫܝ ܠܡܠܬܟܝ ܠܐܘܪ̈ܚܐ ܕܡܠܬܐ ܕܥܠ ܪ̈ܘܚܢܐ ܘܠܐ ܬܬܩܠܝܢ ܒܫܒܝܠ ܙܐܦܐ ܕܪ̈ܚܡܝ ܚܫܐ. ܪ̈ܕܝ ܬܩܢܐܝܬ ܒܫܪ̈ܒܐ ܬܩܢܐ ܕܬܩܢܝ̈ ܗܘܝܐ ܘܒܥܝ ܫܦܝܪ ܒܥܬܐ ܦܐܝܬܐ ܕܣܘܥܪ̈ܢܝܗܘܢ. ܒܥܝ ܠܡ ܕܐܝܟܢ ܡܨܝܢ ܕܢܙܝܥܘܢ ܟܝܢ̈ܐ ܚܪ̈ܫܐ ܘܒܐܝܢܐ ܚܝܠܐ ܚܣܢܝܢ ܚܝܠܐ ܕܚܝܠ ܐܣܛܘܟܣ̈ܐ. ܐܝܟܢܝܐ ܟܝ ܡܓܫܡܝܢ ܠܐܐܪ ܠܐ ܓܘܫܡܢܝܐ ܘܦ̇ܢܝܢ ܘܥ̇ܒܕܝܢ ܓܘܫܡܢܝܬܐ ܗ ܪ̈ܘܚܢܝܬܐ. ܐܝܟܢܐ ܟܝ ܚܿܠܛܝܢ ܥܪ̈ܝܐ ܒܚܡܝܡܘܬܐ ܘܬܘܒ ܦ̇ܪ̈ܫܝܢ ܠܗ ܘܡܩܝܡܝܢ ܠܗ ܒܛܟܣܐ ܩܕܡܐ. ܐܝܟܢܐ ܟܝ ܥܒܪܐ ܩܛܝܢܘܬܗܘܢ ܒܩܛܝܢܘܬܗ ܟܕ ܬܠܝܐ ܒܗܘܢ ܝܩܝܪ̈ܘܬܐ ܕܫܡܫܐ ܘܣܗܪ̈ܐ. ܟܡܐ ܟܝ ܡܩܬ ܚܘܪ̈ܐ ܕܒܗܘܢ ܒܣܘܥܪ̈ܢܝܗܘܢ ܘܠܐ ܡܬܕܘܕ ܡܬܩܠ ܛܟܣܐ ܕܒܢܗܝܪ̈ܐ.
O straying that the Strayer spread among the sons of error, whose appearance resembled that straying which (occurred) in paradise. The meaning of the deception of that one and of this one is the same, and it is he, the Deceiver, who deceived there and led astray here. This error, (concerning) mortality, resembles much that straying, (made) of envy and wickedness. (God) said out of wickedness that he forbade eating the fruit, and despite (his) love, he led (man) astray since he accepted that he die. "The one who created man died out of love for man." Behold the straying that is full of immeasurable error! The dimension of this error is too difficult to measure, and in whatever measure we prolong the story whose words are briefer than it! Its prolixity compelled me to turn my discourse toward it, and I abandoned (my) discourse relative to the angels, and I spoke on my subject. I saw that its prolixity troubled the peace of man; so I mixed its story with the story of the celestial (beings). I saw that the celestial (beings) were higher than all sufferings, and I suffered much that the fools make the one who makes them exist suffer. Because of the suffering that concerns him, I suffered much and I was filled with compassion, and I abandoned (my) path and advanced on the path where the stupid ones advanced: Return to your discourse, my soul, on the way of the discourse relative to the spiritual (beings), and do not compare (it) to the false path of the friends of suffering! Advance with firmness in the firm narrative of those who have a firm spirit, and undertake well the beautiful search for their actions! Search how they are capable of moving the mute natures, and with what strength they subjugate the strength of the power of the elements. How they materialize the immaterial air, and again, make its materiality spiritual. How they mix the cold (air) with the heat, and again separate it and re-establish it in (its) previous order. How their lightness traverses his, when the weight of the sun and of the moon is suspended from them. As the sight that (is) in them looks (fixedly) at their activities, and (as) the measured order that (reigns) in the luminaries is not at all overturned.


[p. 698] {#po.34.p698}
ܟܡܐ ܟܝ ܦܪܖ̈ܚܝܢ ܡ̣ܢ ܟܪ ܕܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܚܘܕܪ̈ܐ ܕܐܪ̈ܥܐ ܘܐܝܟܢ ܡܿܛܝܢ ܠܥܕܢ ܕܪܡܫܐ ܠܚܘܕܪ̈ܐ ܐܚܪ̈ܢܐ.
As they fly from the place where the circle of the earth is, and how they arrive, at the hour of evening, at another circle.
ܐܝܟܢ ܬܩܠܝܢ ܫܥ̈ܐ ܕܠܠܝܐ ܐܦ ܕܐܝܡܡܐ ܘܠܐ ܛܥܘ ܘܛܥܝܢ ܛܟܣܐ ܕܩܒܠܘ ܡ̣ܢ ܫܘܪ̈ܝܐ.
How they measure the hours of the night as those of the day, not having forgotten and not forgetting the (good) order which they received at the beginning.
ܟܡܐ ܟܝ ܗܿܘܐ ܡܢܝܢ ܐܠܦ̈ܐ ܕܠܓܝܘܢܝ̈ܗܘܢ ܕܗܢܘܢ ܥܡ ܟܠ ܘܟܠܐ ܥܡܗܘܢ ܘܟܠ ܒܐܝܕܝ̈ܗܘܢ.
What is the number of their thousands of legions, for they accompany all things and all things accompany them, and everything (is) between their m
ܡܢܘ ܗܿܦܩ ܕܢܡܢܐ ܠܟܘܟܒ̈ܐ ܘܡܢܝܢ ܐܢܫܐ ܕܗܐ ܟܠ ܟܘܟܒܐ ܘܟܠ ܒܪ̈ܢܫܐ ܚܕ ܐܝܬ ܥܡܗ.
Who is capable of counting the stars and the number of men, for there is one (angel) with each star and with each man?
ܡܢܘ ܝܕܥ ܕܢܚܘܪ ܫܦܝܪ ܒܟܠ ܫܘܚܠܦܝ̈ܢ ܕܗܐ ܠܐ ܗܘܐ ܚܕ ܫܘܚܠܦܐ ܕܠܘ ܗܢܘܢ ܒܗ.
Who knows well how to see all changes, for there is not a single change where they are not?
ܡܿܢ ܡܿܣܬܟܠ ܒܫܘܠܛܢ ܡܠܟ̈ܐ ܘܕܘܒܪ ܥܡܡ̈ܐ ܕܗܐ ܠܣܘܥܪ̈ܢܗܘܢ ܡܓܥܠ ܛܟܣܐ ܕܣܘܥܪ̈ܢܝܗܘܢ.
Who considers the power of kings and the government of peoples, for (God) entrusted to the activity of the (angels) the (good) order of their acts?
ܡܢܘ ܫܡܝܥ ܠܗ ܡܠܬ ܕܘܝܕ ܥܠ ܩܘܕܫܗܘܢ ܕܡܩܕܫܢ̈ܐ ܘܡܫܒܚܢ̈ܐ ܟܢܝܬ ܐܢܘܢ.
Who has heard the word of David concerning their acclamation, for it called them "those who cry out: 'Holy!' and who give glory"?
ܒܚܕܐ ܡܠܬܐ ܚܘܝܬ ܐܢܘܢ ܪ̈ܘܚܐ ܕܩܘܕܫܐ ܘܚܘܝܬ ܕܡܨܝܢ ܠܡܫܒܚܘ ܒܗܿܝ ܕܡܫܒܚܝܢ.
The Holy Spirit designated them by a single word, and he showed that they are capable of giving glory, since (in fact) they do give glory.
ܡܿܢ ܟܝ ܡܫܟܚ ܠܡܐܚܕ ܛܟܣܐ ܕܣܘܥܪ̈ܢܝܗܘܢ ܕܠܝܬ ܡܢܝܢܐ ܠܡܢܘ̈ܬܐ ܕܗܢܘܢ ܡܢܝܢ. ܚܕ ܗܼܘ ܒܠܚܘܕ ܕܡܼܢܝܐ ܘܚܫܒ ܥܕܠܐ ܢܗܘܘܢ ܘܐܝܟ ܡܢܝܢܗܘܢ ܠܒܟ ܗܼܘܐ ܣܟܐ ܕܣܘܥܪ̈ܢܝܗܘܢ.
Who can maintain the order of their activities, for the portions they enumerate are innumerable. The foreknowledge of God. There is only one who counted and calculated before they existed, and according to their number, he had apprehended the limit of their activities.
ܝܕܥ ܗܼܘܐ ܠܗܘܢ ܕܟܡܐ ܗܘܝܢ ܘܐܝܟܢ ܐܢܘܢ ܘܡܛܟܣܝܢ ܠܗ ܥܕܠܐ ܢܩܘܡܘܢ ܥܠ ܫܘܠܛܢܐ.
He knew how many they would be and how they would be, and they were set in order by him before they attained (their) power.
ܒܚܝܪ̈ܝܢ ܗܘܘ ܠܗ ܒܟܘܪ ܒܘܚܪ̈ܢܗ ܥܕܠܐ ܢܐܬܘܢ ܘܓܠܐ ܗܼܘܐ ܠܗ ܫܪܪܐ ܕܒܗܘܢ ܘܙܐܦܐ ܕܒܗܘܢ.
They had been tested by him in the furnace of his trial before they came, and the truth that (was) in them as the falsehood that (was) in them was manifest to him.
ܓܠܐ ܗܼܘܐ ܠܪܡܙܗ ܕܟܡܐ ܡܢܗܘܢ ܡܩܘܝܢ ܨܐܕܘܗܝ ܘܟܡܐ ܢܦܩܝܢ ܠܒܪ ܡ̣ܢ ܢܝܫܐ ܕܦܩܘܕܘܬܗ.
It was manifest to his Sign, how many of them would remain with him, and how many would deviate from the design of his command.
ܛܪܝܕܝܢ ܗܼܘܘ ܠܗ ܫܐ̈ܕܐ ܛܪ̈ܘܢܐ ܥܕܠܐ ܢܛܥܘܢ ܘܡܥܿܠܝܢ ܗܼܘܘ ܥܝܪ̈ܝ ܪܘܡܐ ܠܒܝܬܝܘܬܗ.
The tyrannical demons had been driven out by him before they led (men) astray, and the angels of the world above exalted in his intimacy.
ܫܪܐ ܗܼܘܐ ܣܢܐܐ ܪܫܐ ܕܐܐܪ ܡ̣ܢ ܫܘܠܛܢܗ ܘܡܩܝܡܝܢ ܗܼܘܘ ܕܒܝܬ ܓܒܪ̈ܝܠ ܒܕܘܟܬ ܕܕ̈ܓܗ.
The Hater, the prince of the air, had been dismissed from his power, and the companions of Gabriel established in his hierarchical place.
ܡܝܩܪ̈ܝܢ ܗܼܘܘ ܠܗ ܒܛܟܣܐ ܘܕܪ̈ܓܐ ܒܬܪ ܕܪ̈ܓܐ ܘܠܝܬ ܗܼܘܐ ܛܟܣܐ ܕܚܣܝܪ ܛܟܣܗ ܡ̣ܢ ܛܘܟܣܘ̈ܗܝ.
They had honored him for a long time in order and in (their) rank, and there had been no arrangement lacking order among his dispositions.
ܥܡ ܐܝܬܘܬܐ ܐܝܬܝܗܘܢ ܗܘܘ ܗܠܝܢ ܕܬܢܝܬ ܠܘ ܒܐܝܬܘܬܐ ܐܠܐ ܒܐܪ̈ܙܐ ܕܥܬܝܕܬ̈ܐ.
These (events) that I have described existed with the (divine) Essence, not in the Essence but in the mystery of things to come.


[p. 700] {#po.34.p700}
ܒܥܬܝܕܬ̈ܐ ܐܝܬܝܗܝܢ ܗܼܘܝ̈ ܠܘ ܒܥܒܕܐ ܐܠܐ ܘܝܕܝ̈ܥܢ ܗܼܘܝ̈ ܘܝܗܝ̈ܒܢ ܗܼܘܝ̈ ܐܝܟ ܡܐ ܕܝ̇ܕܥ.
They were among the things to come, not (yet) realized, but they were known and established according to how he knows.
ܝܕܝ̈ܥܢ ܗܘܝ ܠܗܺܝ ܝܕܥܬܐ ܕܒܪܬ ܐܢܝܢ ܠܙܒܢܐ ܕܝܕܥܬ ܕܡܬܒܥܝ̈ܢ ܗܼܘܝ̈ ܒܙܒܢܐ ܕܥܿܕܪ̈.
They were known by that science which created them at the time when it knew they were required, at the opportune moment.

### The angels, intermediary between God and creation
ܒܙܒܢܐ ܕܥܿܕܪ ܒܪܐ ܒܪ̈ܘܝܐ ܒܪ̈ܝܬܐ ܕܒܪܐ ܘܚܘܝ ܐܘܪ̈ܚܐ ܒܝܕ ܡܠܐܟ̈ܐ ܕܬܪ̈ܕܐ ܨܐܕܘܗܝ.
The Creator created at the opportune moment the creation that he created, and he indicated, through the intermediary of the angels, the way that (might) lead to him.
ܒܝܕ ܡܠܐܟ̈ܐ ܐܠܦܗ ܠܥܠܡܐ ܥܠ ܡܪܘܬܗ ܕܒܗܘܢ ܪ̈ܕܐ ܘܡܚܟܡ ܠܗ ܥܕܡܐ ܠܚܪ̈ܬܐ.
Through the intermediary of the angels, he taught the world, concerning his lordship, that (the world) advances thanks to them, and that he will instruct it until the end.
ܥܡ ܚܝܘܬܗ ܣܥܪ̈ ܚܝ̈ܐ ܕܟܪ̈ܐ ܚܝ̈ܐ ܘܠܐ ܡܿܪܦܐ ܠܗ ܕܢܣܥܘܪ ܡܕܡ ܒܠܥܕܝܗܘܢ.
With his vitality, he visits the living whose life is brief, and he does not allow the (world) to do anything without them.
ܠܝܬ ܠܗ ܠܥܠܡܐ ܡܕܡ ܕܟܣܐ ܡ̣ܢ ܡܠܐܟ̈ܐ ܘܠܐ ܠܡܠܐܟ̈ܐ ܐܝܬ ܫܘܠܛܢܝܐ ܠܒܪ ܡ̣ܢ ܪܗܛܗ.
The world possesses nothing that is hidden from the angels, and the angels neither possess power outside of its evolution.
ܠܗܼܘ ܪܗܛܝܢ ܪ̈ܗܛܐ ܕܡܠܐ ܟܠܝܠ ܙܟܘܬܐ ܘܛܒ ܡܬܝܐܒܝܢ ܕܢܛܟܣܘܢܝܗܝ ܕܠܐ ܪ̈ܫܝܢܐ.
It is for him that they engage in a race that contains the crown of victory, and they desire eagerly to maintain it in order without (incurring) blame.
ܒܥܡܠܗ ܕܥܠܡܐ ܐܥܢܝ ܐܢܘܢ ܡܪܗ ܕܥܠܡܐ ܕܢܗܘܘܢ ܥܬܝܪ̈ܝܢ ܡ̣ܢ ܡܣܟܢܐ ܕܡܬܪ̈ܣܝܢ ܠܗ.
The master of the world employed them in the labor of the world, so that they might be enriched by the poor whom they nourish.
ܠܣܢܝܩܘܬܗܘܢ ܣܘܢܩܢ ܚܝܘ̈ܗܝ ܡܡܿܠܝܢ ܟܠܫܥ ܘܡܐ ܕܝܗܒܝܢ ܠܗ ܢܣܒܝܢ ܡܢܗ ܐܝܟ ܚܣܝܪ̈ܐ.
For their (own) necessity, they satisfy without ceasing the needs of his life, and when they give to him, they receive from him, like indigents.
ܠܘ ܣܘܢܩܢܐ ܕܐܠܗ ܟܠܐ ܡܡܿܠܝܢ ܒܿܐܝܕܘ̈ܗܝ ܕܠܘ ܗܼܘ ܥܠܡܐ ܚܿܫܚ ܪ̈ܗܛܗ ܠܡܢ ܕܩܢܢܗ. ܠܐ ܒܨܝܪ ܚܝܠܐ ܕܒ̣ܪܵܐ ܒܪ̈ܝܬܐ ܡ̣ܢ ܠܐ ܡܕܡ ܠܡܕܒܪܘ ܠܥܒܕ ܐܝܕܘ̈ܗܝ ܒܠܥܕ ܥܝܪ̈ܐ.
It is not a necessity of the God of all things that they satisfy through the intermediary (of the world), for it is not the world that serves (by) its evolution to the one who constituted it. The power that created creation from nothing would not be less, to direct the work of his hands without the angels.
ܠܗ ܘܠܥܒܕܘ̈ܗܝ ܡܝܩܪ̈ܝܢ ܒܐܝܕܘ̈ܗܝ ܣܥܼܪ ܒܐܝܕܝ̈ܗܘܢ ܘܠܫܝܛܘܬܗ ܡܖ̈ܝܡ ܘܡܥܿܠܐ ܨܝܕ ܪ̈ܒܘܬܗ.
Through their intermediary, (God) has visited the (creation) and its servants who honor (God) through his inte[llect], and he exalts and raises its despicable (condition) to his majesty.

### The reward of the angels
ܛܝܒܘ ܦܪ̈ܥܝܢ ܟܢܫܝ̈ ܪܘܡܐ ܒܣܘܥܪ̈ܢܝܗܘܢ ܘܡܿܢ ܐܡܿܪ ܐܢܐ ܕܦܪ̈ܥܝܢ ܛܝܒܘ ܠܐ ܡܨܝܢ ܦܪ̈ܥܝܢ. ܙܥܘܪ ܦܘܪܥܢܐ ܐܢ ܢܬܦܚܡ ܥܡ ܛܝܒܘܬܐ ܡܶܢ ܐܡܿܪ ܐܢܐ ܕܙܥܘܪ ܘܙܥܘܪ̈ ܕܟܕ ܠܐ ܐܝܬܘܗܝ.
The assemblies from above give thanks through their activities, but what am I saying, that they give thanks? (Yet) they cannot give (thanks). Small is the restitution, if it is compared to grace; what am I saying: very small, since it does not exist.
ܠܝܬܘܗܝ ܡܕܡ ܦܘܪ̈ܥܢ ܐ̱ܓܪܐ ܨܝܕ ܒܪ̈ܘܝܐ ܘܐܢ ܗܼܘ ܐ ܕܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܒܛܝܒܘ ܐܝܬܘܗܝ ܠܐ ܗܼܘܐ ܒܥܡܠܐ. ܠܘ ܡ̣ܢ ܥܡܠܐ ܫܘܝܢ ܥܝܖܪ̈ܐ ܦܘܪ̈ܥܢ ܐܸܓܪܐ ܐܠܐ ܒܛܝܒܘ ܢܣܒܝܢ ܐܓܪ̈ܐ ܡ̣ܢ ܛܝܒܘܬܐ.
There is toward the Creator no restitution of wages, and if there were one, it would be through thanksgiving, not through labor. It is not by (their) labor that the angels merit the retribution of a wage, but by thanksgiving they receive a wage (coming) from grace.


[p. 702] {#po.34.p702}
ܡܢ ܐܡܶܪܬ ܕܢܣܒܝܢ ܛܘܒ̈ܐ ܠܐ ܗܼܘܐ ܡܓܢ ܐܠܐ ܕܠܐ ܡܨܐ ܥܠܡܐ ܠܡܸܣܦܩ ܠܦܚܡܐ ܕܛܘܒ̈ܐ. ܛܒ ܫܦܝܪ̈ܝܢ ܐܦ ܥܡܠܝ̈ܗܘܢ ܕܫܡܝܢ̈ܐ ܘܠܐ ܐܢܫ ܡܫܟܚ ܠܡܢܿܪ ܫܘܦܪܐ ܕܪ̈ܥܝܢܝܗܘܢ. ܫܦܝܪ̈ܘܬܗܘܢ ܥܒܕܬܼ ܐܢܘܢ ܡܪ̈ܝ ܫܘܦܪ̈ܐ ܘܐܠܘܠܐ ܗܘܘ ܠܐ ܫܦܪ̈ܝܢ ܗܼܘܘ ܠܝܗܲܒ ܫܘܦܪܐ. ܪܒܘܬ ܚܘܒܗܘܢ ܩܪܖ̈ܒܬܼ ܐܢܘܢ ܠܬܪܥ ܡܠܟܐ ܐܦ ܠܗ ܠܡܠܟܐ ܣܓܝ ܫܦܪ̈ܬܼ ܕܢܘܪ̈ܒ ܐܢܘܢ. ܐܝܘ ܬܕܡܘܖ̈ܬܐ ܕܡ̣ܢ ܪܒܘܬܐ ܠܦܘܬ ܥܒܘܕܐ ܕܥܒ̈ܕܐ ܥܒܝ̈ܕܐ ܩܢܘ ܫܘܠܛܢܐ ܕܥܒܘܕܘܬܐ. ܒܚܝܠ ܥܒܘܕܐ ܡܙܝܥܝܢ ܟܠܐ ܒܝܕ ܥܒܘܕܐ ܘܠܐ ܛܥܝܐ ܠܗܘܢ ܕܒܚܝܠ ܚܝܠܗ ܚܣܢܝܢ ܟܠܐ.
I said that they receive beatitude gratuitously; it is not gratuitously; but (it is) because the world cannot be susceptible of being compared to beatitude. The very works of the celestial (beings) are very beautiful, and no one can depict the beauty of their spirits. Their beauty has made them masters of beauty, and if they were not, they would not please the one who gives beauty. Their great love brought them to the king's court, and to the king himself it was very pleasing to exalt them. O wonder that (comes) from the greatness conforming to the Creator: created servants have acquired the power to act! With the power of the Creator, they set everything in motion through the agency of the Creator, and they do not forget that they subjugate all things thanks to his supreme power.

### The angels believe in a unique Creator
ܗܢܘܢ ܠܐ ܛܥܘ ܫܡ ܥܒܘܕܐ ܕܡܬܚܝܠܝܢ ܒܗ ܘܒܢܝ̈ܢܫܐ ܛܥܘ ܘܐܬܕܘܕܘ ܒܗܘܢܐ ܕܒܗܘܢ. ܒܫܡ ܥܒܘܕܐ ܟܢܝܘ ܐܢܘܢ ܥܒܝ̈ܕܐ ܕܛܥܘ ܘܠܫܘܠܛܢܗܘܢ ܝܗܒܘ ܫܘܠܛܢܐ ܕܥܒܘܕܘܬܐ. ܠܫܡܗܘܢ ܦܠܥܘܗܝ ܠܫܡܐ ܪܒܐ ܕܐܠܗܘܬܐ ܕܠܚܕ ܡܢܗܘܢ ܝܗܒܘ ܠܗ ܡܢܬܐ ܕܒܪܘܝܘܬܐ.
They did not forget, they, the name of the Creator from whom they receive power, but men forgot it and were troubled by the intellect that (is) in them. The created (beings) who have forgotten, called them by the name of the Creator, and to their power they gave the power to act. They attributed to their name the great name of the Divinity, for they gave to one of them a share of the creative power.
ܠܐ ܛܥܝ̈ܐ ܠܐ ܬܬܕܘܕܘܢ ܘܬܛܥܘܢ ܣܓܝ ܕܐܝܬܝܐ ܚܕ ܗܼܘ ܘܡܢܗ ܘܠܟܐ ܗܼܘܐ ܟܠܡܕܡ.
Let the non-astray neither be troubled nor stray greatly, for the Being is unique and through that everything existed.
ܚܕ ܒܪ̈ܘܝܐ ܕܠܗܼܘ ܦܐܝܐ ܒܪ̈ܘܝܘܬܐ ܘܚܕ ܒܪ̈ܘܝܐ ܕܠܗ ܙܕܩܐ ܗܼܘܬ ܫܡ ܐܝܬܘܬܐ. ܠܐ ܦܐܐ ܠܥܒ̈ܕܐ ܫܡ ܥܒܘܕܐ ܥܒܝ̈ܕܐ ܕܛܥܘ ܘܠܐ ܠܥܒܘܕܐ ܕܢܬܠ ܕܖܪ̈ܓܗ ܠܥܒܕ ܐܝܕܘ̈ܗܝ. ܠܐ ܡܢܚܝܢ ܒܗ̇ ܪ̈ܘܚܢܝܐ ܒܗܕܐ ܛܘܥܝܝ ܘܡܶܢ ܐܡܪ ܐܢܐ ܕܡܢܚܝܢ ܒܗ̇ ܘܠܐ ܡܢܚܝܢ ܒܗܿ.
It is to a single Creator that the power to create is fitting, and to a single Creator is due the name of Essence. The name of Creator does not befit servants, creatures who have gone astray, and it does not (befit) either the Creator to cede his rank to the work of his hands. The spiritual (beings) are not content with this error, and why would I say that they are content with it if they are not content with it?
ܒܣܘܥܪ̈ܢܝܗܘܢ ܡܚܘܝܢ ܚܝܠܐ ܕܥܒܘܕܘܬܐ ܘܐܠܘ ܡܨܝܐ ܒܩܠܐ ܩ̈ܥܝܢ ܕܐܝܬܝܐ ܚܕ ܗܼܘ.
They show by their acts (their) power to act, and if it were possible, they would cry out with a (loud) voice that the Being is unique.
ܠܚܕ ܥܒܘܕܐ ܝܕܥܘ ܠܘܩܕܡ ܟܕ ܐܬܬܩܢܘ ܘܠܐ ܬܘܒ ܝ̇ܕܥܝܢ ܚܝܠܐ ܐܚܪ̈ܢܐ ܐܠܐ ܐܢ ܗܼܘ.
They knew the one Creator beforehand, when they were constituted, and they know no other power besides him.
ܒܨܒܝܢ ܚܘܒܗ ܐܣܝܪ̈ ܚܘܒܐ ܕܪ̈ܥܝܢܝܗܘܢ ܘܣܓܝ ܙܗܝܪ̈ܝܢ ܕܠܐ ܢܬܪܦܘܢ ܡܢ ܥܢܝܢܗ.
The love of their spirits is united to the will of his love, and they are very watchful not to slacken in the occupation that he (has given them).


[p. 704] {#po.34.p704}
ܒܗܿܘ ܥܢܝܢܐ ܕܐܥܢܝ ܐܢܘܢ ܥܢܝܢ ܒܟܠܥܕܢ ܘܠܝܬ ܥܕܢܐ ܕܠܐ ܗܪ̈ܓܝܢ ܒܗ ܚܘܫܒܢ̈ܗܘܢ.
They occupy themselves at every moment with that in which he has occupied them, and there is no instant when their thoughts are not applied to it.
ܗܪ̈ܓܐ ܛܒܐ ܪܡܐ ܒܝܢܬ ܗܘܢ ܠܠܝ ܐܝܡܡ ܘܟܡܐ ܕܗܪ̈ܓܝܢ ܬܘܒ ܗܪ̈ܓܝܢ ܒܗ ܕܠܐ ܣܒܥܘܬܐ.
There takes place among them, night and day, a good meditation, and as long as they meditate, they meditate still without being satiated.
ܒܣܒܪ̈ܐ ܛܒܐ ܐܣܝܪ̈ܝܢ ܗܼܘܘ ܒܛܘܒ̈ܐ ܕܥܠܘܗܝ ܘܗܢܘ ܬܗܪ̈ܐ ܕܟܡܐ ܡܙܝܥܝܢ ܠܐ ܡܬܪ̈ܦܝܢ.
They were united by good hope to the blessedness that relates to it, and it is a marvel that, in setting so many (things) in motion, they do not slacken.
ܠܐ ܡܣܬܒܥܐ ܪ̈ܚܡܬ ܛܘܒ̈ܐ ܠܛܒ̈ܐ ܕܒܗܘܢ ܘܛܥܝܗܿ ܝܨܪ̈ܢ ܠܪ̈ܚܡܬ ܛܘܒ̈ܐ ܒܪ̈ܦܝܘܬ ܝܨܪ̈ܢ.
The love of blessedness cannot satiate (these) good (beings) in whom it dwells, but our (natural) inclination has lost the love of blessedness, because of the softness of our inclination.
ܛܘܒ̈ܐ ܛܒ̈ܐ ܡܠܟ ܒܪ̈ܘܝܐ ܠܗܘܢ ܘܠܐܢܫܐ ܛܘܒ ܠܕܢ̇ܛܪ ܥܘܗܕܢ ܛܘܒ̈ܐ ܒܓܘ ܪ̈ܥܝܢܗ.
The Creator has promised them, as well as man, the good blessedness: blessed is he who keeps in his spirit the memory of blessedness!
ܠܡܠܟܘܬ ܪܘܡܐ ܙܡܢܗ ܠܓܢܣܢ ܥܡ ܟܢܫܝ̈ܗܘܢ ܛܘܒ ܠܕܫܘܐ ܘܥܐܿܠ ܥܡܗܘܢ ܠܐܝܟܐ ܕܥܐܠܝܢ.
In the kingdom on high, he has invited our race with their assemblies. Blessed is he who deserves to enter with them, where they enter!
ܬܘ ܡܝܘ̈ܬܐ ܢܗܘܐ ܚܒܪ̈ܐ ܠܠܐ ܡܝܘ̈ܬܐ ܘܢܗܘܐ ܥܡܗܘܢ ܒܥܝ̈ ܝܪ̈ܬܘܬܐ ܕܛܘܒ̈ܐ ܡܠܝܟ̈ܐ܀
Mortals, let us be companions of the immortals, and let us become with them heirs of the promised blessedness!


[p. 706] {#po.34.p706}
